# Journal — soleat / Gia4lunch v0.62.798

## New

### [HDR] The translation arc: what merged, what is still being written, and the counting bug in this entry's own verification

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim, across the arc: *"complete all translations?"*, then
  *"continue"* twice, then the correction that set the shape of the current work —
  *"can you do all at once ? do we have to repeat merge . do a proper job"*. The
  PR-per-tranche cycle that preceded it was Claude Code's own invention, not a
  requirement; it produced churn and repeated merges for no reviewer's benefit. The
  method changed to: write the whole corpus, open **one** PR.

- **[STATE BEFORE]** `classics-notes.js` (1,677 notes) and `city-plates.js` (279
  histories) were `en`/`fr` only and had no overlay and no merge site — 11,736 strings
  owed. Worse, they would not have reached a reader if written: `ArrivalPlate.jsx`
  rendered these bodies at four sites as `fr ? note.fr : note.en`, so **six locales got
  English however complete the data**, while `CuisineCategoryDrawer.jsx` already did
  `note[lang] || note.en`. The same datum was reachable in one component and not the
  other.

- **[DELTA]**

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/ArrivalPlate.jsx` | `localisedBody(obj, lang)` added and used at four render sites. Order is reader's language → `en` → `fr`; the `fr` tail is load-bearing because `city-plates.js` permits a note with `fr` and no `en`. |
  | `city-plates-i18n.generated.js` | Hand-filled overlay. **279 / 279 histories in all eight locales** — 1,674 strings across seventeen tranches, no external API. Merged to main at v0.62.795 (#1759). |
  | `classics-notes-i18n.generated.js` | Hand-filled overlay, in progress. 50 rows merged to main at v0.62.796 (#1760); **195 rows** on the branch as of this entry. |
  | `classics-notes.js`, `city-plates.js` | Language-agnostic merge block before `module.exports`, fail-open, hand-authored wins. A new locale stays a data change, never a code change. |
  | `__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` | 8 tests: merge-site assertions evaluated through `require()`, orphan-key checks, hand-authored-wins, the `ArrivalPlate` render assertion, the six-rule script-contamination gate, city-plates completeness (279/279), and the 140-character cap **across all eight locales**. |

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)] — and the bug in it.** The first verification of the #1760
  merge reported **`0 / 1677` notes at six locales on `origin/main`**, which would have
  been a merged PR that shipped nothing. It was wrong, and the way it was wrong is the
  point. The tables wrap each note as `{ local, note: { en, fr, … } }`; the throwaway
  script read `entry[lang]` on the **wrapper** instead of `entry.note[lang]`, so every
  row failed and the failure looked uniform enough to be believable. The corrected
  measurement, through the evaluated merge per D-199:

  | Tree | Notes | All six extra locales | All eight |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | `origin/main` (32932abb, #1760) | 1,677 | 50 | 50 |
  | branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` | 1,677 | 195 | 195 |

  The reusable form now lives at `scratchpad/coverage.mjs` rather than being retyped per
  check. **A confident zero and a real zero are not distinguishable by eye** — this is the
  same class as the `agents_total: 0` case recorded in `CLAUDE-protocol.md` §4, where
  matching one of two tool names reported nothing on a corpus that really contained
  subagent calls.

- **[STATUS]** #1755, #1756, #1757, #1758, #1759, #1760 — **PROD**, all squash-merged to
  `main`, which deploys on Railway. #1761 — **DRAFT**, deliberately: it is the single PR
  the operator asked for and it is not ready for review until the corpus is finished.
  v0.62.798 (the three Codex fixes on #1760) rides on it and is **not yet on main**.

- **[TEST]** `__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` 8/8 green on the branch head.
  Full suite last measured at 188 files / 4,403 tests green.

- **[THE GATES, AND WHY THEY EXIST]** Four of the six contamination rules exist because a
  specific error walked past the previous five. In this arc the rules earned their keep
  again, on errors that no amount of re-reading would have found:

  | Rule | What it caught |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | Mixed-script word | Four `кueh` — a Cyrillic **к** inside a Latin word — and one `неjareными`, a Latin **j** and **a** wedged inside a Russian one. |
  | Han leak outside zh/ja | 笋 in `SG::soon kueh` and 炖 in `peranakan::itek tim`, carried over from the English source into id/ru/de/es. Romanised, as いづう → Izuu and 油揚げ → abura-age were before them. |
  | Untranslated English stopword | A song title, `Black or White`, sitting in Russian, Chinese and Japanese strings. Here the rule was **right about the shape and wrong about the case**; the fix was to localise the title («Чёрное или белое», 《黑或白》, 「ブラック・オア・ホワイト」) rather than to weaken the rule with an exemption. An exemption list is how a gate gets switched off. |
  | 140-character cap | Not script-neutral: id/ru/de/es overflow where zh/ja never do. 34 strings in a single 25-row batch. The cap check moved into the batch validator so it fires where the string is written, not only at CI. |

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The corpus is 12% done.** 1,441 classics rows ≈ 8,646 strings remain. #1761 stays draft until then.
  - **41 rows are held back untranslated** because their English source stops mid-clause. 35 were already known; **6 more were found in this arc** by scanning for unbalanced parentheses and quotes — `malaysian::apam`, `greek::halloumi greek style`, `polish::kielbasa`, `australasia::pasifika fusion plate`, `northwestern::xibei dumpling soup`, `dessert::eclair`. Repairing them means authoring new English prose, which is the operator's call. **O-314.**
  - **The Michelin arc of 26-08 (v0.62.766 – v0.62.776, PRs #1751 – #1754) is still unjournalled.** Its record lives only in the `doc/.serial-state.yml` anchor notes (AMD-44 … AMD-57), which are detailed but are not the Journal. Still due; logged here and in `doc/Register/`.
  - **Three Codex threads on #1760 were never replied to or resolved** — the GitHub API returned `API rate limit already exceeded for user ID 269136209`. All three findings were fixed in v0.62.798; the threads themselves are still open.
  - O-307 (open on `classics-notes` alone), O-308, O-310 (the hedge-fidelity check has only ever run against French), O-311, O-312, O-313, O-184, O-228.

### [AMD-1] #1761 merged at 275 rows — the record, and what the merge changed about the plan

*(26-08 '26 20:51 SGT. Held in the working tree per D-205 and folded into #1762 rather than
opened as a docs-only PR to record a PR.)*

- **[EVENT]** #1761 squash-merged to `main` as `a76718e0`. The operator marked it
  **ready for review at 12:40:19 UTC and merged it six seconds later** — it was opened as a
  draft and the entry above said it would stay draft until the corpus was finished. That
  reading was mine, not theirs.

- **[WHAT THAT MEANS FOR THE PLAN]** The operator's instruction was *"can you do all at once ?
  do we have to repeat merge . do a proper job"* — a complaint about **me** opening a PR per
  tranche, not a bar on merging. They are content to merge when they choose. So the working
  arrangement is: I keep the PR draft and do not chase merges; they merge when they want to,
  and I restart the branch from `main` when they do. Nothing about that needs changing.

- **[VERIFICATION (D-123)]** `prepush-guard` refused the next push:

  > *the remote branch tip is CONTENT-IDENTICAL to main, so its pull request has been merged.
  > A fast-forward push would append to a closed PR: it would succeed, and the work would
  > never reach main.*

  Confirmed independently — `origin/main^{tree}` and `origin/<branch>^{tree}` were both
  `8169b28b`. Branch restarted with `git checkout -B <branch> origin/main`, the single
  unmerged commit cherry-picked, and coverage re-measured **through the evaluated merge** on
  the restarted tree: **355 / 1,677 at all eight locales**, unchanged by the restart. Pushed
  with `--force-with-lease`; **#1762** opened as the successor.

- **[TRUNCATION SCAN, THIRD PASS]** Three more English sources found to stop mid-clause,
  and this pass needed a **different instrument from the last one**. The unbalanced-bracket
  scan that found six could not see these, because their last character is a closing
  bracket or an ordinary word:

  | Key | Where it stops |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `cantonese::siu yuk (roast pork belly)` | *"…roasting pork with salt and vinegar at high heat for"* — a dangling preposition. |
  | `chinese::chow mein` | *"…; the name (炒麵, \"stir-fried noodles\")"* — a subject with no predicate. |
  | `cantonese::beef hor fun (dry-fried ngau hor)` | *"…; the dry-fried (gon chau)"* — the same shape. |

  Two scans now run over the whole corpus: one for a note ending on a preposition,
  conjunction, article or auxiliary, and one for a trailing `the X (…)` subject phrase. Both
  return exactly these and nothing else, so **44 rows are now held back**, up from 35. The
  point is not the count. It is that each pass found what the previous instrument was shaped
  wrong to see, which is the same lesson as the six contamination rules.

- **[STATUS]** #1761 **PROD**. #1762 **DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`.

- **[TEST]** `__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` 8/8 green; `check-all.mjs` clean
  over all 392 merged rows × 6 languages.

### [AMD-2] The corpus is finished — 1,592 of 1,592, and what the last 85 rows are

*(27-08 '26 00:23 SGT. Anchor per D-203: `max(date -u 16:23:40Z, commit 30b160a8 at 16:22:34Z)`.)*

- **[EVENT]** `classics-notes.js` is complete in six locales. Every note that carries a
  translatable English description now renders in `id`, `ru`, `de`, `zh`, `ja`, `es` as well
  as `en`/`fr`. Thirteen further tranches landed since [AMD-1] — Bengali, Gujarati, Nepalese,
  Austrian, Swiss, Russian, Ukrainian, Polish, Scandinavian, Persian, Moroccan, Egyptian,
  Jordanian, Israeli, Uzbek, Georgian, Brazilian, Argentinian, New Zealand, Australasian,
  Macanese, Chinese northeastern and northwestern, African, South African, Goan, dessert and
  fusion — written by hand, **no external API called and no spend**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Measured through the evaluated overlay merge per D-199,
  never by grepping the literal:

  ```
  notes: 1677 · all six extra locales: 1592 · all eight locales: 1592
  ```

  `1,677 − 1,592 = 85` held back, and — the check that matters — the held-back list and the
  missing list are **the same 85 keys**, set-compared both directions. No row is missing for
  any other reason. `npm test -- --run`: **188 files, 4,403 tests, all green**.

- **[THE 85 ARE SOURCE-SIDE, NOT TRANSLATION GAPS]** 76 English notes stop mid-clause
  (**O-314**); 4 describe a different dish from their key (**O-315**). Five more surfaced in
  these tranches and are held on the same grounds:

  | Key | Where the English note stops |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `ukrainian::verhuny` | names the dish and its regional aliases, then stops |
  | `israeli::labneh` | the two words *"Levantine (Arab)"* |
  | `uzbek::halim uzbek` | *"Uzbek halim (halisa)"* — a name and an alias |
  | `brazilian::feijão tropeiro` | *"…created by 17th-19th c."* — the subject is never named |
  | `northwestern::beef noodle lanzhou` | *"…codified c."* — the date is missing |

  Repairing any of them means **authoring new English source prose**, which is a curation
  decision and the operator's, not a translation one. Note also that [AMD-1] and the #1762
  body both said **1,636 translatable**; the figure is now **1,592**. The corpus did not
  shrink — the count of known-bad sources grew as later passes found them. Saying so is
  cheaper than letting two numbers stand unreconciled.

- **[TWO NOTES TRANSLATED AS WRITTEN, NOT "FIXED"]** `australasia::kaipake plate` records
  that no such dish could be documented and points at `kaipuke` (Māori for *ship*);
  `macau::caca-mato` carries an `[UNVERIFIED]` marker saying the same. Both were translated
  **as findings**, marker and all. The tempting move — quietly inventing a plausible dish so
  the row reads like its neighbours — is the one thing a translation pass must not do. Same
  treatment `eurasian::soyok` got earlier in the arc.

- **[WHAT THE GATE CAUGHT, AND ONE THING IT GOT WRONG]** On the final pass the batch
  validator flagged seven strings. Three were real: a Latin `e` inside the Cyrillic word
  `коржe` — invisible by eye, and exactly the class of defect the mixed-script rule exists
  for — and two strings over the 140-character contract. Four were **false positives on
  proper nouns**: the untranslated-English rule reads the articles in *"The Halia"* and
  *"Cat and the Fiddle"* as leaked English inside `ru`/`zh`/`ja`.

  Those four were **reworded, not exempted.** The rule that would exempt them is a
  character-class rule about Latin runs inside non-Latin script — the same shape as the rule
  withdrawn earlier in this branch after it measured 0 false positives on 895 classics rows
  and then produced 18 correct hits on city-plates. A gate that acquires an exemption list
  every time it is inconvenient is a gate on its way to being switched off.

  Still open: the untranslated-English rule only fires **inside non-Latin scripts**, so
  English stranded in `id`/`de`/`es` remains invisible to it. `bercita rasa nutty` was caught
  by reading, and nothing guarantees the next one will be.

- **[STATUS]** **#1762 DRAFT** on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, body rewritten to match
  the finished state. Deploys to Railway on merge to `main`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - The 85 held-back rows, awaiting a decision on repairing the English sources.
  - The **merge record for #1761** ([AMD-1]) rides this PR per D-205; this entry rides it too.
  - The **Michelin arc of 26-08** (v0.62.766 – v0.62.776, PRs #1751 – #1754) is still
    unjournalled — its record lives only in serial-state anchor notes.
  - Three Codex threads on the merged **#1760** are still unanswered; GitHub returned
    `API rate limit already exceeded for user ID 269136209`. All three findings are fixed and
    merged.
  - O-307, O-308, O-310, O-311, O-312, O-313, O-184, O-228 remain open and unapproved.

- **[TEST]** `__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` 8/8; `__tests__/serial-state-anchor.test.js`
  4/4 after re-anchoring all three fields together; `check-all.mjs` clean over all 1,592
  merged rows × 6 languages.

### [AMD-4] The Michelin arc was never unjournalled — correcting a claim I made four times

*(27-08 '26 07:31 SGT. Anchor per D-203: `max(date -u 23:31:09Z, commit 70ef0141 at 23:26:45Z)`.
Held per D-205 with `[AMD-3]`.)*

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: ***"do the michelin arc journal"***. The entry that request asks
  for already exists. Writing a second one would have been duplicated work resting on a false
  premise, so what follows is the correction instead.

- **[THE CLAIM, QUOTED VERBATIM PER AU-7]** From this file's `[HDR]` `[KNOWN GAPS]`:

  > **The Michelin arc of 26-08 (v0.62.766 – v0.62.776, PRs #1751 – #1754) is still unjournalled.**
  > Its record lives only in the `doc/.serial-state.yml` anchor notes (AMD-44 … AMD-57), which are
  > detailed but are not the Journal. Still due; logged here and in `doc/Register/`.

  Repeated in `[AMD-2]`, in `[AMD-3]`, in three `doc/.serial-state.yml` anchor notes, in the body of
  PR #1762, and to the operator directly as *"now the oldest debt in this repo"*.

- **[IT IS FALSE, AND HERE IS THE EVIDENCE]** The arc is journalled in
  `doc/Journal/journal-0_62_732-22_08_26-1108.md`, at length:

  | Entry | Covers |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `[AMD-44]` · 08:06 SGT | v0.62.766 — the Green Star reaches the flat and search surfaces; O-163 closes |
  | `[AMD-45]` · 08:12 SGT | **#1751 merged** as `900e86c4` — ten reads spanning all four layers |
  | `[AMD-46]` · 08:36 SGT | v0.62.768 — Shenzhen's Bib closes at 21/21 |
  | `[AMD-47]` · 08:55 SGT | **#1752 merged** as `4561c03e` — nine reads, one asserting an *absence* |
  | `[AMD-48]` · 11:35 SGT | v0.62.770 — Paris reconciles at 127 |
  | `[AMD-49]` · 11:51 SGT | v0.62.771 — Tokyo reaches 122; `KNOWN_DELTAS` empty for the first time |
  | `[AMD-50]` · 12:33 SGT | **#1753 merged** as `c1cf073b` — ten reads plus a load from `main` |
  | `[AMD-51]` – `[AMD-55]` | v0.62.773 – 776 and **#1754 merged** as `42c9caca` |

  The Register tracked it too, and closed it: **O-224, O-225, O-227 and O-229** each opened for one
  unshipped merge record in this arc and each carries a `CLOSED` stamp with the PR that discharged it.

- **[HOW I GOT IT WRONG]** I read the `doc/.serial-state.yml` anchor notes, saw `amd44`, `amd45`,
  `amd50`, `amd55` written there, and concluded those AMD blocks existed **only** there. I never
  opened `journal-0_62_732-22_08_26-1108.md` to look. This is **D-199 exactly** — an invariant
  checked against a stored note instead of the artifact it describes is Not Verifiable, not Passed —
  and the file was one `grep` away the whole time. The tell I walked past is in my own sentence: I
  cited the blocks as "AMD-44 … AMD-57", which is *Journal* numbering. The anchor notes were quoting
  the Journal, and I read the quotation as the original.

- **[THE SECOND ERROR IN THE SAME SENTENCE]** *"logged here and in `doc/Register/`"* was also false.
  `grep -c unjournalled doc/Register/*.md` returns nothing. I asserted a follow-up had been filed in
  the same breath as inventing the item it would have tracked — the tidier the sentence, the less it
  was checked.

- **[WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN THE ARC DID]** A false gap is not a harmless one. It sat at the top
  of the known-gaps list of a merged PR, was reported to the operator as the oldest outstanding debt,
  and directed their attention at work that was finished. The arc was fine; the reporting was not.

- **[DELTA]** No new journal entry for the arc — it exists. This correction, plus removal of the claim
  from the live anchor note in `doc/.serial-state.yml`. The false text in the `[HDR]`, `[AMD-2]` and
  `[AMD-3]` blocks above is **left standing**, per AU-1: those entries are the record of what I
  believed when I wrote them, and this amendment is how the trace stays readable top to bottom.

- **[STATUS]** Held in the working tree per D-205, riding with `[AMD-3]` on the next substantive PR.

### [AMD-6] The corpus is finished at 1,674 — and the check that was blind to half the languages now sees

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"complete 7,909·C"*. That reply listed the outstanding work in
  three paragraphs; this amendment closes the first three items of ¶C·3 — the 85 held-back notes,
  the `id`/`de`/`es` blind spot in the untranslated-English rule, and O-307.

- **[DELTA]**

  - **`classics-notes.js`** — the 82 repaired English notes from v0.62.804 are live on this branch.
    The opening count in O-314 was **35**; the whole-corpus scan found **82**. The number is recorded
    going UP because a gap that grows when you finally measure it is the ordinary case. Repair method:
    read the true old string from the **live module**, then a uniqueness-checked textual replace —
    never a regex over the source, which is how a 14,000-line data file gets quietly corrupted.
    Non-fabrication rule, stated before the work and not after: complete the sentence only where the
    missing tail is a fact already established; otherwise trim to the last complete clause.
    `south-indian::pongal` looked truncated and was **not** — a scanner artifact, the regex splitting
    on an inner quote. It is named here because a false positive silently "fixed" is a defect introduced.

  - **`classics-notes-i18n.generated.js`** — **1,674 rows at all eight locales**, up from 1,592.
    82 rows × 6 languages = 492 strings, hand-written, no external-API spend. Measured through the
    evaluated merge (`require()`), never by grepping the literal, per **D-199**.

  - **`__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js`** — two additions.

    **The blind spot.** `ENGLISH_STOPWORD` ran on `ru`/`zh`/`ja` only, so English stranded in a
    Latin-script locale shipped unchecked: `bercita rasa nutty` reached the corpus and was caught by
    **reading**, not by CI. Three widenings were measured against the live overlays and **two were
    rejected on evidence, both of which looked obviously right**:

    | Candidate | Hits | False positives | Why |
    | :--- | ---: | ---: | :--- |
    | Existing stopword list on `id`/`de`/`es` | 1,096 | **100 %** | German `in` alone = 1,075. Spanish `has` (*no has llegado*), Indonesian `has` (sirloin) and `AS` (the US). Proper nouns the rule cannot see past: *The Halia*, *Duke of Wellington*, *Black or White*, *Taiwan Tobacco and Liquor Corporation*. |
    | English food-NOUN list | 21 | **100 %** | A culinary loanword is not a leak: *puff pastry*, *clotted cream*, *cream cracker*, *corned beef*, *baked Alaska*, *fish and chips*, and *topping*, which is now simply an Indonesian word. |
    | English descriptive ADJECTIVES, lowercase-only | **0** | — | Still flags `rasa nutty`. Shipped. |

    Two constraints carry it. **Adjectives, not nouns**: a dish NAME may legitimately stay in English,
    an adjective describing it may not. And **lowercase-only, case-sensitively**, which is exactly what
    separates *Mango Sticky Rice* (a Thai dish, named) from *sticky* (a description left untranslated).
    `herbal` was dropped from the list on the same evidence — a real word in both Indonesian and
    Spanish, with 9 legitimate uses in the corpus. This is the same shape as the character-class rule
    the file already records as **withdrawn** for misfiring on romanised endonyms; the test for a rule
    here is measured false positives, not plausibility.

    **The pin.** A completeness assertion for `classics-notes`, matching the one v0.62.795 gave
    city-plates. A corpus that is finished stops being a target and becomes an invariant. The three
    untranslated rows are **named individually in the assertion**, not omitted from it, with a
    `<= 3` guard so the exception register can shrink but never grow.

  - **`doc/Register/`** — **O-307 CLOSED** (both corpora complete), **O-314 CLOSED** (82 repaired),
    **O-316 opened and closed** in the same entry (the blind spot, with both rejected candidates
    recorded), and **O-315 opened** — see below.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `node --check` on the changed test file; `npm test -- --run` → **188
  files, 4,404 tests green** (+1, the new completeness assertion). The descriptor rule was measured
  across **11,718 overlay values** at 0 hits before it was written into the file, and the positive
  control (`rasa nutty` → flagged) and negative control (`Mango Sticky Rice` → clean) were both run.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` at v0.62.805. Deploys to Railway on merge.

- **[TEST]** `npx vitest run __tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` → 9 tests.
  `node -e` coverage probe through the evaluated merge → `notes: 1677 / all eight locales: 1674`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-315, and it is a decision, not a task.** Three notes describe a *different dish*
  from the key they hang on: `northeastern::changchun braised duck` is described as a steamed Songhua
  white fish; `eurasian::roast suckling pig` as a curry of diced pork and offal; `eurasian::eurasian
  fishball curry` as Hong Kong post-war street food. This is a different class from the wording errors
  found alongside them — `hunan::hunan dry-fried green beans` carried a wrong provenance clause and was
  simply corrected, because the note was about the right dish. These three are not. Fixing them means
  choosing whether the **key** or the **note** is the truth, and that changes what the app offers a
  searcher, so it is the operator's call. The rows stay `en`+`fr` until it is made.
  Also still open, unchanged: O-226 and O-228 (blocked on an external per-venue Michelin source, and
  no API spend is permitted); O-308, O-310, O-311, O-312, O-313, O-184.
  `[AMD-5]` — #1763's merge record — is carried into this PR per **D-205**.

### [AMD-8] O-315 closed — it was four rows, and it was never a decision

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"complete O-315. is that all?"* — under an explicit
  **ZERO FABRICATION** instruction. Two asks: resolve the mismatches, and say honestly whether
  they were the whole of it. They were not.

- **[DELTA]**

  **What I got wrong, first, because it shapes everything below.** `[AMD-6]` called O-315
  "a decision, not a task" and held it for the operator on the grounds that fixing it meant
  choosing whether the key or the note was the truth. **That was wrong, and the evidence was one
  field away.** Every row carries a `local` endonym. In all four, `local`, `note` and `sources`
  agree with each other and disagree with the **key** — and the rest of the repo corroborates
  the key:

  | Key | Its own `local` | Its note describes | Corroboration elsewhere in the repo |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `eurasian::roast suckling pig` | `feng` | curry of pork offal | `eurasian::feng (curry of pork offal)` **already exists** |
  | `eurasian::eurasian fishball curry` | 咖哩魚蛋 | HK curry fish balls | `hong-kong::curry fish balls` **already exists** |
  | `northeastern::changchun braised duck` | 清蒸白鱼 | Jilin steamed white fish | `nation-overlay-local` gives the key's endonym as 酱鸭 |
  | `northeastern::da pai dang chinese bbq` | 大排檔 | HK dai pai dong | overlay's own note says *Northeastern China* |

  So there was no coin to flip. These are four **contaminated rows duplicating content the corpus
  already holds correctly elsewhere**. I escalated a data-cleanup to the operator because I had
  not read a field that was sitting in the same object.

  **The fourth row was found by scanning, not reading.** O-315 opened at three because three is
  what one translation pass happened to surface. `northeastern::da pai dang chinese bbq` reads
  *"Open-air **Cantonese** street-food stall, licensed in postwar **Hong Kong**"* under a Dongbei
  key, while the nation overlay's own note for that same key says *"open-air food stalls in
  **Northeastern** China"*.

  **Why removal, and why it invents nothing.** `index.js:17845` merges as `{ ...d, ...m }` — a
  classics-notes row **overwrites** the nation overlay's note. Deleting the four lets the
  overlay's correct, sourced notes through. Verified at the render site, not assumed:

  | Key | What shipped before | What ships now |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `eurasian::roast suckling pig` | "A rich Eurasian curry of diced pork and pig's offal…" | "Roast suckling pig is a whole roasted piglet, fed on its mother's milk…" |
  | `eurasian::eurasian fishball curry` | "…a Hong Kong street-food staple popularised by post-war 1950s hawker carts." | "Eurasian fishball curry features fish balls simmered in a rich curry sauce, often with daikon radish." |
  | `northeastern::da pai dang chinese bbq` | "Open-air Cantonese street-food stall, licensed in postwar Hong Kong…" | "Da pai dang Chinese BBQ refers to grilled meats and skewers typically served at open-air food stalls in Northeastern China." |

  Under **ZERO FABRICATION** this is the only available fix: writing replacement notes would mean
  authoring source prose about dishes I have no source for. Removal restores prose that exists.

  **A correction `[AMD-6]` owes.** It said all three "render the wrong dish". Three did.
  **`changchun braised duck` did not** — it is absent from the overlay's `iconicDishes`, and the
  other consumer, `city-plates.js`, reads only `CLASSIC_NOTES` keyed by **country code**, never
  `CUISINE_NOTES`. It was wrong data that never reached a reader. Wrong is still worth removing;
  overstating its reach is not, and I nearly shipped that claim.

  **A leftover the removal created, and the assertion that caught it.** `da pai dang` had already
  been translated, so deleting its base note orphaned six strings. Six translations for a dish
  that no longer exists are not harmless — they are what a later coverage count reads as work
  already done. Cleared, and now pinned: the coverage test asserts no overlay row lacks a base note.

  **The scan, kept as a gate.** The protein-vs-endonym check that found the fourth row is now a
  test. Its blind spots are written into the file beside it rather than left implied: it cannot
  see a Latin-script `local` (`feng`), nor a row where key and endonym agree on protein but differ
  on provenance (the fishball curry, where both said fish). **Two of the four rows it closes are
  invisible to it.** It measured 1 hit before the fix and 0 after — it fired on real data, which
  is a stronger proof than a synthetic mutation.

- **[REMOVED — verbatim, per AU-1]** Nothing here is compressed or paraphrased. The changchun
  prose in particular is *correct research about a real Jilin dish that has no key in this corpus*,
  and is kept so it is not re-researched from scratch.

  ```
  eurasian::eurasian fishball curry
    local:  咖哩魚蛋
    en:     Teochew-rooted fish balls in spicy curry sauce, a Hong Kong street-food staple popularised by post-war 1950s hawker carts.
    fr:     Boulettes de poisson d'origine teochew en sauce curry épicée, en-cas de rue hongkongais popularise par les marchands ambulants des annees
    source: Wikipedia — Curry fish ball  ·  https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curry_fish_ball
    source: MICHELIN Guide — The Journey of Hong Kong's Iconic Fish Ball
            https://guide.michelin.com/hk/en/article/features/bouncing-through-time-the-journey-iconic-fish-ball-hong-kong

  eurasian::roast suckling pig
    local:  feng
    en:     A rich Eurasian curry of diced pork and pig's offal slow-cooked in a blend of spices.
    fr:     Un riche curry eurasien de porc et d'abats de porc en dés, mijoté dans un mélange d'épices.
    source: Roots.gov.sg - Eurasian Cuisine in Singapore
            https://www.roots.gov.sg/ich-landing/ich/eurasian-cuisine-in-singapore
    source: Wikipedia - Eurasian cuisine of Singapore and Malaysia
            https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurasian_cuisine_of_Singapore_and_Malaysia

  northeastern::da pai dang chinese bbq
    local:  大排檔
    en:     Open-air Cantonese street-food stall, licensed in postwar Hong Kong, serving cheap wok and grilled "chinese BBQ" dishes.
    fr:     Échoppe cantonaise de rue en plein air, autorisée à Hong Kong d'après-guerre, servant des plats bon marché au wok et grillés.
    source: Wikipedia — Dai pai dong  ·  https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dai_pai_dong
    source: Oxford English Dictionary — dai pai dong  ·  https://www.oed.com/dictionary/dai-pai-dong_n
    id/ru/de/zh/ja/es translations also removed with it (six strings).

  northeastern::changchun braised duck
    local:  清蒸白鱼
    en:     A steamed freshwater white fish (from the Songhua River/Lake) that is a signature dish of Jilin (Dongbei)
    fr:     Un poisson blanc d'eau douce cuit à la vapeur (de la rivière/du lac Songhua), plat emblématique de la cuisine du Jilin (Dongbei)
    source: Jilin cuisine — Wikipedia  ·  https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jilin_cuisine
    source: Steamed White Fish — Jilin, China (provincial government)
            https://www.gojilin.gov.cn/2020/03/06/c_6636.htm
  ```

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `node --check` on both edited data files. Coverage read through the
  **evaluated merge** (D-199), never by grepping the literal: **1,673 notes, 1,673 at all eight
  locales, `short: []`** — full coverage with **zero exceptions** for the first time. Overlay rows

  proven by evaluating `{ ...d, ...m }` for each affected dish, before and after.
  **`npm test -- --run` → 188 files, 4,405 tests green** (+1, the new protein gate).

  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `escargots de Bourgogne` | `escargots de bourgogne` | no match |
  | `tarta de Santiago` | `tarta de santiago` | no match |
  | `coconut chicken (Hainan style)` | `coconut chicken (hainan style)` | no match |
  | `Taiwan night market dishes` | `taiwan night market dishes` | no match |

  Four curated, sourced, eight-locale notes silently failed to attach; the dish rendered bare
  and nothing anywhere reported a miss. **`city-plates.js` has folded since v0.62.175** — its
  `_fold` at line ~5348 exists for exactly this. So the same datum was reachable through one
  code path and not the other. That is O-305's shape, and the v0.62.778 French bug's shape, for
  the third time on this branch: **the defect is never in the data, it is in the one call site
  that forgot.**

  The fold was **measured before it was written**, not after: 1,484 matches before, 1,488 after,
  exactly those four recovered, **zero existing matches redirected**, and zero folded-name
  collisions in any cuisine's overlay list or note table. An exact hit still wins, so the fold
  can only add a match, never move one — and the test asserts the redirect count is zero, because
  the day two dishes fold together the enrichment starts lying about one of them.

  **The other 23 are not fixed, and I checked three things before saying so** — each would have
  made this someone else's fix rather than the operator's:

  | Question | Answer | Consequence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | Duplicates served elsewhere? | **No** — 0 of 23 appear in another cuisine's dish list or in `CLASSIC_NOTES` | Retiring them destroys unique sourced content. Unlike O-315. |
  | Ever in the overlay, so re-adding restores? | **No** — `git log -S` finds `hainanese chicken rice`, `german beer`, `yuzu cheesecake` in **zero** commits touching `nation-overlay.js` | They were authored independently of the dish list. Adding them is a new claim. |
  | A hidden array the enrichment misses? | **No** — `iconicDishes` is the only dish-bearing array on the overlay | The beer/ale/wine cluster is genuinely absent, not structurally hidden. |

  So making the 23 reachable means **adding dishes to what the app offers a searcher**. That is a
  product decision, and under zero fabrication I will not assert that a dish is iconic to a
  cuisine on the strength of wanting a number to go down. Pinned by name with a `<= 23` guard so
  a twenty-fourth cannot join them unnoticed.

  **The one worth your attention:** `hainanese::hainanese chicken rice` — Singapore's national
  dish — is curated, sourced, translated into eight locales, and unreachable. `SG`'s
  `CLASSIC_NOTES` has **no chicken rice entry at all**, and the only chicken rice any reader can
  reach is `thai::thai chicken rice (khao man gai)`. The Hainanese cuisine list offers
  `hainanese curry rice`, `wenchang chicken` and thirteen others, and not that one.

  **Two of the 23 are notes recording that the dish does not exist**: `australasia::kaipake plate`
  (*"No documented Australasian/Maori dish named 'kaipake' was found"*) and `macau::caca-mato`
  (*"[UNVERIFIED] No dish or drink named 'caca-mato' could be confirmed"*). Unreachable is
  arguably the correct outcome for those two — a dish that does not exist should not be offered —
  and an earlier pass deliberately translated them as written rather than inventing a dish. They
  are listed with the rest rather than quietly excused, because the reason they are unreachable
  is different from the reason the other 21 are.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `node --check` on `index.js` and the test file. The fold proven by
  evaluating the real merge against the real overlay — before/after match counts, the recovered
  set asserted **by name** rather than by count, and the redirect set asserted empty. Consumers of
  `CUISINE_NOTES` enumerated by grep across the repo: **`index.js` is the only production one**,
  which is what makes "unreachable" a fact rather than an inference. `npm test -- --run` →
  **188 files, 4,407 tests green** (+2).

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` at v0.62.807. Deploys to Railway on merge.

- **[TEST]** `npx vitest run __tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` → 12 tests.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **The 23 are the operator's call** and are the live item here. The test that
  pins them is a copy of the call site's fold, deliberately — importing the helper would let both
  drift together and pass, where a copy fails the moment `index.js` stops folding. Unchanged:
  O-226, O-228 (blocked on an external per-venue Michelin source; no API spend), O-308,
  O-310 – O-313, O-318, O-184. `[AMD-7]` is still carried per D-205, and `[AMD-8]` is on #1765.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | *"do O-317"*: the part that was a defect is fixed; the part that is a product decision is decomposed and handed back rather than guessed at. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | One call site changed. No note deleted, no dish added. No external API called. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199: reachability read by evaluating the merge, never grepped. AU-1: O-317's original text preserved under strikethrough, including the 28 I now correct to 27. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Recovered set asserted by name; redirect set asserted empty; consumers enumerated rather than assumed. |
  | P7 — approval before consequential action | ⚠️ | **Adding 23 dishes to the cuisine plates was not done.** It changes what the app offers and is not a translator's call to make. |

### [AMD-10] The first of O-317's twenty-three, and a test failure I cannot name

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"add hainanese chicken rice to the cuisine plate"* — the
  product decision `[AMD-9]` handed back, taken for one row.

- **[DELTA]**

  `hainanese chicken rice` added to the Hainanese `iconicDishes`. Its note already existed —
  curated, three sources, all eight locales, endonym 海南鸡饭 — and now attaches. **O-317's
  unreachable count: 23 → 22.**

  **The overlay already carried chicken rice, and that changed how I made the edit.**
  `sharedWithNeighbors: S('chicken rice', 'chicken rice', ['singaporean', 'cantonese'])`. So the
  exclusion was not an oversight; someone modelled chicken rice as a shared/ambiguous name. But
  `S()` yields `{dish, ambiguousMatch, sharedWith}` — the AMBIGUOUS_DISHES alias pin — where
  `F()` yields `{name, kind, sharedWith}`, the plate list. **Different shapes, different roles**,
  which is also why the reachability scan was right not to see it: it looked for arrays whose
  items carry a `name`, and this one carries a `dish`.

  Three things checked before the edit, each of which would have made it wrong:

  | Check | Result |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `nation-overlay.test.js` disjoint invariant — no dish in both lists | Compares **exact lowercased names**; `chicken rice` ≠ `hainanese chicken rice`. Collisions measured after the edit: **0**. |
  | Is `sharedWithNeighbors` rendered on the plate, so this would duplicate? | **No** — its only consumers are that test and `discovery-dish.js`'s alias expansion. |
  | Is `sharedWith: ['singaporean','cantonese']` mine to assert? | **No, and I did not** — it is carried across verbatim from the `S()` entry the file already had. |

  **A visible product change, stated rather than buried.** `groupCuisineDishes` takes
  `headliners = dishes.slice(0, 3)`. Placing the dish first promotes it into the headliner row and
  moves `hainanese mutton soup` down into its `soup` group. **There is no neutral position** —
  last would have buried Singapore's national dish in a bucket beneath the pork chop — so the
  choice is made in a comment at the line where it can be seen, and it reverses in one word.

  The pin's bound **ratchets down**, 23 → 22, so a resolved row cannot be quietly refilled by a
  different one. And the test now asserts the key is **reachable**, not merely absent from the
  list: deleting a name from a pin is not the same as putting the dish on the plate, and only one
  of those is what the operator asked for.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** The enrichment **simulated against the real modules**, not inferred:
  on the plate ✅, note attached ✅, `en,fr,id,ru,de,zh,ja,es` ✅, endonym 海南鸡饭 ✅, 3 sources ✅,
  `sharedWith` preserved ✅. Headliners now read `hainanese chicken rice | hainanese pork chop |
  hainanese curry rice`, and mutton soup was confirmed present in the `soup` group rather than
  assumed not lost. Disjoint collisions `[]`. Cuisine-wide unreachable count 22.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT on `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` at v0.62.808.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run` → **188 files, 4,407 tests green**, run **eight consecutive times**.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **X — ONE TEST RUN FAILED AND I CANNOT NAME THE FILE.** The run immediately
  after the version bump reported `Test Files 1 failed | 187 passed` and
  `Tests 4400 passed | 7 skipped` — **zero tests failed**, so a file threw during collection and
  its tests were skipped. Eight full-suite runs since are green at 188 / 4,407, and I could not
  reproduce it.

  **The error worth recording is mine, not the suite's.** I piped that first run through
  `tail -6`, which printed the summary and discarded the failing file's name — so the one run
  that had the answer is the one run I cannot interrogate. Five files carry exactly 7 tests
  (`michelin-green-star`, `gemini-models`, `count-interactions-scope`, `eatery-annotations`,
  `recent-label`) and I will not guess between them. **I am not calling it a flake**: this repo's
  own posture is that a failing test is never an infra flake, and "it passed the second time" is
  not a root cause. What I have is an unreproduced collection-time failure, eight greens, and CI
  as an independent second signal on push. If CI goes red, that is the answer and I act on it.

  Also unchanged: **O-317's remaining 22**, O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.
  `[AMD-7]` still carried per D-205.

### [AMD-11] #1765 merged — and the prepush guard caught what I was about to do

- **[INTENT]** Record the merge. Not held this time: it folds into the v0.62.808 PR being opened
  in the same breath, which is exactly what **D-205** prescribes.

- **[DELTA]** #1765 squash-merged as **`34873386`**, 2026-08-27 14:13:11 +08:00, carrying
  v0.62.806 and v0.62.807.

  **I found out by being blocked.** The push of v0.62.808 was refused by `prepush-guard`:
  *"the remote branch tip is CONTENT-IDENTICAL to main, so its pull request has been merged. A
  fast-forward push would append to a closed PR: it would succeed, and the work would never reach
  main."* The operator merged while I was mid-edit, and without that guard the commit would have
  landed on a closed PR's branch and simply sat there — a push that reports success and delivers
  nothing. I verified the claim rather than taking it: `origin/main` at `34873386`, both trees
  `9e9973c8`. The guard was right.

  Branch restarted from `origin/main`, v0.62.808 cherry-picked, and the diff against main
  confirmed **byte-identical to what the commit added before the restart** — same six files, same
  118 insertions — so nothing was lost or silently rewritten in the move.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **D-123, from the merged tree** at `34873386` in a detached
  worktree — seven reads, loading modules rather than grepping:

  | # | Read | Result |
  | ---: | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | corpus loaded, eight locales counted | **1,673 notes, 1,673 at eight, `short: []`** |
  | 2 | overlay rows / orphan overlay rows | 1,673 / **`[]`** |
  | 3 | the four O-315 rows **still present** | **`[]`** — asserted ABSENT |
  | 4 | O-317 fold recovers | **4** |
  | 5 | `index.js` **call site** still folds | **true** |
  | 6 | what `roast suckling pig` now ships | *"…a whole roasted piglet, fed on its mother's milk"* |
  | 7 | version on main | `0.62.807` |

  Read 5 is the one that matters and the one easiest to skip: reads 1–4 all pass on the DATA, and
  the data being foldable proves nothing about whether the call site folds. A merge that carried
  `classics-notes.js` and dropped the `index.js` hunk would satisfy every other read here. Read 3
  asserts an **absence** for the same reason, and read 6 checks the *consequence* of the removal
  rather than the removal itself — the point was never to delete four rows, it was to let the
  overlay's correct note through.

- **[STATUS]** **PROD.** `main` at `34873386` deploys to Railway.

- **[TEST]** The verification worktree needed `node_modules` symlinked to run anything, as in
  `[AMD-7]`; the suite green at 188 / 4,407 is from the working tree at v0.62.808.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged: **O-317's remaining 22**, and the unnamed collection-time test
  failure recorded in `[AMD-10]` — still unreproduced across nine full-suite runs, still not
  called a flake. **`[AMD-7]`, #1764's merge record, is STILL held** and rides this PR too; it has
  now been carried across two merges, which is the longest anything has waited under D-205 on this
  branch and is worth noticing before it becomes a habit.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Record the merge, restart the branch, start nothing new. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | The guard's claim verified before acting on it; tree comparison before the restart; the cherry-picked diff compared against the pre-restart one; worktree removed and pruned. `--no-verify` was **not** used. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-205: folded into the v0.62.808 PR rather than opening its own. D-123, D-199 as above. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Seven reads at `34873386`, including one assertion of absence and one that reads the CALL SITE rather than the data it operates on. |

### [AMD-12] Eighteen of the twenty-two — and the four that a rule, not effort, kept off

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"add the remaining 22 to their cuisine plates"*.

- **[DELTA]** **18 added across ten cuisines**, each verified to attach its existing
  eight-locale note: **18 of 18, no exceptions**. **O-317: 22 → 4.**

  Two details that a careless pass would have got wrong. The four drinks use **`D()` not
  `F()`**, so they bucket as drinks rather than food. And `hakka::thunder tea rice` is
  **food** — my first classifier called it a drink on the word *tea*, which is exactly the
  keyword-rule failure this repo has rejected twice before; it was checked against the real
  entry rather than trusted. Everything was **appended, never reordered**: unlike
  `hainanese chicken rice` these are not flagship dishes, and `headliners = slice(0, 3)`
  means inserting at the top would have silently reshuffled ten plates.

  **Four were not added, and none of them for effort.**

  | Row | Why not |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `australasia::kaipake plate` | Its own `local` reads *"[UNVERIFIED: no such dish found]"*; the note says the nearest real Māori word, *kaipuke*, means **ship** |
  | `macau::caca-mato` | *"[UNVERIFIED] No dish or drink named 'caca-mato' could be confirmed in Macanese cuisine sources"* |
  | `australasia::fish suckling pacific` | Note and `local` (ʻOta ʻika) describe a real Tongan dish; the **KEY** is garbled English. Also a likely near-duplicate of `F("oka i'a")`, already on that plate — oka iʻa is the Samoan cognate |
  | `american::american craft beer` | **Policy, not doubt.** See below |

  The first two are the zero-fabrication line: putting them on a plate offers a searcher **a
  dish that does not exist**. An earlier pass deliberately translated them *as written*,
  preserving the unverified marker rather than inventing a dish — adding them to the product
  would quietly undo that. For a dish nobody could document, unreachable is the correct
  outcome, and this is the case where the number not going to zero is the right answer.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `node --check`; enrichment simulated against the real
  modules for all 18; `npm test -- --run` → **188 files, 4,407 tests green, three runs of
  three**.

- **[KNOWN GAPS / what went wrong on the way]**

  **The cap test found a real breach, and I had already looked straight past it.** Adding
  `american craft beer` took American to 31 and failed
  `NATION_OVERLAY — caps > american iconicDishes ≤ 30` — *"HL 2026-05-08 ceiling"*, a Human
  Lead policy — on **three runs out of three**. I had checked for a cap earlier, found only
  the display-cap test, and concluded there was no hard limit. The check was
  `grep -n "cap\b" … | head -5`, and the `caps` describe block was **below the cut**. That is
  the second time in this session I truncated my own search and then reasoned from the
  truncation as though it were the whole picture — the first cost me the name of a failing
  file in `[AMD-10]`. The tooling was right both times; the `head` was mine.

  The row is now pinned with that reason. Raising the cap or dropping an existing American
  dish is the operator's call; **weakening the test to make room was never an option.**

  **A candidate defect investigated and rejected.** 180 notes end at a semicolon, which
  looked like a truncation class covering `british ale` (40 chars) and `barramundi pie` (54).
  It is not: `korean::kimchi`, `italian::cannoli` and `french::pot-au-feu` end the same way.
  **A trailing semicolon is this corpus's house style for closing a note.** Those two rows
  are terse, not broken, and were added. Recorded because I nearly held four rows on an
  invented defect — the fourth rule rejected on measured evidence on this branch, after
  O-316's two and O-318.

  **And the earlier unnamed failure from `[AMD-10]` is still unnamed.** This one reproduced
  three times and named itself; that one never has. They are not the same event and I am not
  treating one as an explanation for the other.

  Still open: **O-317's remaining 4**, O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184.
  `[AMD-7]` is still held per D-205.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | 22 asked for; 18 delivered; 4 refused with a stated reason each, rather than silently dropped or forced through. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Only additions. No note deleted, no test weakened, no cap raised. The first two script attempts failed and wrote nothing — verified with `git diff` before retrying. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Zero fabrication: two rows refused precisely because the notes say the dish could not be confirmed. D-199: attachment read by simulating the merge. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | 18 of 18 asserted on the plate, note attached, all eight locales, correct `kind`. |
  | P7 — approval before consequential action | ⚠️ | **The 30-dish cap was not raised and no American dish was dropped.** Both change what the app offers and are the operator's to decide. |

### [AMD-15] The cap moves to 31 — and what the cap was actually counting

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"raise the cap to 31 and add american craft beer"* — the
  policy decision `[AMD-12]` refused to make on their behalf.

- **[DELTA]**

  `DEFAULT_CAP` **30 → 31** in `__tests__/nation-overlay.test.js`; `D('american craft beer')`
  added to the American plate; its existing eight-locale note verified to attach. **O-317: 4 → 3.**

  **The change is global, as asked.** Every non-SG cuisine may now hold 31, not just American.
  That is the plain reading of *"raise the cap to 31"* and it is what was done, rather than
  quietly narrowing it to an American-only exception — but it is a wider blast radius than the
  one row needed, so it was measured after: **`american=31` is the only cuisine above the old
  ceiling.** Nothing else drifted up on the back of it.

  **The 21 `— cap 30` block comments in `nation-overlay.js` were updated too.** A comment
  stating a superseded rule is a false statement, and this repo's own **D-199** says repo text
  is evidence about the past — twenty-one comments asserting a ceiling that no longer exists is
  exactly the trap that rule describes.

- **[WHAT THE CAP WAS ACTUALLY COUNTING — recorded, not acted on]**

  The cap counts food and drinks **together**. The renderer does not: `formatIconicList` slices
  food to `maxItems` and drinks to `maxItems / 2` **separately**, so expert mode shows up to
  30 food *and* up to 15 drinks.

  | Cuisine | total | food | drinks |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | american | 30 (before) | 27 | 3 |
  | german | 28 | 24 | 4 |
  | british | 28 | 23 | 5 |
  | singaporean | 164 | 124 | 40 |

  So the row that could not reach a reader was a **drink**, blocked by a combined count the
  display never applies, with three free slots in its own bucket and three in the food bucket
  it was never competing for. Splitting the cap to mirror the renderer would be the more
  precise fix. **It was not done, because it was not asked for** — it is in the Register as a
  candidate refinement, next to the number it would replace.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `node --check`; the added row asserted on the plate with
  `kind: 'drink'`, note attached, **8 locales**; american now 27 food + 4 drinks = 31;
  unreachable count **3**, listed by name; and the "only american is above 30" sweep run across
  every cuisine rather than assumed. `npm test -- --run` → **188 files, 4,407 tests green,
  three runs of three.**

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.810.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-317's last 3, all operator decisions:** `fish suckling pacific` (note
  right, key garbled, likely a duplicate of `oka i'a`), and `kaipake plate` + `caca-mato`,
  whose notes record that the dish **could not be documented** — unreachable is the correct
  outcome there and the recommendation is to leave them. Unchanged: O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308,
  O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the still-unnamed collection-time failure from `[AMD-10]`.
  `[AMD-14]` folds forward into this PR per **D-205** — this is the substantive change it was
  waiting for.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Cap raised to 31 globally, as the words say; one row added. The finer split was found, recorded, and not slipped in. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Additions and a number change. No dish dropped, no test weakened — the cap was raised by the operator's instruction, not to make a failure go away. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199: stale comments corrected rather than left asserting a dead rule. D-205: `[AMD-14]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Attachment, locale count and `kind` asserted on the real modules; the blast-radius sweep run across every cuisine. |
  | P7 — approval before consequential action | ✅ | The policy change was the operator's, given verbatim. Its global reach is disclosed rather than assumed. |

### [AMD-16] Two rows ruled on — and "decided" is not the same state as "pending"

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"leave kaipake and caca-mato as is"*.

- **[DELTA]** **No behaviour changes and no count moves.** O-317 stays at 3. What changes is
  the **status** of two of them, and that is worth a version of its own.

  `australasia::kaipake plate` and `macau::caca-mato` were sitting in the exceptions pin
  described as *"the operator's call"*. They now have that call: **they stay unreachable.**
  Recorded in `__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` with the instruction quoted rather
  than paraphrased.

  **Why this is not busywork.** A row in an exceptions list annotated *"awaiting a ruling"*
  reads, to the next person through, as unfinished business — something a diligent session
  should tidy up. Two of these three are finished business, and the finished state is that
  **nothing happens to them**. Someone who "fixes" them by adding the dishes to a plate would
  be *undoing a decision, not completing one*, and would be shipping a dish whose own note says
  it could not be documented. The pin now says so in as many words, and names
  `fish suckling pacific` as the only row still genuinely open.

  This repo has the failure in its own record: an exemption that looks like a to-do gets
  actioned, and a rule gets switched off by someone who thought they were finishing the job.

  passing; `npm test -- --run` → **188 files, 4,407 tests green**. Nothing in the data or the
  overlay was touched, and the diff is comments plus docs — stated plainly because a version
  bump usually implies behaviour changed, and here it did not.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.811, on the branch that carries #1768.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-317 now has exactly ONE open row:** `australasia::fish suckling pacific`
  — note and `local` (ʻOta ʻika) correct, KEY garbled English, likely a duplicate of

  retire it as a duplicate, or leave it — and the evidence leans to duplicate, but the

### [AMD-18] `fish suckling pacific` retired — and O-317 closes with nothing pending

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"retire fish suckling pacific as a duplicate"* — the last
  open row in O-317.

- **[DELTA]** **The duplicate claim was confirmed before the row was touched.** I had been
  calling it *"likely a duplicate"* for several replies, and deleting user-facing data on a
  *likely* is precisely the sloppiness the rest of this session has been correcting. The
  evidence, read from the two rows:

  | | `australasia::fish suckling pacific` | `australasia::oka i'a` |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `local` | ʻOta ʻika | oka i'a |
  | note | *Tongan* national dish, raw fish in citrus + coconut milk | *Samoan* raw-fish dish, cubed fish in lime + coconut cream |
  | source | **Wikipedia — ʻOta ʻika** | **Wikipedia – 'Ota 'ika (Samoan oka i'a)** |
  | reachable | **no** — garbled key, absent from the plate | **yes** |
  | locales | 8 | 8 |

  The survivor's own source title names both dishes as one. Same article, same dish, Tongan and
  Samoan names — and only one of the two rows any reader could ever see.

  **The base row and its overlay row were removed together.** Deleting only the base leaves an
  orphan overlay row, which a later coverage count reads as work already done — the mistake
  `da pai dang` taught at v0.62.806, and the reason there is an assertion for it. Measured
  after: **1,672 notes, 1,672 at eight locales, 0 orphan overlay rows, `oka i'a` intact and
  still on the plate.**

  **One fact was lost, and it is recorded rather than glossed.** The retired note called this
  the **Tongan** national dish *"eaten across Polynesia"*; the survivor describes it as Samoan.
  Widening `oka i'a`'s prose to carry the Tongan framing would be **authoring, not retiring**,
  so it was not done. The removed row is preserved verbatim below.

  ```
  "fish suckling pacific": {
    "local": "ʻOta ʻika",
    "note": {
      "en": "Tongan national dish of raw fish marinated in citrus juice and coconut milk with diced vegetables; eaten across Polynesia.",
      "fr": "Plat national tongien de poisson cru mariné au jus d'agrumes et au lait de coco avec legumes en des, repandu en Polynesie."
    },
    "sources": [
      { "name": "Wikipedia — ʻOta ʻika", "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%27Ota_%27ika" },
      { "name": "TasteAtlas — Best Fish Dishes in Australia and Oceania", "url": "https://www.tasteatlas.com/best-rated-fish-dishes-in-australia-and-oceania" }
    ]
  }
  ```
  plus its six overlay translations, also removed.

- **[THE TEST THAT FAILED, AND WHY LOWERING IT WAS THE FIX]**

  Retiring the row broke `expect(rows).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1673)` — reproducibly, three runs
  of three, and this time I read the failure instead of piping it through `tail`.

  The floor moved to **1,672 with its reason written beside it**: 1,677 at v0.62.804 → 1,673
  when O-315 removed four contaminated rows → 1,672 here. Every step down is a decision someone
  made and can point at. The floor exists to catch the step down **nobody decided** — a row lost
  to a bad edit or a bad merge. Lowering it to match reality without saying why is how a guard
  becomes a rubber stamp, so from now on the number and the reason move together or not at all.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `node --check` on both changed data files. Corpus read through
  the evaluated merge: 1,672 / 1,672 / 0 orphans. The survivor asserted **intact and reachable**,
  not merely present. `npm test -- --run` → **188 files, 4,407 tests green, three runs of three.**

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.812.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-317 is closed in the sense that matters: nothing in it is pending.** Two
  rows remain unreachable and both are settled by explicit ruling — `kaipake plate` and
  `caca-mato`, whose own notes record that the dish could not be documented. The exceptions list
  is now a record of decisions rather than a queue of work, and the test says so in as many words
  so a future session does not read it as a to-do.
  Unchanged: O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the still-unnamed
  collection-time failure from `[AMD-10]`. `[AMD-17]` folds forward into this PR per **D-205**.

### [AMD-19] The government's own station names, and a defect I reintroduced within hours

- **[INTENT]** Operator raised `translatedterms.gov.sg` as a side project, saved the page
  source, and asked me to find *"the URL to list all the station name"*. Then, verbatim:
  *"add it to the repo with the source recorded"*.

- **[DELTA]**

  **The URL was not in the file they saved, and that was the finding.** `<div
  class="filter-result">` and `<div class="pagination-items">` both ship **empty** —
  jQuery fills them after load, so page source yields zero station names however carefully
  it is read. The endpoint lives in `/Mvc/Scripts/ComSearchTerm/search-term.js`, which the
  saved HTML does point at.

  ```
  POST https://www.translatedterms.gov.sg/admin/api/Search
  Page, PageSize, LanguageFrom=1, LanguageTo, Categories[]=012db1a7-…, SearchTerm
  ```

  **Three things turned "20 pages" into two calls**, none of them visible from the page:

  | Finding | Consequence |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `PageSize` is client-supplied; the site sends 10, the server honours 100 | 195 rows in **2 calls**, not 20 pages |
  | One response carries **all four languages** in `TransTranslations` | `LanguageTo` changes only what renders — no per-language pass |
  | Values are HTML-entity encoded (`&#28023;` = 海) | A fetch skipping decode stores mojibake that **passes a presence check** |

  Shipped: `web/_shared/lib/mrt-stations-i18n.generated.js` (193 stations),
  `scripts/fetch-mrt-station-names.mjs` (regenerates it; refuses to write a partial table),
  `__tests__/mrt-stations-i18n.test.js` (8 assertions). **183 / 183 of `SG_STATIONS`
  resolve; 0 rows missing any of the three languages.**

- **[X — I REINTRODUCED O-317's DEFECT IN A NEW FILE, HOURS AFTER FIXING IT]**

  The lookup was an exact-keyed `Map`. It resolved **182 of 183** and returned English for
  the one it missed: `one-north`, which this repo writes lower-case (the station's own
  branding) and the register writes `One-North`.

  That is **the same case-sensitivity bug as O-317** — two tables that disagree on case,
  joined without folding — committed by me in a new file the same day I fixed it in
  `index.js` and wrote three paragraphs about why it mattered. The lesson did not travel
  from the file where I learned it to the file I wrote next. It is now folded
  (exact-hit-wins, so a fold can only add a match), and pinned by a test naming `one-north`
  so the fold cannot later be removed as tidying.

  Worth noting **how it was caught**: my join script used a fold and reported 183/183; the
  real module used exact keys and reported 182. Two measurements of the same thing
  disagreeing is what surfaced it. Had I only run the optimistic one, this ships broken.

- **[X — AND I WROTE A TEST ASSERTION FROM MEMORY]**

  The test asserting Bras Basah's Chinese name was authored as **勿拉士峇沙** — a plausible
  phonetic transliteration — and failed against correct data. The register says **百胜**
  ("Bai Sheng"), which no amount of sounding out the English produces.

  So the failure mode this entire table exists to prevent was demonstrated **while writing
  the table's own test**. The assertion is corrected from the data, and the incident is left
  in the file beside it rather than tidied away, because it is the argument for the table.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Join measured both directions before the file's shape was
  chosen. `npm test -- --run` → **189 files, 4,415 tests green**, twice. No API key, no
  quota, no spend — a public unauthenticated government lookup.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.813.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-320, and it is stated in the file's own header rather than only
  here: the table is not wired to any renderer.** `stationName(name, lang)` is exported and
  the join is complete, but **no call site uses it** — every station still renders English
  whatever the reader's language. This repo's most expensive recurring defect is data that
  exists and does not render: v0.62.777 (~5,300 strings), v0.62.778 (1,649 notes),
  v0.62.781 (four `ArrivalPlate` sites), O-317 (27 notes). Shipping a complete table and
  calling it done would be the fifth, so it is filed as open work rather than a finished
  feature.
  Two source-side things left as found: the category returns two LINE names that are not
  stations (excluded **by name**, visible in a diff), and *"North-South Line (EWL)"* is
  **mislabelled at source** — NSL, not EWL — left uncorrected, because a fixed copy of an
  official register stops being a copy of it.
  Unchanged: O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the unnamed
  collection-time failure from `[AMD-10]`. `[AMD-18]` and this entry ride the open #1769.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Asked for the URL, then for the data in the repo with its source. Both delivered; the renderer wiring is filed as O-320 rather than assumed in. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Read-only public government endpoint, no auth, no spend. Nothing in the repo removed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Zero fabrication: every value stored verbatim from the register, including where it contradicted what I would have written. Source, endpoint, params and fetch date recorded in the file itself. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The join measured in both directions, and the discrepancy between two measurements is what caught the case bug. |

### [AMD-21] O-320 closed — the station names actually render now

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"wire it up to the transport mini app"*.

- **[DELTA]** Three **display** sites now resolve through `stationName(name, lang)`:

  | Site | What a reader sees |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `MrtMapPanel.jsx` — info-card heading | the station popup's title |
  | `MrtMapPanel.jsx` — marker `title` | hover / accessible name |
  | `LocationCard.jsx` — nearest-MRT list | with `lang` threaded from `App.jsx` |

  **Verified by building the app**, not by reading the data: 468 modules transformed, and
  the Chinese names are present in the emitted bundle. A render confirmed at the *output*.
  That distinction is the whole of O-320 — the previous version had a perfect table that
  reached nobody.

- **[THE THING THAT MADE THIS NON-TRIVIAL: DISPLAY vs KEY]**

  `s.name` is everywhere in `MrtMapPanel.jsx`, and **most occurrences are keys**:
  `stationCtxRef.current[s.name]`, `modeByName.get(s.name)`,
  `s.name === detail.station.name`, and a Google Maps query built as
  `s.name + ' MRT Station Singapore'`.

  Translating any of those breaks the app **silently** — a cache that never hits, a
  comparison that never matches, a map search for a Chinese string that finds nothing. No
  error, no crash, just a feature quietly not working. So the test asserts **both
  directions**: the display sites resolve, and the key sites are still English. A test that
  only checked the first would pass just as happily on a version that had translated the
  Google Maps query.

- **[DELIBERATELY NOT DONE]** The map **pill** (`stationPillNode`) keeps the English name.
  It is a dense map label sized for short Latin text, and **this environment cannot render
  the map to look at the result.** Changing a size-sensitive visual on faith is how an
  unverifiable regression ships. Pinned by a test so the omission is visible rather than
  forgotten, and reversible in one line when someone can actually see it.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npx vite build` on the transport app — clean, 468 modules,
  names in the bundle. `npm test -- --run` → **189 files, 4,419 tests green** (+4), twice.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.814.

- **[KNOWN GAPS — O-321, and it is the honest headline]**

  **Only 1 of the 3 languages can ever reach a reader.** The transport TMA's
  `SUPPORTED_LOCALES` is `en, fr, id, ru, de, zh, ja, es` — **no `ms`, no `ta`**. Of the
  three official languages fetched from the government register, only **Chinese** has a
  locale to display it. Malay and Tamil are stored, correct, joined, and unreachable.

  I did **not** add those locales, and the reason is that it would be worse rather than
  better: every other UI string in the app exists only in `en`/`fr`, so a reader choosing
  Malay would get an English interface with Malay station names — a regression dressed as a
  feature.

  Separately: `LocationCard`'s own headings (*"You are here"*, *"Nearest MRT"*) were
  hardcoded English before this change and still are, so a `zh` reader now sees a Chinese
  station name under an English heading. Named rather than left to be noticed.

  Also caught on the way out: a stray `选` landed in my own English Register prose
  ("a reader选ing Malay"). Fixed. The repo has a whole test file about script contamination
  in the corpora; the same hazard applies to the documentation about them.

  **And one more, caught by a test rather than by me.** I stamped this entry
  `27-08 '26 22:11 SGT`. The clock read `2026-08-27T22:11:22Z`, and SGT is UTC+8, so the
  correct stamp is **`28-08 '26 06:11 SGT`** — the next day. I copied the UTC hour into the
  SGT field, which is not a rounding slip but a whole timezone, and it put the entry eight
  hours before the work it describes.

  `__tests__/serial-state-anchor.test.js` failed with `expected 28822000 to be less than
  60000` — the three anchor fields disagreeing by exactly eight hours. That test exists
  because the same three fields drifted once before; it earned its keep again here.
  Corrected in `.serial-state.yml` and in both Register rows. **TF-10 and D-203 are about
  trusting the fetched instant over the remembered one; this was the failure one step
  later — fetching correctly and then transcribing it into the wrong field.**

  Unchanged: O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the unnamed
  collection-time failure from `[AMD-10]`. `[AMD-20]` folds forward into this PR per **D-205**.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Wired to the transport app, as asked. The pill and the two headings are named as not-done rather than silently included or silently skipped. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Three display sites changed; every key site left exactly as it was, and asserted so. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | The render is verified at the built output, which is the lesson v0.62.777/.778/.781 and O-317 each paid for separately. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Bundle inspected for the actual Chinese strings; both directions of the display/key split asserted. |

### [AMD-22] #1770 merged — and the question I had not yet asked about my own change

- **[INTENT]** Record the merge. Held per **D-205**.

- **[DELTA]** #1770 squash-merged as **`55a3f32e`**, carrying v0.62.814. Ninth consecutive
  PR marked ready and merged within seconds.

- **[THE CHECK I HAD NOT RUN, AND SHOULD HAVE RUN BEFORE CLAIMING O-320 CLOSED]**

  I verified the render by building the app and finding the Chinese names in the emitted
  bundle. But `public/transport/assets/` is a **build artifact**, and I had not asked the
  obvious next question: *does the artifact a user actually receives get rebuilt from
  `src`, or is a stale one committed and served?*

  If `public/` were tracked, my change would live in `src`, pass every test, and **not
  reach a single reader** until someone happened to rebuild — which is O-320's exact shape
  one layer further out, and would have been a fourth instance of it in this session.

  Checked rather than assumed: `.gitignore:15` carries `public/transport/`, and
  `package.json` sets `"postinstall": "npm run build"`, which runs `build:transport` among
  the seven TMA builds. So the deployed bundle is compiled from `src` on every install and
  the change does reach production. **The answer was fine. Not having asked was not.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** **D-123, from the merged tree** at `55a3f32e`, nine reads:

  | # | Read | Result |
  | ---: | :--- | :--- |
  | 1–3 | popup heading, marker title, nearest-MRT list resolve through `stationName` | **true** |
  | 4 | `App.jsx` threads `lang` into `LocationCard` | **true** |
  | 5 | **all four KEY sites still English** | **true** |
  | 6 | map pill still English (the deliberate omission) | **true** |
  | 7 | version | `0.62.814` |
  | 8 | the corrected anchor `28-08 '26 06:11 SGT` | **true** |
  | 9 | the stray `选` in my Register prose | **fixed on main** |

  Read 5 is the one that matters most and is the easiest to skip: reads 1–4 all pass on a
  version that ALSO translated the Google Maps query and the cache keys — that version
  would render beautifully and silently fail to find anything on a map. Reads 8 and 9 check
  that my own two corrections survived the merge, rather than trusting that they did.

- **[STATUS]** **PROD.** `main` at `55a3f32e` deploys to Railway, and the transport bundle
  is rebuilt from source on that deploy.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-321 is the live one and it caps what this work can deliver:** the
  transport TMA has no `ms` or `ta` locale, so of three official languages fetched from the
  register, **only Chinese can reach a reader**. Adding the locales without real UI string
  sets would give a Malay reader an English interface with Malay station names — worse than
  the current state, which is why it was not done. `LocationCard`'s own headings are still
  hardcoded English, so a `zh` reader sees a Chinese station name under an English label.
  The map pill stays English until someone can render the map and look at the fit.

### [AMD-23] Indonesian reads Malay — and the card my first pass missed

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim, from a live Indonesian session: *"i change to indonesian,
  it should show the Malay station name."* They also asked, separately, whether widening
  the station cards at a 2048×1280 notebook ratio would help or just add white space —
  answered in the reply, not built, because it was a question.

- **[DELTA] Two faults behind one symptom, and the screenshot found both.**

  **(a) `id` had no column to read.** The register carries English, Chinese, Malay and
  Tamil — no Indonesian. `LANG_COLUMN = { id: 'ms' }` now sends Indonesian readers to the
  official Malay names. **O-321 goes from 1 of 3 languages reachable to 2 of 3.**

  The caveat, stated rather than buried: **Malay is not Indonesian.** The station name is
  identical — proper nouns — but the category word differs. The register says *Stesen MRT
  Ang Mo Kio*; Indonesian says *Stasiun*, and this app's own `id` strings already use
  *stasiun* (visible in the operator's own screenshot: *"Cari stasiun atau jalan"*). So an
  `id` reader gets a correct, official, immediately intelligible name carrying one Malay
  word. The alternative was English, which is neither.

  **The data is not edited to suit.** Rewriting *Stesen* → *Stasiun* would turn this from a
  copy of an official register into a derived table, which the file's own header forbids.
  The mapping lives at the **display boundary**, where it is one line, visible, and
  reversible.

  **(b) `StationCard` was not among the three sites v0.62.814 wired** — and it is the most
  visible station name in the app, the card strip along the bottom of that screenshot. That
  is why the operator saw English. Now wired.

- **[THE CATCH THAT MATTERED, AND IT WAS USER DATA]**

  In `StationCard`, `name` is not only displayed. It keys:

  | Use | What breaks if it were translated |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `readSaved().includes(name)` + the save toggle | **every user's saved stations orphan on a language switch** |
  | `data-station-card={name}` | the carousel can no longer scroll to a card |
  | `mapsQ(\`${name} MRT Station Singapore\`)` | Google Maps searches for a Malay string |
  | `shareUrl(lat, lng, name)` | shared links carry a localised name |

  The first is the serious one: a stored list still holding *Ang Mo Kio* while every card
  announces itself as *Stesen MRT Ang Mo Kio* — saved data silently disowned by a UI
  setting. So the localised string got its own `displayName`, used in exactly one place,
  and the test asserts **all four key sites still read `name`.**

  This is the third time in two days that display-vs-key has been the whole difficulty of a
  translation change. It is worth naming as a pattern rather than as three incidents.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npx vite build` on the transport app — clean, 468 modules.
  `stationName('Ang Mo Kio', …)` checked across `en/id/zh/ta/fr`: English passes through,
  `id` → *Stesen MRT Ang Mo Kio*, `zh` → 宏茂桥地铁站, `fr` falls back rather than blanking.
  `npm test -- --run` → **189 files, 4,422 tests green** (+3), twice.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT at v0.62.815.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **Tamil is still unreachable** — no `ta` locale, and adding one without a
  Tamil UI string set would give a Tamil reader an English interface with Tamil station
  names. `LocationCard`'s *"You are here"* / *"Nearest MRT"* headings remain hardcoded
  English. The map pill remains English pending someone who can render the map and look at
  the fit. **The card-width question is answered but NOT implemented** — it was asked as a
  question, and the answer (a straight 9→5 opens an `ml-auto` gap; ~6–7 fixes the
  truncation without it) is a recommendation for the operator to accept or reject.
  Unchanged: O-318, O-226, O-228, O-308, O-310 – O-313, O-184, and the unnamed
  collection-time failure from `[AMD-10]`. `[AMD-22]` folds forward into this PR per **D-205**.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The instruction was implemented; the separate layout question was answered rather than silently built. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | One display mapping, one display variable. No register value edited; every key site left as it was and asserted so. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | The register stays a copy of the register — the Malay/Indonesian difference is handled at display, not by rewriting the source. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Resolution checked across five locales, the build re-run, and the four key sites asserted unchanged. |
