### [AMD-77] The locale-chain audit — and `name-gloss.js` had been binary all along

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: ***"i change from russian to japanese, and the map didnt refresh
  / thoroughly investigate the translation code - check that the sequence are not overdrift"***.
  The map is the known Maps-JS-API constraint (its plan is with the operator, unapproved). This
  entry is the audit.

- **[THE FINDING THAT MATTERS — one boundary explains almost everything]** The card carries
  **six locale-dependent fields**, and they split cleanly by *who computes them*:

  | Field | Computed | Follows the toggle? |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `namePronounce` | server **+ client** (v0.62.846) | ✅ |
  | `nameLocal` | server | ❌ frozen at search time |
  | `nameReading` | server | ❌ frozen |
  | `nameGloss` | server | ❌ frozen |
  | `recentReview` | server | ❌ frozen |
  | `dishes` | server, **never passed a language at all** | ❌ never localised |

  Changing the locale **does not re-run the search** — verified: `lang` appears in exactly two
  `useCallback` deps in the cuisine App and in no search effect. So every client-rendered string
  flips instantly and every server-baked one keeps the language the search ran under. **One of
  six follows the toggle, and it is the one that was explicitly fixed.** That is the drift the
  operator suspected, stated exactly: the boundary is invisible to a reader, who sees a
  half-translated card and reasonably calls it a translation bug.

- **[A DEFECT FOUND BY ACCIDENT, WHICH IS THE POINT OF AUDITING]** Grepping the translation path
  printed `grep: name-gloss.js: binary file matches` instead of a line. **`name-gloss.js` — the
  module that glosses a foreign venue name into the reader's language — contained two literal
  `0x00` bytes**, a Redis "no gloss" sentinel written raw instead of escaped. Valid JavaScript,
  runs correctly, and **git classified the file as binary**: every diff of it has rendered as an
  unreviewable blob, and grep has been skipping it silently, for as long as it has existed.

  This is the second instance. `pronounce-client.js` had three, caught before shipping
  (v0.62.841) by reading `numstat` and seeing a `-  -` row where a line count belonged. Twice is
  a pattern, so it is now a **test** over every tracked source file. A second file was caught by
  it: `.vibe-journal/templates/app.js` (5).

  **No behavioural risk, which is why it did not need a decision:** the six-character escape and
  the raw byte are the *same one-character string* at runtime, so every value already cached
  under the old sentinel still matches. This was an encoding fix, not a contract change.

- **[AND MY FIRST SCAN WAS A FALSE NEGATIVE]** It used `grep -P` with a NUL escape, which matches
  **nothing** — it reported the repo clean while two files were affected. Caught only because I
  already knew `name-gloss.js` was bad and the scan disagreed with a measurement I had in hand.
  **A scan that cannot fail is worse than no scan**, so the guard now proves its own detector
  fires on a known-bad string before its verdict is allowed to mean anything.

- **[OTHER DRIFT, MEASURED]**
  - **Ten copies** of `['en','fr','id','ru','de','zh','ja','es']` across the codebase. They agree
    *today*. v0.62.825 is the precedent for why that is not reassuring: a hand-copied five-locale
    version silently dropped ja/zh/es from the Mini App toggle.
  - **95 rows** in `cuisine-i18n.js` (26 `VENUE_TYPES` + 69 `NAMES`) carry fr/zh/ja/es and are
    **missing ru/de/id on every single row** — which is why the operator's Russian card reads
    "Barbecue". This is O-336, still open.
  - **10 `['en','fr']` gates and 60 `=== 'fr' ?` ternaries** in `index.js` — the bot side, and
    the set already measured and declined this arc.
  - The cuisine **map info-card** renders `s.name` raw (never calls `stationName()`, which
    exists and has official zh/ms for all 193 stations), hardcodes `Google Map ↗` while its
    neighbours use `tr()`, and has no pronunciation line at all.

- **[DELTA]** `name-gloss.js` and `.vibe-journal/templates/app.js` (NUL → escape),
  `__tests__/no-nul-bytes.test.js` (3 new). `package.json` → **0.62.849**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Mutation: a NUL reintroduced into `name-gloss.js` → 2 failures;
  restored → green. `file` now reports both as UTF-8 text rather than `data`. **212 files,
  4,752 passed.**

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, on the branch already carrying `[AMD-75]` and `[AMD-76]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** The six-field drift above is **reported, not fixed** — closing it means either
  re-running the search on a locale change (real Places/Gemini spend per toggle) or giving
  `nameReading` / `nameGloss` client endpoints like `/api/pronounce`. Both are the operator's
  call and were put to them. Map reload plan still unapproved. O-336, O-337 and the rest stand.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Audit requested; audit delivered, with the unambiguous defect fixed and the costed ones escalated. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Sentinel value unchanged, so no cache invalidated; no new paid path. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Every count measured by script; the scan's own detector proved to fire before its clean verdict was trusted. |

### [AMD-78] O-336 closed, the map card wired, and the address gets a "how to say it" line

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"proceed"***, against the free tier (1–4) plus the address
  transliteration I had recommended as Route B. The map-language reload is deliberately NOT in
  this change — it is a distinct behaviour change and is next.

- **[① O-336 — CLOSED]** Measured before: `cuisine-i18n.js` held **95 rows** (26 `VENUE_TYPES` +
  69 `NAMES`) carrying fr/zh/ja/es on every row and **ru/de/id on none**. Three of eight shipped
  locales fell through to English for every cuisine and venue type — which is why the operator's
  Russian card read "Barbecue" under Cyrillic chrome. **285 cells added**, verified per row:
  every row now carries all seven non-English locales.

  The file's own comment said *"`id`/`ru`/`de` fall through to English throughout — that is
  O-336"*. It was accurate when written and false by the end of this change, so it was rewritten
  rather than left; **a test now asserts coverage per row**, because the prose was exactly the
  thing that went stale.

- **[② station names on the map card]** `transitBlockHtml` printed `s.name` raw, so a Chinese or
  Malay reader saw "Clarke Quay" on the map popup while the *same station* read 克拉码头 in the
  Transport app — from a table **already bundled in this build**. `lang` threaded in,
  `stationName()` called. Free; it was simply never wired.

- **[③ the hardcoded label]** `Google Map ↗` was an English literal sitting between two
  neighbours that both used `tr()`. Now `card.googleMap`, added across all eight locales
  (`Google Карты`, `谷歌地图`, `Google マップ` — Google's own localisations, since it is a brand).

- **[④ the map popup's missing guides]** The popup had no pronunciation line at all: a Japanese
  reader got the guide on the card below and nothing on the marker they had just tapped. Both
  lines added, read straight from the client cache — **no extra request**.

- **[⑤ THE ADDRESS, AND WHY IT IS TRANSLITERATION]** Operator: *"would the foreign address being
  translated help?"*, with their own examples — «Телок Бланга Драйв», テロック・ブランガ・ドライブ.
  Those are **transliterations, not translations**, and that decides the design: a street name
  is a proper noun, so «Проезд Телок Бланга» would be wrong *and* would destroy the only part a
  taxi driver can read. **The English line stays; the guide is additive.**

  `streetOf()` keys on the street, not the whole address, so the dozens of venues on one road
  collapse into a single cached answer and unit numbers never reach the model. It finds the
  street **by its type word**, not by position — the operator's own address is
  `"Block 49, Telok Blangah Drive"`, where segment one is a block number and `parts[0]` would
  have paid the model to pronounce a numeral. It is **strict**: an address with no recognisable
  street type returns `''` and gets no line, because a silent miss beats silent spend. An
  earlier draft fell back to `parts[0]` and returned `"Singapore 059291"` — a paid call for a
  postcode.

- **[COST]** Route A (Places `languageCode`, O-337) was rejected on evidence, not preference:
  `pipeline.js:1205` sets `name` from `displayName` and `:1206` sets `area` from
  `formattedAddress` — **one `languageCode` governs both**, so there is no way to localise the
  address without also changing `venue.name`, the thing the operator asked to leave alone. It
  also gives every locale its own Places cache namespace. Route B adds no Places calls, reuses
  the existing endpoint, and street-keying makes the marginal cost near zero.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Five mutations, each proved applied then fatal: `ru` stripped from
  one row; `streetOf` falling back to `parts[0]`; the English address line replaced rather than
  supplemented; the station name back to raw; the batch key dropping streets. **213 files,
  4,767 passed** (+15). Seven builds clean, `test:render` 6/6, German formality gate green.

- **[FOUR PINNED EXPECTATIONS UPDATED DELIBERATELY, NOT LOOSENED]** `map-and-chrome-i18n` carried
  an assertion written to fail when O-336 closed — *"Pinned so that closing O-336 fails HERE and
  has to update this line deliberately."* **It did exactly that; the pin worked.** Plus two batch-key
  assertions and `tma-i18n-coverage`'s cuisine count, 379 → **380** (+1, `card.googleMap`).

- **[AND THE IMPORT GUARD EARNED ITS KEEP AGAIN]** My new test imported
  `web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/i18n.js` to check the new key end-to-end. That resolves in this
  sandbox and **fails in CI**, where `npm ci` runs at the root only and the module imports React
  — the exact defect v0.62.834 shipped. `test-import-graph-guard` caught it **before CI did**.
  Fixed with the established `vi.mock('react')` stub, which throws if a hook is actually called.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, on the branch carrying `[AMD-75]`–`[AMD-77]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **The map-language reload is next**, plan approved in shape but not yet built.
  Still open: the five server-baked fields frozen at search time (`[AMD-77]`); dish names never
  passed a language; O-337 addresses from Places; the eight `low` hawker rows and a native
  reader's pass — **and that pass now matters more**, since 285 freshly written cells have had no
  native review. O-105, Korean, the `serial-state.yml` rotation.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | "proceed" taken against the tier I had recommended; the map reload deliberately excluded and named. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No Places `languageCode` change, so no cache namespace multiplied and no billed re-reads. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Coverage measured per row, not counted; five mutations proved applied then fatal. |

### [AMD-79] #1792 merged — four records shipped at once, and the review raced the merge a third time

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. Recorded per the standing rule; **held in the working
  tree per D-205** — ninth consecutive fold-forward.

- **[DELTA]** Squash **`c29e62bd`**, carrying **v0.62.850** and, with it, four journal entries
  that had been folded forward: `[AMD-76]` (the pipe-in-a-name fix), `[AMD-77]` (the locale-chain
  audit and the binary `name-gloss.js`), `[AMD-78]` (O-336 closed, the map card wired, the address
  guide), plus `[AMD-75]`, the #1791 merge record. The fold-forward rule worked exactly as
  intended here: **four records reached `main` inside one substantive PR, and none of them needed
  a PR of its own.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** By content, per D-123, and checking the **properties that were the
  defects** rather than file presence: empty diff against `origin/main`; `package.json` at
  **0.62.850**; **95** `ru:` cells in `cuisine-i18n.js` (one per row, which is the O-336 close);
  `streetOf` exported from `pronounce-client.js`; `stationName(s.name…)` present in `MapPanel.jsx`;
  and **0 NUL bytes** in `name-gloss.js` — the file that had been binary since it was written.

- **[THE MERGE RACED THE REVIEW A THIRD TIME]** Marked ready 10:25:48, merged 10:25:56 — **eight
  seconds** — while Codex's review had started at 10:25:54 and was still `Running`. The tally for
  the day: #1790 merged 40 s after ready and its review then returned **two real P1s**; #1791
  merged 7 s after and returned **one**; #1792 merged 8 s after and is still open at the time of
  writing. Two of three post-merge reviews found genuine bugs that were already in production,
  including the operator's own reported symptom.

  **This is not a malfunction** — auto-merge is doing what it is configured to do, and it was
  raised with the operator rather than changed. But the consequence is worth restating where it
  will be read later: **on this repo Codex is a post-merge audit, not a merge gate**, and the
  session is auto-unsubscribed the instant a PR merges, so a finding that lands afterwards
  reaches nobody unless something deliberately goes looking. A follow-up check was scheduled for
  that reason, as it was for #1791 — where it is precisely what caught the pipe bug.

- **[STATUS]** #1792 is **PROD** on `main` as `c29e62bd`. This record is **DRAFT**, held for the
  next substantive PR — which, unless the operator redirects, is the map-language reload.

- **[TEST]** ±0. 4,767 stands.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged from `[AMD-78]`, and one worth keeping visible: **285 locale cells

### [AMD-80] Three more Codex findings — including "fixed at the root" turning out not to be

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. The scheduled post-merge check on #1792 (armed precisely
  because that PR merged **eight seconds** after its review began) returned **three P2s**. All
  three verified against source and shipped data before acting; all three real; all three mine.
  Carries `[AMD-79]` forward per D-205.

- **[P2-1 — THE MAP GUIDES ONLY WORKED ON A SECOND VISIT]** `MapPanel`'s marker-sync effect
  depends on `[venues, userLoc, searchCenter]` — **none of which change when the pronunciation
  fetch resolves**. So the popup HTML was built from a cold cache and never rebuilt, and
  `[AMD-78]`'s new map lines appeared only when `localStorage` happened to be warm.

  **That is the worst shape a bug can take: it works for whoever tests it twice.** I tested it
  twice. Fixed by keeping the hook's projection in App (it was being discarded), passing it to
  `MapPanel`, and putting it in the effect's deps — its `useMemo` identity changes exactly when
  the answers change, so no separate version counter is needed. The popup now reads the **same
  projection the cards use**, so a marker and the card beneath it cannot disagree.

- **[P2-2 — THE RECOGNISER WAS WRITTEN FROM MEMORY, NOT FROM THE DATA]** `STREET_TYPE` listed
  what a street "usually" ends in. Codex named two real shipped addresses it silently dropped —
  `925 Yishun Central 1` and `19 Riverina View`. **Counting the corpus showed the problem was
  much bigger than the two examples: Wy 476, Central 355, Lp 324, Rise 184.** Every venue on
  those roads was getting no address guide at all.

  Widened to Singapore's official street-type list plus the Malay forms and abbreviations —
  and building words (Plaza, Mall, Centre, Tower) deliberately excluded, with a test asserting
  their absence, because matching those would key the cache on a building rather than a road.

  **The test was the actual defect.** `[AMD-78]`'s suite used addresses *I invented*, so it
  proved the recogniser handled the cases I had thought of. There is now a test that walks the
  **shipped corpus** and requires >80 % of real Singapore addresses to resolve.

- **[P2-3 — AND THIS ONE IS ABOUT A CLAIM I MADE]** `[AMD-76]` removed the `join('|')` /
  `split('|')` round-trip from the **hook's** dependency key and stated the class was fixed *"at
  the root"*. It was not. The **client's in-flight key** was still `batch.join('|')`, so
  `['a|b']` and `['a','b']` still collapsed into one shared request and the second caller read
  the first's answers under different names.

  The lesson is about the claim rather than the line: **"fixed at the root" needed a grep for
  every other instance of the pattern, and it did not get one.** A test now drives two
  genuinely-concurrent calls, and a second test asserts that legitimate de-duplication still
  works — the obvious way to "fix" a collision is to make every key unique, which would quietly
  double the request count.

- **[DELTA]** `web/_shared/lib/pronounce-client.js` (JSON in-flight key, widened
  `STREET_TYPE`), `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/MapPanel.jsx` (`pronunciations` prop, `sayOf`,
  effect deps), `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx` (keeps and passes the projection),
  `__tests__/pronounce-codex-1792.test.js` (11 new). `package.json` → **0.62.851**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Four mutations, each proved applied then fatal: the in-flight key
  back to a pipe join; the street list back to its pre-Codex version; `pronunciations` dropped
  from the sync deps; App discarding the projection again. **214 files, 4,778 passed** (+11).
  Seven builds clean, `test:render` 6/6 — and the cuisine app now paints **99 nodes, up from
  95**, which is the four new lines actually rendering.

- **[MY TESTS PINNED AN EXPRESSION FOR THE FOURTH TIME]** `locale-coverage-and-address` failed on
  this refactor while the behaviour it names was intact. Re-pointed at the requirement. Four
  occurrences is no longer a slip; the habit is writing the assertion by copying the line just
  written, and the fix is to write it from the requirement before looking at the code.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, carrying `[AMD-79]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged. The **map-language reload remains unapproved and unstarted**;
  **285 locale cells are in production with no native review**; the five server-baked fields are
  still frozen at search time; travel-times 403, `setMyDescription`, Weather v2, the Michelin
  pronunciation path, the stale `doc/Register/`, O-337/339/342/334, O-105, Korean, and the
  `serial-state.yml` rotation all stand.

  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No new paid path; the street widening only adds cache keys that were previously getting no answer at all. |

### [AMD-81] The review in the reader's language, station guides on the pin card — and a claim in #1793 I have to withdraw

- **[INTENT]** Operator, on a Japanese card: ***"card's review text isn't taken from japanese
  version - still show english"*** and ***"the pin card's mrt station name isn't translated"***.
  The same screenshot confirms `[AMD-78]` working — アリーヤ・ヌサンタラ… under the name and
  ディクソン・ロード under the address.

- **[① THE REVIEW — AND A DIAGNOSIS I GOT WRONG FIRST]** My first grep was scoped to `index.js`
  and found `enrichVenuesWithTranslatedReview` only on the Michelin and chat paths, so I stated
  that *"the main TMA search path never translates the review at all."* **That was false** — the
  call is in `cuisine-enrich.js:183`, with `targetLang: ctx.csLang`, i.e. the right language.
  Corrected to the operator in the same turn.

  The real cause is narrower and more interesting: that helper **returns immediately unless the
  search carries a nationality cuisine slug**, and even then it hunts for a review written in
  *that nationality's* language so it can print "( 🇮🇩 translated)". It is a different feature.
  Everything else falls through to `reviews[0]` and keeps whatever language Google returned —
  in Singapore, almost always English. **A call being present is not the same as the behaviour
  being present**, and reading the call site was not enough; reading its gate was.

  Fixed by localising the finalised `recentReview` into `csLang` through the **existing**
  `translateReview` (30-day Redis cache on placeId+index+src+tgt). Placed **after** the finalise
  loop, because that loop is still assigning `recentReview` — translating earlier would localise
  a value that is then overwritten.

- **[COST, STATED PLAINLY BECAUSE THIS IS THE LEAST DEDUPABLE THING IN THE ARC]** Venue names and
  streets collapse across venues; **a review does not**. This is one call per (venue review,
  locale), cached 30 days — squarely inside the operator's stated envelope, *"minimum token
  Gemini model call per venue per locale"*, and nowhere beyond it. Two gates keep the common
  case free: nothing runs for an English reader, and nothing runs when the review's own Places
  `languageCode` already matches the reader. Both are mutation-tested, because a cost control
  that is not tested is a comment.

- **[② THE STATION]** `[AMD-78]` wired `stationName()` on the pin card — correct, and
  insufficient: the register covers **zh and ms only**, so a Japanese reader still saw "Jalan
  Besar". **There is no official Japanese station name to show.** What helps is knowing how to
  SAY it, which is the answer already given for venue names and streets. The popup now asks for
  guides for exactly the stations the register does not cover, then renders once. Stations recur
  heavily and the client caches per (name, locale), so it is one request the first time a
  station is seen in a locale and free after. The guide sits **beside** the name, never instead
  of it.

- **[A CLAIM FROM #1793 I HAVE TO WITHDRAW]** That PR body said *"cuisine now paints **99 nodes,
  up from 95**, which is the new lines actually rendering."* I ran the harness three times here:
  **99, 95, 95.** The count is **not stable**, so it was never evidence of anything — the render
  smoke asserts that an app *paints*, not how much. I read a number that happened to move and
  gave it a meaning. The mutation tests carry that claim; the node count never did.

- **[DELTA]** `cuisine-enrich.js` (review localisation),
  `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/MapPanel.jsx` (station guides, `sayStation`),
  `__tests__/review-and-station-locale.test.js` (12 new), one pin re-pointed.
  `package.json` → **0.62.852**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Five mutations, each proved applied then fatal: the English no-op
  gate removed; the same-language skip removed; re-translating what the nationality helper
  already did; the station guide replacing the name instead of joining it; asking for stations
  the register already covers. **215 files, 4,790 passed** (+12). Seven builds clean,
  `test:render` 6/6.

- **[AND THE FIFTH EXPRESSION PIN]** `locale-coverage-and-address` broke twice in this one change
  — once on `stationName` moving into a `const`, once on `transitBlockHtml` gaining a third
  argument. Both times the behaviour was intact. Re-pointed at the requirement; the arity is no
  longer part of the assertion.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, on the branch carrying `[AMD-79]` and `[AMD-80]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** The map-language reload **remains unapproved and unstarted**. The **285 locale
  cells are still in production with no native review**, and the review localisation now adds a
  second body of machine-written text nobody has read in ja/ru/de/id/es. `nameLocal`,
  `nameReading` and `nameGloss` are still frozen at search time; `dishes` still gets no language.
  travel-times 403, `setMyDescription`, Weather v2, the Michelin pronunciation path, the stale
  `doc/Register/`, O-337/339/342/334, O-105, Korean, and the `serial-state.yml` rotation stand.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Two reported defects; both fixed, nothing else widened. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | New spend is bounded by the operator's stated per-venue-per-locale envelope and gated so the English case costs nothing. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The first diagnosis was wrong and is recorded as such; five mutations proved applied then fatal; a prior claim withdrawn after measuring it three times. |

### [AMD-82] The operator stated the rule in one sentence, and it exposed a hole I had just shipped

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: ***"translation only applies if the device language isnt the
  same"***. Read first as a confirmation of `[AMD-81]`'s gating. It is not — it is one condition,
  and v0.62.852 shipped two.

- **[THE HOLE]** `[AMD-81]` gated the review localisation as:

  ```js
  if (ctx.csLang && ctx.csLang !== 'en') {   // …then, inside: if (srcLang === csLang) return;
  ```

  The second clause is the operator's rule. The first is an extra restriction I added as a cost
  optimisation, on the assumption that reviews are English and only non-English readers need
  anything. **That assumption fails in exactly one direction: a Japanese-language review shown
  to an ENGLISH reader stayed Japanese**, because the reader's own locale short-circuited the
  whole block before the review's source language was ever consulted.

  Reviews are written by visitors. A foreign-language review on a Singapore venue is ordinary,
  not exotic — so this was not a corner case, it was the mirror image of the case the operator
  reported in the first place.

- **[FIXED TO THE RULE AND NOTHING ELSE]** The gate is now `if (ctx.csLang)`, with the single
  decision `srcLang === ctx.csLang → skip`, and no locale privileged over any other. **The
  common case still costs nothing**: an English review for an English reader is caught one line
  further in, by the review's own Places `languageCode` — so removing the outer clause did not
  turn every English search into a model call. What it does add is spend when an English reader
  meets a foreign review, which is precisely what the rule asks for.

- **[AND THE TEST WAS PART OF THE PROBLEM]** `[AMD-81]`'s suite asserted the extra clause
  directly — *"never fires for an English reader — the no-op path must cost nothing"* — so a
  **test encoded an optimisation as if it were the requirement**, and pinned the hole in place.
  That is a new variant of the recurring fault: the previous five were assertions copied from
  the implementation, this one was an assertion copied from an *assumption*. Re-pointed at the
  rule, with the case the old gate dropped now asserted explicitly.

- **[DELTA]** `cuisine-enrich.js` (gate), `__tests__/review-and-station-locale.test.js`
  (one assertion replaced by three). `package.json` → **0.62.853**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Two mutations, each proved applied then fatal: the `en`
  short-circuit restored (2 failures); the same-language skip removed (3 failures). The second
  matters as much as the first — the obvious over-correction is to translate unconditionally,
  which would pay to render English as English. **215 files, 4,792 passed.** Seven builds clean,
  `test:render` 6/6.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, on the branch carrying `[AMD-79]`–`[AMD-81]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged. Map-language reload unapproved and unstarted; 285 locale cells plus
  now a second body of machine-translated review text with **no native review**; `nameLocal`,
  `nameReading`, `nameGloss` frozen at search time; `dishes` gets no language; travel-times 403;
  `setMyDescription`; Weather v2; the Michelin pronunciation path; the stale `doc/Register/`;
  O-337/339/342/334; O-105; Korean; the `serial-state.yml` rotation.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | One sentence, read as a rule rather than as approval; the gate now matches it exactly. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Net spend still bounded by "languages differ"; the English-to-English case remains free. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Two mutations proved applied then fatal, covering both the hole and its over-correction. |

### [AMD-84] Dishes are REPLACED, not doubled — and that turns out to be a rule, not a preference

- **[INTENT]** Operator, on a Japanese card whose dish line had wrapped to three:
  ***"i am concerns about having both english and japanese (translated) for dishes, can we show
  dishes in translated rather than both to save line spacing"***.

- **[THE ANSWER IS YES, AND THE REASON IS BETTER THAN THE SPACE]** The card now carries three
  localised elements, and the operator's instinct separates them correctly:

  | Field | Treatment | Why |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | Venue name | English **+** guide | something depends on the English — matching the real venue, and saying it aloud |
  | Address | English **+** guide | something depends on the English — navigating, and showing a driver |
  | **Dishes** | **REPLACED** | **nothing** depends on the English; it is descriptive prose |

  So this is the first field where replacing is right, and the space saving is the *benefit*
  rather than the justification. Had it been the venue name, the same request would have had to
  be refused.

- **[THE ICONIC-NAME RULE ALREADY EXISTED]** `prompt-locale.js` carries `ICONIC_SG_DISHES` and
  the instruction *"keep iconic Singapore dish names in their ORIGINAL form, untranslated;
  translate the descriptive prose around them"* — written for the narration prompts.
  **Reused rather than re-invented**: two rules about the same thing drift, and this repo has
  the scar to prove it (v0.62.825, a hand-copied locale list three short of the real one).

  Matched on the **whole string**, not by substring: "laksa" is a name, but
  "laksa-inspired pasta with prawns" is a description that happens to contain it, and a
  substring match would have frozen the second one in English. Mutation-tested in both
  directions.

- **[COST — under the operator's cap, by construction]**
  - **Iconic dishes never reach the model.** Free, and more correct than a translation.
  - **Keyed per DISH, not per venue.** Dozens of venues serve chicken rice; one cached answer
    covers all of them. A venue-keyed cache would re-buy it every time — asserted by a test that
    three venues sharing a dish cost **one** call.
  - **One batched call** for the remainder, lite model first, 30-day TTL.
  - **Nothing runs for an English reader** — English is the extraction language.
  - The expensive part, extracting dishes from reviews, is **untouched and still English**, so
    its cache stays shared across all eight locales rather than fragmenting eight ways.

- **[A MISMATCHED REPLY IS REJECTED WHOLE]** The model returns one line per dish, in order. A
  short or long reply means it dropped or merged lines, and pairing them positionally anyway
  would attach **the wrong translation to the wrong dish** — worse than showing English, and
  invisible to anyone who cannot read the target language. The batch is discarded instead.

- **[DELTA]** `translate-dishes.js` (new), `cuisine-enrich.js` (wired after all four
  `v.dishes = filtered` sites), `__tests__/translate-dishes.test.js` (15 new).
  `package.json` → **0.62.854**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Five mutations, each proved applied then fatal: iconic dishes sent
  anyway; iconic matched by substring; a short reply paired positionally; appending instead of
  replacing; and localisation moved before the assignments. **216 files, 4,807 passed** (+15).

- **[AND THE FIFTH MUTATION FAILED TO FIRE ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT — because the MUTATION was
  wrong]** I moved the call to just before `// Review finalise`, which is still *after* all four
  assignments, so the test correctly passed. Re-done as a genuine reordering — before the first
  assignment — and it failed as it should. Worth recording because the tempting read of a
  non-firing mutation is "the test is weak"; here the test was fine and **the mutation did not
  express what I thought it did**. A mutation is itself a claim, and it needs checking like any
  other.

- **[THE SOURCE-SCAN-MATCHES-ITS-OWN-PROSE FAULT, THIRD TIME]** The wiring test counted **five**
  `v.dishes = filtered` sites and failed. The fifth was this module's own comment, which quotes
  the string it scans for. Comments are now stripped first — the same fix applied to the bare-`fr`
  guard and to `pronounce-pipe-names`. Three occurrences make it a standing rule rather than a
  one-off: **strip comments before any structural source assertion.**

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, on the branch carrying `[AMD-82]` and `[AMD-83]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** This adds a **third** body of machine-written text with no native review, on
  top of the 285 locale cells and the translated review quotes. The map-language reload is still
  unapproved and unstarted. Everything else stands.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | "translated rather than both"; replace, and the reason it is safe stated rather than assumed. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Spend bounded by iconic-skip, per-dish keying and an English short-circuit; the expensive extraction is untouched. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Journal + serial-state before the push. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Five mutations proved applied then fatal — including one re-done after it proved to be a bad mutation rather than a weak test. |

### [AMD-86] "Both languages" means TWO — and one field was quietly showing four

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"only address, restaurant names and transport name can show both
  languages"***. Two claims in one sentence, and they needed separate checks.

- **[① WHICH FIELDS — already held]** Cuisine type, dishes and the review quote are all
  **replaced** rather than doubled (the last two as of v0.62.852/854). Nothing outside the
  allowed three shows two languages. Audited rather than assumed, and asserted so it stays true.

- **[② HOW MANY — this is where it failed]** "Both" is **two**, and the cuisine card could
  render **four**. `translate-name.js` skips when `nameLocal` is set, so those two cannot
  co-occur — but **`nameGloss` and `namePronounce` guard only their OWN field**, so a venue
  could stack `name` + `nameLocal` + `nameGloss` + `namePronounce`. Four lines for one name.

  **Hawker and Transport were already correct** and are worth naming as the precedent: both use
  *mutually exclusive* conditions — with a curated name present, the guide is suppressed because
  that row already IS the answer. The cuisine card was the one place that had grown by
  accretion, each feature added without knowing about the last.

- **[NOTHING WAS DELETED]** All four guides remain; exactly one renders, by precedence:

  | | Guide | Why it ranks there |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 1 | `nameLocal` | the official native-script name — authoritative, not derived |
  | 2 | `nameReading` | a romanisation of a script the reader cannot read |
  | 3 | `namePronounce` | how to SAY it — the operator's original request |
  | 4 | `nameGloss` | what it MEANS |

  Curated-before-derived, the same ordering the hawker card already used. **For the case the
  operator actually reported** — a Singapore venue read in Japanese — the first two are absent,
  so the pronunciation line still wins and the screenshot is unchanged.

- **[THE ADDRESS KEEPS BOTH, AND THAT IS THE POINT OF THE ALLOWED LIST]** A test asserts the
  Latin address line survives beside its guide. It is on the list because something depends on
  the English: a reader shows it to a driver or types it into Maps. Collapsing it would have
  been the same mistake as doubling the dishes, in the opposite direction.

- **[DELTA]** `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/ResultCard.jsx` (four blocks → one precedence
  chain), `__tests__/both-languages-rule.test.js` (8 new), two earlier pins re-pointed.
  `package.json` → **0.62.855**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Three mutations, each proved applied then fatal: `nameGloss`
  restored as its own line; precedence reversed so derived beats curated; the address collapsed
  to its guide alone. **217 files, 4,815 passed** (+8).

- **[TWO MORE PROCESS FAULTS, BOTH ALREADY NAMED IN THIS ARC]**
  1. **A mutation that did not apply.** M1's anchor did not match, so the suite passed and for a
     moment that looked like a weak test. It was not a result at all. Re-run with a verified
     anchor, it failed as it should. This is the second time in two entries; the rule stands —
     **print the occurrence count before reading the outcome.**
  2. **An assertion measuring the wrong thing while looking authoritative.** The precedence test
     used `indexOf` over the whole file, and `sayNow`'s first occurrence is its *declaration*
     near the top of the component, not its rank in the chain. Scoped to the chain.
  3. And the pins: **sixth and seventh** expression pins re-pointed (`pronounce-name`,
     `pronounce-reactive`), both failing on structure while the behaviour they name held.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT**, on the branch carrying `[AMD-84]` and `[AMD-85]`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** A **spend** consequence now worth naming: if only one guide is ever shown,
  computing all four is waste — `nameGloss` and `namePronounce` are separate Gemini calls that
  may never be displayed. Server-side gating (skip the pronunciation call when a reading or
  local name already exists) would save real money, but it is a behaviour change on a paid path
  and is **left for the operator** rather than taken. Otherwise unchanged: three bodies of
  machine-written text with no native review; the map-language reload unapproved.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

### [AMD-88] #1796 merged on request — and the anchor that recorded #1795 was 45 minutes in the future

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: ***"mark it ready and merge"***. First merge this arc the
  operator delegated rather than performed. Everything below the merge itself is the standing
  post-merge routine; **this record is held per D-205**, shipping with the next substantive PR —
  thirteenth consecutive fold-forward.

- **[DELTA]** #1796 undrafted and **squash-merged as `f18f515c`, 2026-08-30T20:17:35+08:00**,
  carrying **v0.62.855**: `[AMD-86]`'s single-guide precedence chain on the cuisine card, plus
  `[AMD-87]`, the #1795 merge record, folded forward. 7 files, +293/−41.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** By content per D-123, read back **out of `origin/main`** rather
  than inferred from the merge notice (D-199 — a notification is a claim about the past):

  | Property | Read from `origin/main` |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `package.json` | `0.62.855` |
  | the chain exists | `const nameGuide =` at :494, `data-name-guide={nameGuide.key}` at :507 |
  | exactly one guide renders | `{venue.nameLocal && (` **0**, `nameReading` **0**, `nameGloss` **0**, `{sayNow && (` **0** |
  | precedence order | `nameLocal` → `nameReading` → `sayNow` → `nameGloss`, read in sequence |

- **[THE FINDING — an anchor cannot be in the future]** `[AMD-87]` stamped
  `last_anchor_time: 30-08 '26 20:30 SGT`. It was written at **19:45:57 SGT** (`aa689c04`), and
  the only event it cited — the #1795 squash — is at **19:41:22 SGT** (`e3f88c84`). So the anchor
  sat **45 minutes ahead of both** of the two values D-203 takes the `max()` of, while
  `last_anchor_source` read `system_clock_d203_max_sensor_wins_over_pr_1795_merge`: a computation
  **asserted but never performed**. That is X-13's shape exactly — a rule announced as rigour in
  the same breath as being broken — and it is the second time in this file's history.

  It survived because `__tests__/serial-state-anchor.test.js` compares the three representations
  **to each other**, and all three were written together and agreed perfectly. Three concordant
  wrong values pass that gate cleanly. The new assertion needs no knowledge of what the writer was
  looking at: both of D-203's inputs are in the past, so their `max()` is too, and *ahead of now*
  is a place the rule cannot reach under any input. Proved by replaying the exact defect — anchor
  moved forward in all three representations at once (occurrence counts 1→0 printed before the
  run); the new check failed, **the three concordance checks still passed**, which is the finding
  restated as a result.

- **[THE SECOND DEFECT — the rotation was dropping a note per pass]** Measured, not described:
  `[AMD-85]`'s note is **gone** from `doc/.serial-state.yml` (0 occurrences) because the rotation
  overwrites `prior100` instead of shifting the chain. The chain had also accumulated **duplicate
  keys at 100–110**, where YAML keeps only the last of each pair — so the loss was silent and
  wider than one entry. The ISO chain leaked the same way: `20:20` was overwritten by `20:30`
  without ever being rotated in. Chain renumbered here **losslessly** — 108 notes in, 109 out,
  every prior value still present, all keys unique — and both dropped ISO values restored and
  labelled rather than erased, per AU-1. This closes the `serial-state.yml` rotation defect
  carried in `[KNOWN GAPS]` since 22-08.

- **[STATUS]** #1796 is **PROD** on `main` at **v0.62.855**; Railway deploys from `main`. This
  record is **DRAFT**, held for the next substantive PR.

- **[TEST]** +2 (`serial-state-anchor.test.js`, 4 → 6).

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged and unclosed by this merge: three bodies of machine-written text now
  live with **no native review** (285 locale cells, translated review quotes, translated dish
  names); the map-language reload still unapproved and unstarted; the `nameGloss`/`namePronounce`
  spend question with the operator. Newly stated: the post-merge Codex review on #1796 had not
  landed when this was written, and a check-in is armed to read it — the session unsubscribes at
  merge, so nothing else would.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-123 by content, D-199 read from the tree, D-203 anchor computed with a tool, D-205 held. |

### [AMD-89] v0.62.856 — the pronunciation was switched off for the venues it was built for

- **[INTENT]** Codex filed one **P2** on #1796, at `2026-08-30T12:19:37Z` — **two minutes after
  the merge**, the fifth time it has raced a merge on this repo. Verified against source rather
  than accepted, then put to the operator, who chose: ***"Pronunciation wins for foreign
  script"***.

- **[THE FINDING, AND WHAT VERIFYING IT CHANGED]** Codex: *"When a venue carries `nameLocal` or
  `nameReading` … the first two arms always win, so `sayNow` can never render."* True. Checking
  the blast radius made it both **narrower and worse** than reported:

  - **Narrower.** `nameLocal` is attached only by `local-name.js` and `nameReading` only by
    `translate-name.js`, both gated on `LOCAL_LANG_BY_CC = {JP, KR, CN, TW, HK, MO, TH}`.
    Singapore and Malaysia set **neither**, so nothing in production regressed and the
    operator's own Japanese screenshots were right. Codex's "English-to-French toggle" framing
    reads as general; it is not.
  - **Worse.** Inside those seven countries the shadowing is *total*, and those are exactly
    where a foreign-script name lives. The pronunciation line was asked for in the words *"help
    foreigner to pronoun … learn to pronounce the restaurant name"* — so curated-first had
    quietly switched the feature off for its **best case** while passing every test.

  Not decided unilaterally: it is a visible behaviour change on a paid path, so it went to the
  operator with the trade stated.

- **[DELTA]**
  - **new `web/_shared/lib/name-guide.js`** — `hasForeignScript()` + `pickNameGuide()`. Order is
    `say → local → reading → gloss` when `venue.name` carries CJK/Hangul/Thai, and the
    v0.62.855 order `local → reading → say → gloss` otherwise. Still exactly one line.
  - **`ResultCard.jsx`** — renders the function's answer; the inline chain is gone. The icon is
    chosen from the returned key so the module stays JSX-free and callable in Node.
  - **`package.json`** → 0.62.856.

- **[THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE SCRIPT, NOT `nameLocal`]** The obvious implementation — "suppress
  `nameLocal` when a pronunciation exists" — is wrong, and wrong in a way that would have looked
  like the fix. *Blue Note Tokyo* is Latin-named and **also** carries a Japanese `nameLocal`,
  because `local-name.js` gates on the script of the name it fetches from Google, not the one on
  the card. That reader can already say the name; the Japanese form is the line they hold up to
  a driver — the same reasoning the address rule already settled. Keying on `nameLocal` would
  have closed the finding by breaking that. Tested in both directions.

  The regex mirrors `RE_CJK_THAI` in `translate-name.js` — what the **server** uses to answer the
  same question about the same string — and deliberately **not** the wider `HAS_LOCAL_SCRIPT` in
  `local-name.js`, which begins at U+3000 and would flip a Latin name containing one full-width
  space onto the foreign branch. A mutation proves that distinction fires.

- **[WHY IT MOVED INTO A MODULE]** Not tidying. Five tests had pinned this chain by **scanning the
  source**, and four broke on a refactor while the behaviour held. Pins six and seven are
  re-pointed here, and all three now assert by **calling the function** — the thing the previous
  five re-points kept wishing for.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Three mutations, each proved applied by occurrence count (1 → 0)
  **before** the run, each fatal to its intended assertions and nothing else: reverting to
  curated-first failed 3; widening the regex failed the full-width-space test; passing the static
  payload instead of the live projection failed 2. **218 files, 4,829 tests green.** Seven builds
  clean, `test:render` **6/6** — run after the build, per the rule that a smoke passes on a stale
  bundle when the build failed.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** at v0.62.856. Ships `[AMD-88]`, the #1796 merge record, folded forward
  per D-205 — fourteenth consecutive.

- **[TEST]** +14 (`name-guide-precedence.test.js`, 12 new; `serial-state-anchor.test.js`, +2 from
  `[AMD-88]`). 4,815 → 4,829.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged: three bodies of machine-written text live with **no native review**;
  the map-language reload approved in shape but not started; `doc/Register/` stale. The
  `nameGloss`/`namePronounce` **spend** question is now sharper, not answered — with the
  pronunciation winning for foreign script, `nameGloss` is the field most likely computed and
  never shown.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | One P2, verified, then the choice put to the operator with the trade stated. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Additive module; nothing deleted; all four guides still reachable. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-205 fold-forward; D-203 anchor computed with a tool; preflight run in full. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Blast radius measured from `LOCAL_LANG_BY_CC`, not from the report; three mutations with occurrence counts printed. |

### [AMD-91] v0.62.857 — items 1 and 3: the guides the Michelin path lacked, and the ones nobody should buy

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"can we do 1, 3, 4, 6"*** — from the outstanding list in reply
  8,774. This entry covers **1** (Michelin path missing `attachPronunciations` and
  `attachNameGloss`) and **3** (`nameGloss`/`namePronounce` spend gating). They are one thing
  seen from two sides — which guides exist on a path, and which are worth buying — so they ship
  together. Items **4** and **6** follow separately.

- **[① THE MICHELIN PATH NEVER HAD TWO OF THE FOUR]** It attached `nameLocal` (:11033) and
  `nameReading` (:11043) and neither paid guide. Survivable while the card rendered whatever
  happened to be present; **it stopped being survivable at v0.62.856**, when the operator chose
  *"pronunciation wins for foreign script"*. On this path a Tokyo venue carries `nameLocal` and
  no pronunciation, so `pickNameGuide` falls straight through to the native-script line — the
  decision the operator made two hours ago **looked implemented and was not delivered**. The
  precedence was never the problem; the field was never populated. Both calls added, in the
  reader's language (`csLang || michDeviceLang`), applying v0.62.845's correction rather than
  waiting to rediscover it here.

- **[② EXACTLY ONE GUIDE RENDERS, SO THREE ARE WASTE]** Two of the three cost a Gemini call per
  venue per locale. New `name-guide-server.js` answers *can this guide still reach the screen?*
  and both paid attachers now receive only the venues where it can.

- **[THE BOUNDARY, AND WHY THIS IS NOT A SECOND COPY OF THE RULE]** The gate cannot import
  `web/_shared/lib/name-guide.js`: this is CommonJS, `web/cuisine` is `"type": "module"`, and
  Rollup refuses the mix. **That is measured, not assumed** — `open-hours.js:375` records the
  exact error from trying, and `index.js:13230` reaches the same conclusion independently. The
  repo's own answer is not to keep two implementations (the drift O-335 was about), so the gate
  encodes as little as it can:

  - `nameGloss` is **last in both orders**, so "reachable" is exactly "nothing else exists" —
    one fact true in both branches, not a copy of the ordering;
  - `namePronounce` is unreachable in exactly one case, a Latin name with a curated guide;
  - the script test is **not** reimplemented — `translate-name.js` already exports
    `nameScriptLang`, is already CommonJS, and is already the server's authority on it.

  The residual drift risk is guarded by a test that imports the ESM module and the CJS one
  **together** and cross-checks them over a 40-case matrix: for every shape where the gate
  refuses to buy, supplying the guide must not change what the card renders. Vitest loads what
  the bundler will not. A comment asking the next writer to keep two things in sync has failed
  twice in this repo already — `doc/.serial-state.yml`'s own header records both — so it is a
  test.

- **[FAILS OPEN, DELIBERATELY]** Anything the gate does not understand returns *reachable*. A
  bug then costs money rather than silently removing a line the reader was meant to see. The
  operator asked to stop waste, not to risk blanking the feature.

- **[A THIRD DEFECT, FOUND WHILE READING THE PATH BEING GATED]** `name-gloss.js:57` read
  `dev === 'fr' ? 'French' : 'English'` — so the gloss was **only ever written in English or
  French**, and a Japanese reader was told in English what a Vietnamese name means. Exactly the
  class AMD-62 swept out of the seven narration prompts; it survived because this file was not
  one of them. Now `langName(dev)`, gated on the app's own `APP_LOCALES` so `langName('kr')`
  cannot tell the model to write in "KR" — the trap AMD-62 hit. **The cache version is split
  rather than bumped**: en/fr entries are correct and stay on `v1`, so the two highest-volume
  locales are not re-bought to fix six others; the other six hold English text under a
  non-English key — worse than a miss, because it looks like a hit — and move to `v2`.

- **[A PROCESS FAULT, AND IT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE CODE]** Restarting the branch from
  `origin/main` at the start of this batch — the routine, six-times-rehearsed recovery from the
  pre-push guard — **silently discarded `3490bac0`, the held `[AMD-90]` merge record**. The two
  rules collide by construction: D-205 holds a record on the *branch*, `origin/main` does not
  carry it, so `git checkout -B <branch> origin/main` deletes exactly the artefact D-205 exists
  to preserve. Nothing failed; the tree simply came back one commit lighter, and the next
  substantive PR would have shipped without the record while every other check stayed green.

  It was caught only because a counter bump printed **False** and that was read rather than
  skimmed — the same habit that turned two non-applying mutations into findings earlier in this
  arc. Recovered by resetting to `3490bac0` and replaying the code half as a patch, so nothing
  was retyped from memory. **The rule this leaves:** before restarting the branch, check whether
  it carries a held record that `main` does not — a merge record folded forward is unpushed work
  by design, and the guard's recovery treats unpushed work as disposable.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Four mutations, each proved applied by occurrence count (1 → 0)
  before running, each fatal only to its own assertions: dropping the foreign-script branch from
  the gate (3), failing closed instead of open (2), reverting the gloss ternary (2), and putting
  the phone's language back on the new Michelin call (2). **219 files, 4,843 green.** Seven
  builds, `test:render` 6/6 — run although `web/` is untouched, because "no web change" is a
  claim and the smoke is cheap.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** at v0.62.857. Ships `[AMD-90]`, the #1797 merge record, folded forward
  per D-205 — sixteenth consecutive.

- **[TEST]** +14 (`guide-spend-gate.test.js`). 4,829 → 4,843.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Items **4** (map-language reload) and **6** (77 display-string ternaries
  outside `web/`, of which 60 are in `index.js`) are approved and not yet started — this entry
  closes half the operator's request, and saying which half is the point. Unchanged: **no native
  reader** has seen the 285 locale cells, the translated review quotes or the translated dish
  names. The gate reduces `nameGloss` spend but does not answer whether a gloss is worth buying
  at all now that it is fourth of four.

  | :--- | :--- | :--- |

### [AMD-92] v0.62.858 — item 4: the map re-languages by reloading, and the search comes back

- **[INTENT]** Operator, from the outstanding list: ***"can we do 1, 3, 4, 6"***; item 4 is the
  map-language reload, shaped earlier as ***"can you first keep the search results and then
  reload the map and present it back?"***. Asked which of two implementations, the operator
  chose **the full app reload with the results restored**.

- **[THE CHOICE, AND WHY IT WAS PUT RATHER THAN TAKEN]** Google fixes the Maps SDK's
  `language` at injection and offers no way to change it after, so "reload the map" means a new
  SDK. Two routes: re-inject in place (app stays mounted, results never leave React state) or
  reload the page and restore. The first sounds strictly better **and is the one I could not
  prove** — Google does not support unloading the SDK, and nothing here exercises a real one:
  `test:render` runs without Maps entirely. It would have shipped unverified onto a live map.
  That asymmetry, not a preference, is what made it a question.

- **[IT OVERRULES A DECISION THE REPO HAD ALREADY MADE]** `gmaps-language.js` argued:
  *"Reloading the SDK to fix it would blank and re-tile the map under the reader's finger,
  which is worse than the seam."* Fair for reloading **alone**; wrong once the operator supplied
  the missing half. With the results preserved the trade inverts — a one-second reload that
  returns you to the same list beats a map permanently labelled in the language you just left.
  The sentence is **rewritten, not left standing**, and a test holds it to that: a file arguing
  against what the codebase does is worse than no comment, because the next reader believes it.

- **[DELTA]** New `web/_shared/lib/locale-reload.js` — `loadedMapsLanguage()`,
  `mapsLanguageIsStale()`, `stashAndReload()`, `takeStash()`. `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx`
  gains a mount restore and a `gia:locale` listener. `gmaps-language.js` comment corrected.

- **[THE THREE DECISIONS INSIDE IT]**
  1. **It reloads only when a map is actually stale.** Most locale switches happen before a
     search, and then the next injection is already correct. Staleness is read off the script
     tag in the page, never inferred from "the locale changed" — which would reload on every
     toggle. And the comparison goes through `mapsLanguage()`, because `zh` is not one of
     Google's codes and a correctly-loaded `zh-CN` map would otherwise look stale forever.
  2. **The scan reads any Maps URL, not `script[data-gmaps]`.** Only one of the four loaders
     stamps that attribute — `gmaps-loader.js` records it — and the one that does not is
     cuisine's `MapPanel`, the very panel this feature is for. Asserted, so a future migration
     that adds the attribute does not silently make the scan look redundant.
  3. **The reload is deferred 400 ms.** `setActiveLocale` writes localStorage, dispatches, and
     only then dynamically imports `api.js` to POST the preference that makes bot replies
     follow the toggle. Reloading synchronously would race that import. The map's language is
     safe regardless — it is read back out of localStorage, written before the dispatch — so
     the delay protects the server-side half only. **It is a mitigation, not a guarantee**, and
     is written as one: if the import is slower, the preference is re-sent on the next toggle,
     which is why the reload is not gated on the POST completing.

- **[WHAT IT DOES NOT RESTORE, SAID PLAINLY]** Four fields come back — `venues`,
  `searchCenter`, `userLoc`, `selectedCityLocation` — plus scroll position. `App.jsx` holds 97
  `useState` calls; filter chips and transient UI reset to defaults. That is the search and the
  map position, which is what the operator asked for, and not "the app exactly as it was".

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Four mutations, each proved applied by occurrence count (1 → 0),
  each fatal only to its own assertions: reloading whenever the locale changes (1), comparing
  the raw locale instead of the Google mapping (1), reading the stash without clearing it (2),
  and letting the listener close over the first render (1). **220 files, 4,859 green.** Seven
  builds, `test:render` 6/6. The cuisine node count read 95 where the previous run read 99 —
  that figure has been unstable across runs all arc and was withdrawn as evidence once already;
  it is noted here so nobody re-reads it as a regression.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** at v0.62.858, pushed onto **#1798** rather than a third pull request —
  the operator asked for four items in one breath and `[AMD-90]` is already riding there.

- **[TEST]** +16 (`locale-reload.test.js`). 4,843 → 4,859.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Item **6** (77 display-string ternaries outside `web/`, 60 in `index.js`)
  remains. Unchanged: **no native reader** has seen the 285 locale cells, the translated review
  quotes or the translated dish names. Untested by anything here: whether a Telegram webview
  restores scroll the way a browser does — the effect uses `requestAnimationFrame`, which is the
  right shape, but only a device confirms it.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Item 4 as the operator shaped it; the implementation fork put to them rather than chosen. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Additive module; the reload fires only on a stale map and is injectable in tests. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Preflight in full; TDZ position asserted from both sides, the rule v0.62.841 and v0.62.843 cost us. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The prior decision read out of `gmaps-language.js` and corrected there; four mutations with counts. |

### [AMD-94] v0.62.859 — item 6: the bot's two-locale ternaries, keyed

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"can we do 1, 3, 4, 6"***. This is the last of the four — the bot and
  server half of the ternary sweep AMD-59/61 did for `web/` and deliberately left.

- **[THE DEFECT]** Sites reading `lang === 'fr' ? '<French>' : '<English>'`, written when the app
  had two locales and never revisited when it reached eight. Every one served **English** to the
  six locales added since — under chrome the same user had already switched to Japanese or
  Russian. **130 call sites** are now keyed against **129 new `bot.*` keys** in `i18n.js`.

- **[FIVE SHAPES, AND ONLY THE FIRST WAS OBVIOUS]** The count moved from 113 to 130 because the
  remainder was read each time rather than the first pattern being trusted:

  | # | Shape | Sites | How it was found |
  | :-- | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | 1 | single-line, literal arms | 113 | the extractor |
  | 2 | `const isFr = lang === 'fr'`, then `isFr ? … : …` | 9 | grepping the *family*, not the pattern |
  | 3 | the same, split across lines so `?` starts line 2 | 7 | neither of the above saw them |
  | 4 | double-quoted **French** arm (it contains an apostrophe) | 2 | auditing what was left over |
  | 5 | multi-line with a double-quoted **English** arm | 1 | the sweep's own test, stricter than the tool |

  Shape 5 is the one worth keeping: the **test admits double quotes where the extractor's regex
  did not**, so the check caught what the tool had missed. That is the only reason the count is
  130 and not 129.

- **[THE FALSE POSITIVE THAT WOULD HAVE BROKEN LANGUAGE SELECTION ITSELF]** **18 sites** read
  `lang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'` — locale-code **plumbing**, character-for-character the same
  shape as a display ternary. Keying them would have replaced the code that *picks* a language
  with a translation of the word "fr". Excluded by rule, and the rule is now a test: `clip-store`,
  `pipeline` and `bot-fun-facts` are asserted to still contain theirs.

- **[TWO MORE DEFECTS FOUND ON THE WAY]**
  1. **The `/language` notice only knew two language names.** Two nested ternaries produced
     `'French'` or `'English'` and nothing else, so a Japanese reader was told — in Japanese —
     that their client was in "English". Now `bot.langname.*`: eight names × eight reading
     locales.
  2. **Dates followed nobody.** `toLocaleDateString(lang === 'fr' ? 'fr-FR' : 'en-GB')` formatted
     six locales in British English. Now a `DATE_LOCALE` table — data, so a ninth locale is one
     line rather than a hunt.

- **[BREAKAGE I INTRODUCED, AND WHY `node --check` DID NOT SEE IT]** The rewrite emitted `t(…)`
  into four files that **do not import it**, and into `venue-templates.js`, which imports it
  under the alias `tr`. Every one still passed `node --check`, because an undefined identifier is
  a **runtime** error, not a syntax error. Caught by loading each module and by the suite. There
  is now a test asserting that any file calling `t('bot.…')` can actually reach it.

- **[en AND fr WERE NEVER RETYPED]** Both were lifted from the ternary arms **by script**, so
  they cannot drift from what shipped — the AMD-61 discipline. Placeholders are **named**, not
  positional: AMD-61 measured that `{p1}/{p2}` filled by index silently transposes for zh and ja,
  which reorder the count and the noun. `tn()` already substituted by name, so nothing new was
  built for it.

- **[THE EXTRACTOR MATCHED ITS OWN PROSE — FOURTH TIME THIS ARC]** Its first run produced a key
  called `bot.namegloss.english` from a **comment** in `name-gloss.js` quoting the very ternary
  the previous commit had removed. Comments are now masked *length-preservingly* before the scan,
  so offsets stay valid for the rewrite that follows. The rule was already reflexive in two test
  files; it is now in the tooling.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Five mutations, each proved applied by occurrence count (1 → 0),
  each fatal only to its own assertions: one locale cell left as English (1), an invented
  placeholder (2), a display ternary restored (1), locale plumbing swept (1), and the module-level
  import removed (1). A **script-contamination scan** over all 129 × 8 cells caught one real slip
  — a stray CJK character inside a Russian string — which reading would not have. A **plural
  check** caught `{a} мест{b}` and `{a}件{b}` rendering as "3 местs" and "3件s", because the arm's
  `{b}` is an English plural suffix; six locales now drop it. **221 files, 4,870 green.** Seven
  builds, `test:render` 6/6.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** at v0.62.859. All four requested items are now delivered: 1 and 3 in
  #1798 (PROD), 4 in #1799, 6 here.

- **[TEST]** +11 (`bot-ternary-sweep.test.js`). 4,859 → 4,870.

- **[KNOWN GAPS — AND THIS ONE IS LARGE]** **~750 machine-authored strings in six languages, and
  no native speaker has read one of them.** That is not a caveat on this entry; it is the entry's
  main risk. It joins the 285 locale cells, the translated review quotes and the translated dish
  names already live. The operator was offered the native reader's pass as its own item and chose
  the other four, so this is proceeding on an informed decision — but the pile has roughly doubled
  and it is now bot text, which users read as the product speaking, not card decoration.

  Not swept, deliberately, and named so nobody reads the sweep as total: **five sites index a
  DATA table by locale** (`disambig.disclosure[lang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en']`,
  `touristExplainer[…]`) — the two-locale limit is in the data, not the string, so widening them
  is a different job. And `gemini-client.js:1757` picks between two **variables**
  (`lang === 'fr' ? labelFr : labelEn`), which no literal-arm sweep can reach.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

### [AMD-96] v0.62.860 — the Wrong-Log: 226 lessons, and the one that was in the repo

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"I scanned that there are more than 77 times the word 'wrong' appear
  in the conversation for the past 3 days. Can you copy those paragraphs into the vibe-coding
  journal with a section called Wrong-Log. These are good learning lessons."***

- **[MEASURED FIRST]** Their ">77" was a good estimate of the window they looked at: **93
  occurrences in 84 paragraphs** across 28–30 Aug. Asked whether to keep that window, the
  operator chose the whole transcript — **226 paragraphs, 181 replies, 12 days, ~58 KB**. Two
  spikes dominate, both of **69**: 22-08 and 30-08.

- **[VERBATIM, AND ALL OF THEM]** No curation. The operator said *copy*, AU-1 says add never
  compress, and choosing which of my own mistakes are worth keeping is exactly the judgement
  that should not be mine. Signal was measured before deciding rather than assumed: of the 84
  sampled paragraphs, **zero** were "nothing wrong" false positives.

- **[TWO OPERATOR DECISIONS THAT COLLIDE, AND WHICH ONE WON]** They chose the hosted panel gated
  behind `VIBE_JOURNAL_KEY`, and then — shown that `ang-kl/gia` is **public** and that
  `data/session-replies.ndjson` is already committed to it — chose the data **never committed**.
  **Railway deploys from git, so a gitignored file can never reach `/doc/vibe-journal.html`.**
  The two cannot both hold. The stricter one, chosen second and with the public-repo fact in
  hand, wins: no panel, no route, nothing new served. Written into the script header, the
  `.gitignore` and `VibeCodingRecord.md` so a later reader sees a **reversed decision**, not a
  gap to fill.

- **[COMMIT THE TOOL, NEVER THE DATA]** `extract-wrong-log.mjs` + `build-wrong-log.mjs` carry no
  paragraphs, so they commit safely and run anywhere — including the operator's local corpus
  (6,822+ replies across `gia` and `gia-web`), which sees far more than this container.

- **[REUSE, NOT A SECOND COPY]** The redaction pipeline is **lifted verbatim** into
  `redact.mjs` and imported by both extractors. It is the product of five Codex findings across
  #1721–#1725, each a real leak or a real over-mask; a paraphrase would have lost them and the
  drift would have been silent until it leaked.

- **[THE ZERO THAT PROVES NOTHING]** The run reported `redactions: 0` from 83 collected operator
  secrets — the exact shape of number the original author warned "reads as assurance". So the
  tests prove the pipeline **fires**, on planted credentials, rather than inferring health from
  the count.

- **[TWO MUTATIONS THAT DID NOT KILL ANYTHING, WHICH IS THE POINT OF RUNNING THEM]** Deleting
  the operator-secret layer, and deleting the over-masking guard, both left the suite green.
  Diagnosed rather than shrugged at:
  - my word-shaped fixture `SomeWordShapedValue9` scores **3.68 bits** and is caught by
    `looksRandom` — the test *named* layer 2b and *exercised* layer 2a. Measured isolator:
    `forkiasee2languages`, no uppercase, so entropy rejects it and only the positional rule can
    find it.
  - my config fixture sat **inside a sentence**, so it failed the bare-line test for an
    unrelated reason and never reached the guard at all.

  Both re-pointed; both mutations now fire. A test that cannot see the code it names is not a
  test.

- **[AND THE LESSON THAT WAS SITTING IN THE REPO]** The pre-commit scan found **5** matches. Four
  were my own all-zero fixtures. The fifth was the operator's **real** `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN`
  value, verbatim in the comment my refactor was moving — and it is on **`origin/main` in a
  public repo**. Not a new find: it is Register **O-325**, already open, already recorded in
  `[KNOWN GAPS]` as *"not mine to close"*, with the token flagged for rotation.

  What was mine to do was **stop carrying it forward**. Moving the block was the chance; the
  literal is dropped and the lesson kept, since it needs the token's *shape*, not its value.
  Net effect on source: **1 → 0**. The remaining diff match is a **deletion line** — a diff
  recording a secret being removed is the fix, not the leak. **O-325 still needs the operator to
  rotate the token**, because git history and `doc/Chat/` cannot be edited away under AU-1.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Counts reproduced independently before and after (226/181/12,
  and 85 for `--since 2026-08-28` — 85 not 84 because 31-08 SGT had begun, confirmed from the
  per-day table rather than waved through). Four mutations applied by occurrence count, all
  fatal after the two fixture repairs. `public/doc/vibe-journal.html` **byte-identical** after a
  regenerate. `git check-ignore` asserted, and `git ls-files` shows **0** of the three outputs
  tracked. **222 files, 4,886 green.**

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** at v0.62.860. The artifact itself is handed to the operator directly,
  since it cannot travel through git.

- **[TEST]** +16 (`wrong-log.test.js`). 4,870 → 4,886.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-325 — rotate `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN`.** Still open, still the operator's.
  The Wrong-Log is ephemeral by design: nothing in git means nothing survives this container, and

### [AMD-97] v0.62.861 — the review cache dropped one field, and it explained two symptoms

- **[INTENT]** Operator, on a French session over Singapore: ***"i re-search — french restname
  and address are okay but review still in japanese"***. Diagnosed before the Wrong-Log request
  arrived; announced as "implementing now", then queued behind it. Picked back up rather than
  dropped, because an announced fix that quietly disappears is worse than one never promised.

- **[THE CLUE THEY DIDN'T MENTION]** Their screenshot also read `Essayez: 生煎包 • 龍井レモンティー`
  — **the dish names were Japanese too**. That is what made the cause findable: any explanation
  had to account for both, which ruled out every theory about the review path alone.

- **[THE CHAIN, EACH LINK READ IN SOURCE]**
  1. `vibe-suggest.js:105` fetches reviews with **no `languageCode`**, so Google returns each in
     its original language — Japanese here.
  2. `vault-index.js` cached them as `{ text, rating, publishTime }`. **`languageCode` dropped.**
  3. `cuisine-enrich.js:200` reads that cache, and the review snippet **and the dish names** are
     both extracted from it — so both came out Japanese.
  4. `reviewLanguagePrimary()` had no code to read, returned **null**, and the caller fell back
     to `'en'`.
  5. The translator was told a Japanese paragraph was English. It returned it unchanged, the
     `out.trim() !== rr` guard rejected the result, and the Japanese survived.

  Nothing threw, nothing logged. The name, address and pronunciation were correctly French
  because **none of them come from that cache** — which is precisely why it read as a
  translation bug rather than a cache bug.

- **[WHAT I HAD WRONG, AND IT IS HALF THE DIAGNOSIS]** I told the operator that
  `cuisine-enrich.js:32`'s `reviewText` required an object and so broke on the cached string
  form. **It does not** — it has handled both shapes all along. The break was entirely in the
  language detection. Had I "fixed" that line I would have changed working code and left the
  bug. Pinned in a test so the correction outlives the reply.

- **[DELTA]** Two changes, one per end of the chain:
  - `vault-index.js` keeps `languageCode`, taken from **whichever field the text came from** —
    `text` is Google's translation, `originalText` is the author's, and pairing one's text with
    the other's code would mislabel it, which is the same class of error as the bug.
  - `reviewLanguagePrimary()` falls back to `nameScriptLang()` when no code is present. This
    **heals the already-cached entries immediately** instead of waiting out their 24-hour TTL,
    and costs nothing: that function is already the server's authority on the question and
    already CommonJS.

- **[TWO THINGS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE]**
  - **The cache key was not made locale-specific.** It holds the *original* text and always did;
    keying it per reader would re-fetch every venue's reviews once per locale — eight times the
    Places calls — to buy a translation we already perform.
  - **It does not guess.** A Latin-script review with no code stays `null`, and the caller's
    `'en'` default handles it. A language *guesser* would be the wrong repair: a **wrong** source
    language tells the translator to convert from something the text is not, which is exactly the
    failure being fixed. Stated as a limit, not papered over.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Four mutations, each proved applied by occurrence count, each
  fatal only to its own assertions: removing the script fallback (3), letting the script override
  an explicit `languageCode` (1), guessing on Latin text (1), and dropping `languageCode` at the
  writer again (2). **223 files, 4,897 green.** Seven builds, `test:render` 6/6.

- **[STATUS]** **DRAFT** at v0.62.861, on the branch behind #1800.

- **[TEST]** +11 (`review-language-cache.test.js`).

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** The operator's **first** report — *"map didn't switch to french"* — was a
  deployment race, not a defect: Railway's record shows v0.62.858 reached SUCCESS at 22:51:12Z,
  two and a half minutes after they tested. Unconfirmed until they retest, and it stays open
  until they do. Also unchanged: **O-325**, rotate `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN`; ~750 machine-authored
  bot strings with no native reader.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Both reported symptoms traced to one cause; the deployment race separated from the real defect. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Additive field; no cache key change, so no re-fetch and no cost spike. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | Preflight in full; the correction to my own diagnosis recorded rather than quietly dropped. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Every link read in source; four mutations with counts; the `reviewText` claim retracted by reading the line. |

### [AMD-98] #1800 merged — and a blast radius I under-reported when I shipped it

- **[INTENT]** No operator instruction. Recorded per the standing rule; **held per D-205**,
  nineteenth consecutive fold-forward.

- **[DELTA]** #1800 marked ready at `00:17:59Z` and squash-merged as **`334dd654`,
  2026-08-31T08:18:02+08:00** — five seconds later, the fourth PR running to carry a
  five-second ready window. Carries **v0.62.860** (the Wrong-Log tooling) and **v0.62.861** (the
  review-language cache fix), plus `[AMD-95]` and `[AMD-97]`.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** By content per D-123, from the merged tree — and **three of my own
  checks were wrong before any of them was right**, which is most of what this entry is worth:

  | Property | Result |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `package.json` | `0.62.861` |
  | `redact.mjs` + both wrong-log scripts | present, tracked |
  | **wrong-log outputs tracked** | **0** — on disk, ignored, uncommitted |
  | script fallback in `cuisine-review-language.js` | present |
  | `languageCode` preserved in `vault-index.js` | present |
  | `[AMD-96]` + `[AMD-97]` | both |

  **The three bad checks.** (1) `ls doc/VibeCodingRecord/data/ | grep -c wrong-log` returned
  **2** and read as a failure — it was measuring the **local filesystem**, where the gitignored
  outputs correctly still sit, not the tree. (2) `git log origin/main..HEAD` showed **3
  "unmerged" commits** — a squash merge always differs by SHA, so the test had to be a **content
  diff**, which is empty. (3) `git ls-files | grep -c wrong-log` returned **3**, which were the
  three *scripts* — they belong tracked; the loose grep could not tell a tool from its output.
  Corrected to `git ls-files <exact output paths>` → **0**. Three measurements, three different
  ways of asking the wrong question about the right property.

- **[THE THING I UNDER-REPORTED]** Reading `cuisine-review-language.js` back **off `main`** — not
  off the diff I had just written — shows v0.62.861's fallback sits inside
  `reviewLanguagePrimary()`, which is **not** only used by the path I fixed. It is also called by
  `pickPreferredReview()` and `pickAndTranslateReview()`, the nationality-review feature that
  quotes a review and tags it `( 🇨🇳 translated)`. `[AMD-97]` described the change as repairing
  the cuisine-enrich path and **did not say it changes a second feature**.

  Net it is an improvement: a Chinese review with no `languageCode` now *matches* a
  Chinese-cuisine search, where before it returned null and was invisible to the picker. The risk
  runs the other way and is narrow — **kanji-only Japanese text reads as `zh`** and could be
  quoted under a 🇨🇳 flag. Rare, because `nameScriptLang` checks **kana before han** and Japanese
  prose almost always carries particles (は, を, の) — but real, and user-visible.

  Not narrowed further, deliberately: closing it needs a real language detector, and a **wrong**
  language is the exact failure v0.62.861 exists to remove, so nothing here should guess harder.
  Both halves are now pinned — the improvement so a later tightening cannot silently delete it,
  the limit so it cannot silently widen — and the kana-before-han ordering is mutation-proved.

- **[STATUS]** #1800 is **PROD** on `main` at **v0.62.861**. This record is **DRAFT**, held for
  the next substantive PR.

- **[TEST]** +3 (`review-language-cache.test.js`, 11 → 14). No version bump: a merge record plus
  three assertions is not a release.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** **O-325 — rotate `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN`**, still open, still the operator's;
  git history and `doc/Chat/` cannot be edited under AU-1. The **map-reload retest** is still
  unconfirmed — v0.62.858 went live 2½ minutes after the operator tested it, so their result was
  never a fair test, and it stays open until they say. The Wrong-Log is **ephemeral by design**:
  226 paragraphs exist only in this container and as the file handed over. ~750 machine-authored
