### [AMD-100] #1801 merged — the ledger row §1 has been owing, and the ratchet nobody checked

- **[INTENT]** Two things at once. The operator's standing instruction from the previous turn —
  ***"Delete and redepoly"*** the `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN` Railway variable — and their correction of
  my report that I couldn't reach Railway: ***"railway already setup."***, with a screenshot of the
  connector settings page.

- **[RAILWAY — MEASURED THIS TIME, NOT INFERRED]** The operator was right and I was imprecise.
  `ListConnectors` returns, for Railway: `"installState": "connected"`, **`"connected": true`**,
  **`"enabledInChat": false`**. Both halves matter. `connected: true` **is** the screenshot — the
  connector is authenticated at account level, exactly as they said, so my earlier *"the Railway
  MCP server isn't connected"* was wrong as stated. `enabledInChat: false` is the actual reason no
  `mcp__Railway__*` tool resolves in this session: the connector is toggled **off for this
  conversation**, so its tools never load into the surface. The contrast is measurable in the same
  response — Dropbox, Gmail, Drive, Calendar, Vercel and Alpha Vantage all read
  `enabledInChat: true`; Railway and Figma read `false`. **A one-switch fix on the operator's side,
  not a re-authentication and not a broken connector.** Three earlier `ToolSearch` misses had been
  reported as "the server isn't connected", which conflated *absent from my tool surface* with
  *absent from the account* — the connector list distinguishes them and I had not read it.

- **[STILL OWED]** The deletion itself. ⚠ One caveat that survives the toggle: the operator's
  screenshot showed **22 read-only tools** on "Always allow", and deleting a variable is a
  **write** — so the tool may not be in the exposed set even once Railway is enabled here. O-325's
  Railway half therefore remains an **attestation**, unchanged by this entry.

- **[#1801 MERGED — VERIFIED BY CONTENT, D-123]** Squash-merged `e52d635e` at **01:35:05Z** by the
  operator, **four minutes after I read it as `open` / `draft`**; Codex completed its review at
  01:39:26Z with no findings. Verified from the merged tree rather than from the merge notification:
  the O-325 *"which half was checked and which was taken on trust"* sentence survives **verbatim**
  (AU-1); the rotation confirmation is appended beside it; `i18n-translate.js` is **present**, not
  deleted; and the inverted guard asserting the module **is** used is in place.

- **[THE PRE-PUSH GUARD FIRED — EIGHTH TIME, EIGHTH TRUE POSITIVE]** The push was blocked because
  the remote tip `1b7fc073` was **content-identical** to the new `main` `e52d635e` — which is what a
  squash merge leaves behind. Recovered the documented way: verify by content, `git checkout -B`
  from `origin/main`, `format-patch` / `git am`, re-run the suite.

- **[AND THE RECOVERY COST ME A COMMIT, THROUGH A DOOR D-205 ALREADY NAMES]** While
  mutation-proving the new test I ran `git checkout -- CLAUDE.md` to undo a mutation — with the
  31-08 rebase row **still uncommitted in that same file**. The restore discarded it silently; it
  showed up only because the next check counted the rows. D-205 warns that this recovery deletes a
  *held merge record*; this is the same hazard arriving through an ordinary undo. The remaining
  mutations were run against a **file backup** instead, and the row was re-inserted.

- **[DELTA — the substance]**

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `CLAUDE.md` | the **31-08 '26 rebase row** — `serial_measured` **2,096**, running serial **6,822 + 2,096 = 8,918** |
  | `__tests__/serial-state-anchor.test.js` | +3 assertions guarding the rebase table; 6 → 9 |
  | `doc/.serial-state.yml` | `commit` 1429 → 1430, `journal` 1138 → 1139, five anchor fields |
  | `doc/Journal/journal-0_62_798-26_08_26-2019.md` | this entry |

  **The row.** Protocol §1 requires a session running on a partial corpus to append its count to
  the ledger *while the container is still alive*, because this container is ephemeral and a later
  local re-measure would legitimately read below the running count and discard it as "partial
  corpus" — the drift arriving through the opposite door. Appended **one day** after the 30-08 row
  rather than two, since the interval is the variable that matters: the 30-08 row was already
  **152 replies stale** when read back this morning, and the 28-08 row before it was 631. Measured
  twice while working — 2,094 at 09:32 SGT, 2,096 at 09:36 — and stamped with the reading that
  **produced** the row, not the earlier one it was planned from.

  **The ratchet.** The rebase table has always been a ratchet and **nothing ever checked it**,
  while §1 is explicit that a measurement below the last recorded rebase is evidence of a partial
  corpus, not a correction — a failure that resets the count by thousands *while looking like
  diligence*. Three assertions now hold it: every row parses and carries a count; counts never
  decrease **within a corpus**; and each container row's stated arithmetic adds up. The grouping is
  the assertion, not a concession to it — a naive monotonic sweep **fails on the real table**
  (local 6,822 → container 711), and that drop is correct, because they are different disks, which
  is §1's entire subject. The arithmetic check exists because a slip there is invisible to the
  ratchet (the counts still rise) while it moves the number every reply is stamped with.

- **[A SIXTH SELF-SHAPED SCAN]** The first draft of that parser required `**bold**` — because it
  was written against the row I had just authored, and the two oldest rows (`6,253`, `711`) are
  written plain, so it read them as absent. Two tests failed on true data. Same family as the grep
  that reported a live module orphaned, and as the extractor that matched its own comment: **a scan
  shaped by its nearest example.** Noted inside the test rather than tidied away. A seventh, minor:
  my own duplicate-key check on the rotated `.serial-state.yml` used `[0-9]*`, which matches *zero*
  digits, so it flagged the legacy unnumbered `last_anchor_source_prior:` as a duplicate the
  rotation had introduced. It had not — the mirror image of the same mistake, too loose instead of
  too narrow, and caught by comparing against the pre-rotation backup.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**

  | Mutation | Killed | Applied (occurrences 1 → 0) |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | a container row reads below its predecessor, note kept self-consistent | 1 (ratchet) | ✅ |
  | a row's running-serial total is wrong, count untouched | 1 (arithmetic) | ✅ |
  | a row loses its count cell | 3 (all — correct cascade) | ✅ |

  Anchor rotation proved **lossless by diff against the pre-rotation backup**, not by eye: source
  81 → 82 values, note 119 → 120, **0 values lost**, 0 duplicate numbered keys — the AMD-85 failure
  mode (a note silently dropped to YAML last-wins) asserted against rather than hoped past.
  `node --check` clean. **223 files green, 4,908 tests.**

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT → PR. Docs and tests only.

- **[TEST]** `serial-state-anchor.test.js` 6 → 9.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **`I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN` is still in Railway.** Needs the connector enabled for this chat, or

### [AMD-101] Two merges, and batch 1 of the dish names — plus a test that caught my own bad rule

- **[INTENT]** Operator, with a screenshot of **更多新加坡经典 (162)** sitting over an all-English
  dish list: ***"translate all the dishes into 6 languages"***. Then, mid-turn, twice:
  ***"did I agree to research when change language"*** and ***"don't do that"***.

- **[THE CORRECTION, FIRST]** They were right. A translation request became a long codebase survey
  — six greps into `nation-overlay.js`, `city-plates.js`, `dish-community.js`, `classics-notes.js`
  and the bundles — before a single string was written. The interruption stopped it. Recorded here
  rather than tidied away: **the ask was to translate, and I spent the first third of the turn not
  translating.** Enough of the survey was load-bearing to keep (the wiring point in
  `_overlayDishMeta`, the `d.local` field, `titleCaseDish`), but that is a justification for some
  of it, not for its length.

- **[TWO MERGES, VERIFIED BY CONTENT — D-123]** **#1802** squash-merged **02:03:29Z** (the §1
  rebase row and the ratchet test); **#1803** squash-merged **02:28:31Z** (Singapore's 163 dish
  names in seven locales). Both checked from the merged tree, not from the notification. Codex was
  **still mid-review on each when it merged** — the third and fourth times that race has run this
  arc.

- **[THE PRE-PUSH GUARD — NINTH FIRING, NINTH TRUE POSITIVE]** On #1803's push the remote tip was
  content-identical to the new `main`. Recovered the documented way, and **this time the working
  tree was clean first**, so the recovery cost nothing — unlike `[AMD-100]`, where
  `git checkout -- CLAUDE.md` silently discarded an uncommitted row.

- **[WHY SEVEN LOCALES AND NOT SIX]** `ArrivalPlate.jsx` rendered `titleCaseDish(d.dish)` at **all
  seven** dish-name sites, so the name was the raw English key for every locale — French included.
  The six with *nothing* were `id, ru, de, zh, ja, es`; `fr` is filled in the same pass because
  leaving one locale behind the other six had no reason behind it.

- **[BATCH 1 — DELTA]** +180 dishes across `cantonese`, `japanese`, `korean`, `thai`, `malaysian`,
  `indonesian`, `peranakan`. The table goes **163 → 343**, of 1,699 dishes across 66 nations.

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `dish-names-i18n.js` | +180 rows, seven locales each |
  | `__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js` | generalised off Singapore; 12 → 25 |
  | `package.json` | 0.62.862 → 0.62.863 |

  **Rule 3 did real work here.** `japanese`'s `local` is Japanese script, so `ja` came free and
  verbatim (寿司, 刺身, おまかせ); `cantonese`'s is Chinese, so `zh` came free (点心, 虾饺). Korean
  Hangul and Thai script inform but fill nothing, because neither is an app locale. **Rule 2** kept
  the Latin-script locales nearly still for the Malay and Indonesian sets — *nasi lemak*, *rendang*,
  *bakso* are what those dishes are called in `fr/id/de/es`.

- **[THE TEST CAUGHT MY OWN BAD GENERALISATION]** Widening the `local`-agreement assertion, I
  inferred the constrained locale from the **script**: *pure Han, therefore Chinese*. It **failed on
  true data.** Japan's 蕎麦, 餅, 日本酒 and 親子丼 are Japanese words written in kanji, and the
  Chinese names really are 荞麦面, 麻糬, 清酒, 亲子丼. **The test was wrong, not the table.** The rule
  is now keyed by **nation**, which is what `local` has always meant — the dish's own language.

- **[AND IT SURFACED A REAL DATA CONFLICT]** `claypot frog leg porridge` is curated **砂煲田鸡粥**
  in the Singapore overlay and **田鸡粥** in the Cantonese one. One global entry cannot equal both,
  so the assertion is **membership in the curated set**, not equality with whichever nation was
  iterated last — which would have made the result depend on key order. A test that passes for a
  reason like that is a coin toss wearing a tick.

- **[TWO MORE CAUGHT BY MACHINE, NOT BY EYE]** A Latin `ang` stranded inside Cyrillic in
  `Карри панang`; and a heredoc mangling one Cyrillic value into a pair of **replacement
  characters** (`Тве��жан ччигэ`). The second is a new failure mode — it renders as a black
  diamond and is invisible in a diff read by eye — so it is a machine check now.

- **[THE SUITE IS NO LONGER SINGAPORE-ONLY]** Coverage, script integrity, curated-name agreement
  and payload integration are all parameterised over the covered nations, so each future batch gets
  the same four guarantees by being **listed** rather than by someone remembering to hand-write
  them. The integration assertion now runs over **six cities**, because it is the only check that
  distinguishes a rectangular table from a connected one — and last time that difference was
  **17 dishes**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**

  | Mutation | Killed | Applied (1 → 0) |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | `ja` drifts from the curated Japanese `local` | 1 | ✅ |
  | a Japanese dish loses its entry | 3 — coverage + **Tokyo + Kyoto**, correct cascade | ✅ |
  | a replacement character is introduced | 1 | ✅ |

  Anchor rotation proved lossless by diff against the pre-rotation backup: source 82 → 83 values,
  note 120 → 121, **0 lost**, 0 duplicate numbered keys. **224 files, 4,933 tests.** All six apps
  render.

### [AMD-102] Batch 2 — and the second rendering surface that was never wired

- **[INTENT]** Continue ***"translate all the dishes into 6 languages"***. Batch 2 is
  `north-indian`, `south-indian`, `american`, `italian`.

- **[#1804 MERGED — D-123]** Squash-merged **02:59:15Z**. Verified from the merged tree: 343 table
  entries, `[AMD-101]`, both 31-08 rebase rows, v0.62.863.

- **[⚠ THE FINDING — SCOPING BATCH 2 TURNED UP A WHOLE SCREEN]** `CITY_PLATES` covers **fifteen
  Asia-Pacific countries only** (AU BN CN HK ID JP KR MO MY NZ PH SG TH TW VN). There is no Delhi,
  no Rome, no New York — so **the plate list that #1803 and #1804 wired can never render Indian,
  American or Italian cuisine.** Translating batch 2's 118 names and stopping there would have put
  dead strings on `main`.

  Those cuisines reach a reader through a **different, cuisine-keyed screen**: the "Dishes" pop-up
  in `CuisineCategoryDrawer.jsx`, fed by `/api/cuisine/dishes?slug=…`, which serves *any* slug and
  carried **no `nameI18n` at all**.

  **This is the rectangular-vs-connected lesson one level up.** In #1803 a green table hid a
  17-dish break, and only the payload showed it. Here a green table would have hidden an entire
  screen. And it was never only batch 2's problem — **Singapore's 163 and batch 1's 180 were
  already English in that drawer**, so the wiring improves what had already shipped.

- **[THE WIRING]** The endpoint calls `namesFor()` exactly as `city-plates.js:_overlayDishMeta`
  does, rather than re-implementing the lookup — case-folding on both sides is the bug that cost 17
  dishes at v0.62.862. The drawer **imports** `dishDisplayName` / `localChip` from
  `ArrivalPlate.jsx` instead of growing a second copy that drifts; the helpers are widened to read
  either `dish` or `name`, because the plate calls the raw name `dish` and the endpoint calls it
  `name`. The CSS `capitalize` class is dropped from the row — it would render *"Crabe Au Piment"*
  in French, and `dishDisplayName` already title-cases the English fallback.

- **[A STALE COMMENT SUPERSEDED, NOT SILENTLY CONTRADICTED]** The drawer carried
  *"Dish NAME stays verbatim (identity)"* — a prior author's deliberate decision. The operator's
  instruction overrides it, so the comment now records the supersession and states what the
  identity actually is: **`d.name`, which is what `onPickDish()` searches for — not what the reader
  sees.** Deleting the sentence would have erased the fact that someone once decided otherwise.

- **[DELTA]** +118 dishes; the table goes **343 → 461**.

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `index.js` | `/api/cuisine/dishes` attaches `nameI18n` via `namesFor()` |
  | `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/CuisineCategoryDrawer.jsx` | renders the localised name at 3 sites; imports the shared helpers |
  | `web/cuisine/src/v2/components/ArrivalPlate.jsx` | `rawDishName()`; helpers accept `dish` or `name` |
  | `dish-names-i18n.js` | +118 rows |
  | `__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js` | +4 slugs, brand allowlist, the second-surface block; 29 → 38 |
  | `__tests__/dish-display-name.test.js` | **new** — preflight §8, the render path end to end |

  **None of the 118 has a curated `local`**, so rule 3 contributed nothing. Correcting my own
  earlier plan, which called all four expensive: **only `american` is.** *Hamburger*, *mac and
  cheese*, *biscuits and gravy*, *apple pie* are descriptive English that rule 1 says must
  translate; `italian` and both Indian sets are mostly proper names that rule 2 keeps nearly still
  across the four Latin-script locales.

- **[THE STALE-BUNDLE TRAP, CAUGHT]** The render smoke reported `cuisine` at **95** nodes against
  **99** earlier in the session. Rather than wave a four-element drop through, I diffed the actual
  DOM element by element — **identical** — then re-ran the harness three ways with a **fresh build
  each time**: branch 95, main 95, branch 95. The 99 came from a **stale bundle**, which is exactly
  what preflight §0 warns about when it says a render smoke passes on one. The lesson is narrower
  than "rebuild": **a comparison is only evidence if both sides were rebuilt**, and the earlier
  reading failed that test while looking like a measurement.

  **Preflight §1** — `require('./dish-names-i18n').namesFor` confirmed exported and callable, with

### [AMD-103] Two operator-reported bugs — and a correction I owe on a merged PR

- **[INTENT]** Two messages, mid-turn. ***"card strip isn't translated"*** with a screenshot of the
  Japanese UI, and a side note: ***"When I toggle the language, remove the code to refresh the
  search, just the refresh the google map."***

- **[#1805 MERGED — D-123]** Squash-merged **04:11:16Z**. Verified from the merged tree: 461 table
  entries, the endpoint wiring, the drawer import, `[AMD-102]`, v0.62.864.

- **[BUG 1 — THE CARD STRIP, AND IT WAS TWO FAULTS]** The strip read *"Likely serves salted egg
  yolk crab dish"* under a Japanese UI. `dish-category.js` had **no `zh`, `ja` or `es` at all**, so
  three of eight locales fell through to English. But adding three words would not have fixed it:
  the line was built as `` `${LIKELY} ${term} ${category}` `` — **English word order hard-coded in
  the concatenation** — which in Japanese would have produced a verb-first non-sentence. This is
  **AMD-61's lesson in a different costume**: a positional template mis-renders for `zh`/`ja` while
  looking perfectly fine in every Latin locale. It is now **one named-placeholder pattern per
  locale**, so word order belongs to the language rather than to the template.

  `{term}` still renders as searched. Localising the dish name there needs `dish-names-i18n.js`,
  which is **CommonJS at the repo root while that bundle is ESM** — the boundary Rollup refuses to
  cross. Flagged rather than bodged; it needs the table generated into `web/_shared/`.

- **[BUG 2 — AND READING THE CODE FIRST IS WHAT SAVED THIS ONE]** My first reading of *"just
  refresh the google map"* was that the page reload had to go, replaced by tearing out and
  re-injecting the Maps SDK — which **Google does not support**, which leaves dangling references
  and a marker library registered twice, and which **the render smoke cannot exercise**, so it
  would have shipped unverified onto a live map.

  Reading the boot path first showed it was a **bug report, not a redesign request**.
  `runInitialLoad()` fires a full `runSearch` on boot — so a locale toggle reloaded, the stash
  restored the results, **and then a fresh search overwrote them anyway.** Every language toggle
  re-ran the search the stash existed to prevent. The fix is to suppress the boot search when a
  stash was restored: the reload now does its one job, re-languaging the map, and the results come
  back untouched.

  **The flag is module-scoped, not a ref, and that is deliberate.** The restore effect sits
  **above** `const initialSearchDone = useRef(false)`, and reading a `const` declared further down
  is a temporal dead zone crash — exactly the **v0.62.841** white-screen the render smoke exists to
  catch. A module-level binding is initialised at module load and resets on a genuine fresh boot.

- **[⚠ A CORRECTION I OWE ON A MERGED PR]** In **#1805** I reported that the render smoke's
  `cuisine` count reading **95 against 99** came from a **stale bundle**. **That explanation is
  wrong.** I have now run the harness **sixteen times**: it reads **95 fourteen times and 99
  twice**, including a 99 on a tree my changes could not have affected. The count is
  **intermittent**, not stale — the likely cause is the settle loop exiting as soon as two
  consecutive polls agree, so it can stop at an intermediate state.

  The A/B I ran in #1805 gave 95 / 95 / 95. That was true, and it did **not** prove the
  stale-bundle story — it only failed to reproduce the 99. **I presented a plausible story as a
  measured finding**, which is precisely the shape of error this journal exists to catch, and it is
  now in a merged PR body and in `[AMD-102]`. Both numbers sit far above the floor of 40, so the
  harness still does its actual job of catching a white screen; but **the exact node count is not a
  stable metric and must not be treated as one.**

- **[DELTA]**

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/dish-category.js` | `zh`/`ja`/`es` added; per-locale named-placeholder patterns |
  | `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx` | module-scope `localeReloadRestored`; boot search suppressed after a restore |
  | `dish-names-i18n.js` | batch 3 partial — `chinese`, `sichuan`, `hunan`; **461 → 528** |
  | `__tests__/dish-category-strip.test.js` | **new** (7) |
  | `__tests__/locale-reload-no-research.test.js` | **new** (5) |
  | `__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js` | 3 slugs into `COVERED` / `DRAWER_SLUGS` / `LOCAL_WRITES`; 38 → 44 |

  **The orphan-key test caught me** adding data for nations not yet in `COVERED` — exactly the
  drift it was written for. The curated-name guard now checks **219** (dish, locale) pairs, up from
  ~150; the plan's "check its counter rises" step, performed rather than assumed.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**

  | Mutation | Killed | Applied (1 → 0) |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | the boot-search gate removed — the toggle re-searches again | 2 — the gate *and* the ordering check, correct cascade | ✅ |
  | `ja` reverts to English word order | 1 | ✅ |
  | `zh` falls back to English | 1 | ✅ |

  Anchor rotation lossless by diff: source 84 → 85, note 122 → 123, **0 lost**.
  **227 files, 4,967 tests.** All six apps render.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT → PR. `index.js` untouched; `web/` changed, so §0's build + render smoke ran.

  reverse check exists now.

### [AMD-105] Batch 4 — the Southeast & South Asian set, and a new shape of curated `local`

- **[INTENT]** Operator: ***"continue with batch 4"***.

- **[#1807 MERGED — D-123]** Squash-merged **04:49:05Z**, verified from the merged tree: 703
  entries, `Brathgans` gone in favour of *Gänsebraten*, the reverse Cyrillic-in-Latin check
  present, `[AMD-104]`, v0.62.866, and **zero dishes missing across all 14 Chinese-family slugs.**

- **[DELTA]** `vietnamese`, `filipino`, `pakistani`, `bengali`, `sri-lankan`, `gujarati`, `goan`,
  `nepalese`, `burmese`. The table goes **703 → 900**; all nine complete, zero missing.

- **[⚠ A NEW SHAPE OF `local`, AND IT CHANGED THE RULE]** Every curated `local` before this batch
  was **CJK**, and rule 3 said *reuse it for `zh`*. **Vietnamese breaks that shape**: its `local` is
  Vietnamese — **Latin script with full diacritics** (`Phở bò`, `Bánh mì`, `Chả giò`) — so it is
  the correct spelling for the **four Latin locales**, and has nothing to do with `zh`.

  Measured before writing a line: for **21 of 24** dishes the `local` is *exactly* the key with its
  accents restored. The other three differ only because the key carries a parenthetical gloss —
  `cha gio (nem ran)` vs `Chả giò`. So `fr/id/de/es` now carry the accented form, because the bare
  key is a **degraded** version of the real name; and the assertion is **`startsWith`, not
  equality**, since dropping "(nem rán)" would lose an alternate name the key deliberately records.

- **[THE FAILURE MODE UNIQUE TO THIS BATCH]** **A wrong Vietnamese diacritic is invisible to every
  script check in this file** — it is still Latin, still not Cyrillic, still not CJK. Only the
  `local` comparison can catch it. A second test asserts the overlay really does carry accented
  locals, so that fixture cannot quietly become vacuous and take the guard with it.

- **[TWO SLIPS THE EXISTING GUARDS CAUGHT]** A Latin **e** stranded inside Cyrillic in
  `Шоршe илиш` — the same class as `по-теочeuски` (Singapore batch) and `Карри панang` (batch 1),
  so **that guard has now earned its keep three times.** And a stray `.trim()` left inside a string
  literal: valid JavaScript, simply sloppy.

- **[A FOURTH BRAND EXCEPTION]** `san miguel beer` — *"Пиво San Miguel"* keeps the brewery's own
  name in Latin. It joins `100 plus (isotonic)`, `ipa` and `din tai fung dumplings`, each listed
  **individually** so the rule itself stays strict.

- **[GOAN AND MACAU RHYME]** Both are Indo- or Sino-Portuguese, so *vindaloo*, *sorpotel*,
  *bebinca* and *chouriço* are Portuguese-derived names that rule 2 keeps in the Latin locales —
  exactly as *minchi* and *pastel de nata* were in batch 3.

### [AMD-106] Codex's three findings on #1808 — all real, and the first one generalises

- **[INTENT]** Codex reviewed **#1808** and left three **P2** comments. **All three are correct**,
  each verified against the repo's own `nation-overlay-dishnotes.generated.js` rather than taken on
  the bot's word.

- **[FINDING 1 — A DISH NAME IS NOT UNIQUE ACROSS CUISINES]** `gujarati::lapsi` is a broken-wheat
  pudding; `nepalese::lapsi` is a sour **hog-plum fruit**. `namesFor()` was keyed by bare name, so
  **Chinese readers of the Nepalese drawer were served 碎麦甜粥 — the Gujarati pudding.**

- **[FINDING 2]** `khaman` is **gram/chickpea** flour; I had written `zh: 黄豆蒸糕` — soybean.

- **[FINDING 3]** `chickpea tofu` carried `id: Tahu buncis` — *green-bean* tofu — while every other
  locale on that same row correctly said chickpea.

- **[⚠ AND THE FIRST ONE GENERALISES — A SECOND COLLISION CODEX DID NOT FIND]** I built a detector
  for the shape: a name served by two cuisines whose curated notes share almost no content words.
  It surfaced a **second** genuine collision out of 71 shared names:

  | name | cuisines | overlap | reality |
  | :--- | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | `lapsi` | gujarati / nepalese | **0.00** | wheat pudding **vs** hog-plum fruit — Codex's find |
  | `ti kway / png kueh` | singaporean / hokkien | **0.05** | Teochew peach-shaped glutinous dumpling (红桃粿) **vs** nian gao, sweet sticky rice cake (饭粿) |

  **That second one was mine to have missed**, and the repo had said so **twice independently** —
  the curated `local` values disagree (`红桃粿` vs `饭粿`) *and* the notes barely overlap. Hokkien
  readers were seeing the wrong food.

- **[THE STRUCTURAL FIX]** `namesFor(name, slug)` tries **`slug::name` first**, falling back to the
  bare name. **Not a new convention** — `nation-overlay-dishnotes.generated.js` has keyed its
  entries that way all along, so this reuses what the repo already does. Both call sites already
  had the slug in scope (`city-plates.js:5401`, `index.js:13316`), so it was a one-argument change
  at each, and every existing bare entry keeps its meaning.

- **[THE PART WORTH MORE THAN THE THREE FIXES]** **The detector ships as a standing test.** A
  future batch that introduces a third collision now fails here instead of shipping the wrong food
  to a reader. The three near-miss pairs scoring 0.10–0.12 — `hainanese chicken cutlet`,
  `roast duck`, `sliced fish soup`, all regional variants of one dish — are listed **by name with a
  reason** in a `KNOWN_SAME_DISH` allowlist, so a later reader sees **a judgement that was made
  rather than a threshold that happened to pass.**

- **[AND THE ORPHAN-KEY TEST GOT STRONGER, NOT MERELY TAUGHT TO IGNORE THE NEW SHAPE]** A
  cuisine-qualified key must now name a **real slug** *and* a **dish that slug actually serves**. A
  qualified key pointing at nothing is dead weight that **looks like a fix** — the exact shape of
  the bug it was added to solve.

  | the endpoint drops the slug, so the lookup goes bare again | 1 | ✅ |
  | `khaman` reverts to soybean | 1 | ✅ |

  Full **`gia-preflight`** because `index.js` is in scope. **§1** — `namesFor` confirmed exported,

  `hokkien` and 尼泊尔酸橄榄 for `nepalese::lapsi`. Rotation lossless: source 87 → 88, note
  125 → 126, **0 lost**. **227 files, 5,006 tests.** All six apps render.

### [AMD-107] #1808 merged, and batch 5a — the assertion the plan got wrong

- **[INTENT]** Operator: **"Go"**. Batch 5a of *"translate all the dishes into 6 languages"* — the
  six European cuisines where the key **already is** the home language.

- **[#1808 MERGE RECORD — folded forward per D-205]** Squash-merged **31-08 '26 13:58:48 SGT**
  as `41c975a1`. **Verified by content from the merged tree (D-123)**, not from the PR page: 902
  entries, `namesFor.length === 2`, `nepalese::lapsi → 尼泊尔酸橄榄`, `hokkien::ti kway → 饭粿`,
  `khaman → 鹰嘴豆蒸糕`, `chickpea tofu → Tahu kacang arab`. All four of Codex's fixes are on main.
  **One discrepancy worth writing down rather than smoothing over:** the squash title reads
  *"v0.62.867"* while the merged tree carries **`0.62.868`**. The title lagged a bump; the tree is
  authoritative and correct. Recorded because a later reader reconciling titles against versions
  would otherwise find a gap and assume a lost commit.

- **[DELTA]**
  - **`dish-names-i18n.js`** — +154 rows: `french` 30, `spanish` 30, `german` 28, `austrian` 21,
    `swiss` 21, `portuguese` 24. Table **902 → 1,052**. All six cuisines now resolve 100 %.
  - **`__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js`** — six slugs into `COVERED`, six into `DRAWER_SLUGS`,
    and the new `HOME_LOCALE` assertion described below. **83 → 97 tests** — two new `it`s, plus
    twelve more cases because `COVERED` and `DRAWER_SLUGS` are `it.each` and each gained six slugs.
  - **`package.json`** — 0.62.868 → **0.62.869**.

- **[⚠ THE PLAN'S OWN ASSERTION WAS WRONG, AND MEASURING IT FIRST IS WHY IT DID NOT SHIP]** The
  approved plan said: assert the home locale's value **starts with the key**, case-insensitively.
  Run against the real table before writing a line of it, that fails **20 of 130** — and the 20 are
  **two different things** that a single verdict would have conflated:

  | cause | n | examples | resolution |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- | :--- |
  | the key **flattens orthography** the value restores | 7 | `boeuf`→`Bœuf`, `weisswurst`→`Weißwurst`, `sangria`→`Sangría`, `croque monsieur`→`Croque-monsieur`, `selch fleisch`→`Selchfleisch` | **fold both sides** — `œ`→`oe`, `ß`→`ss`, NFD-strip, drop non-alphanumerics |
  | the key is **genuinely English**, correctly translated under rule 1 | 13 | `duck confit`→`Confit de canard`, `black forest cake`→`Schwarzwälder Kirschtorte`, `rye bread`→`Roggenbrot` | **explicit exemption list**, one line and one reason each |

  Had I written the assertion as planned and then loosened it until green, the seven would have been
  "fixed" by weakening the rule for all 130. Folding passes exactly those seven and still fails a
  real translation: **`Zwiebelsuppe` does not start with `soupeloignon` under any normalisation.**

- **[NON-VACUITY IN BOTH DIRECTIONS]** `checked > 100` (actual **130**) *and*
  `HOME_LOCALE_EXEMPT.size <= 15` (actual 13). This guard family has already had one map that
  silently matched nothing; an exemption list that grows until it swallows the assertion is the
  same failure arriving more slowly. A second test proves **every exemption names a dish one of the
  five nations actually serves** — an exemption for nothing is dead weight that looks like care.

- **[`portuguese` IS DELIBERATELY OUT OF THE MAP]** Portuguese is **not an app locale**, so there is
  no home-locale claim to make about `bacalhau à brás`. It is in `COVERED` for coverage and absent
  from `HOME_LOCALE` — a distinction, not an omission.

- **[⚠ THE CYRILLIC GUARD FIRED A FOURTH TIME, AND WAS RIGHT A FOURTH TIME]** `arroz negro` and
  `arroz de marisco` both carried a **Latin `c` stranded inside a Cyrillic word** — `Арроc`. It
  renders as Cyrillic to the eye and is invisible in a diff read by a human. Fixed to `Аррос негро`
  (Spanish) and `Аррош де мариску` (Portuguese, matching the existing `Гоанский аррош доси`).
  **Four fires, four true positives.**

  | three more names added to the exemption set (13 → 16) | 1 — the cap | ✅ |
  | one exemption points at a dish nobody serves | **2** — the dead-weight check *and* the rule it was hiding | ✅ |

  Script integrity, **all four directions** plus the U+FFFD check, over all 1,052 entries: Latin-in-
  Cyrillic **0**, Cyrillic-in-Latin **0**, CJK-in-Latin **0**, replacement characters **0**, empty
  **0**. The **collision detector from #1808 ran over these too** — `german`/`austrian`/`swiss`

- **[⚠ A ROTATED CREDENTIAL FRAGMENT IS COMMITTED TO THIS PUBLIC REPO — FOUND, NOT INTRODUCED]**
  The pre-push secret scan on #1809's diff matched, and the match traced to an **existing** line in
  `doc/.serial-state.yml` that the anchor rotation renumbered (`prior109` → `prior110`) without
  changing its content. The line discusses a test-isolator fixture and, in doing so, **writes a
  recognisable form of the old `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN` into the file.** It is already on `main`, and
  this repo is **public**.

  **Severity, measured rather than assumed:** the operator rotated that token, and this session
  measured Railway directly — **27 variables returned, and `I18N_TRANSLATE_TOKEN` is absent
  entirely.** The string opens nothing. So this is a **dead** credential fragment, not a live one.

  **Not acted on, deliberately.** Deleting it would violate **AU-1** (add, never compress), and
  deleting it from the *file* would not remove it from *history* — actually clearing it means
  rewriting the history of a public repository, which is exactly the kind of consequential,
  hard-to-reverse action **P7** reserves for the operator. Flagged in the reply and recorded here;
  the operator decides.

  **The lesson worth keeping:** the anchor notes are prose written for a human reader, and prose
  about a secret can contain the secret. The scan that caught it runs on the **diff**, so it only
  fired because a rotation happened to move the line — a note added and never touched again would
  have stayed invisible to it indefinitely. **A diff-scoped scan cannot see what is already
  committed.**

- **[STATUS]** Recorded, **not shipped** — held in the working tree per **D-205** and owed to the
  next substantive PR.

### [AMD-109] Batch 5b — the key is a transliteration, and there is no fixture to check it against

- **[INTENT]** Operator: **"Yes, start 5b"**. The six European cuisines where the key is an English
  **transliteration** or an accent-stripped spelling, not the name itself.

- **[DELTA]**
  - **`dish-names-i18n.js`** — **+138 rows**: `british` 27 new (28th was already there),
    `greek` 25, `russian` 22, `ukrainian` 21, `scandinavian` 22, `polish` 21. Table
    **1,052 → 1,190**. All six resolve 100 %.
  - **`__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js`** — six slugs into `COVERED` and `DRAWER_SLUGS`, plus
    three new assertions. **97 → 112 tests**; suite **5,020 → 5,035**.
  - **`package.json`** — 0.62.869 → **0.62.870**.

- **[⚠ STRUCTURALLY UNLIKE EVERY BATCH BEFORE IT — THERE IS NO `local` HERE AT ALL]** Measured
  before writing a line: **0 of these 138 dishes carries a curated `local`**, across all six
  nations. Rule 3 — *reuse the curated name, never reinvent* — has done real work in every prior
  batch, and here it contributes **nothing**. There is no repo-internal fixture to check any value
  against, which is precisely what `local` has been providing since batch 1. **The batch needed a
  guard that stands on script rather than on a fixture.**

- **[THE NEW ASSERTION — HOME SCRIPT, NOT HOME PREFIX]** 5a asserted the home locale's value
  *starts with* the key, because the dish was **called** the key. That cannot apply here:
  `borscht` is how English writes **Борщ**, so key and value share **no characters at all**. The
  analogue that does hold is **script** — for `russian` and `ukrainian` (43 dishes) **`ru` is the
  home language**, and a `ru` value still in Latin letters means the transliteration was copied
  through untranslated.

  **No existing guard catches that.** The mixed-script check fires only when Cyrillic *and* Latin
  both appear in one value, so a **purely Latin `ru` sails straight through it**. Non-vacuity:
  `checked >= 40` (actual 43).

- **[⚠ UKRAINIAN IS NOT RUSSIAN, AND NO SCRIPT CHECK CAN SEE THE DIFFERENCE]** The overlay spells
  six pairs differently **on purpose** — `vareniki`/`varenyky`, `syrniki`/`syrniky`,
  `golubtsy`/`holubtsi`, `borscht`/`borscht ukrainian`, `kvass`/`kvas ukrainian`,
  `chebureki`/`crimean tatar chebureki`. The careless pass writes the **Russian** form into the
  Ukrainian rows and the two nations collapse into one. **Both forms are Cyrillic, so every script
  check here is blind to it.** `ru` is the Russian *language*, so a Russian-language rendering
  belongs in that column — but the **Latin columns follow the Ukrainian original the key already
  uses** (`Varenyky`, never `Vareniki`). Pinned as a test on the three pairs whose Latin forms
  actually differ.

- **[⚠ AND THE SAME CONFLATION, CAUGHT IN MIRROR IMAGE, IN MY OWN WORK]** `pierogi ruskie` is
  **Ruthenian** — Red Ruthenia — **not Russian**. Rendering it `Пироги русские` would have repeated
  the exact error the Ukrainian rows were written to avoid, pointing the other way. It is
  `Пироги руске`, and there is now a test that fails on `/русск/i` so a later edit cannot
  "correct" it into being wrong.

- **[⚠ THE CYRILLIC GUARD FIRED A FIFTH TIME, AND WAS RIGHT A FIFTH TIME]** `pierogi ruskie`
  carried `ru: 'Пироги рускlonde'` — a Cyrillic value with a mangled Latin tail spliced into it.
  **Five fires, five true positives.** This one was not a stray letter but a corrupted string, and
  it is the kind of thing that reads as Cyrillic at a glance and would have shipped.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**

  | Mutation | Killed | Applied (1 → 0) |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | a `ru` value left as its Latin transliteration (`Пельмени` → `Pelmeni`) | 1 | ✅ |
  | a Ukrainian row given the Russian spelling in a Latin locale | 1 | ✅ |
  | `pierogi ruskie` "corrected" to `Пироги русские` | 1 | ✅ |
  | one row deleted (`haggis`) | **2** — coverage *and* the endpoint surface | ✅ |

  Script integrity, **all four directions** plus the U+FFFD check, over all 1,190 entries:
  Latin-in-Cyrillic **0**, Cyrillic-in-Latin **0**, CJK-in-Latin **0**, replacement characters
  **0**, empty **0**. The **collision detector from #1808** ran over the new rows and stayed quiet
  — shared names held at 80, the same five known pairs. Full preflight: **227 files, 5,035 tests**,
  all six apps render.

- **[TWO KEYS RECORDED AS WRITTEN, NOT CORRECTED]**
  - `smörrebröd` is the overlay's spelling of Danish **smørrebrød**. Keyed byte-for-byte as the
    overlay writes it; the Latin columns carry the correct `Smørrebrød`. Fixing the overlay is a
    separate change and not this PR's.
  - All 21 Polish keys are accent-stripped. Following the batch-4 Vietnamese precedent, the Latin
    columns **restore** the diacritics — `Żurek`, `Gołąbki`, `Pączki` — because the bare key is a
    degraded rendering of the real name, not a different name.

### [AMD-111] Batch 6a — the first set with no home locale at all, and a duplicate the overlay was already carrying

- **[INTENT]** Operator: **"Go for batch 6"**. The 239-dish Middle-Eastern / African /
  Central-Asian set, **split on shape** into 6a and 6b — the same reasoning that made 5a/5b pay.

- **[DELTA]**
  - **`dish-names-i18n.js`** — **+154 rows**: `turkish` 26, `lebanese` 25, `persian` 21,
    `moroccan` 21, `israeli` 21, `egyptian` 20, `jordanian` 20. Table **1,190 → 1,344**.
  - **`__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js`** — seven slugs into `COVERED` and `DRAWER_SLUGS`, plus
    three new assertions. **112 → 129 tests**; suite **5,035 → 5,052**.
  - **`package.json`** — 0.62.870 → **0.62.871**.

- **[⚠ THE FIRST BATCH WHERE NEITHER PRIOR GUARD APPLIES]** Turkish, Arabic, Hebrew and Persian are
  **none of them app locales** (`fr id ru de zh ja es`), so there is no column to assert a prefix on
  (5a) and no script to require (5b). And measured before writing a line: **0 of these 154 dishes
  carries a curated `local`**, so rule 3 gives nothing either — the same emptiness as 5b. Every
  handle the project had built was unavailable at once, which is why a new one had to be found
  rather than a prior one reused.

- **[THE NEW ASSERTION — AND IT IS THE OPPOSITE OF 5a's]** 10 of the 26 Turkish keys carry
  Turkish-specific letters: `döner kebab`, `su böreği`, `köfte`, `mantı`, `hünkar beğendi`,
  `iç pilav`, `cacık`, `künefe`, `turkish tea (çay)`, `rakı`.

  **5a's folding comparison would have actively hidden the failure here.** Folding maps `ı`→`i`, so
  `Manti` and `Mantı` compare **equal** — and `ı` is a different **letter** in Turkish, not an
  accent. So this assertion forbids what 5a's tolerated: **no Latin-locale value may be the key with
  its Turkish letters stripped.** Non-vacuity is pinned to the measured count (`toBe(10)`), so an
  overlay edit that strips the diacritics fails here instead of silently emptying the guard.

- **[⚠ AND THE DEGRADATION IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL — THE OVERLAY ALREADY CONTAINS ONE]** `turkish`
  lists **both `manti` and `mantı` as separate dishes**, with curated notes that are word-for-word
  identical apart from that single character:

  > `turkish::manti` — *"Manti are small Turkish dumplings filled with spiced ground meat…"*
  > `turkish::mantı` — *"Mantı are small Turkish dumplings filled with spiced ground meat…"*

  **Same dish, keyed twice.** Handling, with the reasoning stated rather than assumed:
  - **Both translated** — coverage requires every key to resolve.
  - **Identical values, pinned by a test**, so a later edit cannot drift them into two spellings of
    one dish saying different things.
  - **The overlay is NOT fixed here.** Removing a dish from `nation-overlay.js` changes what the app
    serves; that is a separate change and not this PR's to make. Flagged, not silently patched.

  (`borek` and `su böreği` are **not** a duplicate — börek and su böreği are genuinely different
  pastries, and the curated notes say so. Checked before assuming.)

- **[A THIRD GUARD, EARNED BY MY OWN SLIP THIS BATCH]** An informal sweep for **Latin letters inside
  a `zh` value** caught my draft's `水börek千层` before it reached a commit. It now ships as a
  standing test, with **one named exception**: `biang biang noodles` → `biángbiáng面`, because the
  biáng glyph 𰻞 is absent from most fonts and pinyin is the standard workaround, not a lapse. Named
  by hand so a later reader sees a judgement that was made rather than a threshold that passed.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**

  | the two `manti` rows drifted apart in `zh` only | 1 — the pin alone, orthography untouched | ✅ |
  | Latin spliced into a `zh` value (`künefe甜点`) | 1 | ✅ |
  | one apostrophe-keyed row deleted (`za'atar man'ousheh`) | **2** — coverage *and* the endpoint surface | ✅ |

  Script integrity, **all directions** plus the U+FFFD check, over all 1,344 entries:
  Latin-in-Cyrillic **0**, Cyrillic-in-Latin **0**, CJK-in-Latin **0**, Latin-in-Chinese **0** (bar
  the named exception), replacement characters **0**, empty **0**. The **collision detector from

### [AMD-113] Batch 6b — and a defect in already-shipped data, plus a guard I nearly got wrong

- **[INTENT]** Close the Middle-Eastern / African / Central-Asian set: `uzbek` 21, `georgian` 21,
  `african` 21, `south-african` 22. Table **1,344 → 1,425**. **57 of 66 nations done.**

- **[DELTA]**
  - **`dish-names-i18n.js`** — +85 rows, and **one corrected value on shipped data** (below).
  - **`__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js`** — four slugs registered; `georgian: null` added to
    `LOCAL_WRITES` with its reason; the same-`local` agreement assertion. **129 → 139 tests**;
    suite **5,052 → 5,062**.
  - **`package.json`** — 0.62.871 → **0.62.872**.

- **[GEORGIAN BRINGS RULE 3 BACK, IN A SCRIPT NO APP LOCALE USES]** All 21 Georgian dishes carry a
  curated `local` in **Georgian** (`ხაჭაპური აჭარული`, `ხინკალი`). Authoritative about the dish and
  constraining **no column**, because no app locale is written in it — the **`korean`/`thai` shape**.
  So it is mapped as `georgian: null` *with the reason written down*, rather than either asserted
  against (noise dressed as rigour) or left out (a silent gap).

- **[THE FOUR SHARED NAMES ARE THE SAME DISH — READ, NOT ASSUMED]** `bobotie`, `bunny chow`,
  `chakalaka`, `biltong` appear in both `african` and `south-african`. All four pairs of curated
  notes agree in substance; `african` is a continent-wide catch-all that repeats South African
  dishes. **No cuisine-qualified override was warranted**, and the collision detector stayed quiet,
  which is the right answer rather than a lucky one.

- **[⚠ A DEFECT IN ALREADY-SHIPPED DATA, FOUND WHILE PLANNING]** Measuring for 6b surfaced **11
  pairs across the overlay where one cuisine keys the same dish under two names**, identifiable by
  an identical curated `local`. Georgian's `phali` / `walnut-paste pkhali` (both `ფხალი`) is one;
  **ten were already on `main`.**

- **[⚠ AND THE GUARD I NEARLY WROTE FOR IT WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG]** The obvious rule — *same `local`
  ⇒ same translation* — is **false**, and this is the part worth keeping. The two keys are usually a
  **transliteration and an English gloss**: `lei cha` / `pounded tea`, `liang pi` /
  `cold skin noodles liangpi`, `mei cai kou rou` / `preserved vegetable braised pork`. Rule 2 keeps
  the first, rule 1 translates the second, so `Lei cha` **and** `Thé pilé` are both correct and a
  reader searching either term is well served. **That guard would have broken nine correct pairs to
  catch one bug** — a test that manufactures work while looking like diligence.

  The rule that **is** justified is narrower: where the curated `local` is written in an app locale,
  two dishes sharing it must agree **in that one locale** — the column the curated name actually
  governs. Measured: **9 pairs checked, exactly 1 disagreeing.**

  | cuisine | curated `local` | was | now |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `teochew` | 蚝烙 | `teochew oyster cake` → **蚝饼**, while its sibling `orh luak` → 蚝烙 | **蚝烙** |

  One cuisine's drawer was showing **two different Chinese names for one dish**.

- **[WHY THE EXISTING TEST COULD NOT SEE IT]** The `never invents a name` assertion checks
  membership in the curated **set**, not equality — added deliberately at v0.62.863, because
  `claypot frog leg porridge` is 砂煲田鸡粥 in Singapore and 田鸡粥 in Cantonese and one global entry
  cannot equal both. `teochew oyster cake` is curated as 蚝饼 by the **`singaporean`** overlay, so
  蚝饼 was in the set and passed. **The set rule is right; it simply cannot see a mismatch within a
  single cuisine.** The new assertion sits beside it rather than replacing it.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**

  | Mutation | Killed | Applied (1 → 0) |
  | :--- | ---: | :--- |
  | the teochew fix reverted to 蚝饼 | **2** — the agreement guard *and* the value pin | ✅ |
  | `moon cake` → 中秋饼 — a violation in a **different** cuisine | 1 — proving the guard is general, not pinned to the row that motivated it | ✅ |
  | one Georgian row deleted (`churchkhela`) | **2** — coverage *and* the endpoint surface | ✅ |

  Script integrity, all directions plus U+FFFD and Latin-in-`zh`, over all **1,425** entries: zero
  on every one. Collision detector quiet. Full preflight: **227 files, 5,062 tests**, all six apps
  render.

### [AMD-115] Batch 7a — Spanish is an app locale, so 5a returns

- **[INTENT]** The Americas and Oceania: `mexican` 30, `argentinian` 22, `brazilian` 22,
  `australian` 24, `new-zealand` 21, `australasia` 18. Table **1,425 → 1,562**. **63 of 66 nations.**

- **[WHY THE LAST 190 WERE SPLIT]** Six of the nine remaining slugs are ordinary national cuisines;
  three are **not nations at all** and each breaks a rule differently — `fusion` needs a **within-key**
  rule (proper name survives, dish-form word translates), `dessert` makes `fr` behave like a home
  locale inside a non-national slug, and `eurasian` carries the third overlay duplicate. Splitting on
  shape has paid for itself twice already (5a/5b, 6a/6b), so 7b takes those three.

- **[5a's RULE RETURNS UNCHANGED, AND SO DOES ITS HELPER]** For `mexican` and `argentinian` the key
  is already Spanish — and unlike Turkish, Arabic, Hebrew or Persian, **`es` IS one of the seven
  columns**. So the folded `startsWith` applies directly. **`fold` was hoisted to module scope as
  `foldForHomeLocale` and reused**, rather than copied into a third near-identical helper: a second
  definition that drifts from the first is how one guard quietly stops matching the other.

- **[THE EXEMPTIONS WERE MEASURED BEFORE THE ASSERTION WAS WRITTEN]** That discipline is what made
  5a's guard correct, and it paid again: **52 checked, exactly ONE failing** — `vacio steak`, whose
  key carries the English word "steak". Notably `churros mexican` is **not** among them:
  `Churros mexicanos` folds to a string that *does* start with the key, so folding earned its keep
  a second time rather than merely being reused.

- **[A SECOND ASSERTION FOR THE HAZARD THAT HAS NO HOME LOCALE]** Brazilian Portuguese carries
  **seven diacritic keys** (`pão de queijo`, `feijão tropeiro`, `vatapá`, `acarajé`, `açai bowl`,
  `pão de mel`, `arroz com feijão`) and Portuguese is not an app locale, so no home-locale claim
  exists. What *can* be asserted is that the Latin columns never ASCII-flatten the name — the same
  shape as 6a's Turkish rule, and equally invisible to every script check.

### [AMD-117] Batch 7b — the last 52, a guard declined, and the guarantee that outlives COVERED

- **[INTENT]** Finish it. `fusion` 24, `eurasian` 15, `dessert` **13** — not 14, because `pavlova`
  resolved in 7a as a shared bare key. **Measured, not carried forward from the plan.**

- **[DELTA]** `dish-names-i18n.js` **+52 rows** (1,562 → **1,614** entries);
  `__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js` **154 → 163 tests**; suite **5,077 → 5,086**;
  `package.json` → **0.62.874**.

- **[⚠ THE ONLY SET WHERE RULES 1 AND 2 SPLIT INSIDE ONE KEY]** Every earlier batch applied **one**
  rule per key. `fusion` splits them: `laksa carbonara`, `foie gras char siu`,
  `truffle char kway teow` — the **proper name must survive** (rule 2) while the **dish-form word
  translates** (rule 1). Getting it backwards is invisible to every other guard here, because
  *"Carbonara aux nouilles épicées"* is fluent French that has simply lost the dish.

  The assertion is a hand-listed **protected token** per key, required in all four Latin locales,
  folded. **`zh`/`ja` are deliberately excluded**: they render the proper name in their own script
  (叻沙, ラクサ), so a Latin-token check there would fail on correct data — the same mistake 6b's
  guard was narrowed to avoid, in another form. Non-vacuity: **≥ 18 of 24**, plus every token key
  must be a dish `fusion` actually serves.

- **[⚠ AND IT CAUGHT MY OWN DRAFT, THE FOURTH TIME THIS SESSION]** `chilli crab pasta` was written
  `fr: 'Pâtes au crabe chilli'` — which splits *chilli crab* into *crabe chilli* and loses the dish
  name as a unit. Fixed to `Pâtes au chilli crab`. A guard written in the same change that it then
  catches is the best evidence it is worth having.

- **[⚠ A GUARD I DECLINED TO WRITE, AND THE NUMBER THAT DECIDED IT]** `dessert` is French classics
  (`éclair`, `croquembouche`, `religieuse`), so `fr` **looks** like a home locale and 5a's rule
  looked reusable a third time. Measured **before** writing it: **8 of 13 would pass, 5 would need
  exemptions.**

  | batch | exemptions / checked | ratio |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: |
  | 5a | 13 / 154 | 8 % |
  | 7a | 1 / 52 | 2 % |
  | **`dessert`** | **5 / 13** | **38 %** |

  At 38 % the exemption list nearly rivals the rule it qualifies. A guard that is one-third
  hand-written exceptions does not constrain a future edit — **it documents one.** So `dessert` gets
  no home-locale assertion, and the *decision, with its number,* is the artefact instead. Declining
  to ship a weak guard because the pattern was available is the point.

- **[THE THIRD OVERLAY DUPLICATE, HANDLED AS ITS OWN SHAPE]** `devil curry` and `curry debal alt`
  are one dish — the curated notes say so outright. They are **pinned as DIFFERENT**, the opposite
  of `manti`/`mantı`: two *names*, one English and one Kristang, so rule 1 translates the first and
  rule 2 keeps the second. That is the `lei cha` / `pounded tea` shape. Pinning them equal would
  have repeated exactly the mistake 6b's guard was narrowed to avoid — the distinction was made
  deliberately, not by whichever precedent came to mind first.

- **[★ THE GUARANTEE THAT OUTLIVES `COVERED`]** `COVERED` is a **hand-maintained list**, and a dish
  added to a slug nobody remembered to list slips past it — the same class of gap as the 17 dishes
  missed at v0.62.862. The new assertion iterates **every slug in the overlay** and requires zero
  untranslated, with non-vacuity on both dimensions (**≥ 60 slugs, ≥ 1,500 dishes**) so a failed
  import cannot pass it by iterating nothing.

  **Measured: 66 slugs, 1,700 dish rows, 1,612 globally distinct names, 1,614 table entries
  (two cuisine-qualified overrides), 0 untranslated.** The `1,612` figure carried through this
  whole arc **re-measured exactly** at the end.

### [AMD-119] #1815 merged, and the duplicate cards a reader could actually see

- **[THE MERGE]** **#1815** squash-merged **01-09 '26 07:20:04 SGT** as `a119e38a`. Verified by
  content: version `0.62.875`, O-325's measurement present, `[AMD-118]` present with the
  correction, the `2,403` rebase row landed, and the completion still holds — 66 slugs, 0
  untranslated.

- **[INTENT]** Operator: **"can you do these 3, 2, 1"** — the three overlay duplicates, then
  #1815's record, then the VibeCodingRecord regeneration. This entry covers the first two; the
  regeneration follows in its own PR because it rewrites a publicly served artefact.

- **[⚠ WHAT INVESTIGATION CHANGED, BEFORE ANYTHING WAS DELETED]** I had been calling these three
  "overlay duplicates" for four PRs. Reading the data rather than my own notes changed the shape of
  the job three times over:

  1. **`iconicDishes` feeds DISPLAY only** — the cuisine drawer, the city plates, the food groups —
     **not the search matcher**, and the drawer endpoint does not dedupe. So each pair renders as
     **two near-identical cards a reader sees.** That is the actual defect; "duplicate data" was the
     symptom.
  2. **There were four pairs, not three.** `chinese: moon cake / mooncake` — identical notes,
     identical `local` (月饼) — I had never flagged it.
  3. **And then a fifth thing, which was worse:** `singaporean` lists **`teh tarik` twice under the
     exact same name**, once `F()` (food) and once `D()` (drink). The two entries **disagreed about
     what kind of thing it is**, while the curated note calls it *"a hot milk tea beverage"*.

- **[THE OPERATOR'S RULING, AND WHY IT WAS ASKED FOR]** The four pairs were not one kind of thing,
  so the choice was put to the operator rather than made for them:

  | kind | pairs | deleting costs |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | **spelling duplicates** — fold to the same string, same note | `manti`/`mantı`, `moon cake`/`mooncake` | **nothing** |
  | **one dish, two real names** — own notes, own wording | `phali`/`walnut-paste pkhali`, `devil curry`/`curry debal alt` | a name a reader may know |

  Operator chose **the spelling duplicates only**. So `mantı`, `mooncake` and the food-kind
  `teh tarik` are retired; `phali`/`walnut-paste pkhali` and `devil curry`/`curry debal alt` **stay
  on purpose**. Each drawer now shows one card: `manti`, `moon cake`, `teh tarik (drink)`.

- **[THE GUARD, WHICH THE OPERATOR ALSO ASKED FOR]** Within one cuisine, no two dish keys may fold
  to the same string. **Proved non-vacuous by restoring the pre-fix overlay: it fires and names all
  three** — `singaporean: teh tarik || teh tarik`, `chinese: moon cake || mooncake`,
  `turkish: manti || mantı`. Deliberately narrower than "one dish, two names": **folding is what
  separates a spelling duplicate from an alternate name**, which is why the two kept pairs pass it.

- **[⚠ THE REPO'S OWN GUARDS CAUGHT THREE CONSEQUENCES I HAD NOT PLANNED FOR]** This is the part
  worth keeping. Deleting two lines from `nation-overlay.js` was not the change; **finding what
  those two lines held up was**:

  | fired | what it caught | resolution |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | 6a's Turkish orthography guard, `toBe(10)` | the diacritic fixture dropped to **9** when `mantı` left | count updated to 9, with the reason inline |
  | `classics-city-plates-i18n` reachability (O-317) | `classics-notes.js` still held a `turkish::mantı` note pointing at a dish nobody serves | note block removed |
  | the same file's *"removing a base note must remove its translations too"* | the **i18n** row was still there — *"six strings for a dish that no longer exists are what a later coverage count reads as work already done"* | `classics-notes-i18n.generated.js` row removed |

  Hand-editing that last file is **not** a generated-file violation: its own header says
  *"GENERATED SHAPE, HAND-FILLED CONTENT"*, because the translate script needs a paid key the
  operator declined.

- **[A FLOOR LOWERED THE WAY ITS OWN COMMENT DEMANDS]** The locale-coverage row floor said:
  *"the number and the reason move together or not at all."* It went **1,671 → 1,670**, and the
  reason is written into the existing chain of rulings (1,677 → 1,673 → 1,672 → 1,671 → 1,670),
  naming what was retired and what was deliberately kept. The 6b same-`local` floor is now sitting
  **exactly on 8**; that is annotated so the next person re-derives it rather than decrementing it
  to get green.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** Full `gia-preflight`, because `nation-overlay.js` is core data.
  **227 files, 5,087 tests**, all six apps render. The completeness guarantee re-checked after the
  deletions: **66 slugs, 1,697 distinct dishes, 0 untranslated, 1,612 table entries.** The guard
  proved non-vacuous by restoring the pre-fix tree, and the overlay restored afterwards.

### [AMD-120] The Vibe Journal caught up — 44 PRs, and the redaction step earning its keep

- **[INTENT]** Operator: **"can you do these 3, 2, 1"** — this is **item 1**, the only one that
  **publishes**. It rewrites `public/doc/vibe-journal.{json,html}`, served at
  **soleat.net/doc/vibe-journal.html**, which is why it was held back for its own PR.

- **[DELTA]** Ledger **1,743 → 1,787 rows**, `max(n)` **1771 → 1815**, no gaps.
  `data/pr-files.tsv` +44 lines. `GEN_DATE` 2026-08-28 → **2026-09-01**. All four artefacts
  regenerated. `package.json` → **0.62.877**.

- **[⚠ THE REDACTION STEP FOUND SOMETHING, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT OF IT]**
  `VibeCodingRecord.md` calls the sweep *"not optional and not a one-off"* and warns that
  *"I checked and it was clean" is not a safeguard a future run can inherit.* **I assumed these 44
  bodies were clean because I wrote them. The sweep returned four hits.**

  Measured precisely rather than reported as alarm: all four are the **prefix-and-glob form** —
  the token name's leading word followed by `.`, not the value — in #1772–#1775, in sentences
  *about* the token needing rotation. **No full
  credential value and no chat id appear anywhere in the 44** — verified by searching for the
  suffix and the id directly. Redacted anyway, because the doc's posture is unconditional and the
  cost is one word of readability. **Four conservative redactions of a reference, not a leak** —
  and the assumption that made me expect zero is exactly the one the doc says not to hold.

- **[AND ONE PRE-EXISTING HIT, NOT RE-FLAGGED AS A DISCOVERY]** `prs.ndjson` carries **one**
  occurrence in row **#1722** — a sentence *documenting the redaction rule itself*. That is the
  committed-markdown category O-325 already assessed and closed. **All four generated artefacts
  scan 0.** Saying more about it than that would repeat the framing error `[AMD-118]` corrects.

- **[⚠ THREE THINGS THE DOC WOULD HAVE HAD ME GET WRONG]** Following `VibeCodingRecord.md`
  literally produces a corrupt ledger, because the file describes a shape that has since drifted:

  | the doc says | the last 30 rows actually use |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `"state":"closed"` | **`"MERGED"`** |
  | `"merged":"…Z"` | **`…+00:00`** |
  | *"first ~360 chars… single line"* | **full bodies, 1,970–8,116 chars, newlines kept** |

  Measured before writing, then **the doc was corrected in the same change** so the next person
  reads the shape in use rather than its history.

  A fourth trap avoided by checking: **squash-commit bodies are NOT the PR descriptions.** They are
  the commit messages, written separately. There is no cheap `git log` path.

- **[HOW THE FETCH WAS DONE, AND WHY IT MATTERS]** The repo is public, so the PR list is readable
  **unauthenticated**, and `curl` piped straight to disk — **the 44 bodies never passed through the
  model's context at all**. That is not only cheaper: a body that is never read cannot be
  paraphrased, so the rows are the API's bytes, decoded and redacted by script.

- **[⚠ AND A THIRD, CAUGHT BY MY OWN PRE-PUSH SCAN, OF THE KIND `[AMD-108]` ALREADY NAMED]**
  The first draft of *this entry* reproduced the prefix literally while explaining that the prefix
  had been redacted. `[AMD-108]` records the lesson verbatim — *"prose about a secret can contain
  the secret"* — and I wrote it into the sentence describing the redaction. Caught by the same
  diff scan that runs before every push in this session, and replaced with a description that
  loses nothing. **Knowing the failure mode is not the same as not committing it.**

- **[TWO MISTAKES OF MY OWN, BOTH CAUGHT BY THE SUITE]**
  1. I put the catch-up narrative **inside the `GEN_DATE` string**. It contains apostrophes; the
     string is single-quoted; the module stopped parsing. The convention is a **date in the value
     and the narrative in the trailing comment** — I had misread a `grep` hit that showed both.
  2. Corrected, it still failed: `vibe-journal-feature-area.test.js` reads only the **first 800
     characters** of the markdown, and a 696-character clause pushed the `(#1–#1815)` banner past
     that window. **The guard was right and my prose was too long** — the fix was to shorten the
     prose, not widen the window.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **Rows** 1,743 → **1,787**; max 1815; **0 gaps** across 1772–1815; **0 duplicate `n`**.
  - **Schema**: all five keys present, `state === "MERGED"`, `merged` matches `+00:00`, **no HTML
    entities**, every body > 100 chars.
  - **`pr-files.tsv`**: 44 new lines, all tab-separated, none with an empty file list.
  - **Re-scan of every published artefact**: `records.tsv`, `vibe-coding-record.md`,
    `vibe-journal.json`, `vibe-journal.html` — **0 hits each**.
  - **Served JSON**: `generated` = `2026-09-01`, `count` = 1787 = row count.
  - **227 files, 5,087 tests** green, including `wrong-log.test.js` (the gitignore still holds) and
    `vibe-summary-guard.test.js`.

- **[OUT OF SCOPE, DELIBERATELY]** **`data/session-replies.ndjson` was NOT refreshed.** It is
  transcript-derived, and publishing it would put this session's replies on a public page — a
  separate decision with its own blast radius, and one the operator has already ruled on in the
  same territory (the Wrong-Log is gitignored precisely for this). The generator embeds whatever

### [AMD-121] #1816 merged — and the pre-push guard was right an eleventh time

- **[THE MERGE]** **#1816** squash-merged **01-09 '26 08:02:01 SGT** as `1f2f8fbc`. Verified by
  content from the merged tree: version `0.62.876`; `F('mantı')` and `F('mooncake')` gone; exactly
  **one** `F('teh tarik')` left (the Malaysian one, as ruled); `[AMD-119]` present; the folding
  guard present.

- **[⚠ THE GUARD CAUGHT ME MID-PUSH, AND IT WAS RIGHT]** I had committed the regeneration onto the
  branch while #1816 was still open, judged that pushing would widen a PR under review, and said so.
  The stop hook pushed back; I reconsidered and decided that bundling was a fair reading of the
  operator's *"do these 3, 2, 1"* — **and the push was refused anyway**, because in the interval
  #1816 had merged and the remote tip was now content-identical to `main`. **Eleven fires, eleven
  true positives.** My reasoning about whether to push was moot; the state had moved under it.

  The lesson is the smaller one: **I decided from a PR state I had read minutes earlier.** The guard
  reads it at push time, which is the only moment that counts.

- **[RECOVERY, AND WHAT IT MADE POSSIBLE]** Restarted from `main`, re-applied by `format-patch`/
  `git am`. One snag worth recording: the patch directory held **a stale patch from an earlier
  session**, and `git am <dir>/*.patch` applied that one first and conflicted. Aborted, cleared the
  directory, re-applied the single intended patch. **`*.patch` is not a safe glob into a directory
  that persists across sessions.**

  Because the restart happened *after* #1816 merged, **#1816's own row could be added** — so the
  ledger is genuinely current at **1,788 rows through #1816**, rather than shipping one PR behind
  as it would have if the push had succeeded. The guard's interruption improved the result.

- **[DELTA]** `prs.ndjson` 1,787 → **1,788**; `pr-files.tsv` +1 (10 files); the `GEN_DATE` clause
  now names **#1772–#1816**; all four artefacts regenerated. **Redaction on #1816's row: 0 hits.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `generated` = `2026-09-01`, `count` = **1,788** = row count; the
  `(#1–#1816)` banner sits inside the 800-character window the test inspects; **every published
  artefact re-scans at 0**; **227 files, 5,087 tests** green.

- **[⚠ THE SCAN THAT MATTERED, RUN AGAIN ON `main`]** This is the one artefact set that reaches the

### [AMD-123] Korean K1 — 405 units, and the count that was wrong by two

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"The 'teh tarik kind' is drink. Also do a big PR on the Korean
  language toggle, plan how you want to do it"*, then **full parity** and **"full parity, so guards
  need no change"** in answer to the scoping question. This is **K1** of the eleven-PR arc: the
  bot's own `i18n.js`, plus the `teh tarik` ruling — and it is also the substantive PR that
  discharges `[AMD-122]`'s ride under D-205.

- **[⚠ I RE-AUTHORED `[AMD-122]` BECAUSE I COULD NOT SEE IT]** This session restarted the branch from
  `origin/main` and worked from there. `[AMD-122]` and its `CLAUDE.md` rebase row at **2,475** were
  committed to the **remote branch** as `f774f62f`, one commit ahead of `main` — so they were absent
  from the local tree and I wrote a second `[AMD-122]` from scratch, along with a `CLAUDE.md` row
  claiming the 2,475 row had been *lost*. **It had not been lost; it had been pushed.** The push was
  refused as non-fast-forward, the remote was read, and the duplicate was **dropped in favour of the
  record written at merge time** — the one contemporaneous with the event it records. Nothing was
  force-pushed over. The lesson is narrow and repeatable: **`origin/main` is not the same starting
  point as `origin/<branch>`**, and a branch restart discards whatever the remote branch holds beyond
  the merge base. Read the remote branch before concluding something is missing.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `i18n.js` — **397 `ko:` values added** across a 405-entry table. Three shapes had to be handled
    (247 one-locale-per-line, 129 single-line from the two-locale sweep, and two array-of-lines
    bodies), so the object span is found by brace-matching from the key and the insertion placed
    relative to the **last property**, not by a line pattern. Quote style is copied from the `es`
    value in the same entry. `ko` is absent from every `SUPPORTED` list.
  - `nation-overlay.js:889` — `F('teh tarik')` → `D('teh tarik')` for `malaysian`. Both cuisines now
    say **drink**, closing the disagreement `[AMD-119]` reported and `[AMD-121]`/`[AMD-122]` carried.
  - `__tests__/dish-names-i18n.test.js` — the kind-conflict guard: no dish name may carry a
    different `kind` in two cuisines, plus a named assertion that `teh tarik` is a drink everywhere.
  - `__tests__/i18n-korean.test.js` — **new**, 10 tests. Every existing guard is blind to `ko` while
    `SUPPORTED` is the eight, so the content would otherwise arrive unguarded.

- **[⚠ THE SURFACE IS 405, NOT 403]** The plan's figure came from an extractor whose key pattern read
  `(?:[a-z]+\.)+`, which silently skips every key with a camelCase or digit-bearing segment. Twelve
  were invisible to it — `wake2.body`, `hidden.anchorAmbiguous.got`, `loc.searchArea.set` and nine
  more — and two entries, `privacy.body` and `legal.body`, never reached the authoring list at all.
  **The undercount was found by the insertion script refusing to place 12 values**, not by re-reading
  the plan. The corrected count is asserted in the test so it cannot recur quietly.

- **[⚠ WHY EIGHT ENTRIES HAVE NO KOREAN, AND WHY THAT IS THE CORRECT ANSWER]** The first pass gave
  all 405 a `ko` column. `bot.lang.set.<code>` is keyed by the **target** language and read as
  `t('bot.lang.set.ja', 'ja')` — the value is written once, in that target language, which is why its
  six locales after `fr` have no column at all. A `ko` column there is **unreachable** (only
  `lang === 'ko'` reads it, and `lang` always equals the code in the key) and, if it ever were
  reached, would say *"language set to Japanese"* in Korean. **Parity with the siblings here means
  having no column, not having an unreachable one**, so the eight were removed. `language.btn.*`
  looks identical and is the opposite case — `display` there is the *reader's* language, so `ko` is
  read and must hold `🇷🇺 Русский` unchanged. The two families were separated by reading their call
  sites, not by their shape.

- **[⚠ A DEFECT FOUND AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE]** 19 keys carry a **literal backslash-n** or
  **backslash-backtick** rather than a newline or a backtick — an artefact of the two-locale sweep,
  present in **all eight locales including English**, and sent with `parse_mode: 'Markdown'`, where
  backslash is not an escape character. So `📍 *Location saved*\n{place}` renders the `\n` to the
  user. Confirmed from the parse mode **at the call site**, per D-199, not inferred from the string's
  shape. This is an English-first defect across 19 × 8 strings, not a translation defect, and
  repairing it inside a PR that adds a locale would put an unrelated behaviour change into a
  405-entry diff. **Korean matches whatever its siblings do**, so it is neither better nor worse than
  what ships; the test asserts the match and names the reason. **Reported, not fixed.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **Round-trip**: all 397 values read back out of the parsed module **byte-identical** to what was
    authored, and **not one of the 8 × 405 pre-existing values changed** — checked by loading the
    pre- and post-insert files side by side, because a splice that rewrote a neighbour would be
    invisible in a diff this size.
  - **Structural parity with the English**: placeholder names, HTML tags, real newlines and literal
    escapes all match. Two deviations surfaced and both are `ja`'s too, so they are the file's
    convention rather than my error — which is why the checker prints `ja` beside every mismatch
    instead of only `en`.
  - **Script integrity, including the direction Korean adds**: no Cyrillic, no kana, no U+FFFD. A
    blanket "no Latin in Hangul" rule was **measured and rejected** — 88 distinct Latin words survive
    stripping, nearly all proper nouns, acronyms, units or examples the user must type verbatim. The
    guard that *is* written is the Korean analogue of "identical to en": a value with no Hangul is a
    defect **unless the English has no translatable word either**, which exempts 13 entries by rule
    and only the 8-key language menu by name — 3.2 %, against the 38 % that made me decline a guard
    in 7b.
  - **Eight mutations, each proved applied by occurrence count 1 → 0 before the outcome was read**,
    and **every one detected**: a blanked value, a renamed placeholder, a dropped `</i>`, Cyrillic in
    a Korean value, a value left in English, an unreachable `ko` column returning to
    `bot.lang.set.en`, `ko` added to `SUPPORTED` early, and an entry deleted from the table. The
    ninth attempt — renaming a key rather than deleting it — was **not** detected, correctly: it left
    the count at 405, so the **mutation** was invalid, not the guard. Recorded rather than quietly
    dropped. Tree restored byte-identical afterwards.
  - **228 files, 5,099 tests** green. `node --check` on all four changed `.js` files. Credential scan
    over the staged diff: **0 hits**.
  - **⚠ The anchor guard caught me stamping the future.** `last_anchor_time` was first written
    `09:35 SGT` while the clock read `09:28` — a figure typed from intention rather than fetched,
    which is the same class of error as the invented rebase count corrected in the paragraph below.
    D-203 says the anchor is `max(sensor, latest known event)`, and a value **above** the sensor is
    not a conservative rounding, it is a verification dated before the thing it verifies. Re-fetched
    and corrected to `09:29`. The guard existed because `journal-0_62_723` made this exact mistake.
  - **⚠ And the rebase row was first written at 2,516, a number I did not measure.** Corrected to
    the script's reading of **2,503** before the commit. Both slips share one shape — a plausible
    figure written where a measured one belongs — which is why they are recorded together.

- **[TEST]** `t('bot.index.cancel', 'ko')` still returns `"Cancel"`; `pickLang('ko')` still returns
  `'en'`; `SUPPORTED` is still the eight. **The Korean is inert until K11**, which is what makes
  staging it safe — and is asserted rather than assumed.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened from `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`. Ships on merge to `main`;
  Railway deploys from `main`. **No user-visible change** beyond `teh tarik`'s kind.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **K2–K11 remain**: `cuisine` (381), `clipboard` + `transport` (374), `hawker` + `menu` (162),
    dish names (1,612), dish notes (1,682), then the flip.
  - **`start.intro` says "8 options"** in all nine columns. Correct today; **K11 must move it to 9 in

  - **K11 must add a new key `bot.lang.set.ko`**, since that family is target-keyed and gains a
    **row**, not a column.
  - **`ko` is now more complete than six shipped locales** — 397 of 405 against 389 — because
    `id`/`ru`/`de`/`zh`/`ja`/`es` legitimately skip the 16 language-menu entries. Noted so the
    asymmetry is not later read as a Korean gap.

### [AMD-124] #1818 merged — and Codex was asked the question in the same minute it was bypassed

- **[THE MERGE]** **#1818** squash-merged **01-09 '26 09:43:49 SGT** as `3f9f58ef`, by the operator.
  Verified by content from the merged tree, ten claims re-derived rather than re-read off the PR
  body: **405** table entries; **397** carrying `ko`; all eight `bot.lang.set.*` **uncolumned**;
  `SUPPORTED` still `['en','fr','id','ru','de','zh','ja','es']`; `pickLang('ko')` → `'en'` and
  `t('bot.index.cancel','ko')` → `"Cancel"`, so **the Korean is still inert on production**;
  version `0.62.878`; **zero** `F('teh tarik')` and **two** `D('teh tarik')`;
  `__tests__/i18n-korean.test.js` present; `[AMD-122]` and `[AMD-123]` both present; the
  `CLAUDE.md` row at **2,503** present.

- **[⚠ READY AND MERGED IN THE SAME SECOND, SO CODEX NEVER SAW IT]** The `ready_for_review` webhook
  and `merged_at` both carry **`2026-09-01T01:43:49Z`**. Codex's own trigger list — read off its
  review on #1808 — is *open a PR for review*, *mark a draft as ready*, or *comment `@codex
  review`*. Marking ready fires it; merging in the same second gives it nothing to review. **So
  #1818 shipped with no second reader**, and that is worth writing down rather than noticing later
  as an absence.

  It is also a coincidence with a shape: the operator asked *"Can you drop codex activities without
  affecting this project"* four minutes earlier, and the answer given was that the project has **no
  mechanical dependency** on Codex — 8 workflows and none of them Codex, not one of the 4 CI checks,
  56 JS files mentioning it and **zero executable references** once comments are masked — but that
  its findings are recorded against **21 distinct PRs**, including **X-13**, where Codex caught a
  standing rule being installed in `CLAUDE.md` before the operator had approved it. **The question
  is still open**; this entry records only that the merge happened without a Codex pass, not that a
  decision was taken.

- **[STATUS]** PROD — merged to `main`; Railway deploys from `main`. **No user-visible change**
  beyond `teh tarik`'s kind, since `ko` is not offered.

- **[HELD PER D-205]** This record does not get a PR of its own. It is committed to the branch —
  rather than left in the working tree — because this container is ephemeral, and it **rides K2**,
  the next substantive PR. If no substantive PR follows, it is owed and must be logged as still due.
