### [AMD-125] Korean K2 — 916 units across five Mini Apps, and two numbers the plan had wrong

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"Continue with Korean language translation. Finish it"*, then **full
  parity — all 5,852**, **the flip includes scripts**, and **"Complete in 5-8 PR"**. This is **K2**
  of the re-staged five: every Mini App's UI. It also discharges **`[AMD-124]`**'s ride under D-205.

- **[⚠ TWO MEASUREMENTS THAT CHANGED THE PLAN, BOTH FOUND ON `main`]**
  - **Dish notes are 3,354, not 1,682.** There are **two independent note sets** —
    `nation-overlay-dishnotes-i18n.generated.js` (1,684) and `classics-notes-i18n.generated.js`
    (1,670). They share **1,491 keys** and carry **different prose for each**: `african::akara` has
    a cuisine note *and* a classics note, and neither is a copy of the other. The old plan counted
    one file's worth.
  - **The flip touches ~40 sites, not ~12.** The plan named the app-facing lists and missed
    `pronounce-name.js`, `prompt-locale.js`, `vibe-summary.js`, `fun-facts.js`'s `OVERLAY_LANGS`,
    20+ test `LOCALES` arrays, and 6 `scripts/*.mjs`. Both were put to the operator with the
    corrected numbers **before** any authoring, because a scope chosen against a wrong total is not
    a scope that was chosen.

- **[⚠ AND ONE THING I BRIEFLY GOT WRONG ABOUT THE OLD PLAN]** I reported that the promised rule-3
  payoff did not exist, because a grep for `local:\s*'…한글…'` over `nation-overlay.js` returns
  **zero**. It was right and I was wrong: all 30 `korean` dishes do carry a curated Hangul `local`
  (비빔밥, 불고기), living in `nation-overlay-local.generated.js` as `'korean::bibimbap': '비빔밥'`
  and folded on at load. **Read data through the module, not the file** — the source text and the
  loaded object are different things, and only one of them is the data.

- **[DELTA]** — 916 units, in the two shapes these files actually use:
  - **Four `KO_STRINGS` blocks** — `cuisine` (380), `transport` (123), `hawker` (82), `menu` (78).
    These apps keep `en`/`fr` inline and each later locale in its own object folded on at load, so
    Korean is **one new object and one merge line**, not 380 per-entry splices. The merge is
    appended after the *last* existing loop so load order is unchanged, and written in whichever of
    the two idioms that file already uses (`for…in` vs `Object.entries`).
  - **One inline column** — `clipboard` (251), which keeps all eight locales inside each entry.
    K1's brace-matching inserter, reused unchanged.
  - `__tests__/tma-i18n-korean.test.js` — **new**, 21 tests.

- **[⚠ TWO KEYS DELIBERATELY LEFT WITHOUT KOREAN]** `menu|tile.sketchbook.label` and
  `menu|footer.brand` are pinned in `tma-i18n-coverage.test.js` as absent from all six later
  locales with reason `proper-noun`. Korean matches its siblings by **also having no cell**. This is
  K1's `bot.lang.set.*` lesson in a second form: **parity means matching the shape, not filling
  every slot.** The pins gain `'ko'` at the K6 flip.

- **[THE GUARD THAT ONLY EXISTS BECAUSE THE SHAPE CHANGED]** A `KO_STRINGS` block can be
  syntactically perfect, parse cleanly, satisfy every completeness count — and reach no reader,
  because nothing folds it onto `STRINGS`. That failure is **invisible to source-parsing guards**,
  which is precisely why it gets its own assertion. It was proved by deleting `hawker`'s merge loop
  and leaving the block in place.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **Round-trip**: all 914 authored values read back out of the written files **byte-identical**.
  - **Nothing pre-existing moved**: **11,306** `en`/`fr`/`id`/`ru`/`de`/`zh`/`ja`/`es` cells parsed
    from the before and after files side by side — **0 changed**.
  - **Structural parity**: placeholders and HTML tags match the English in all 914.
  - **Script integrity**: no Cyrillic, no kana, no U+FFFD. The no-Hangul rule cost **five** named
    exemptions — `header.versionOnly` (`v{v}`), two `Sketchbook`s, two `1.0 ~ 5.0` — and **every one
    matches what `ja` does with the same key**, so they are the files' convention rather than my
    omission. 3 of 914, against the 38 % that made me decline a guard in 7b.
  - **Nine mutations, each proved applied before its outcome was read, all nine detected**: a
    blanked value, a renamed placeholder, Cyrillic in a Korean value, a value left in English, the
    merge loop deleted, a pinned proper noun gaining a cell, `ko` added to `SUPPORTED` early,
    `clipboard` growing a block it must not have, and an inline cell emptied.
  - **⚠ Two of those nine are INSERTIONS**, whose replacement contains the anchor — so the
    occurrence count cannot fall and `1 → 0` is the wrong proof for them. The first run printed
    `1 → 1 (mutation applied)`, which is a mislabel. **Re-proved by byte length** (+25 and +35) and
    recorded here rather than left standing, since the point of proving a mutation applied is
    defeated by a proof that cannot fail.
  - **229 files, 5,120 tests** green. `node --check` on all five. **All six apps build** and
    `test:render` paints all six.
  - **⚠ The rebase-row guard fired, for the second time in this session.** The `CLAUDE.md` row at
    **2,516** was first inserted after the **2,503** row rather than after **2,507**, and a count
    out of order reads as a decrease whether it is a real regression or a misplacement. Moved, not
    renumbered. The first fire was on 01-09 at 2,475; both were placement, not arithmetic, which is
    worth knowing about the guard: it catches where a row goes, not just what it says.

- **[TEST]** Every app's `SUPPORTED`/`SUPPORTED_LOCALES` is still the eight, and every app still

### [AMD-126] Korean K3 — 1,612 dish names, and a key pattern that was too narrow for the second time

- **[INTENT]** K3 of the five: `dish-names-i18n.js`, the whole table. **1,582 written by hand, 30
  free under rule 3.**

- **[DELTA]**
  - `dish-names-i18n.js` — an inline `ko:` column on all **1,612** entries.
  - The Korean section's header comment **extended, not rewritten** (AU-1). It said the curated
    Hangul `local` *"informs these but fills nothing"*, which stopped being the whole truth the
    moment `ko` became a column. The old paragraph stays because it still explains why `zh`/`ja`
    read as they do; the new one says what changed.
  - `__tests__/dish-names-korean.test.js` — **new**, 8 tests.

- **[⚠ THE KEY PATTERN WAS TOO NARROW — SECOND TIME IN THIS ARC]** The inserter matched **1,598 of
  1,612**. The 14 it missed all contain an apostrophe and are therefore written with a
  **double-quoted key** — `"po' boy"`, `"shepherd's pie"`, `"oka i'a"` — and the pattern excluded the
  quote character from the key body. K1 lost 12 entries to `(?:[a-z]+\.)+` for the same reason:
  **a key pattern narrower than the keys.** What caught it both times was not reading the regex but
  **comparing the match count against the table's own size**, so the inserter now prints that
  comparison and says explicitly when entries were skipped *silently*.

- **[⚠ TWO SLIPS THE LATIN GUARD CAUGHT, NEITHER A TRANSLATION ERROR]** `rawon` came out as
  **`라won`** and `barberry polo` as **`잘barberry 폴로`** — Hangul and Latin fused mid-word. These are
  composition slips, invisible to anyone who does not read the script and invisible to every guard
  that only checks for *foreign* scripts. The rule that catches them is the inverse of K1's: not
  "no Latin", which is hopeless, but **"no Latin left after the allowlisted initialisms are
  removed"**. The allowlist is two entries, `IPA` and `XO`, and is capped in the test.

- **[RULE 3, AND WHERE THE DATA ACTUALLY LIVES]** All 30 `korean` dishes take their Korean straight
  from the curated Hangul `local` — 비빔밥, 불고기 — asserted equal, not merely present. Worth
  restating because I got it wrong once already: a grep for a Hangul `local:` over
  `nation-overlay.js` finds **nothing**; the values live in `nation-overlay-local.generated.js` and
  are folded on at load. **Read data through the module, not the file.**

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **Round-trip**: all 1,612 values read back **byte-identical**; **11,284** pre-existing cells
    across the seven shipped locales compared before and after — **0 changed**; key count 1,612 → 1,612.
  - **Completeness stated over the OVERLAY, not this table**: every dish in every slug resolves to a

  separately so the headroom cannot be widened without the number moving, and the distribution is

### [AMD-128] #1819 merged — and the pre-push guard was right a twelfth time

- **[WHAT HAPPENED]** PR #1819 (K2 + K3) was **squash-merged by the operator at 01-09 '26 11:01:52
  SGT as `3d1c0c4f`**. I did not know that when K4 was committed: the branch tip still held
  `bcf888aa`, and **the pre-push guard is what told me** — it refused the push on the grounds that
  the remote tip was now content-identical to `main`, so a fast-forward would append to a closed PR
  and the work would never reach `main`. Twelve fires, twelve true positives.

- **[VERIFIED BY CONTENT FROM THE MERGED TREE]** per D-123, re-derived rather than read off the PR
  body: version **0.62.880**; `cuisine` **380**, `transport` **123**, `hawker` **82**, `menu` **78**
  `KO_STRINGS` keys with a merge loop present in all four; `clipboard` **251** inline `ko:` cells;
  `dish-names-i18n.js` **1,612**; `__tests__/tma-i18n-korean.test.js` and
  `__tests__/dish-names-korean.test.js` both present; `[AMD-124]`, `[AMD-125]` and `[AMD-126]` all
  present; the `CLAUDE.md` row at **2,520** present. **`SUPPORTED` is still the eight** in both
  `i18n.js` and `user-prefs.js`, and `t('bot.index.cancel', 'ko')` still returns `"Cancel"` — so
  **5,852 units of Korean now sit on production and not one of them reaches a reader.** That is the
  staging working, not a defect, and it stays true until K6.

- **[⚠ A PROBE OF MINE WAS WRONG AND THE TREE WAS NOT]** My first check reported the merge loop
  **absent** from `transport`, `hawker` and `menu`. It searched forward from `lastIndexOf('KO_STRINGS')`,
  which in those files is the loop itself — so the probe was looking for the loop *after* the loop.
  Grepping the three files directly shows all three present at lines 1099, 802 and 769. Recorded
  because the failure mode is the one that matters: **a verification tool that reports absence is
  making a claim, and its own correctness is not evidenced by the claim being plausible.** The green
  suite — `tma-i18n-korean.test.js` carries a dedicated merge-loop guard — had already said so.

- **[RECOVERY]** Restarted the branch from `origin/main` and reapplied K4 by `format-patch` +
  `git am`, which applied cleanly because `bcf888aa` and `3d1c0c4f` are content-identical. Nothing
  force-pushed over. **231 files / 5,137 tests** green again on the new base.

- **[ANCHOR CORRECTED, NOT REWRITTEN AWAY]** `doc/.serial-state.yml`'s `last_anchor_source` was
  written before the merge was known and named `bcf888aa` (10:51:14) as the latest event. #1819's
  merge at **11:01:52** is later than that. The **anchor value is unaffected** — D-203 takes
  `max(sensor, latest known event)` and the 11:42 sensor reading wins either way — but the source
  string described the wrong runner-up, so it is corrected to name the merge.

- **[STATUS]** Held per D-205 — a merge record does not get a PR of its own. It **rides K4**, the
  substantive PR it was written alongside, rather than sitting in an ephemeral container's working
  tree.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Unchanged from `[AMD-127]`: K5 (1,670 classics notes) and K6 (the flip) remain;
  the two orphan note rows are still owed; the `parse_mode: 'Markdown'` defect from `[AMD-123]` is
  still owed; **no native speaker has read any of this**; the language toggle is still untested
  since #1806.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

### [AMD-130] Korean K5 — 1,670 classics dish notes, and the one shipped guard that could see the column

- **[INTENT]** K5 of six, and **the last content PR of the arc**: `classics-notes-i18n.generated.js`,
  the 📜 notes behind `CLASSIC_NOTES` and `CUISINE_NOTES`. **All 1,670 hand-written.** After this
  only K6, the flip, remains.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `classics-notes-i18n.generated.js` — one `ko:` line per entry, **1,670** of them.
    **No product code changed.** The fold at `classics-notes.js:27869` copies
    `Object.entries(loc)` rather than a hardcoded locale list — its own comment says so — and
    carries `ko` to `note.ko` the moment K6 flips the lists.
  - Header comment **extended, not rewritten** (AU-1).
  - `__tests__/classics-notes-korean.test.js` — **new**, 11 tests.
  - `__tests__/classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js` — **three edits to a shipped guard**, explained
    below. Its logic is untouched; its script tables gained `ko`.

- **[⚠ A SHIPPED GUARD SAW THE COLUMN, AND THE PLAN SAID NONE COULD]** The arc rests on a stated
  property: every completeness guard is **presence-based**, so `ko` can be added everywhere while
  `ko` stays out of every `SUPPORTED` list and the suite stays green until K6. That is true of the
  completeness guards. It is **not** true of the *script-contamination* guard in
  `classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js`, which iterates `Object.entries(langs)` and judges **whatever
  keys it finds**. Its `FORBIDDEN` table said in as many words: *"Hangul belongs to NO target
  language."* All **1,670** rows failed on the first run.
  - **That is the guard working, not failing.** Its premise expired the moment the file gained a
    Korean column, and the fix is to say so: `allowed: new Set(['ko'])`, with the original sentence
    kept and a new paragraph beside it (AU-1) recording why the empty set stopped being right.
  - **The same pass STRENGTHENED it in two places**, which is the part worth having. `ko` was added
    to `SCRIPT_RULES` (a Korean value must contain Hangul — 0 of 1,670 failed) and to
    `NON_LATIN_LANGS`, which runs the English-stopword rule. That second one was **measured before
    it was added, not after**, and it found a real defect: **one** row, `SG::michael jackson`, had
    kept *"Black or White"* in Latin, while `ja` writes 「ブラック・オア・ホワイト」 and `zh` writes 《黑或白》.
    **The corpus renders a title in its own script for every non-Latin locale and the Korean had
    not.** The note was rewritten to match, and the count is now 0. Widening a rule and fixing the
    row it catches are the same change; widening it and exempting the row would not have been.

- **[⚠ AND THE FIRST LENGTH BAND WAS RIGHT WHILE ITS THRESHOLD WAS STILL WRONG FOR THIS CORPUS]**
  K4's `ko`/`ja` band is `[0.4, 1.6]`. **Reusing it here would have rejected roughly a fifth of
  these rows.** These two note sets share 1,491 keys and **not one shared `ja` value**; `ja`
  compresses much harder in K4. Measured across all 1,670 here: **min 0.53, median 1.15, p99 1.58,
  max 1.87**, so the band is `[0.45, 2.1]` and the extremes are asserted separately. A guard copied
  between corpora is a guard reporting on the corpus it was measured against.

- **[⚠ THE MUTATION THAT WASN'T DETECTED, AND WHAT IT BOUGHT]** **M8b — doubling a SHORT note —
  passed.** `new-zealand::green-lipped mussel` repeated verbatim runs 42 characters at ratio 1.62:
  inside the band, far under the cap, invisible. **Length is the wrong instrument for that defect**,
  so it got its own: the longest substring a note repeats within itself. Measured across the corpus
  before the threshold was chosen — the real maximum is **nine characters**, 1,023 rows repeat at
  most two, only three exceed six — so a threshold of 16 sits far above honest prose and below any
  duplicated clause. M8b is detected now. An earlier attempt, **M8, is recorded as INVALID rather
  than as a gap**: it was a same-length rewrite that happened to repeat a phrase, not a doubling.

- **[THE 140-CHARACTER CAP, WHICH K4 DID NOT HAVE TO OBEY]** Every note is capped at 140 characters
  in every locale by a shipped test — which iterates a hardcoded eight-locale list and so **cannot
  see `ko` until K6**. Rather than let the flip discover a violation, the K5 guard asserts the cap
  directly. It was checked before authoring, not after: K4's measured `ko`/`en` ratio (median 0.43,
  max 0.64) predicted 0 of 1,670 over the cap, and the finished column runs **max 85, median 61**.

- **[179 TRUNCATED ENGLISH BODIES, AND THE CONVENTION FOR THEM]** 179 of the English notes end on a
  dangling semicolon or an unclosed clause — an English-side defect that predates this column. The
  shipped locales already answer it the same way: render the content that exists and punctuate it
  normally. `ko` follows them rather than mirroring the truncation. **Recorded, not fixed** — it is
  an English-first defect and does not belong in a locale-addition PR.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **Round-trip**: all 1,670 values read back byte-identical; key count **and key order**
    unchanged; every entry gained **exactly one** new key, and that key is `ko`; **10,020**
    pre-existing cells across the six shipped locales compared before and after — **0 changed**.
  - **Line-based insertion**, as in K4 and for the same reason: 1,670 opened / 1,670 closed /
    1,670 filled / 0 unfilled, asserted **before anything was written**.
  - **Pairing asserted in both directions as numbers** — 1,670 ↔ 1,670, 0 orphans either way.
    K4's lesson stated as counts rather than as "complete".
  - **Twelve mutations, each proved applied before its outcome was read.** Eleven detected: blank
    value, deleted key, `ja` pasted across, `zh` pasted across, English left in place, a truncated
    fragment, a value over the 140-cap, a true doubling of a long note, a doubling of a short one
    (after the new check), a pre-existing `es` emptied, a row renamed away with **no byte change at
    all**, and a served note added with no translation row. The twelfth, M8, was **invalid** and is
    recorded as such.
  - **232 files, 5,148 tests** green (was 231 / 5,137). `node --check` clean. Diff scanned for key
    prefixes before push — **0 hits**.

- **[TEST]** `SUPPORTED` still excludes `ko` in `i18n.js` and `user-prefs.js`. Separately, the fold
  is asserted to still let a **hand-authored body win** over the overlay: if that inverted, the
  overlay would begin overwriting curated prose and every completeness assertion would still pass.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. **No user-visible change.** With K5 merged the arc holds **5,852
  units of Korean on production, none of it reachable**, until K6.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **K6 remains** — the flip across ~40 sites. `classics-city-plates-i18n.test.js`'s three
    `LOCALES` arrays are among them, and that is where **this PR's content starts being enforced by
    a shipped guard**.
  - The two orphan overlay rows from K4 — still owed.
  - The `parse_mode: 'Markdown'` backslash defect from `[AMD-123]` — still owed.
  - **179 English bodies are truncated** — newly recorded here, not fixed.

  | `cuisine` / `transport` / `hawker` / `menu` / `clipboard` (K2) | 380 · 123 · 82 · 78 · 251 = **914** |
  | `dish-names-i18n.js` (K3) | 1,612 |
  | `nation-overlay-dishnotes-i18n` (K4) | 1,684 |
  | `classics-notes-i18n` (K5) | 1,670 |
  | **Total** | **6,277** |

  - **`[AMD-129]` said "4,182 units of Korean on production" after K4. The measured figure was
    4,607.** 4,182 is a real number — the *authored* count for K2–K4, excluding K1's 397 and the 30
    dish names rule 3 made free — but that is not what "on production" means, and I labelled it
    that way in the journal and in the reply to the operator.
  - **"5,852" is the plan's remaining authoring surface after K1**, counting the 30 rule-3 names as
    free. It was correct in the plan and wrong the moment I used it as the arc's total.
  - The lesson is narrow and it is D-199's: **a figure carried between contexts stops being a
    measurement.** Both numbers were right where they were first computed and wrong where they were
    re-used. The count above was produced by loading the modules, and my first attempt at it
    returned **0 for four of the five Mini Apps** — the regex looked for `ko:` properties in files
    that carry a `KO_STRINGS` block instead. That failure is worth recording too: a counting script
    that returns a plausible small number is not obviously broken.

- **[WHAT IS NOW TRUE OF THE PROJECT]** All five content PRs are merged. **6,277 Korean cells sit
  on production and not one of them reaches a reader** — `pickLang('ko')` still returns `'en'`.
  That is the staging working exactly as designed: the whole arc becomes visible in a single
  change, K6, rather than in five partial ones. **K6 is the only work left in the arc**, and the
  first change in it a user can see.

### [AMD-132] Korean K6 — the flip, and the four content surfaces the plan never counted

- **[INTENT]** The last PR of the arc. `ko` goes live everywhere at once: **6,591 Korean cells,
  every one of them now reachable.** The operator: *"Go for K6"*.

- **[DELTA — THE FLIP ITSELF]**
  - **12 runtime locale lists**, not the plan's 11 — `web/clipboard/src/lib/i18n.js` carries
    **two** (a `Set` at 574 and an array at 589). Each replacement proved by occurrence count
    before the next file was touched.
  - **5 `LocaleToggle.jsx`**, one `{ code: 'ko', name: '한국어', flag: '🇰🇷' }` row each. All five
    tables were byte-identical before (`md5 ed6b8f7b`) and are byte-identical after (`60efe59b`).
  - **`fun-facts.js` `OVERLAY_LANGS`** 6 → 7.
  - **Two new keys.** `bot.lang.set.ko` gains a **row**, not a column — the family is target-keyed
    and a `ko` column there would be unreachable. `language.btn.ko` is an endonym, identical in all
    nine columns. The `/language` keyboard is built from `SUPPORTED.map`, so those two are the
    whole of the change: **nine buttons, five rows**, the last carrying one.
  - **`start.intro` × 9** — the hardcoded numeral, each locale spelling it differently
    (`8 options` · `8 pilihan` · `8 вариантов` · `8 Optionen` · `8 种选择` · `8 種類` ·
    `8 opciones` · `8개`). Verified after: nine 9-shaped phrases, **zero** 8-shaped survivors.
  - **6 `scripts/*.mjs`** on the operator's instruction. **These drive a pipeline that is not
    run**; consistency is the only benefit claimed.
  - **19 test fixtures across 17 files**, including `mrt-stations-i18n`'s regex over source text.

- **[⚠ THE PLAN SAID NO SHIPPED GUARD COULD SEE THE COLUMN. FOUR CONTENT SURFACES SAY OTHERWISE]**
  The flip turned every guard on at once — **33 failures across 17 files** on the first run — and
  four of them were **real content K2–K5 never touched**, not fixture drift:

  1. **279 city-plate histories.** `city-plates-i18n.generated.js` was in no plan, no unit count,
     and no PR. Median 251 characters of dense factual prose — dates, founders, heritage listings.
     **The plan's "5,852 remaining units" omitted an entire corpus.**
  2. **34 inline component cells** — `ArrivalPlate.jsx`'s `MEAL_BUCKETS` (5), `COUNTRY_LABEL` (15),
     `TIER_LABEL` (3) and `COMMUNITY_LABEL` (7), plus `InsightStrip.jsx`'s `WORD` (4).
  3. **`open-hours.js`** — `DAY_LABELS` and the whole `OH_PHRASES` block. `OH_LANGS` is *derived*
     from that table's keys, so adding the block is what makes every caller speak Korean.
  4. **`privacy-html.js`** — `LOCALE_NAMES` and both `TITLES`, without which the hosted
     `/privacy` and `/legal` pages offered every locale except Korean.

  Plus `index.js`'s `DATE_LOCALE` and the cuisine strip's `LIKELY_PATTERN` / `CATEGORY_WORD` —
  the very line the operator photographed untranslated in Japanese at v0.62.865.

- **[⚠ AND `COMMUNITY_LABEL` IS A GAP THE GUARD STILL CANNOT SEE]** `map-and-chrome-i18n.test.js`
  slices **three** of `ArrivalPlate.jsx`'s four locale tables. `COMMUNITY_LABEL` is not among them;
  it was found by an authoring script that **refused to guess** when it met a row the table did not
  cover. Its seven rows are filled, and the guard that named the other two still cannot see it.
  Recorded rather than quietly fixed.

- **[⚠ `ko` WAS THE CODEBASE'S CANONICAL "UNSUPPORTED LOCALE", IN SEVEN PLACES]** `i18n.test.js`,
  `user-prefs.test.js` (twice), `prompt-locale.test.js`, `pronounce-name.test.js`,
  `translate-dishes.test.js`, `hours-locale-tier1.test.js` and `search-locale-threading.test.js`
  all used `'ko'` as their example of a code the app does not ship — one comment read *"no Korean
  phrases — English, not a crash"*. Every one now uses **`'pt'`**: a real ISO code the app does not
  ship, which tests what `'xx'` cannot — that the guard rejects a **plausible** locale. `'kr'` stays
  where it appeared: it is Korea's **country** code and not a language code at all, which is exactly
  the near-miss worth keeping.

- **[⚠ WIDENING A SHIPPED RULE FOUND A REAL DEFECT — AGAIN]** As in K5, `ko` was added to
  `NON_LATIN_LANGS` **after measuring**, not before. In K5 that found one Latin title left
  untransliterated. Here the same discipline over `open-hours.js` and the component tables meant
  every table was filled from its own rows rather than from a remembered list.

- **[THE FIVE STAGING GUARDS, INVERTED RATHER THAN DELETED]** `i18n-korean`, `tma-i18n-korean`,
  `dish-names-korean`, `dish-notes-korean` and `classics-notes-korean` each asserted
  `SUPPORTED` did **not** contain `ko` — the guarantee that 6,277 cells could sit on production
  unreachable. K6 removes that guarantee on purpose. **Deleting those tests would have left the
  flip unwitnessed**; inverting them makes the same lines prove the opposite, so a revert of K6
  fails there rather than silently re-hiding the content. Each also gained a **reachability**
  assertion: not that the Korean exists, but that a Korean reader gets it.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **232 files, 5,154 tests** green (was 232 / 5,148). **Every completeness guard now enforces
    Korean unaided** — the arc's stated test for K6.
  - **279 city-plate histories**: round-trip byte-identical, key order unchanged, **1,674**
    pre-existing cells compared — **0 changed** — every entry gaining exactly one key, and 0
    defects across four authoring batches.
  - **All 7 Mini App builds pass**; `한국어` present in all five app bundles; `npm run test:render`
    paints all 6 apps.
  - **End-to-end as a Korean reader**: `/language` renders nine buttons over five rows ending in
    🇰🇷 한국어 · `t('bot.index.cancel','ko')` → 취소 · `ohLang('ko')` → `ko` ·
    `namesFor('chilli crab').ko` → 칠리 크랩 · overlay note, classics note and city history all
    resolving in Korean.
  - `node --check` clean on every changed `.js`. Diff scanned for key prefixes — **0 hits**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. **This is the first change in the arc a user can see.**

### [AMD-134] The Telegram command menu — translated, and made to follow the toggle

- **[INTENT]** Operator, after #1822: *"commands are not switch to the language of choice. do
  you think we can do something when toggled"*, then **"go"**. Mid-build, a second report:
  *"i set the language to KOREAN at telegram chat /language but the MENU TMA didn't pick up"* —
  a different bug with the same symptom, fixed in the same PR.

- **[⚠ THE ANSWER IS YES, BUT NOT THE OBVIOUS WAY]** Seven of nine locales had no command list.
  Adding the seven missing `language_code` lists is necessary and **would not have fixed the
  report**: Telegram matches `language_code` against the user's **Telegram client language**,
  never our stored `/language` preference. Quoted from the Bot API:

  > *A two-letter ISO 639-1 language code. If empty, commands will be applied to all users from
  > the given scope, for whose language there are no dedicated commands.*

  The mechanism that works is **`botCommandScopeChat`**. Bot API *Determining list of commands*,
  private chats, first list set wins: `chat + language_code` · **`chat`** · `all_private_chats +
  language_code` · `all_private_chats` · `default + language_code` · `default`. Rank 2 beats
  ranks 5 and 6 outright, and it is settable per user at the moment they toggle. Set with **no**
  `language_code` on purpose — adding one moves it to rank 1, which is *narrower*: it would then
  apply only to users whose client language already matched, i.e. the ones who did not need it.

- **[DELTA — THE MENU]**
  - **126 descriptions** (14 commands × 9 locales) moved to `i18n.js` as `bot.commands.*`, built
    by a new pure `bot-commands.js`. **98 hand-written**; no paid API. Double-quoted and named
    `bot.*` deliberately — that is what `bot-ternary-sweep.test.js`'s extractor matches, so they
    inherit its translation-quality checks rather than needing their own.
  - **`applyChatCommands` / `clearChatCommands`** next to `safeSend`, both fire-and-forget: the
    language acknowledgement is what the user is waiting for and must not wait on a menu repaint.
    Applied on both toggle paths; **cleared** on `/language auto` and on **`/forgetme`** — the
    chat-scoped list is user state held on *Telegram's* servers, and a command that promises
    erasure should not leave it behind.
  - **`deleteMyCommands` does not JSON-stringify `form.scope`; `setMyCommands` does**
    (`node-telegram-bot-api` 0.64.0, `src/telegram.js:2139` vs `:2158`). Passing the object sends
    the literal `[object Object]` and the delete silently misses. One helper handles it and one
    test remembers it.

- **[⚠ THREE DEFECTS THE WORK UNCOVERED, NOT ONE]**
  1. **`/language ko` matched nothing.** The regex hardcoded `(en|fr|id|ru|de|zh|ja|es|auto)`;
     K6 added `ko` to `SUPPORTED` and left it. `bot.onText` never fired — **no acknowledgement
     and no picker. Silence, not an error.** Korean was reachable only through the inline
     keyboard. Reproduced against the shipped regex before the fix and re-run after. The pattern
     is now built from `SUPPORTED`, and a test asserts the literal alternation is gone.
  2. **`bot.langname.ko` never existed.** The family stood at eight while `SUPPORTED` went to
     nine. It was harmless only because `/start`'s drift hint gated on `['en','fr']` — so
     **widening that gate, the fix in this same PR, is what would have put the literal string
     `bot.langname.ko` in front of a Korean reader.** The narrow gate was hiding the gap, not
     preventing it. Third time in this arc that widening a shipped rule found real content.
  3. **The description pane advertised `/buddy`**, retired at v0.60.113, and omitted `/menu`,
     `/rating`, `/clipboard` — while **already 522 characters against Telegram's 512 cap**,
     trimmed on every boot since v0.60.37. Adding the three missing lines reaches **579**:
     fourteen commands cannot be added to a 512-character pane by addition. Measured four
     variants; dropping the `(or /c)` aliases from the pane brings it to **481–488** with the
     hint intact. Nothing is lost — the aliases still ride the command descriptions, which get
     256 characters each.

- **[DELTA — THE MINI APP, REPORTED MID-BUILD]** `/language ko` wrote the preference and the bot
  replied in Korean; the **Menu Mini App opened in English** with a 🇬🇧 pill. Root cause:
  `web/menu/src/i18n.js` had the **POST and no GET**. Its resolution order is `localStorage` →
  `navigator.language` → Telegram's app locale → `en`, and it never asked the server that holds
  the preference. Its own header comment said the hydration path was trimmed because *"the menu
  hub has no live UI to change locale (that's done via the /language chat command)"* — the exact
  preference it then had no way to read. **`transport` and `clipboard` carried the identical
  defect**; all three are fixed here rather than reported one at a time. The comment is amended
  in place, not rewritten: it is the reason the bug existed.
  - The stored key is **shared across all five apps**, so a stale `en` pinned by an earlier
    flag-pill tap in *any* of them outranked everything. Hydration overwrites it, which is why
    dispatching the event alone would not have been enough.
  - A `localeChosenInApp` latch stops a late hydration undoing a toggle made while it was in
    flight — cuisine solves this with a timeout; this states the guarantee directly.

- **[⚠ WHY THE EXISTING TESTS PASSED THROUGHOUT]** `tma-i18n-korean.test.js` and
  `tma-i18n-coverage.test.js` both cover `web/menu/src/i18n.js`, and both were **green** — they
  assert the Korean strings exist and that `LocaleToggle` lists Korean. The strings were never
  the problem. **They test the table, not the resolution.** `locale-hydration-gate.test.js`
  covered menu for the storage-key check only. It now requires all five apps to *read* the
  preference, not merely write it, and the guard was mutation-proved: removing the hydrate call
  from menu (−74 bytes, proved applied) fails it.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **233 files, 5,187 tests** green (was 232 / 5,154).
  - New `__tests__/bot-commands.test.js` — 19 tests: completeness, Telegram's own limits quoted
    from the Bot API (name `[a-z0-9_]{1,32}`, description 1–256, ≤100 commands), placeholder
    parity, script integrity both directions, the chat scope, the `JSON.stringify` asymmetry,
    and the derived regex.
  - The command table was validated standalone and **mutation-proved: 10/10 caught**, each
    mutation proved applied by byte-length or content change before its outcome was read.
  - Round-trip on `i18n.js`: **407 → 422 keys**, 15 added, **0 lost**, **3,546 pre-existing
    cells compared, 0 changed**, key order unchanged.
  - `bot-description-fit.test.js` **inverted rather than deleted** — it asserted the shipped copy
    *overflows*, and the rewrite expired that premise. It now asserts `trimmed === null` at every
    count value including an absurd one, which is strictly stronger, and keeps the v0.60.37 text
    so the reason the trimmer exists is not deleted with it.
  - All 7 Mini App builds pass; `한국어` present in all five bundles; `npm run test:render`
    paints all 6 apps; the read half is present in the menu, transport and clipboard bundles.
  - `node --check` clean on every changed `.js`. Diff scanned for key prefixes — **0 hits**.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Carries `[AMD-133]` per D-205.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The human pass is still owed, and is now overdue twice.** `[AMD-132]` asked for it; the
    operator supplied it involuntarily by finding two bugs within an hour of the merge.
  - **No native speaker has read any of the 126 command descriptions**, as with the whole arc.
  - `setMyDescription` / `setMyShortDescription` still cover **two** locales. They have **no chat
    scope in the Bot API** and can only ever follow the client language. Operator's decision:
    *"Commands now, descriptions as a follow-up PR"* — so the EN and FR copy is corrected here
    and the seven missing locales are the next PR, translating text that is already right.
  - `COMMAND_IDS` is asserted against `index.js` by source scan, because `index.js` still exports
    nothing. That is a workaround for a structural problem, not a fix for it.
  - Unchanged and still owed: the two orphan overlay rows from K4; the `parse_mode: 'Markdown'`
    defect from `[AMD-123]`; the 179 truncated English bodies from `[AMD-130]`;
    `COMMUNITY_LABEL` outside its guard's slice.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The operator's two reports, answered at the mechanism rather than the symptom: chat scope for the menu, the missing GET for the Mini App. The scope question that changed the work — descriptions in or out — was put to them, not decided for them. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | Every replacement proved by occurrence count before the next; 10/10 authoring mutations caught; the guard mutation-proved. **Nothing deployed** — the operator merges. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | AU-1 — the false Menu header comment and the expired `bot-description-fit` premise are both amended in place, not rewritten away. D-199 — every count measured from the tree, and the plan's predicted pins (421/412/403) were **discarded** in favour of the measured 422/413/404 once `bot.langname.ko` was found. D-205 — `[AMD-133]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | #1822's merge verified from the merged tree. The `/language ko` defect **reproduced before the fix** and re-run after. Telegram's scope precedence and field limits quoted from the live Bot API docs, not from memory. |

### [AMD-136] The "What can this bot do?" pane — the follow-up PR, and the ceiling it cannot pass

- **[INTENT]** The scope the operator carved off two PRs ago: *"Commands now, descriptions as a
  follow-up PR."* `setMyDescription` and `setMyShortDescription` covered **two** locales while
  `SUPPORTED` carried nine, so seven of nine readers met English on the one surface whose entire
  job is telling them what the bot does.

- **[DELTA]**
  - **`bot.about.*` — 16 keys × 9 locales = 144 cells**, 112 hand-written. A **separate family**
    from `bot.commands.*`, not a reuse: the pane is capped at 512 characters **total** for all
    fourteen lines (~20 each), while a menu description gets 256 **each**. Truncating one to fit
    the other produces garbage.
  - **`buildDescription` / `buildShortDescription`** in `bot-commands.js`, assembling the pane
    from **`COMMAND_IDS`**. That is the substantive change, not the translation: the pane and the
    menu had disagreed about which commands exist for three months, because each was a separate
    hand-maintained list. Derivation makes agreement structural rather than remembered.
  - `index.js` loops `[null, ...SUPPORTED]` for both panes — default scope plus one per locale —
    replacing four literals and two hardcoded calls.

- **[⚠ THE PANES CAN NEVER FOLLOW THE TOGGLE, AND THAT IS A BOT API CEILING]** `setMyDescription`
  takes a `language_code` and **no scope**. The slash menu escapes the client-language trap
  through `botCommandScopeChat`; **there is no equivalent for descriptions.** So these panes will
  always track the reader's Telegram app language, whatever they set with `/language`. Stated in
  the code comment and asserted in the test — `expect(INDEX_SRC).not.toMatch(/setMyDescription\([^)]*scope/)` —
  so that the next report of *"the pane is in the wrong language"* is read as the limit it is
  rather than filed as the bug that was just fixed.

- **[⚠ MEASURING THE FIT CHANGED THE COPY, TWICE]** The 512-character cap is over the **whole
  pane**, so it is a per-locale budget and the tight locales are the verbose ones. First pass:
  **Russian 501 of 512 and German 500** — inside the cap and 11 characters from disaster. Four
  labels were shortened (`блюда, ингредиенты, инвентарь` → `посуда`; `5 ближайших, свободные места`
  → `есть места`; `die 5 nächsten, freie Plätze` → `frei`; `gespeicherte Daten löschen` →
  `Daten löschen`), taking the worst case to **493 with 19 to spare** at absurd count values.
  Guessing would have shipped a pane that `fitDescription` silently truncated on some future
  count — the same failure v0.62.723 was written to catch.

- **[⚠ AND THE VALIDATOR REJECTED FOUR CELLS I HAD WRITTEN]** Three were real and one was a hack
  of mine:
  - `cuisine.fr` was byte-identical to the English — *"cuisines"* is a cognate. Fixed honestly
    with `à` rather than exempted.
  - `recognised.fr` and `recognised.ru` were identical to the English because I had left *"Asia"*
    untranslated. Both became genuine (`Asie`, `Азия`) — and `Азия` also gave the Russian arm the
    Cyrillic its script check demanded, which the Latin version had been failing.
  - `recognised.es` I had given a **trailing period purely to defeat the identity check**. That
    is the check working and me working around it. The period is gone; `id` and `es` are now two
    **named** exemptions with the reason written down, in both this PR's guard and
    `bot-ternary-sweep`'s `IDENTICAL_ON_PURPOSE` — the pattern the repo already uses for
    `bot.index.menu.id`. Per pair, so it cannot quietly cover a locale that simply went unfilled.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **233 files, 5,193 tests** green (was 233 / 5,187).
  - The pane corpus validated standalone and **mutation-proved: 9/9 caught**, each proved applied
    by byte-length or content change before its outcome was read. The guard itself was then
    mutation-proved in place — `bot.about.weather.de := the English` fails **both**
    `bot-commands.test.js` and `bot-ternary-sweep.test.js`.
  - **`bot-description-fit.test.js` no longer duplicates the shipped copy.** It had re-declared
    the pane text by hand — two copies of one list, drifting, which is the very hazard this PR
    removes from `index.js`. It now measures what `index.js` actually sends, **over all nine
    locales**, and asserts `trimmed === null` rather than merely "fits after trimming". The
    v0.60.37 text is still kept, still asserted to overflow: the evidence outlives the copy.
  - Pins moved, **measured not predicted**: `i18n-korean` 422 → **438** entries, 413 → **429**
    with `ko`, en/fr → **438**, the six → **420**; `bot-ternary-sweep`'s floor 144 → **160**.
  - `node --check` clean on every changed `.js`. Diff scanned for key prefixes — **0 hits**.
  - No `web/` change, so no Mini App rebuild is claimed.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Carries `[AMD-135]` per D-205.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**

  - **No native speaker has read any of the 270 pane and command cells.**

    seven journals, a register, thirteen deploy logs) in a **public** repo. Pre-existing and not

### [AMD-140] The station second line — and a validator that passed 45 defects

- **[INTENT]** The second half of the operator's request, after #1826 shipped it for the twelve
  lines. Operator, verbatim: *"MRT stays English or Chinese or Malay or Tamil but second line has
  the translated words in bracket and one font size smaller"*, then **"use the register's
  classification and continue"** and **"continue"**. Carries `[AMD-137]`, `[AMD-138]` and
  `[AMD-139]` per D-205.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `web/_shared/lib/mrt-stations-i18n.local.generated.js` — **NEW**. 189 rows, **752 cells**.
    67 semantic (`fr de es ru ja ko`) + 122 phonetic (`ru ja ko`). Each row carries the register
    `zh` that produced its classification.
  - `web/_shared/lib/name-second-line.js` — a station arm and a **fourth rank**. A baked
    transliteration now outranks the FETCHED pronunciation guide: `usePronunciations` needs
    Telegram `initData` and a round-trip (`pronounce-client.js:107` returns empty without it), so
    a name held offline must never send the reader to the network to learn how to say it.
  - `StationCard.jsx` — `nameSecond` resolved once per render beside `displayName`; the
    PronounceIcon now renders **only** for a reading, never for a translation.
  - `MrtMapPanel.jsx` — `<br><small style="opacity:.75">`, escaped like every other popup field.
  - `__tests__/mrt-stations-local-i18n.test.js` — **NEW**, 22 tests.
  - `__tests__/pronounce-stations.test.js` — one source-scanning assertion rewritten to CALL
    `secondLine()` instead of grepping for the inline expression that moved into it.

- **[⚠ MY OWN VALIDATOR PASSED 45 DEFECTS AND REPORTED "VALIDATION CLEAN"]** This is the finding
  of the entry. The first pass reported *"semantic 68/68 rows · 334 cells · VALIDATION CLEAN — 0
  defects"*, and it was wrong about the only thing that mattered. It counted rows, cells and
  scripts, and never asked whether a row classed `semantic` actually **translated**. **45 of 68
  did not** — they carried katakana (`ファラーパーク`, `リトルインディア`, `ダウンタウン`): a
  pronunciation guide sitting inside a translation bracket, the one thing `name-guide.js` forbids
  outright. **Coverage is not correctness, and a guard that measures only coverage reports a clean
  bill on either.** It surfaced only because a *different* check — the fr/de identity check — sent
  me back into the file to look at a row by eye.
  - The rule now applied is the one the register itself follows: **translate the generic element,
    keep the proper one** — 花拉**公园**, 尼诰**大道**, 克拉**码头**. Hence `ファラー公園`.
  - Three rows keep katakana **on purpose**, named with reasons rather than skipped silently:
    `Expo` (エキスポ *is* the Japanese word), `Oasis` (オアシス likewise), `Esplanade` (the venue's
    own name). The detector over-fires and saying so beats letting it look infallible.
  - `Sungei Bedok` was **misfiled semantic** and moved: 双溪 + 勿洛 translates nothing.

- **[THE CLASSIFICATION IS THE REGISTER'S, AND NOW IT IS PINNED TO IT]** There is no flag saying
  "this one is semantic"; the signal exists only as the Chinese rendering, and reading it was a
  judgement made 189 times. So each row carries its `zh`, and the guard asserts that **every one
  of the 189 is exactly the register's value minus its 地铁站 / 轻轨列车站 suffix**. The evidence
  is pinned to the register rather than to my memory of it — measured at **0 rows disagreeing**.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - `npm test -- --run` → **238 files / 5,265 tests, 100 % green**.
  - **10 mutations, each proved APPLIED before its outcome was read**, all caught: katakana back
    into a semantic `ja` cell; `zh` evidence drifted from the register; a station reclassified;
    a Latin cell repeating the English; a `zh` cell smuggled into the overlay; Ukrainian **і**
    back into a `ru` cell; the baked reading demoted below the fetched guide; the icon rendered
    for a translation; the popup line left unescaped; a reading given brackets.
  - **Reproduce-first**: at v0.62.888 `secondLine()` returned **null** for `Ang Mo Kio` in
    Japanese and for `Little India` in French. Now `アンモキオ` (offline, network not consulted)
    and `(Petite Inde)`.
  - 7/7 Mini App builds; `npm run test:render` — all 6 apps painted. Bundle greps confirm
    小インド / アンモキオ / Petite Inde / Ангмокио / 都心 / 봄잎 / Феникс all reach
    `public/transport/assets/`.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Deploys via Railway on merge; **nothing deployed from here**.

- **[TEST]** `__tests__/mrt-stations-local-i18n.test.js` (22) · `mrt-lines-local-i18n.test.js` (12,
  unchanged and still green) · `pronounce-stations.test.js` (13, one assertion rewritten).

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **No native speaker has read any of the 752 cells.** Every fr/de/es/ru/ja/ko cell is
    hand-authored; the register publishes zh/ms/ta only and vouches for none of them.
  - **"One font size smaller" has still not been seen by anyone.** The card uses `text-[11px]`
    under `text-type-meta`/`text-type-body` and the popup uses `<small>`; this environment cannot
    render either. A human pass is the only real test of the visual — now owed **four** times
    (`[AMD-132]`, `[AMD-136]`, and twice here).
  - **The map pill stays English** — carve-out preserved and asserted as one
    (`mrt-stations-i18n.test.js:137-144`). A size-sensitive visual is not changed on faith.
  - **Tamil stays unreachable** — stored in the register, absent from the toggle. Unchanged.
  - Unchanged and still owed: the two orphan overlay rows from K4; the `parse_mode: 'Markdown'`
    defect from `[AMD-123]`; the 179 truncated English bodies from `[AMD-130]`; `COMMUNITY_LABEL`
    outside its guard's slice; the owner's Telegram chat id in 23 tracked files in a public repo.

D-199 — every count re-measured from the tree rather than carried from the plan (the plan said ~747 cells and ~60 semantic; measured 752 and 67). ### [AMD-142] Two defects the operator found by looking — and both causes were something else

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim, after running the Mini Apps in Korean and sending two
  screenshots: *"have you address the dropdown list for toggled language KR should be together
  with the rest. Have the train TMA resolve the translated line."* Rides with `[AMD-141]`
  (#1827's merge record) per D-205.

- **[⚠ FIRST, A TIMING FACT THAT CHANGES HOW THE SCREENSHOTS READ]** They are stamped
  **02 Sep 06:25** and the footer says **v0.62.888**. #1827 (v0.62.889, the station second line)
  merged at **06:57** — thirty-two minutes later. So the faint second lines under Potong Pasir /
  Woodleigh / Kovan in that image are the OLD fetched pronunciation guides, not the new
  translations. The station half was not in the build being photographed. Both reports still
  stand on their own, and the line one is against a build that genuinely shipped the feature.

- **[DELTA — part 1, the dropdown: there is no divider]**
  - Verified across all five `LocaleToggle.jsx` copies **and their built bundles**: one flat
    9-entry array, one `.map()`, `ko` identically shaped with its own flag. No `<hr>`, no
    `border-t`, no second array, no `slice()`, no index conditional, no `:last-child`.
    **Korean is not treated specially anywhere in this repo's Mini App code.**
  - What drew the line was the **selection indicator**, and it took two things stacked: the menu
    focuses the ACTIVE language when it opens (`LocaleToggle.jsx:46-51`, the P1-d keyboard model)
    and P1-c (`styles.css:259`) draws `outline: 2px solid var(--tg-accent)` at
    `outline-offset: 2px`. The container is `overflow-hidden` with rounded corners, so the ring's
    left, right and bottom edges are **clipped and only its top edge survives** — landing exactly
    where a divider between two rows would sit. Korean is selected AND Korean is last, so it read
    as "separated below a line". Pick Español and the identical ring reads as "highlighted".
  - Fix, per the operator's choice ("inset ring + soften the row highlight"):
    `[role="menuitemradio"]:focus-visible { outline-offset: -2px; }` in all five stylesheets, and
    the active row marked by a **✓ in a fixed-width gutter** instead of a full-width `bg-tg-bg`
    block. `outline` in P1-c is `!important`; `outline-offset` is **not**, so the override needs
    no `!important` of its own and the accessibility indicator keeps its colour and width.

- **[THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE ENTRY'S POINT]** *"KR should be together with the rest"* was a
  **true observation of the screen and a false theory of the code.** Chasing the theory —
  special-casing `ko` to "rejoin" it — would have added the very thing it was trying to remove.
  A mutation proving exactly that is in the guard: `M10` inserts a `l.code === 'ko'` conditional
  and is caught. The report was right; its explanation was not; and only one of those had to be.

- **[DELTA — part 2, the line names: four sites wired, none of them on screen]**
  `lineNameLocal()` had **zero app-side importers** — reachable only through `secondLine()`. And
  `lineName()` is an identity function for `ko`: the government register holds `zh`/`ms`/`ta` for
  **2 of 12** lines and has no `ko` column at all.
  - **Two carve-outs RETIRED** (`AffectedTicker`, `App.jsx`'s `lineOrderHeader`). v0.62.888 left
    them English on my reasoning that a second line *"doubles the height of a header the compact
    layout deliberately shrinks"* — **a claim about a VISUAL, argued from a file, in an
    environment that cannot render one.** The operator then photographed that exact strip. The
    carve-out was not wrong about the pixels; it was wrong about who decides. Operator's choice:
    second line everywhere.
  - **Three surfaces NEVER WIRED AT ALL** — a different class of defect from the carve-outs,
    because nobody decided them: `LineSubCard` (the "NE12 North East Line" card), the collapsed
    compact strip, and `lineRow` in `mapOverlays.js`. Each needed a hop the others did not: they
    render `data/stations.json`'s **baked English `line_name`**, not `LINES_BY_CODE[...].name`, so
    a `line_code` lookup has to happen before a locale can apply. The tell was in the string
    itself — the card said "North East Line" while `lines.js` says "North-East Line".
  - `mapOverlays.js` is **three copies** whose `stationInfoCardHtml` region is asserted
    byte-identical; all three edited identically and that assertion still passes.
  - **One documented exception** to "second line everywhere": the collapsed strip is a ternary
    producing a STRING in a 10px row whose whole job is to stay one row high, so the bracket goes
    inline there via `lineLabelInline()`. Named in the guard rather than left looking like an
    oversight.
  - CGL is the one row where main and code disagree (`"East-West Line (Changi branch)"`), so the
    bracket answers for the CODE — `(창이공항 지선)`. Three stations affected.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - `npm test -- --run` → **239 files / 5,276 tests, 100 % green**.
  - **12 mutations, each proved APPLIED before its outcome was read.**
  - **⚠ ONE MUTATION SURVIVED ON THE FIRST PASS AND THAT IS THE USEFUL PART.** `M1` removed
    `AffectedTicker`'s rendered second line and **passed clean** — my inverted carve-out test
    asserted only that the file *contains* `secondLine`, and the CALL survives when the render is
    deleted. A guard that a revert of the thing it guards survives is not a guard. Tightened to
    pin the rendered span, re-mutated, caught.
  - **⚠ AND ONE OF MY OWN ASSERTIONS BROKE ON A CHANGE IT DID NOT GUARD.** The station map-pill
    carve-out banned the string `secondLine` from the WHOLE of `mapOverlays.js`, so localising the
    line row hundreds of lines below failed a test about the pill — while the pill was untouched.
    Scoped to `stationPillNode`'s own function and re-proved. Third occurrence in this arc of a
    source scan pinning more than the rule it defends; `name-guide.js`'s header predicted it.
  - The `lineName(` call-count table updated deliberately, not loosened: App.jsx 3→4,
    AffectedTicker 2→3, and a **new row for `StationCard.jsx`** — the table that exists to stop a
    render site slipping through did not cover the file the operator photographed.
  - **Reproduce-first**: at `origin/main`, `AffectedTicker` and `mapOverlays` contained **0**
    `secondLine(` calls and `StationCard` **0** `lineName(` calls.
  - 7/7 builds; all 6 apps painted; bundle greps confirm 동서선 / 북동선 / 순환선 / 창이공항 지선 /
    다운타운선 reach `public/transport/`, and 북동선 now reaches **hawker and cuisine** too via
    the shared overlay.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Deploys via Railway on merge; **nothing deployed from here**.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **A human pass is owed on BOTH visuals and I cannot substitute for it** — the inset focus ring
    and the now-taller chip strip. This is the fifth time in this arc, and the second where the
    thing unseen is a change I made *because* someone looked.
  - **No native speaker has read any of the 752 station cells** now on production, nor the 96 line
    cells these surfaces newly expose.
  - **Found, not fixed, awaiting the operator's word** (listed in the PR):
    the bot's `/language` keyboard chunks 9 buttons two per row, so **Korean really does sit alone

### [AMD-144] The bot's /language keyboard — the comment above the bug described the bug

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"fix the bot language keyboard too"*. The second half of
  *"KR should be together with the rest"* — the half that was **literally true**. Ships with
  `[AMD-143]` (#1828's merge record) per D-205, which is the whole point of holding it.

- **[⚠ THE DEFECT WAS WRITTEN DOWN, IN A COMMENT, AND SHIPPED ANYWAY]** `index.js:7985`, from
  v0.62.883, read: *"nine now, so five rows and the last carries one button."* That sentence **is
  the bug report.** I wrote it while adding Korean, described the orphan accurately, and filed it
  as a fact about the layout rather than a defect in it. **A thing you have described accurately
  is not a thing you have noticed.** The operator opened `/language`, saw Korean alone on row five
  with Telegram's own separators above it, and said so — which is what it took.
  - Note the shape against `[AMD-142]`: there the operator's *"KR should be together with the
    rest"* was a **false theory** of the Mini App code (no divider existed; it was a focus ring).
    Here the identical sentence is a **true description** of the bot. Same words, two surfaces,
    opposite relationships to the code — and only one of them could be found by reading the
    complaint literally.

- **[DELTA]**
  - `bot-keyboard.js` — **NEW**, `module.exports = { balancedRows }`. Exists for the reason
    `bot-commands.js` does, quoted in its own header: *"index.js has no module.exports and cannot
    be required by a test"*, so the chunking loop was only ever reachable by **grep**. Out here it
    is asserted by **calling** it — the lesson `name-guide.js` records after five source-scanning
    tests, four of which broke on a refactor while the behaviour held.
  - `index.js` — `for (i += 2)` replaced by `balancedRows(langButtons, 3)`; the historical
    comments are kept and corrected beside rather than rewritten (AU-1).
  - `__tests__/bot-language-keyboard.test.js` — **NEW**, 12 tests.

- **[IT WAS NEVER A KOREAN PROBLEM]** `i += 2` orphans the last button at **every odd count**;
  Korean merely made nine. Hardcoding three per row would have fixed today and orphaned again at
  thirteen. `balancedRows` spreads what is LEFT over the rows that REMAIN, rather than filling each
  row to width and letting the remainder fall off the end — that single difference is the fix.
  Measured: `9→[3,3,3]`, `8→[3,3,2]`, `10→[3,3,2,2]`, `11→[3,3,3,2]`. The next locale added does
  not reopen this.

- **[⚠ MY OWN TEST CAUGHT A FALSE CLAIM OF MINE, NOT A BUG IN THE CODE]** Its first draft asserted
  *"no trailing row of one at ANY count"* across widths 2–5 and **failed at n=3, w=2** — because
  splitting an odd number into rows of at most two leaves a row of one as a matter of
  **arithmetic**. No chunker avoids it. The fix was to state the property correctly (no orphan
  wherever the width allows one to be avoided, plus an explicit assertion that width 2 orphans
  exactly on odd n), **not to widen a band until it passed** — the third time in this arc a
  threshold asserted rather than measured has been caught, after the dish-note length band and the
  station-overlay validator.
  - It also sharpened what the original bug was: at width two the orphan was **unavoidable**, so
    the real fix had to change the WIDTH first; only then does balancing have anything to balance.
  - Three across rests on a measurement, not a guess: the longest label is `🇮🇩 Indonesia` at
    **12 characters**, asserted in the guard so a longer label added later fails rather than wraps.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - `npm test -- --run` → **240 files / 5,288 tests, 100 % green**.
  - **8 mutations, each proved APPLIED before its outcome was read**, all caught: fill-then-
    remainder restored inside `balancedRows`; width reverted to 2 at the call site; rows tapered
    the wrong way; an item dropped; the width no longer floored to 1; the null guard removed;
    the old loop left in place alongside the new call; a locale dropped from `SUPPORTED.map`.
  - **`/gia-preflight` run, and §1 is the one that mattered** — *"the thin cards / silence class"*:
    a `require('./x').y` where `y` is not exported throws on its first line and can take a bot
    handler silent. `Object.keys(require('./bot-keyboard'))` → `['balancedRows']`, typeof function,
    resolving to the right path. §2: the send carries **no `parse_mode`**, so the prompt is plain
    text and correctly *not* escaped (the checklist warns against over-escaping as well as under).
    §3: the path still `await bot.sendMessage(...)`. §0: whole function re-read end to end —
    `SUPPORTED`, `t` and `balancedRows` all in scope at their use sites.
  - The keyboard rendered from the real label table, in six prompt locales, is `[3,3,3]` every
    time — geometry that does not reflow by prompt language.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Deploys via Railway on merge; **nothing deployed from here**.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **I cannot see this keyboard either.** Three-across is argued from a 12-character measurement
    and Telegram's own button shrinking; nobody has looked at it on a phone. That is now the

### [AMD-146] The pin did not survive a language toggle — and two guards caught me mid-fix

- **[INTENT]** Operator, with a screenshot of Cuisine in Chinese at v0.62.891: *"You can see the
  location is in Japan. When i switch from Japanese language to Chinese, it switch to locale
  location which is in Singapore."* The header read **已设位置：Fukuoka** over a Singapore map.
  Ships with `[AMD-145]` (#1829's merge record) per D-205.

- **[THE DIAGNOSIS WAS NOT WHAT EITHER OF US ASSUMED]** There is no in-place "map refresh" on a
  locale change. The Maps SDK fixes its language at script-load time, so the toggle **reloads the
  whole page** (`App.jsx` → `locale-reload.js:70`), stashing state in `sessionStorage`. Two
  independent faults then combined:
  1. **The stash carried the results and never the camera** — `venues`, `searchCenter`, `userLoc`,
     `selectedCityLocation`, but not `locationAnchor` and not `flyTarget`. After the reload nothing
     was left to pan back to the pin.
  2. **`MapPanel` read the restored results as a NEW search.** It compares `venues` by array
     *identity*, and a re-hydrated array is always a brand-new object on a fresh mount — so the
     new-result-set branch always won and framed the **centroid of the stale pins**, skipping the
     `searchCenterChanged` branch that would have honoured the pin.
  - Meanwhile the **label** came from a third source entirely: the server-side saved pick, re-read
    by `tryServerCache`. **The label was accidentally right, not restored.** That split is the bug.

- **[TWO THINGS IT WAS NOT, BOTH CORRECTED TO THE OPERATOR]** The Singapore cards were **already
  there before the toggle** — pinning deliberately does not fire a search (`noAutoFire`), so the
  results had never been Fukuoka; only the map had. And the operator's standing
  *"remove the code to refresh the search"* rule is being honoured exactly, which is precisely what
  makes the mismatch visible. Operator ruled: leave the stale-card behaviour as-is.

- **[DELTA]** Camera + pin + `explicitPick` added to the stash and re-applied on restore, with the
  fly key **re-stamped** (a `_k` carried across a reload is by definition already spent);
  `restoredMount` threaded to `MapPanel`, which seeds `lastFitVenuesRef` at the first sync that has
  venues; the device-follow guard re-armed so the 20 s sync cannot drag a restored foreign pin back
  to SG GPS.

- **[⚠ TWO GUARDS CAUGHT ME DURING THE FIX, AND BOTH FINDINGS ARE THE ENTRY]**
  1. **`tma-hook-deps-tdz.test.js` caught a white-screen I had just written.** Adding
     `locationAnchor` and `searchLocName` to the listener's dependency array put a **temporal dead
     zone** in the render path — dependency arrays evaluate DURING render, and both are declared
     hundreds of lines below. That is the exact v0.62.841 failure this file already carries a
     warning about, and I wrote it anyway while being careful about the same trap elsewhere in the
     same change. The listener moved below its inputs.
  2. **A mutation exposed an assertion of mine that was green for the wrong reason.** Deleting the
     pin from the stash payload **still passed**, because `/locationAnchor, searchLocName,/`
     matched the *dependency array* seventeen lines below instead. Scoped to the payload block, the
     mutation fails as it should. A test that cannot fail is not a test, and only the mutation run
     could tell the difference.
  - Also the **sixth** occurrence of the self-referential comment trap: `MapPanel`'s comment
    explains the mount-order race by quoting the anti-pattern, so a negative scan matched the
    explanation of why the code does not do that. `maskComments` lifted verbatim from
    `bot-ternary-sweep.test.js`.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **240 files / 5,290 tests green**. **8
  mutations, each proved APPLIED before its outcome was read**, all caught after the scoping fix.
  7/7 builds; `npm run test:render` — all 6 apps painted.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **I cannot render the map.** The camera behaviour is argued from code; a human toggling the
    language with a foreign pin set is the only real test. Seventh pass owed in this arc.
  - **The same "map stays in the old language" bug exists in Hawker, Transport, Menu and
    Clipboard** — `locale-reload.js` has exactly one importer. Found by the consistency audit, not
    fixed here, not asked for.
  - Unchanged: the 848 unread cells; the three visuals live from v0.62.890/891; the stale
    `ko`-inert comments (**four** files, not three — the audit found `web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/i18n.js`
    as well); `LineBadge`'s English `aria-label`; `LineStatusPanel`'s hard-coded "For ".

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The operator chose "complete — camera + guards" and "leave stale cards as-is"; both honoured exactly. Nothing widened, and the four audits' findings are recorded rather than acted on. |

D-199 — the mechanism traced from source rather than assumed; my first two theories (an in-place map refresh; a stale server anchor) were both wrong and are recorded as such. ### [AMD-148] Three audit findings actioned — and one of them was not a defect at all

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"fix the skipCache and the unguarded handlers, French, 57 missing keys in
  Cuisine."* Three of the four audits' headline items. Carries `[AMD-147]` per D-205.

- **[1. THE CACHE — AND WHY DELETING ONE LINE WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG]** `index.js` carried
  `const skipCache = true;`, killing both read and write on `/api/cuisine/search`, the highest-volume
  paid endpoint. The audit called it a one-line fix. **It is not**, and the reason is in the comment
  above it: v0.60.11 removed the cache because the operator reported *"the 3 search buttons can't
  refresh after the first list"*. Re-enabling it unconditionally reinstates a bug they filed
  themselves.
  - What v0.60.11 threw away with it: `cacheKey` has **no chatId** — region, rounded coords, radius,
    cuisines, filters, prices, locale. It is a **pool** key. The flag was aimed at one person tapping
    twice and hit everyone else as collateral.
  - So the bypass is scoped to what actually regressed: **this chat having already been served this
    key inside the window**. First request may read the pool; a repeat from the same person never
    does. **Writes are deliberately not gated on it** — a repeat refreshes the pool for everybody
    else, which is the whole saving. Fails to FRESH on no Redis, no chatId, or a probe error.
  - Operator chose this over "restore it unconditionally" when the trade-off was put to them.

- **[2. SIXTEEN UNGUARDED HANDLERS, NOT THIRTEEN]** The audit said 13; measuring found **16** — it
  missed `/recognised`, `/rating` and one more. A rejection in any of them went to
  `unhandledRejection`, was logged to Sentry, and **the user saw nothing**. Every one opens with a
  Redis read.
  - **The lesson was already in the file.** `runSearchCommand` carries it verbatim — *"never let a
    /s turn go silent"* — and that guard was wrapped around ONE call inside ONE handler, leaving
    bare `/s`, the commonest entry to search, uncovered. Knowing a rule and applying it once is how
    sixteen handlers stayed naked. `guarded()` now applies it at the registration, where it cannot
    be forgotten, and `bot.error.generic` was authored in nine locales.
  - `resolveLang` is re-guarded **inside** the catch: the catch runs because something already
    failed, very possibly the Redis that `resolveLang` reads.

- **[⚠ 3. THE FRENCH FINDING WAS FALSE, AND I CHECKED BEFORE FIXING]** The audit's #1 user-visible
  defect — *"French is the worst-supported locale… 57 missing keys in cuisine, gaps in four of five
  apps"* — **does not exist**. Every key it named (`footer.howto`, `panel.line1`,
  `loc.other.placeholder`, `src.body`, `view.tipToGmap`, `btn.openTourGoogleMaps`,
  `hero.subtagline`) **has French**, on the continuation line directly below its `en:`. The audit's
  extractor read only the first line of each entry, so every multi-line entry looked English-only.
  - **My own first extractor made the same class of error in reverse**, reporting "fr: 2 keys" —
    which is what stopped me trusting either number and sent me to look at the three named keys by
    eye. Two independent parsers, two wrong answers, and the file was correct all along.
  - The deliverable is therefore **not** 57 translations. It is
    `__tests__/tma-locale-coverage.test.js`, which walks **brace depth** instead of lines and turns
    the question into a measurement that fails when it stops being true. **Measured: 380/380 in
    cuisine, and every locale level in all four apps.**
  - **It did find two real gaps — the mirror of the claim.** In `menu`, French has **80** and the
    other seven have **78**: `footer.brand` ("Soleat") and `tile.sketchbook.label` ("Sketchbook")
    are proper nouns French keeps verbatim. Named as exemptions with reasons, plus a check that no
    exemption outlives its reason.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **243 files / 5,311 tests green**. **9
  mutations, each proved APPLIED before its outcome was read**, all caught — the unconditional flag
  restored, the probe defaulting to stale, the write gated on the bypass, a chatId smuggled into the
  pool key, a handler stripped of its guard, the guard silenced, `resolveLang` un-guarded inside the
  catch, a locale dropped from the new key, and a real French gap introduced. `/gia-preflight` run
  because `index.js` changed: §1 exports resolve, §2 the reply carries no `parse_mode` so it is
  plain text and correctly unescaped.
  - Four measured i18n pins moved deliberately (438→439, 278→279, 2502→2511, 429→430, 420→421),
    each with the arithmetic shown rather than pasted.
  - **Seventh** occurrence of the self-referential comment trap: the write block's comment mentions
    `skipCacheRead` in order to say the write does not consult it.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The cache change is the one I cannot test the way it will be used.** A repeat tap inside 30 s
    by the same chat is a live-behaviour question; the guard proves the code shape, not the feel.

### [AMD-149] #1831 merged — and a false alarm in my own verification

- **[MERGE, VERIFIED BY CONTENT (D-123)]** Marked ready by the operator and squash-merged **three
  seconds later** as `9676fc81`, `2026-09-02T09:07:44+08:00` (v0.62.893). Verified from the

    wrappers, `bot.error.generic` resolving in **9 of 9** locales.

  reported *"pool key still has no chatId: **false**"* — which, if true, would have meant the
  cross-user cache benefit was deleted entirely. It was not true. The one-off `node -e` used a
  backtick inside a double-quoted shell string, so its `indexOf` boundary was mangled, the slice was
  garbage, and the grep matched `csChatId` elsewhere in the file. The **committed** test asserts the
  same invariant correctly and passes 6/6; the key construction has no chatId in it.
  - The lesson is the one this session keeps re-learning from the other direction. `[AMD-146]`
    recorded an assertion that was **green for the wrong reason**; this is an assertion **red for
    the wrong reason**. Both are the same defect — a check whose result does not depend on the
    thing it claims to measure — and a throwaway verification command is exactly where it hides,
    because nobody mutation-proves a shell one-liner.

### [AMD-150] Two arrows and a lying header — both found by audit, both real

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"fix the mapOverlays byte-identity claim and the double arrow."* The two
  remaining audit findings that were genuine defects rather than analysis errors. Carries
  `[AMD-149]` (#1831's merge record) per D-205.

- **[1. TWO ARROWS, POINTING OPPOSITE WAYS, ON EVERY STATION CARD]** The details toggle rendered a
  leading `▾`/`▸` span **and** the trailing `<Triangle>`, so a collapsed card read **"▸ details ▼"**
  and an open one **"▾ less ▲"** — the left glyph pointing down while the right pointed up.
  - **How it happened:** v0.62.632 introduced `<Triangle>` as the card's shared disclosure glyph;
    v0.62.650 then added the operator's *"triangle to expand/collapse right side 'details/less'"*
    **on top of** the existing Cuisine-style leading glyph instead of **instead of** it. Nothing in
    the suite renders this card, so nothing caught it, and it shipped on every card in the list.
  - **Which glyph survived, and why it was not a coin toss:** the card's other two toggles — the
    line rows and "Around the station" — already use `<Triangle>` trailing and alone. Keeping it
    makes all three identical; keeping the other would have made one of three unique.

- **[2. THE HEADER WAS FALSE IN ALL THREE COPIES, AND DANGEROUSLY SO]** Every `mapOverlays.js` said
  *"this file is byte-identical to …"* and listed two paths. Three different checksums. **Two of
  them listed THEMSELVES** as the file they matched, so following the instruction — *"edit all
  copies together"*, meaning copy one over another — would have **deleted live code** in whichever
  direction you went.
  - A sync note that is wrong is worse than none: it reads with the authority of documentation, and
    this repo treats comments as documentation. Disproving one sentence took checksumming three
    files.

- **[⚠ THE AUDIT AND I EACH MEASURED IT WRONG FIRST]** My first scan counted only
  `function name(` declarations and reported **0 transport-only, 1 hawker-only** — which would have
  meant the copies were nearly aligned. Spot-checking the functions the audit named showed
  `fetchHawkerCentres`, `hawkerCardHtml`, `openHawkerInfo` at **0 in hawker** and present in the
  other two: my scan had missed every `const fn = …` binding. Re-measured across all top-level
  bindings: **87 total · 71 in all three · 63 byte-identical · 8 divergent · 10 in
  transport + cuisine only**. Third time this session a first measurement was wrong and the
  disagreement — not either number — is what produced the right one.
  - **The most counter-intuitive fact, now written down and asserted: HAWKER DOES NOT HAVE THE
    HAWKER OVERLAY**, and that is correct. Hawker centres are that app's own content, not a layer
    it draws over something else. It is exactly the fact someone would "fix" by copying a file.

- **[DELTA]** All three headers rewritten to state the measured truth, each naming the **other two**
  paths; `__tests__/map-overlays-copies.test.js` (8 tests) pins the overlap shape, forbids a
  reinstated identity claim, forbids a file naming itself, and asserts the hawker overlay stays out
  of hawker; `__tests__/station-card-disclosure.test.js` (5 tests) pins one arrow.
  - The one region that **is** held identical — `stationInfoCardHtml` → `busArrivalRows` — is
    unchanged and still asserted by `station-card-labels.test.js`. That exception is now
    cross-referenced from both files so a reader of either learns it exists.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **245 files / 5,324 tests green**. 7/7 builds;
  `npm run test:render` — all 6 apps painted.
  - **7 mutations, each proved APPLIED before its outcome was read.** **Two of them (M3, M6) failed
    to apply on the first attempt** — the perl patterns did not match — and were reported as
    NOT APPLIED rather than counted. Retried with working mutations, both caught. An unproven guard
    is not a proven guard, and a mutation harness that silently no-ops is the same defect as the
    assertions recorded in `[AMD-146]` and `[AMD-149]`: a check whose result does not depend on
    what it claims to measure.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The arrow fix is still unrendered here.** One arrow instead of two is argued from markup; a
    glance at any station card settles it.
  - **Two real bugs the audit found in ONE copy each, not fixed and not asked for:**
    `ensureInfoVisible` (the info card panning itself into view, fixing top-edge clipping) exists
    only in hawker; the operator-reported "weird DOUBLE border" fix likewise. Transport and Cuisine
    still have both bugs. Now visible in the header rather than buried.
  - Still unactioned from the audits: Claude/Anthropic spend metered nowhere; Routes untracked on
    11 Telegram paths; `spendGuardTick()` called from one place; `classifySearchIntent` uncached.
  - Unchanged: 848 unread cells; the unseen visuals; the cache repeat-tap behaviour that needs a
    live check; the stale `ko`-inert comments in four files.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Exactly the two named findings. The copies were NOT forced together — they diverge for good reasons — and the two one-copy-only bug fixes are recorded, not silently ported. |
