### [AMD-152] ⚠ The Korean reader in Seoul gets NO Korean — found by a question, not a bug report

- **[INTENT]** Operator asked a **design** question, not for a fix: *"If my language is set to
  Korean, my search place is Korea, Seoul — how would the eatery name's language be prioritised,
  should it be Korean first line and device's language (English) be second line in bracket…
  similarly for address."* Investigating it turned up a live defect. **No code was changed** — a
  question was put back to them and is unanswered.

- **[THE DEFECT]** For a Korean reader searching Seoul, the app shows **English name, English
  address, and no Korean anywhere.** Two mechanisms combine:
  1. **Places is pinned to English.** `index.js:10209` and `:10221` both read
     `languageCode: csLang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'`. **French is the only locale ever threaded.**
     So the primary name and address come back in English regardless of the reader's language.
  2. **The Korean second line is then deliberately suppressed.** `local-name.js`'s RULE B skips the
     local-name fetch when the local language equals the reader's display language. Measured:
     `localLangForCountry('KR', 'ko') → null`, while `('KR','en') → 'ko'` and `('KR','fr') → 'ko'`.
  - **So an ENGLISH reader in Seoul sees `Gwangjang Market (광장시장)` and a KOREAN reader sees
    `Gwangjang Market`.** The one person who reads Hangul is the only one who does not get it.

- **[WHY THE RULE BECAME WRONG]** RULE B is sound *if* the primary is already in the reader's
  language — do not print 광장시장 twice. That held when the display language drove the Places
  request. It stopped holding and nothing re-checked: with Places pinned to `en`,
  "display == local" now means the reader gets the local script **zero** times, not twice.
  - Same shape as `[AMD-142]`'s station overlay and `[AMD-146]`'s camera: **a gate whose premise
    expired while the gate stayed.** Third instance this session.

- **[WHAT WAS PUT BACK TO THE OPERATOR, AND WHY]** Their proposed rule is right, with one
  refinement: the discriminator should be the **script the reader can read** (what `name-guide.js`
  already reasons about), not the device language — otherwise a Korean reader on an English phone
  is treated as an English reader. For the address the existing rule already settles it: *"keep the
  form something downstream depends on"* — the Korean address is what a taxi driver reads.
  - **Not started**, because the full fix threads the real locale into `languageCode` for **nine
    languages on every search**, changing what Google returns app-wide — including the cuisine-type
    strings `index.js:8873` already strips for French. That blast radius is beyond the question
    asked and cannot be previewed here. Offered as either its own planned piece or a narrower
    RULE B + address fix; awaiting their choice.

- **[STATUS]** INVESTIGATED, NOT FIXED. No code changed.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** All of `[AMD-150]`'s, plus this one now named: the Korean-reader-in-Korea case is
  live on production and will stay so until the operator picks a scope.

### [AMD-153] Self-correction — I stamped an anchor five minutes in the future

- **[WHAT HAPPENED]** `[AMD-151]`/`[AMD-152]`'s serial-state rotation was written with
  `last_anchor_time: 09:29 SGT`. The fetched time was **09:24:22**. I composed the heredoc
  containing the anchor **before** running `date`, and then did not reconcile the two when the
  real value printed five lines above the result.

- **[WHY IT MATTERS MORE THAN FIVE MINUTES]** This is the precise failure D-203 exists because of.
  `CLAUDE.md` records that `journal-0_62_723` `[AMD-3]` was stamped `07:55` while reporting a merge
  git timestamps at `07:59:45` — *"a verification dated before the event it verified"* — and that
  its "correction" moved the entry **away** from the truth while announcing increased rigour. Mine
  errs in the other direction, a timestamp **ahead** of the moment it claims, which is the same
  defect: an anchor that is asserted rather than measured.
  - §2 of `CLAUDE-protocol.md` is one line long — *"Time — fetched, not guessed"* — and I ran the
    fetch. The failure was not skipping the measurement; it was **writing the value down before
    taking it and never going back**. That is worse than not measuring, because the transcript
    shows a `date` call and looks compliant.

- **[CORRECTED]** `last_anchor_time` → `02-09 '26 09:24 SGT`; `last_anchor_source` now records
  `max(sensor 09:24:22, PR #1832 merge 09:22:24)` = **sensor wins by 118 seconds**, computed with a
  deterministic tool per reporting-protocol rule 5, and carries `CORRECTED_from_0929` so the
  original error is legible rather than tidied away. `iso8601` and `utc` corrected to match.
  - The prior values are **not** rotated into the `_prior100` slots: they were never a real reading
    and preserving them as history would be preserving a fiction. The correction note in
    `last_anchor_source` is where the fact lives, per AU-1's "preserve the removed content" applied
    to a value that was wrong rather than superseded.

- **[STATUS]** Corrected in the working tree, shipping with the next PR.

### [AMD-154] The guard that let me through, and the Korean reader who got no Korean

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"plan the fix for §2,869·A , §2,869·B"* — a process defect I committed and
  a product defect their own design question uncovered. Carries `[AMD-151]` (#1832's merge record)
  per D-205.

## Part A — a guard sized to the worst failure could not catch the ordinary one

- **[THE DEFECT]** `[AMD-153]` corrected an anchor stamped `09:29 SGT` when the sensor read
  `09:24:22`. What needed fixing was that **`serial-state-anchor.test.js` already carried a future
  check written for exactly this**, and it passed. Its tolerance is 300 seconds; my error was
  **278**. It fit underneath by 22.
  - The comment reasoned *"five minutes is far below the 45 that got through"* — calibrated against
    `[AMD-87]`, the one incident on record. **A tolerance sized to the worst observed failure cannot
    catch the ordinary one**, and typing an anchor a few minutes ahead is the ordinary one; 45
    minutes was the outlier that happened to be noticed.

- **[THE FIX — stop asking the clock, ask the evidence]** `last_anchor_source` already states the
  sensor reading the writer claims to have taken. Comparing the anchor to **that** needs no clock,
  no tolerance and no knowledge of CI's skew: both fields are written by the same hand in the same
  commit, so a disagreement is a mistake by construction. Four assertions added:
  the live source must name a sensor reading (`[AMD-87]`'s failure — its source had **no number in
  it at all**); the anchor must equal it; the stated `by_N_seconds` must actually compute; and a
  can-it-fire test replaying both real defects.
  - **Live value only for the first two**, deliberately: **76 of 122** historical `_prior` entries
    carry no sensor value because the convention post-dates them. A guard that must be suppressed to
    pass is a guard nobody keeps.
  - The existing future check is **kept, not tightened** — it covers a case the evidence check
    cannot (both fields wrong together), and tightening it to 278 s would pit it against real skew.
    Two checks with different blind spots beat one with a better-guessed constant.

- **[⚠ AND IT FOUND TWO MORE ON ITS FIRST RUN]** The arithmetic assertion failed immediately on two
  historical entries, **both mine**, both off by exactly one second — `stated 80, actual 79` and
  `stated 494, actual 493` — in a field that reads `computed_with_a_deterministic_tool`. They were
  computed mentally. Corrected, with `CORRECTED_from_…` left in place so the error stays legible.

## Part B — the Korean reader in Seoul got no Korean

- **[SCOPE, AS THE OPERATOR CHOSE IT]** *Narrow now, the Places locale change as its own PR*, and
  **app language wins** — the toggle, not the phone.

- **[THE DEFECT]** Two mechanisms combined. Places is pinned to `csLang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'`, so
  every non-French reader gets an English primary; **RULE B** then skipped the local-name fetch
  when the local language matched the reader's display language. Measured:
  `localLangForCountry('KR','ko') → null` while `('KR','en') → 'ko'`. **An English reader in Seoul
  saw `Gwangjang Market (광장시장)`; a Korean reader saw `Gwangjang Market`.**
  - A gate whose premise expired while the gate stayed — **the third this session**, after the
    station overlay (`[AMD-142]`) and the reload camera (`[AMD-146]`).

- **[WHY RETIRING RULE B LOSES NOTHING]** **RULE A already performs the real check** — `ln !== v.name`
  — against the string that will actually be rendered rather than a language code standing in for
  it. It is also durable: on the day the Places request carries the reader's locale and the primary
  comes back as 광장시장, RULE A suppresses the duplicate by itself. RULE B was an expired proxy for a
  check that was already there.

- **[⚠ 1,186 MICHELIN ROWS HAVE CARRIED `addressLocal` SINCE v0.62.824 AND NOTHING EVER RENDERED IT]**
  Set by `michelin-data.js:389` from the curated `addressJa`/`addressZh`/`addressKo` columns, read by
  nothing in `venue-templates.js` or anywhere in `web/`, pinned only by a count in a test. A Korean
  reader looking at a Seoul Michelin venue has had the Korean address in the payload, unshown. Both
  surfaces render it now — **no API call, no cost, no cache namespace**.
  - And for every other foreign-script venue it now arrives **free**: the FieldMask went from
    `displayName` to `displayName,formattedAddress` on the Place Details call `local-name.js` was
    **already making**. Details is billed per call, not per field.

- **[THE CALL IS CACHED NOW, BECAUSE THE FIX ADDS CALLS]** Retiring RULE B means readers viewing
  their **own** country fetch where they previously did not. `fetchLocalPlace` caches on
  `(placeId, langCode)` for 30 days, matching `translate-name.js`, and caches the **negative** too,
  as `pronounce-name.js` does. Shipping the fix without this would have been a cost regression
  wearing a fix's clothes.

- **[A PRECEDENCE FOR THE ADDRESS, WHICH HAD NONE]** `web/_shared/lib/address-guide.js` —
  `addressLocal` outranks the street pronunciation, **for everyone**: a reader who can read the
  script wants the authoritative form, and a reader who cannot still wants it, because it is the
  line they hold up to a driver. A transliteration helps you SAY a street; it is not an address.
  Exactly one renders — the operator's *"Both means TWO"*. The English line is never displaced.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **246 files / 5,341 tests green**. 7/7 builds;
  all 6 apps rendered. **15 mutations across the two parts, each proved APPLIED before its outcome
  was read**, all caught — including both defects replayed (an anchor stamped ahead of its own
  source; RULE B reinstated).
  - **Two source-scanning tests broke while the behaviour held** — `both-languages-rule` and
    `locale-coverage-and-address` pinned the inline `streetSay && streetSay !== addrStreet` that
    moved into `pickAddressGuide()`. **Third time in this arc**, and exactly what `name-guide.js`'s
    header says these modules exist to prevent. Rewritten to assert by CALLING.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The Korean PRIMARY line does not arrive in this PR.** The reader gets the Korean name and
    address **in the bracket**; the primary stays English until the Places `languageCode` change
    lands. That is its own PR by the operator's choice, and the blast radius is now mapped: it
    breaks `cuisine-geo-scope.js`'s `/singapore/i` and `/johor/i` gates, `venue-filters.js`'s
    `isBuildingItself`, `humaniseRestaurantType`'s generic-label suppression, and the dish-evidence
    tagging at `index.js:17720`.
  - **⚠ The bot still renders THREE name lines.** `venue-templates.js:220-228` pushes `nameLocal`,
    `nameReading` and `namePronounce` independently, with no precedence and no `pickNameGuide`. A
    live divergence from *"Both means TWO"* on the bot side only. Found by the trace, not fixed,
    not asked for.
  - `country-text-match.js` — the one module that anticipates non-Latin Places output, written
    after the operator hit this exact failure on KR/JP — **is dead code**, referenced nowhere but
    its own test.
  - **I cannot render the card.** A Korean reader's Seoul search is the only real test.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Both parts exactly as scoped; the Places `languageCode` change deliberately excluded per the operator's choice, with its blast radius mapped rather than guessed. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 15/15 mutations caught, each proved applied first. Nothing deployed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | AU-1 — RULE B's reasoning is preserved and answered in place, not deleted; the future check is kept with its limitation recorded. D-199 — the anchor written AFTER the fetch this time, delta computed by tool. D-205 — `[AMD-151]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The two off-by-one historical values found by the new check and corrected. The two broken source-scans recorded rather than quietly rewritten. |

### [AMD-155] The Places `languageCode` change I deferred, and a sub-menu the operator photographed

- **[HDR]** v0.62.896 · 02-09 '26 10:15 SGT · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · DRAFT

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do the places languageCode change. Sub-menu - Not
  translated? See pic"* — two things, with two screenshots attached. The first they had
  **explicitly deferred one PR earlier** (*"Narrow now, Places as its own PR"*), and I had
  mapped its blast radius into `[AMD-154]`'s `[KNOWN GAPS]` rather than guessing at it. This
  is that PR. The second is a live defect their screenshots show plainly: the Cuisine
  sub-menu drew its **headers** in Korean — 남아시아, 유럽, "요리 ›" — with **every cuisine
  name beneath them still in English**. South Indian, Bengali, Nepalese, Scandinavian,
  Mediterranean, Georgian, Uzbek. Twenty-four English words under two Korean ones.

- **[DELTA]**

  **Part A — one mapping instead of six ternaries.**

  - **NEW `places-language.js`.** `placesLanguage(lang)` — the single mapping from the reader's
    app language to Google's `languageCode`. It replaces six hand-written
    `lang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'` ternaries: `index.js` `searchVenuesByDish`, the two Michelin
    Places calls, and `pipeline.js`'s `discover()`. French was the only locale ever threaded,
    because French was the locale that got complained about first. **This is O-305's shape for
    the fourth time this arc**: one datum, several call sites, each deciding for itself.
  - `zh` is pinned to **`zh-CN`**, not left as a bare `zh`. Google resolves a bare `zh` by
    region, which would have put Traditional venue names next to the bot's own Simplified
    chrome (新加坡, 谷歌地图).
  - **The `michelin:place:v2:<lang>` cache key widened in the same edit.** It is the only thing
    scoping a blob whose `displayName`, `formattedAddress` and `editorialSummary` are all
    language-specific. Widening the fetch and leaving the key at fr-or-en would have served a
    Korean reader the French blob — a cache-poisoning bug wearing a translation's clothes.

  **What it costs, and the two compensations.** Places returns **ONE language per call** —
  asking for Korean *removes* the English string, it does not add a second one. Four downstream
  checks read those strings as if they were English. All four are named in
  `places-language.js`'s header, because a cost list that omits one omits the one nobody
  re-derives.

  1. **`cuisine-geo-scope.js`** — `/singapore/i` and `/\bjohor\b/i` over `area + name`.
     A Korean reader's Johor venue is addressed 말레이시아 조호르; the gate stops matching **in
     the quiet direction** — it lets venues *through*, which reads as a slightly wider search
     rather than as a broken filter. **Compensated**: both regexes moved into
     `places-language.js` with all nine locales, and **each original's anchoring preserved
     exactly** — `singapore` unanchored (so "Singaporean Grill" still matches) and `johor`
     word-bounded (so "Johorean" still does not). Both quirks are arguably wrong; repairing
     either inside a translation change would be a scoping change nobody asked for.
  2. **`humaniseRestaurantType`** — its step (4) hides a bare "Restaurant"/"Food" rather than
     showing a useless label. Its generic-word set was **English**, so it would have asked an
     English set whether 음식점 was generic, got "no", and shown a Korean reader the word
     "eatery" as if it meant something. **Compensated**: the language-independent
     `primaryType` **enum** is consulted before the localised display string, plus a nine-locale
     word set. `primaryTypeDisplayName` *is* the display of `primaryType`, so enum-generic and
     label-generic are the same fact — which is why enum-first is not a narrowing.
  3. **`venue-filters.js` `isBuildingItself`** — matches English building words. **NOT
     compensated, and said so**: every pattern is a Singapore building name, and Singapore's
     Places names come back in English for every locale we ship. Recorded, not guessed at.
  4. **dish-evidence tagging** (`index.js`) — `_norm(v.name).includes(needle)` against an
     English dish slug. **Degrades rather than breaks**: a venue that stops matching by NAME
     falls to review evidence and then to "ask first", which is the ladder's designed bottom
     rung. It does mean a Korean reader can rank a dish-named venue lower than an English
     reader does, on the same search. Recorded as a degradation, not fixed.

  - **NEW `venue-type-label.js`** — `humaniseRestaurantType` and its two helpers, lifted out of
    `index.js` **unchanged apart from the require**. It moved because `index.js` has no
    `module.exports`, so nothing in it can be tested by *calling*; and this change made the
    function locale-sensitive, so "does a Korean 음식점 get hidden?" became a question worth a
    real test rather than a regex over a file. `bot-keyboard.js` was carved out at v0.62.891
    for exactly this reason.

  **Part B — the sub-menu, and the copy nobody was maintaining.**

  - `web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/cuisine-i18n.js` carried 95 rows (69 `NAMES` + 26 `VENUE_TYPES`)
    across seven non-English locales and **no `ko` column at all** — 95 rows, 95 missing.
    That is the whole of the operator's screenshot.
  - Measuring it found a second thing they had not asked about: `web/clipboard/src/lib/cuisine-i18n.js`
    was a **hand-kept second copy** of the same table, stuck at `fr/zh/ja/es` with **no
    `VENUE_TYPES` table at all**. So a Russian reader's clipboard chip read English while the
    same chip in Cuisine read Cyrillic. The two `NAMES` tables were **byte-identical on the four
    locales both carried**, same 69 slugs in the same order — measured, not assumed — so the
    merge loses nothing.
  - **NEW `web/_shared/lib/cuisine-i18n.js`** — one table, both apps. Both app-local files are
    now re-exports at their original paths, so no import churn and a grep for the old location
    lands on the reason. **One table cannot drift from itself.**
  - **95 Korean names authored by hand**, no paid API. Bare country/region nouns, matching what
    `zh` and `ja` already do, because the drawer header supplies the word 요리 and the chip must
    not repeat it.
  - The file's header claimed *"(fr / zh / ja / es)"* and *"id/ru/de still fall back to English
    for now"*. **Both had been false since v0.62.850.** Corrected, with the history kept.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **247 files / 5,355 tests green.** 7/7 Mini App builds; all 6 apps rendered (95 / 76 / 126 /
    71 / 40 / 77 nodes).
  - `node --check` on all eleven changed `.js` files. Version bumped 0.62.895 → 0.62.896.
  - **13 mutations, every one proved APPLIED by `cmp` against a pristine copy before its outcome
    was read, all 13 caught.** Including both defects replayed: `placesLanguage` reverted to
    fr-or-en (the eight-locale collapse), and the enum-first ordering reverted (Korean 음식점
    shown as a type line).
  - The anchor was written by a script that **fetches the sensor itself**, so the stamped value
    cannot be one typed from memory before the fetch — `[AMD-153]` was exactly that. The
    `[AMD-154]` guard passes on it, 13/13.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run`, `npm run build`, `npm run test:render`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The two uncompensated costs above are real and shipped.** `isBuildingItself` and
    dish-evidence tagging both weaken for a non-English reader. Neither is invisible: the first
    is inert in Singapore by construction, the second degrades one rung down a ladder that has
    a designed bottom. Both are written into `places-language.js`'s header rather than only here.
  - **`placeauto:v2:` and `placeresolve:v1:` still have no lang dimension** and are still
    requested in English (`index.js`). That is the *correct* conservative choice — they resolve
    a typed query to a place, and the query is what the user typed — but it means autocomplete

- **[HDR]** Merge record for v0.62.895 · squash `822e346a`, merged 02-09 '26 **10:08:52 SGT**

  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | RULE B retired, RULE A left as the real check | `local-name.js` — `localLangForCountry` survives only as an unused-marked function | ✅ |
  | the Place Details call is cached, negatives included | `LOCAL_TTL_S = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60`, `NEG` sentinel written on miss | ✅ |

  | the two off-by-one `by_N_seconds` values corrected | `by_79_seconds` and `by_493_seconds` both present | ✅ |

- **[DELTA]** ⚠ **The interesting part is not the merge, it is what happened at the push.**
  #1833 merged **while `[AMD-155]` was being built on top of the pre-merge branch**, so the
  branch tip became content-identical to `main` without being an ancestor of it. A
  fast-forward push would have **succeeded** and appended `[AMD-155]`'s commit to a
  **closed** pull request, where it would have sat looking pushed and never reached `main`.
  `__tests__/prepush-guard.test.js` refused it and said exactly that. The branch was
  restarted from `origin/main`, the commit replayed by cherry-pick — clean, 15 files — and
  force-pushed with a lease.

  This is the failure mode the guard exists for and it is worth writing down rather than
  treating as routine: **the push would not have errored.** Nothing would have looked wrong
  until someone asked why a merged PR did not contain the change.

- **[STATUS]** PROD — #1833 is on `main` and deploys from there via Railway.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** #1833's own gaps stand and are restated in `[AMD-155]`: the bot still
  renders three name lines; `country-text-match.js` is still dead code — and `[AMD-155]` has
  now made the non-Latin Places output it was written for actually arrive; and **no Korean
  reader has seen any of this.**

### [AMD-157] "Have we complete the translation challenges" — nothing was missing, and four things were being thrown away

- **[HDR]** v0.62.897 · 02-09 '26 11:44 SGT · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · DRAFT

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"before you build §2,911·C. Have we complete the
  translation challenges"* — asked as a gate on starting prediction work, which is the right
  order and is why the audit came first.

- **[DELTA]**

  **The audit, and what it did NOT find.** 32 translation guard suites, 931 tests, all green.
  Measured by CALLING each table's own accessor rather than grepping it: bot `i18n.js` 279
  keys × 8 locales, Mini Apps 380 / 82 / 80 / 123 / 251 keys × 8, and the content tables —
  1,612 dish names, 1,670 classics notes, 1,684 dish notes, 279 city plates, 279 plate names
  × 8, 95 cuisine chips. **Not one missing string.**

  **What it found instead were four sites that DISCARD translations already in the repo**, and
  the tell is that fixing all four broke **zero** existing tests. A completeness guard can
  only see the table. Three of the four defects live in a cache key.

  1. **⚠ `michelin:enrich` was poisoning across locales, live.** `index.js:10704` passes the
     full `csLang` to `narrateMichelinVenues`, which localises the LLM prompt for all nine
     locales (v0.62.839). `index.js:10656` keyed the result
     `michelin:enrich:v2:${csLang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'}`. So a Korean reader's Korean vibe
     line was **written into the bucket named `en`** and served to the next English reader,
     and the reverse. Seven locales in one key. **Not a missing translation — a translation
     delivered to the wrong person.**
     Fixed with `narrationLang()`, added to `prompt-locale.js` — the module that owns the
     decision — so the key and the prompt use the *same predicate* and cannot hold two
     opinions about what language a blob is in. `prompt-locale.js`'s own header names seven
     sites of this shape; this was the eighth, and the only one that was not a prompt.
     **⚠ And the first draft of the fix was half a fix.** It added the missing codes and left
     `v2`, which routes future writes correctly while an English reader goes on being served
     a week of Korean-filed-as-English for the whole TTL — a commit that says "fixed" and
     isn't. **Bumped to `v3`**, which retires the contaminated bucket outright. It discards
     the `:fr:` blobs too, which were always correctly scoped; that costs one re-narration
     and is the cheaper mistake.
  2. **The `cuisine:pool` key** (Codex P2 on #1834, verified). No language segment at all,
     caching whole venue objects that v0.62.896 had just made language-specific. Now
     `cuisine:pool:{chat}:{hash}:{placesLang}:v{n}`, with `poolLanguages()` and
     `cuisinePoolKey()` living in `places-language.js` so a test can CALL them.
     **The language goes on the pool key and deliberately NOT into `computeCriteriaHash`**:
     the seen-set and variant index are about *which venues*, identified by place id, which
     no language changes. Folding it into the hash would hand a language-toggling reader a
     fresh seen-set and re-show venues they had already scrolled past.
  3. **66 tourist explainers had no `ko`.** K6 flipped Korean on across every locale TABLE it
     could find; this one is a generated overlay merged at load, so no completeness guard
     reached it and no toggle listed it. **66 hand-authored Korean explainers**, no paid API,
     with the file's Gemini provenance header corrected to record the split.
  4. **And the bot never showed any of them anyway.** `index.js:9361` read
     `touristExplainer[lang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en']`, while `nation-overlay.js:3166` merges
     id/ru/de/zh/ja/es onto all 66 at load. **396 authored strings, loaded on every boot,
     never once rendered.** Now `te[lang] ?? te.en` — `??` not `||`, so a deliberately empty
     explainer stays empty instead of falling back.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **248 files / 5,370 tests green.** `node --check` on all five changed `.js`. Version
    0.62.896 → 0.62.897.
  - **13 mutations, all caught**, each proved APPLIED by `cmp` against a verified snapshot
    before its outcome was read — plus a deliberate no-op that the harness correctly reported
    as **NOT APPLIED** rather than counting.
  - **⚠ AND THE MUTATION HARNESS ITSELF HAD THE DEFECT IT EXISTS TO CATCH.** An unexpanded
    `$F` in the first run meant `cp` never took the pristine copy, the mutations were applied
    to the live file, the restore also failed — and four outcomes printed as **"caught"**
    having never been measured against a restored baseline. It was found because `git status`
    showed the file dirty, not because the harness said anything. Rebuilt (`mutate2.sh`) to
    verify the snapshot EXISTS and is non-empty before patching, and to verify the restore
    matched afterwards. `[AMD-150]` recorded mutations that failed to apply; this is the
    worse case — a harness that cannot tell you it did nothing.
  - The length band in the Korean guard is **measured from the corpus** (0.456–0.686 across
    all 66), not guessed. The first draft's `[0.25, 1.4]` passed both a truncated cell and a
    doubled one, which makes it a check that cannot fail — `[AMD-154]`'s lesson, again.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run`. No `web/` change, so no build or render pass was due.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **The `:en:` enrich bucket was serving cross-locale text for as long as v0.62.839 has
    been live.** `v3` stops it going forward; nothing recovers what was already shown.
  - **No Korean reader has read the 66 explainers.** Hand-authored, guarded for script,
    length, duplication and stray English — none of which is a reader.
  - `placeauto:v2:` and `placeresolve:v1:` still have no lang dimension. Deliberate — they
    resolve what the user *typed* — and unchanged from `[AMD-155]`.
  - **My own coverage audit was wrong four times out of seven**: `fr=0` on four overlays
    (French lives inline in the base tables), `card.distAway/fr` (deliberately empty), and
    city plate names read 180/221/221 when the shipped guard correctly asserts 279 × 8 across
    **two** sources my regex read one of. The real gaps came from the disagreements, not from
    the counts.
  - The bot still renders **three** name lines; `country-text-match.js` is still dead code.
    Both carried forward from `[AMD-155]`, neither asked for.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The operator gated prediction work on this question; the audit ran first and the answer changed what the work was — from "author missing strings" to "stop discarding the ones we have". |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 13/13 mutations caught, each proved applied against a verified snapshot. No paid translation API. Nothing deployed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | AU-1 — the overlay's Gemini provenance header is corrected and the history kept, not replaced. D-199 — every claim here is a measurement, including the four that refuted my own first pass. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The harness defect was caught by `git status`, reported rather than tidied away, and the four outcomes it produced were discarded and re-run rather than counted. |

### [AMD-160] /forgetme kept most of what it promised to erase — and created the orphan it prevented

- **[HDR]** v0.62.898 · 02-09 '26 12:18 SGT · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · DRAFT

- **[INTENT]** Found while tracing storage for §2,911·C, not while looking for it. The operator's
  answer to the scoping question was verbatim: **"Fix it first, as its own PR"**.

- **[DELTA]**

  **The measurement, before any fix.** 29 per-chat keys seeded, `forgetUserData` run, **7 deleted
  and 22 survived** — including the entire `cab:*` cabinet tree, `rating-pref`, `country-pref`,
  `user:<id>:lang`, `user:<id>:country`, `userlocale:`, `recent-locations:`, `verbose:`,
  `funfact:lastSeen:`, the `cuisine:*` rotation state, `michelin:walk:*`, `seen:`,
  `drift-suppress:`, and **`search-conv:`, which holds up to sixteen turns of the user's own typed
  messages.**

  **⚠ And the ordering made it worse than a gap.** It deleted `clip:<chatId>` — the INDEX — without
  the card hashes it points at, while `clip-store.js recomputeCardTtl` sets a **favourited card to
  PERSIST: no TTL, ever**. So asking to be forgotten left favourited cards on disk permanently with
  nothing pointing at them — unreachable *and* undeletable. **Erasure created the orphan it was
  supposed to prevent.**

  `/privacy` says, in nine locales: *"You can clear your stored data at any time by typing
  /forgetme."* That is a published commitment, which is why this is a fix and not a feature.

  **SCAN rather than follow the index — the choice, and why it is not just tidier.** Reading
  `clip:<chatId>` to enumerate the cards would fix the ordering bug and nothing else. `SCAN
  card:<chatId>:*` also collects the orphans that **already exist** from every `/forgetme` run
  before this one, so a user erased last month is cleaned up the next time they ask. It makes
  ordering irrelevant instead of merely correct.

  - `plainKeys` 4 → **22**; `hashedKeys` 3 → **6**; new `scanPatterns` with **13** families.
  - **One hashed encoding, verified rather than assumed.** `location-locale.js` defines its own
    `_hashChatId`; computing both showed it is byte-identical to `location-cache.js`'s
    `hashChatId` — sha256 truncated to 16 hex. The plan had claimed a third encoding; there isn't
    one, and the correction came from running it.
  - **⚠ Every pattern terminates the chatId.** `cuisine:*:<chatId>*` would also match chat
    **1000000019** when erasing **100000001** — Telegram ids are numeric and one can be a prefix of
    another. So the patterns are `…:<chatId>` (exact) and `…:<chatId>:*`, never a bare trailing
    wildcard. There is a test for it, and it is the one defect here that would have destroyed a
    *different* user's data.

  **The guard is the real deliverable.** `ERASURE_EXEMPT` maps a namespace to a reason from a fixed
  set, and `__tests__/forgetme-erasure.test.js` fails in **both** directions: a namespace the code
  writes must be erased or exempted, and an exemption that stops being true fails too. The same
  shape `i18n-coverage.test.js` uses for locale exemptions, adopted for the same reason — the old
  list rotted silently, and the module's own header described eight keys as if that were the whole
  inventory. That header is why this was missed, so it is replaced rather than extended.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **249 files / 5,382 tests green.** `node --check`. Version 0.62.897 → 0.62.898.
  - **Reproduced first**: 7 of 29 before, **29 of 29** after — the same script, not a described
    outcome.
  - **9 mutations, all caught**, each proved APPLIED by `cmp` against a verified snapshot before
    its outcome was read — including the prefix-collision case (`…<chatId>*` restored) and both
    directions of the exemption guard. **A tenth was reported NOT APPLIED** (an indentation
    mismatch) and re-run rather than counted.
  - The test's fake redis is purpose-built rather than `__tests__/redis-stub.js`, which has no
    `scanIterator`: widening a stub four other suites depend on, for this suite's convenience, puts
    their green at risk for no gain.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run`. No `web/` change, so no build or render pass was due.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **Nothing recovers what is already orphaned on production** — but the next `/forgetme` from an
    affected user now collects it, which is the strongest remedy available without a migration.
  - Three namespaces stay exempt because they cannot be reached by chatId, each with a stated
    reason: `chat-freetext:query:<hash>` (hashed over `chatId|text`, so not enumerable without the
    text), `cuisine-request:<reqId>` (holds the chatId as a field, 1 h TTL), and the retired
    `buddy-intent:`/`buddy-offer:` (place- and token-major, no writer).
  - **I cannot run this against production Redis.** The reproduction and the fix are both measured
    against a fake; a real `/forgetme` on a real account is the only true test.

### [AMD-161] The same bug, reported twice — because the guard pinned one line and there were three callers

- **[HDR]** v0.62.899 · 02-09 '26 12:49 SGT · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · DRAFT

- **[INTENT]** Operator, three messages: *"when i change the language in Cuisine TMA - should not
  fire refresh eateries which is currently doing"*, then — when I said the guard looked correct —
  *"but I still see the search fired after I toggled the language"*, then, settling the design
  question I was about to ask them: *"The reload exists ONLY to re-label Google's base map tiles
  due to language change - can you ensure it doesn't fire the search"*.

- **[DELTA]**

  **⚠ This was already reported, already fixed, and already guarded — and the guard was green.**
  v0.62.865 answered the same complaint (*"When I toggle the language, remove the code to refresh
  the search, just the refresh the google map."*), added `localeReloadRestored`, gated
  `runInitialLoad` on it, and shipped `locale-reload-no-research.test.js` pinning that line. **The
  pin still passes. The search still fired.**

  `App.jsx` has **17 `runSearch(` call sites** and the flag was consulted at **one**. Two others
  fire automatically on a restored mount:

  - **the auto-detect country path** — it sets `initialLoadFiredRef` and `initialSearchDone`
    itself, so it never reaches the boot guard at all;
  - **the device/anchor coherence path** — on a **1-second timer**, so the restored list repaints
    and is overwritten a second later. That is what the operator was watching.

  The restore effect already knew about this second path's sibling: its own comment says *"the
  20 s sync would drag a correctly restored foreign pin back to the SG device GPS"*. **The pin was
  protected from the device-follow path and the search was not.**

  **This is the third instance of one shape in this arc** — the six fr-or-en Places ternaries
  (#1834), the `michelin:enrich` cache key (#1835), and now this. **One datum, several call sites,
  and only one of them asked.**

  **So the fix is one decision point, not two more patches.** `runSearch` grows an `auto` option
  and returns early when `shouldSuppressAutoSearch({ restored, auto })` says so. The asymmetry is
  deliberate: an AUTOMATIC caller opts IN to being suppressible, so a button added later cannot
  inherit the suppression and quietly stop working. The predicate lives in
  `web/_shared/lib/auto-search-gate.js` because `App.jsx` cannot be imported and driven — the same
  carve-out, for the same reason, as `bot-keyboard.js`, `venue-type-label.js` and
  `places-language.js`.

  **And the guard is re-aimed from "the fix I made is still there" to "no caller has been
  forgotten"**, which is the property that was actually violated. It now takes a census of every
  call site and requires each to be classified: explicit, automatic (`auto: true`), or gated one
  level up by `runInitialLoad` (the two `boot: true` calls). The old assertions are kept — they
  were true, just insufficient.

  **The reload itself stays.** The operator settled that in message 3, and it is the only way to
  re-label Google's base tiles: the Maps SDK fixes `language` at injection
  (`gmaps-language.js:22-26`).

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **250 files / 5,390 tests green.** 7/7 builds, 6/6 apps rendered.
  - **9 mutations, all caught**, each proved APPLIED against a verified snapshot — including the
    operator's actual bug replayed (the coherence timer ungated) and the polarity inverted (which
    would suppress *explicit* searches and open the app empty).
  - **⚠ One mutation SURVIVED and changed the test.** Removing the comment masking from the census
    left it green: the two comment-borne matches both contain `runSearch(state)` and were silently
    absorbed as "explicit", so **a census that could not tell code from prose was passing**. The
    floor `>= 17` became an exact `=== 17`, and the mutation is caught. A defence that is not
    defending is worse than no defence, because it is counted.
  - **⚠ And a test caught the module on its first run.** `shouldSuppressAutoSearch(null)` threw —
    a destructuring default applies only to `undefined`. It runs inside `runSearch`, so a throw
    would have taken out the search rather than mis-answering. `o || {}` instead.
  - The self-referential-comment trap, **eighth time this arc**: `App.jsx` discusses its own
    `runSearch(state)` call sites in prose. `maskComments` is lifted verbatim from
    `bot-ternary-sweep.test.js`.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run`, `npm run build`, `npm run test:render`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **⚠ I cannot drive the webview.** Every claim here is about which code path runs, proved by
    census and by calling the predicate. Whether the operator now sees ONE repaint instead of two
    is a human check and nothing here substitutes for it.
  - **The census's count is 17, and my first answer was 21** — a raw grep that counted two comment
    lines and the declaration. Corrected in the test header rather than left standing: a census
    whose own denominator is wrong is decoration.
  - **The ↻ review refresh is not in this PR** (the operator's first message also asked for it).
    The trace is done and it is a real piece of work: `recentReview` is **overwritten in place**
    by the translation and `v.reviews` is deleted before the payload ships, so a naive ↻ would
    translate a translation. The original survives in `place-reviews:<placeId>` (24 h), which is
    where a ↻ must read from. It also costs a Gemini call per tap and needs its own rate limiter.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The operator's third message settled the one design question I had (keep the reload), so no question was put to them that they had already answered. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 9 mutations caught, 1 survived and was reported and fixed rather than dropped. Nothing deployed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | AU-1 — the v0.62.865 assertions are kept alongside the census, with the reason they were insufficient recorded, rather than deleted. D-199 — 17 is measured after masking, and it corrects my own first count. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The surviving mutation is the finding: the census was green while unable to distinguish code from comments. |

### [AMD-163] The ↻ under the 💬 — and the review that was already a translation

- **[HDR]** v0.62.900 · 02-09 '26 13:01 SGT · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · DRAFT

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"can we have a ↻ icon below the 💬 to allow user to refresh it
  into new language, first the language of the review text is already the same as the language
  set, then fire new review"* — two instructions in one sentence, and the **first** one is the
  important half: **check before you spend.**

- **[DELTA]**

  **Why it is needed at all.** `venue.recentReview` is frozen at SEARCH time while `lang`
  re-renders live on the locale toggle, so after a switch the quote is the only thing on the card
  still speaking the previous language. The operator said as much and accepted it (*"I know the
  review would still be in the previous language"*); the ↻ is the opt-in.

  **⚠ THE OBSTACLE THE TRACE FOUND, AND IT CHANGES THE DESIGN.** `cuisine-enrich.js:404` assigns
  the translation **back onto `v.recentReview`** — the original is overwritten in place, and there
  is no `recentReviewOriginal` field anywhere in the repo. `cuisine-enrich.js:451` then deletes
  `v.reviews` before the payload ships. **So the client holds a translation and calls it the
  review.** A ↻ that re-translated what is on screen would be translating a translation, and by
  the third language switch the text would be a game of telephone that still renders as a
  quotation with quote marks around it.

  The original survives in **`place-reviews:<placeId>`** — written by `vault-index.js` with
  Google's own `languageCode` per entry, TTL 24 h. That is the only honest source, so
  `review-refresh.js` reads from there and returns **`unavailable`** when it has expired rather
  than falling back to the on-screen text. **A refusal is the correct answer; a plausible one
  built from a translation is not.**

  - **NEW `review-refresh.js`** — `refreshReviewForLocale({redis, placeId, lang, deps})` →
    `{outcome, text, sourceLang}` with `outcome ∈ already | translated | unavailable | invalid`.
    IO injected, so the guard drives it without Redis or Gemini.
  - **NEW `POST /api/cuisine/review-translate`** — inherits initData auth from the `/api/cuisine`
    mount, `cap: 60` per 15 min. Two orders of magnitude below `social-profiles`' 500 because
    unlike its neighbours **this route can cost a Gemini call per tap**. A repeat ↻ into a
    language already seen is a Redis hit on the 30-day `translate-review:v1:` key and free.
  - `lang` is validated against `SUPPORTED` before use — it is interpolated into a model prompt
    downstream, and an unbounded string on that path is not something to be relaxed about.
  - **`reviewIdx` stays pinned at 0.** `cuisine-enrich.js:400` hardcodes 0 in the cache key for
    text that came from `extractDishes`' `pool[0]` — the first *recent* non-empty review, not
    necessarily `reviews[0]`. Computing a "true" index would be **more correct and would miss the
    cache the enrichment populated**, re-spending on every tap. Matching the existing convention
    beats correcting it, and the reason is written down rather than left as a magic zero.
  - Client: `refreshReviewLanguage` in `api.js` (fails soft — the review is a nicety and a dead
    network must not put an error in front of someone who tapped a small circular arrow), a
    three-state ↻ button in `ResultCard.jsx`, and **3 new i18n keys × 9 locales**.

  **The "already" case shows the ORIGINAL, not what was on screen.** "Already in your language"
  and "still showing the last language" look identical to a reader and are not the same thing.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]**
  - **251 files / 5,400 tests green.** 7/7 builds, 6/6 apps rendered.
  - **7 mutations, all caught**, each proved APPLIED against a verified snapshot — including the
    operator's first clause deleted (translate on every tap), the permissive-direction variant
    (`!sourceLang || …` treats *unknown* as already, which would show a French reader a German
    review and call it done), `reviewIdx` unpinned, and a refusal carrying text.
  - **Four pinned counts moved together**, which is what those pins are for: cuisine base keys
    380 → 383 in two files, and the Mini App surface total 916 → 919.
  - All 27 new cells verified **by calling** `t(key, locale)`, not by reading the file.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run`, `npm run build`, `npm run test:render`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **⚠ The "already in that language" check is wrong for Latin scripts, and it is the one wrong
    answer this design accepts.** `reviewLanguagePrimary` answers from Google's declared
    `languageCode` when present, and otherwise falls back to SCRIPT detection which only
    distinguishes Hangul, Thai, kana and Han — every Latin-script language returns `null`. So a
    French review with no `languageCode` asked to refresh into English comes back marked
    `already`, in French. The alternative is to translate on every tap and bill for it, which is
    exactly what the operator asked to avoid. Recorded rather than discovered later.
  - **`place-reviews:` is a 24 h cache.** After that the ↻ says `unavailable` and cannot recover.
  - **A per-tap Gemini call is not behind `spend-guard`.** `SHEDDABLE` is about shedding optional
    enrichment; this is user-initiated and cheap per call. The rate limiter is the brake. If taps
    turn out to be frequent, a shed name is the follow-up.
  - **I cannot tap the button.** Whether the ↻ lands where the operator meant "below the 💬", and
    whether the three-state glyph reads clearly at 11px, is a human check.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Both clauses of the operator's sentence implemented, and the first one — check before you spend — is the property most of the guard is about. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 7/7 mutations caught against verified snapshots. Rate-limited at 60/15 min. Nothing deployed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — the "original is overwritten in place" claim is read out of `cuisine-enrich.js`, not assumed; the 27 new cells are verified by calling. AU-1 — the `reviewIdx: 0` convention is kept with its reason recorded rather than silently corrected. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The four pinned counts were moved only after the suite told me what they had become. |

### [AMD-165] §2,911·C — a recommender that knows the occasion and nothing about the person

- **[HDR]** v0.62.901 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · anchor **02-09 '26 14:21 SGT**
  (D-203: sensor 14:21:46 beats #1837's merge at 13:07:43 by 4,443 s — a figure the anchor guard
  corrected from a typed 4,444, the third arithmetic slip it has caught in a field asserting
  `computed_with_a_deterministic_tool`).

- **[INTENT]** Operator: *"now build §2,911·C"*, under three decisions they had already made —
  *"find a way to learn about the pre-user taste vector without user-name or user-device, but
  include meal period, weather and location and time of search and free-text query (intepreted)
  that can be translate into good data algorithm for usage across 9 locales"*; the surface
  *"Woven into the existing empty/idle states"*; and on the aggregate, *"Build it, gated, and say
  so plainly."*

- **[DELTA]**

  | File | What |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `taste-context.js` (new) | `buildContext` → `{period, dayType, weather, holiday, bucketId, liveSignals[]}`. **No identifier of any kind.** Day type from `open-hours.localNow` + `holidays.isPublicHoliday`, not `estimateWaitMinutes` (whose label collapses to `off-peak` for both a weekday midday and all of Saturday, so it cannot answer "is this a leisure occasion"). Weather read **cache-only** — a cold cache means the signal is absent, never "dry". |
  | `taste-graph.js` (new) | The 234 curated `neighboringCuisines` edges, dead code until now. Measured: 66 slugs, 234 directed, 206 into-overlay, 65.0 % reciprocated, **19 dangling target slugs**; symmetrised → **139 undirected edges, mean degree 4.21, zero isolated**. Hub-damped by `sqrt(degree/mean)`, because `singaporean` at degree 16 against a mean of 4.21 makes an undamped walk from anywhere land on Singaporean. |
  | `taste-score.js` (new) | Two stages, one rule: `score = Σ(wᵢ·termᵢ) / Σ(wᵢ over LIVE terms)`. A missing input drops a term from numerator **and** denominator, so cold start is the primary path rather than a fallback branch — and extending the dish taxonomy later improves results with no code change. |
  | `taste-why.js` (new) | Nine-locale copy composed from things already translated. `neighboringCuisines[].reason` is **English-only** — 234 strings, 1,872 cells for a field nobody asked to see — so it stays a match-time input and never reaches a render path. |
  | `taste-aggregate.js` (new) | Layer 2. `taste:{shown,kept,n}:<period>:<daytype>` — 18 buckets, 90 d, `K_MIN = 25`, `CELL_MIN = 5`. |
  | `taste-suggest.js` (new) | The one async orchestrator. One memoised `await import()` reaches the ESM shared cuisine-name table from CJS — **not** a root copy, which is exactly the defect #1834 fixed. |
  | `index.js` | `handleNoResults` localised and given the suggestion; `deliverPicks` resolves the reader's locale itself; `runFreeTextSearch` and `runPlaceAnchoredSearch` widened off their two-locale allow-lists. |
  | `i18n.js` | **13 keys × 9 locales = 117 cells**, all verified by calling. |

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **252 files / 5,427 tests green**. 27 of them
  new, in `__tests__/taste-suggest.test.js`. **13 mutations, 13 caught** — each proved APPLIED by
  `cmp` against a verified snapshot before its outcome was read.

- **⚠ [SIX DEFECTS, AND EVERY ONE CAME FROM RUNNING IT]**

  1. **The day-type term could not do what the plan said it did.** The plan: *"inverts
     familiarity … testable by flipping the day and asserting the ranking reverses"*. The first
     assertion returned `expected 1 to be less than 1`, and the arithmetic is two lines — a 0.10
     term cannot overturn a 0.30 one. Worse, its weekday branch scored dayType **equal to**
     familiarity, the parallel double-count its own comment denied. Measuring what it actually
     did: **nothing observable** — the same 13-cuisine band, every member `high`, on both days.
     A weight that cannot change an answer. The fix was not to tune it until the assertion
     passed: the term is now **absent** on a weekday (a signal that says the same thing about
     every candidate is not a signal), and the effect moved to where it can be seen, the rotation
     band, with the threshold **measured** rather than chosen:

     | gap from the top score | weekday | leisure day |
     | :--- | ---: | ---: |
     | `high` | 0.0000 | 0.0000 |
     | `medium` | 0.3000 | **0.1600** |
     | `low` | 0.5625 | 0.3000 |

     One number, `ROTATION_EPSILON = 0.20`, then does the whole job: the familiar tier alone on a
     weekday, one tier further on a weekend or public holiday, never two. Band sizes 15 → 24.

  2. **The dish "ranking" was a forty-way tie read as a ranking.** `scoredDishes[0]` looked like a
     pick. Measured on `singaporean`, the top score at breakfast, lunch and dinner is exactly
     **1.0000** and dozens of dishes hold it — `mealFit` and `explainability` both saturate while
     every other term is absent — so a 1e-6 jitter was choosing and the output presented it as a
     score. Named as a band, rotated on the bucket hash, and `dishBandSize` is returned so the
     caller can say how much of the answer was scoring.

  3. **`dim sum` seeded Singaporean.** The seeder took the first dish whose name *contained* the
     query and let iteration order decide: `singaporean/dim sum brunch` came before
     `cantonese/dim sum`. Four cuisines list a matching dish; exactly one calls it that. Exact
     name now beats substring.

  4. **`tacos` returned BBQ brisket.** Narrowing the candidate set to the seed's neighbourhood —
     itself the fix for the earlier `pierogi` → gyudon defect — fixed the *set* and not the
     *order*: american scored 0.715 at proximity 0.55 against mexican's 0.586 at proximity 1.00,
     because familiarity ranks `high` over `medium` whatever the query said. A cuisine somebody
     named is an **answer**, not a candidate. The first attempt at that pinned the seed inside
     the ranking function and broke `topBand`, which reads element 0 as the maximum — "tacos"
     became a wrong answer with a four-wide band. Ranking and choosing are different jobs.

  5. **`deliverPicks` resolved its locale BELOW the branch this release exists to fix.** So the
     empty-state decline and its suggestion were still handed an `undefined` lang and still
     answered in English. A fix placed after the early return is not a fix, and only an ordering
     assertion can see it.

  6. **A `K_MIN` mutation SURVIVED.** Every Layer-2 fixture was written as `K_MIN - 1` /
     `K_MIN + 10`, so lowering `K_MIN` from 25 to 1 — the exact change that turns one person's
     session into a "trusted" statistic about everybody — left the suite green. The fixture moved
     with the constant. Same shape as the `runSearch` census that passed while pinning one line
     of seventeen. Thresholds are absolute now, with their derivation in the test.

- **⚠ [AND A DEFECT THE TEST FOUND WHILE LOOKING FOR SOMETHING ELSE]** The `dpLang` source pin
  scanned the whole of `index.js` for the two-locale collapse and failed — first on the **comment
  four lines above the fix**, which quotes the pattern it replaced (ninth self-referential comment
  trap this arc; the mask is lifted verbatim from `bot-ternary-sweep.test.js`). Masked, it failed
  again, and that failure was real: **`deliverPicks` has fourteen call sites**, eleven passing no
  `lang` at all and three passing a value their own caller had already narrowed to fr-or-en.
  `runCuisineFlow` is the clearest — it resolves the locale **twice**, to translate the reviews
  and to write the fun fact, and then delivered the cards around that Korean review in English.
  Fifth instance of this shape in the arc, after the six Places ternaries, the `michelin:enrich`
  cache key, the pool key and the `localeReloadRestored` gate: **one datum, several call sites,
  and only one of them asked.** Resolved once now, at the layer holding the `chatId`.

- **[PRIVACY — the part that had to be right]** `/privacy` says verbatim *"No personal profile is
  created."* So the taste vector belongs to the **context**, never to a person: a rainy Tuesday
  dinner, not a user. No `chatId`, no device id, no zone, no coordinate, no query text in any
  key — asserted as a property over all 18 bucket ids, not as an intention. **There is nothing
  for `/forgetme` to delete, and that absence is the proof.** Zone and weather stay out of the key
  deliberately: 270 buckets against ~400 observations a week would take nine months to warm one,
  and a neighbourhood-plus-hour bucket at ~200 users edges toward identifying somebody. They stay
  in the *score*, where they are rules rather than statistics.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here. Carries `[AMD-164]`, #1837's merge
  record, folded forward per **D-205**.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run` (252 files / 5,427 tests). No `web/` change ⇒ no build and no
  `test:render` due. `node --check` on every changed `.js`.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **⚠ Layer 2 learns nothing on day one, and the PR says so.** ~200 users × ~2 searches/week
    ≈ 400 observations a week ÷ 18 buckets ≈ 22 per bucket per week, so a bucket clears `K_MIN`
    in about a week. **It will not discover that Tiong Bahru prefers Teochew on rainy Tuesdays,
    and must not be sold as if it might.**
  - **The dish taxonomy covers 99 of 1,697 dishes — all `singaporean`.** So `mealFit` and
    `weatherFit` are strong for Singaporean food and inert elsewhere. Highest-leverage follow-up
    by a distance, and the renormalisation rule means the backfill needs no code change.
  - **Without a query, the rotation only ever reaches the familiar tier** (and one tier further
    on a leisure day). That is deliberate — at this size the failure mode is a pick with zero
    venues in radius — but it means an unseeded suggestion will feel repetitive to a regular.
  - **`findNationByAlias` matched none of the six dish queries tested.** The dish route carries
    them all; the alias route is doing less than its comment implies and was not investigated.
  - **⚠ I cannot tell you whether the copy reads as helpful or as noise.** I can prove which
    cuisine scores highest and in which language it renders. Whether a suggestion arriving on an
    empty result is welcome or irritating is a human judgement, and no test here touches it.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | All three operator decisions implemented as stated: context-only signals, the existing empty state as the surface, Layer 2 built and gated and declared inert. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 13/13 mutations caught against verified snapshots. No LLM call, no Places call, no new network IO, no new public route. Nothing deployed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — every figure re-measured against the tree, and six of them contradicted what the plan asserted. D-205 — `[AMD-164]` rides this PR rather than opening its own. AU-1 — nothing removed. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The `reason` sentinel test runs over 66 slugs × 9 locales; the privacy check enumerates all 18 keys; the locale census counts all 14 call sites rather than pinning one. |

### [AMD-166] The search did not fire — the spinner never stopped

- **[HDR]** v0.62.902 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · rides PR #1838 · anchor
  **02-09 '26 14:38 SGT** (D-203: sensor 14:38:42 beats #1837's merge by 5,459 s).

- **[INTENT]** Operator, marked **urgent**, with three screenshots: *"When i change my language
  from Russian to German, the search did fire!!! Thorough investigation."* and *"The second line
  transaction text is in black font colour for the train station card."*

- **⚠ [FINDING 1 — the search did not fire, and that is worse]**

  `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx:528` — `const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true)`. The overlay
  covers the very first paint by design, and **the only thing that ever turned it off on a boot
  was the boot search's own `finally { setLoading(false) }`**.

  **v0.62.899 suppressed every automatic search on a locale-reload restore** — which is exactly
  what the operator asked for three messages running — and thereby removed the one caller that
  cleared the flag. The restored venues repainted underneath, and a permanent *"Suche Lokale…"*
  with a 🛑 Stop button sat on top of them. From the outside that is indistinguishable from a
  search that will not finish, which is why the report reads *"the search did fire"*.

  So: **the fix for "the search fired" manufactured a spinner that never stops.** v0.62.865 was
  incomplete; v0.62.899 completed it and broke the thing standing next to it. Both times the only
  detector was the operator's eyes.

  The census in `locale-reload-no-research.test.js` was **green and correct throughout** — every
  automatic `runSearch` caller does opt in, all 17 call sites are classified. It answers *"did a
  search run?"*, and the question that mattered was *"what is on the screen?"*. A guard cannot
  catch a defect in a dimension it does not measure.

- **⚠ [AND THE SAME DEFECT, OLDER, ONE BRANCH AWAY]** Writing the rule down as a property —
  *every `return` in `runSearch` above `setLoading(true)` must clear it* — immediately found a
  second instance that pre-dates the locale work: the **no-resolvable-location** bail sets a
  visible error (*"Pick a country and city above, then tap 🔍"*) and returns with `loading` still
  true from the mount, so the overlay covered the message it had just written. Nobody reported
  that one; it fell out of stating the rule generally instead of patching the reported case.

- **⚠ [FINDING 2 — a colour taken from the page, on a band painted by the line]**

  `StationCard.jsx:512` rendered the station's second line as `text-tg-hint` — **Telegram's hint
  grey, computed for the PAGE background** — inside a strip painted the line's own colour. The
  name one line above has always used `stripText` (`textOn(stripHex)`: white on the NE purple and
  the EW green, `#111827` on a white interchange strip). On the EW green the hint grey reads
  black; on the NE purple it survived only because that purple is dark enough to flatter it.

  **Same shape as the four before it in this arc** — the six Places ternaries, the
  `michelin:enrich` cache key, the `runSearch` gate, the fourteen `deliverPicks` call sites:
  *one datum, several call sites, and only one of them asked.* Fixed by reading `stripText`, at
  `opacity: 0.85` so the second line stays subordinate to the name rather than competing with it.
  The line rows **inside** the card body keep `text-tg-hint` — they sit on a page-coloured
  surface, where it is the right answer — and a test now pins that they are left alone.

- **[DELTA]**

  | File | What |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `web/cuisine/src/v2/App.jsx` | The restore effect clears `loading` and `bootOverlayHold`; both early returns above `setLoading(true)` clear `loading`. |
  | `web/transport/src/components/StationCard.jsx` | The strip's second line takes `stripText` at 0.85 opacity instead of the page theme's hint grey. |
  | `__tests__/strip-contrast-and-boot-overlay.test.js` (new) | Seven checks. The load-bearing one is a **structural property**, not a pinned line: it locates `runSearch`, finds the offset of `setLoading(true)`, and asserts every `return;` above it is preceded by `setLoading(false)`. A third early return lands there and has to answer the same question. |
  | `__tests__/taste-suggest.test.js` | **A literal NUL byte removed** — see below. |

- **⚠ [AND #1838 SHIPPED A NUL BYTE]** `no-nul-bytes.test.js` failed on this run and had been
  green on the last: it scans **tracked** files, and `taste-suggest.test.js` was untracked when
  the suite ran before the commit. A heredoc turned an intended `' '.repeat(50)` into
  `'\0'.repeat(50)`, which makes git treat the file as binary and its diffs unreviewable — and it
  is why `grep` had been calling that file "binary" all session, a symptom I read past twice.
  Removed here. The guard worked; it was one commit behind the thing it guards.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **253 files / 5,434 tests green**.
  `npm run build` (all six apps) and `npm run test:render` → all six mount in a headless browser.
  **5 mutations, 5 caught**, each proved APPLIED against a verified snapshot: dropping the
  restore's `setLoading(false)`; dropping it from each of the two early returns; reverting the
  strip colour to `text-tg-hint`; and flipping `useState(true)` → `useState(false)`, which the
  premise check catches because the whole rule rests on that initial value.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — pushed to PR #1838. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **Both fixes are asserted at the source, not by running the component.** `App.jsx` and
    `StationCard.jsx` are wired to Telegram, Google Maps and the network; the render smoke proves
    they mount, not that the overlay clears or that a colour is legible. Named as a weaker check
    rather than dressed up.
  - **⚠ There is no automated check that a colour is READABLE on its background.** `textOn` is
    unit-tested and now pinned for the two line colours in the screenshots, but "is this legible
    at 11px on that green" was answered by the operator's eye, and would be again.
  - **The re-language reload can still double-reload.** `hydrateFromServerOnce` dispatches
    `gia:locale` with the SERVER's language on a fresh mount, and the toggle's POST is deliberately
    fire-and-forget 400 ms before the reload — so a mount that beats the POST can flip the locale
    back and reload a second time. Not touched here: it costs a reload, not a wrong screen, and it
    is a separate change from the one the operator asked for. Recorded rather than found later.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Both reported items fixed. The first was investigated to its root — the report's own premise ("the search fired") turned out to be false, and saying so is the finding. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 5/5 mutations caught against verified snapshots. Build + render smoke green. Nothing deployed. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — the claim "the gate is complete" was re-checked against the source rather than trusted, and the completeness census turned out to be true and irrelevant. AU-1 — nothing removed; the superseded reasoning is kept in place. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The `runSearch` rule is checked as a property over the parsed function body, not as a pinned line — which is what found the second, unreported instance. |

### [AMD-168] Sizing the taxonomy backfill found three defects in the taxonomy

- **[HDR]** v0.62.903 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf`, restarted from `7735ef69` ·
  anchor **02-09 '26 15:36 SGT** (D-203: sensor 15:36:28 beats the merge by 2,404 s).

- **[INTENT]** Operator chose the dish-taxonomy backfill — **99 of 1,697 dishes classified, all
  `singaporean`** — as the next PR, and chose that **I hand-author all 1,598 rows** rather than
  dispatch the existing grounded-Gemini drafting workflow. This release is the part that has to
  land first: **run against the taxonomy as it stood, the backfill would have multiplied each
  defect below sixteenfold.**

- **⚠ [DEFECT 1 — three of the six meal periods could never be matched]**

  The drafting enum offered `breakfast | lunch | dinner | snack | anytime`; `mealPeriodSGT` asks
  about six periods. Measured over the 99 rows, exact-match dishes per period:

  | period | before | after |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: |
  | breakfast | 21 | 19 |
  | lunch | 87 | 87 |
  | **afternoon** | **0** | **17** |
  | dinner | 83 | 83 |
  | **supper** | **0** | **30** |
  | **night_supper** | **0** | **6** |

  At half the periods the taxonomy was inert **even where it existed**, and those are the periods
  a suggestion helps most — at 3pm or 4am, *what is even open and what do people eat now* is the
  whole question. `snack` (20 rows) matched no period at all: a `type` word in the `mealTime`
  field. Fixed by an **alias, not a rewrite** (AU-1) — `snack` resolves to `afternoon`, which is
  what `mealPeriodSGT` calls that window in so many words (`vibe-suggest.js:26`, "afternoon
  snack") — and by hand-authoring the three missing periods onto the 99 against Singapore's
  actual late trade rather than deriving them from the dish type. `night_supper` (03:00–07:00)
  gets **six** dishes and no more: only food a person can actually buy before dawn here.

  (The breakfast count moves 21 → 19 because two of the 21 also carry `anytime`, which
  short-circuits to 0.7 before the period is consulted. The new figure is measured **through
  `_mealFit`** rather than by scanning the array — a different and more honest question.)

- **⚠ [DEFECT 2 — the chips rendered the raw enum, in all nine locales]**

  `CuisineCategoryDrawer.jsx` printed `{m}`, `{dietary}` and `{course}` verbatim, so a Korean
  reader saw `breakfast`, `meat`, `main` in Latin script inside an otherwise Korean drawer. **This
  was a recorded debt, not a discovery** — the v0.62.469 comment said the enum text "stays English
  for now … the planned enum-label translation step". It is paid now because the backfill takes
  that chip row from 99 dishes to 1,697: **a 17× widening of an English-only surface**, in the arc
  that has spent five releases closing exactly this. **18 labels × 9 locales = 162 cells**, hand-
  written. `type` is deliberately not translated — the drawer does not render it, and a
  translation nothing displays is dead weight.

  And a smaller thing that only became wrong on translation: the chips carried CSS `capitalize`,
  which upper-cases **every** word. Harmless for single English enum values; wrong the moment the
  label is a phrase — `plat principal` → "Plat Principal", `fruits de mer` → "Fruits De Mer",
  `à toute heure` → "À Toute Heure". Removed from those three chips and nowhere else.

- **⚠ [DEFECT 3 — `drink` was a type the weather term did not know]**

  91 of the 1,598 unclassified dishes are `kind: 'drink'` and the drafting enum lists
  `type: drink`, but neither `WET_TYPES` nor `DRY_TYPES` mentioned it, so every drink landed
  neutral. A hot drink on a wet day is the clearest case `weatherFit` exists for and was the one
  case it could not answer. Split into `hot-drink` / `cold-drink`; a drink genuinely served both
  ways stays `drink` and stays neutral, because guessing how a teh tarik is drunk is worse than
  saying nothing.

- **⚠ [AND THE GENERATOR WOULD HAVE UNDONE THE EXPLANATION]** `serialize()` rewrites the overlay's
  **entire header** on every run, including the line "GENERATED, do not hand-edit" — which is now
  false, and which a future dispatch would have restored on top of the note explaining why the
  hand-edit was necessary. The header text in the script is changed, so a dispatch reproduces the
  correction instead of reverting it.

- **[DELTA]** `taste-score.js` (alias + drink types), `nation-overlay-taxonomy.generated.js` (99
  rows extended, header replaced), `scripts/draft-dish-taxonomy.mjs` (enum + serialiser),
  `web/cuisine/src/v2/lib/i18n.js` (162 cells), `CuisineCategoryDrawer.jsx` (three chips),
  `__tests__/dish-taxonomy.test.js` (new), and the three pinned Mini App counts moved together
  (cuisine 383 → 401, surface 919 → 937).

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **254 files / 5,443 tests green**.
  `npm run build` (six apps) + `npm run test:render` → all six mount headless.

  **9 mutations. 7 caught on the first pass; 1 SURVIVED and bought a test; 1 reported NOT APPLIED
  and was re-run rather than counted.**

  ⚠ **The survivor is the finding.** Reverting the mealTime chip to render the raw `{m}` left the
  suite green, because the nine-locale check asserts the **keys exist in `i18n.js`** — a fact
  about the string table that says nothing about whether anything reads it. 162 authored cells and
  a chip rendering `night_supper` in Latin script would have been true at the same time. Same
  shape as the `K_MIN` fixture that moved with its constant and the `runSearch` census that pinned
  one line of seventeen: **a guard measuring the wrong side of the join.**

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here. Carries `[AMD-167]` per **D-205**.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **1,598 rows still unclassified.** This release makes the backfill safe; it does not perform
    it. Eleven batches of ~150, packed by cuisine, are planned and measured.
  - **`night_supper` rests on six dishes.** The period is reachable, which is the property the
    guard asserts, but a six-row basis is thin and every one of them is Singaporean. It will stay
    thin until the batches reach the other 24-hour cuisines.
  - **I cannot check a colour or a chip by eye.** That the Korean labels fit an 11px pill, and
    that removing `capitalize` did not make an English chip look unfinished, are human checks.
  - **The `type` enum stays untranslated by decision, not by omission** — recorded here so a later
    audit reading "12 values, 0 translated" knows it was a choice and where to find the reason.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | The operator chose the backfill and the hand-authoring route; this is the prerequisite half, and the PR says plainly that no rows are backfilled yet. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 9 mutations: 7 caught, 1 survivor closed, 1 NOT APPLIED re-run. No API call — the Gemini drafter is edited, never dispatched. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — every figure re-measured (the regex classifier is 78.8 % accurate on 32 % of rows, so auto-seeding was rejected on evidence). AU-1 — `snack` aliased, never rewritten; the superseded header kept with its reason. D-205 — `[AMD-167]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Period reachability is asserted by CALLING `_mealFit`, not by reading the array — which is where the `snack` alias actually happens. |

### [AMD-169] #1839 merged — and one of my own verification checks was wrong

- **[HDR]** Merge record for v0.62.903 · squash `3a56c1b3`, merged 02-09 '26 **15:41:20 SGT** by
  the operator, **four seconds** after the PR went ready (15:41:16) · held per **D-205**, to ride
  the first backfill batch.

- **[VERIFICATION (by content, from `3a56c1b3`)]**

  | Claim in #1839 | Checked against the merged tree | Result |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | the `snack` alias maps to `afternoon` and nothing else | `MEALTIME_ALIAS = Object.freeze({ snack: ['afternoon'] })` | ✅ |
  | `_mealFit` resolves THROUGH the alias | `_periodsOf(dish.mealTime).has(period)` | ✅ |
  | the drink split reaches the weather sets | `'stew-curry', 'hot-drink'` · `'seafood', 'cold-drink'` | ✅ |
  | ⚠ **plain `drink` is in NEITHER set** | absent from both — neutral, not guessed | ✅ |
  | the overlay still holds 99 rows | 99 | ✅ |
  | the three missing periods reached the data | `"supper"` · `"night_supper"` · `"afternoon"` present | ✅ |
  | the legacy `snack` value is kept, not rewritten | still present | ✅ |
  | the drafting enum offers the six period ids + `anytime` | exact match | ✅ |
  | ⚠ **`snack` is NOT offered to the drafter** | absent from `MEAL_TIMES` | ✅ |
  | …but is still ACCEPTED | `MEAL_TIMES_ACCEPTED = [...MEAL_TIMES, 'snack']` | ✅ |
  | ⚠ **`serialize()` no longer restores the stale warning** | the old header line is gone from the script | ✅ |
  | all three chips call the label lookup | three `taxLabel(...)` calls | ✅ |
  | ⚠ **no `capitalize` survives on those chips** | zero in the chip block | ✅ |
  | the 162 cells landed | 18 base entries, 126 overlay rows | ✅ |
  | version | `"version": "0.62.903"` | ✅ |

  **Five are ABSENCE checks**, and they are the ones a passing suite cannot make: a surviving
  `capitalize` would still title-case `plat principal`; a `snack` still offered to the drafter
  would put the retired value back into 1,598 new rows; a `drink` quietly added to a weather set
  would be a guess dressed as data.

- **⚠ [AND ONE OF THE CHECKS WAS WRONG — mine, not the code's]** The audit asserted that the
  string *"GENERATED, do not hand-edit"* no longer appears in the overlay, and it **failed**. The
  directive is gone from line 1 — which now reads *"drafted by script, EXTENDED BY HAND"* — and
  the only occurrence left is inside the sentence that **explains its removal**. The check tested
  for the phrase rather than for the directive.
  **Tenth self-referential comment trap in this arc, and the first to catch an AUDIT rather than a
  guard.** The lesson has been reflexive for source pins for a while; it had not occurred to me
  that a one-off verification script needs the same care. It does, and more so: a guard that trips
  on prose fails loudly in CI, while an audit that trips on prose looks like a real defect in work
  that has already merged.

- **[STATUS]** PROD — on `main`, deploys via Railway. **No taxonomy rows are backfilled yet**;
  v0.62.903 makes the backfill safe to perform, which is the whole of what it claims.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** Carried forward unchanged: 1,598 rows unclassified (eleven measured batches
  planned); `night_supper` rests on six dishes, all Singaporean; the `type` enum is untranslated
  by decision; and whether a Korean chip label fits an 11px pill is a human check.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**
