### [AMD-170] Backfill batch 1 — 155 dishes, and the recommender starts telling periods apart

- **[HDR]** v0.62.904 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · anchor **02-09 '26 15:49 SGT**
  (D-203: sensor 15:49:10 beats #1839's merge by 470 s). Carries `[AMD-169]` per **D-205**.

- **[INTENT]** The first of eleven measured batches in the arc the operator chose: hand-author
  `type` / `mealTime` / `dietary` / `course` for the 1,598 unclassified dishes. **v0.62.903 made
  the backfill safe; this is the first of it actually landing.**

- **[DELTA]** **155 rows: the 64 remaining `singaporean` dishes, plus `american` (31),
  `cantonese` (30) and `japanese` (30) in full.** Overlay 99 → **254**. Pinned completeness count
  moved with it.

  `mealTime` is authored against **when a thing is actually sold here**, not against what it
  resembles: a kopitiam pours kopi from 05:00, a dessert stall opens at 15:00 and runs past
  midnight, and `night_supper` (03:00–07:00) is reserved for what a 24-hour coffeeshop pours at
  4am. Of the 64 Singaporean rows, **40 are drinks** — which is what makes the `hot-drink` /
  `cold-drink` split earn itself immediately: on a wet day a `kopi` and a `kopi peng` are not the
  same suggestion, and before v0.62.903 they were indistinguishable.

  **Two rows use the neutral `drink` deliberately** — `bourbon` and `sake`. Both are served hot
  and cold by the same establishment, so `weatherFit` drops out for them rather than asserting a
  temperature nobody chose. Using the neutral value is the point of having split the other two.

- **⚠ [THE VALIDATOR WAS PROVED TO BITE BEFORE ITS CLEAN RUN WAS BELIEVED]** The batch validated
  at **0 defects** — and a clean run from an unproven checker is not evidence, it is a
  reassurance. So nine defects were planted into a copy of the batch first: an invalid `type`, a
  dish that does not exist, a drink carrying a `course`, a food with none, `anytime` beside a real
  period (which short-circuits, so the rest would be dead weight), a duplicate period, an invalid
  `mealTime`, a `kind: drink` row given a food type, and a row that would overwrite an existing
  one. **9 of 9 caught.** Only then was the 0-defect result read as a result.

- **⚠ [THE BACKFILL CHANGES THE ANSWER, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IS NOW A TEST]**

  Reachability, over all classified dishes:

  | period | after v0.62.903 | after batch 1 |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: |
  | breakfast | 19 | **64** |
  | lunch | 87 | **159** |
  | afternoon | 17 | **98** |
  | dinner | 83 | **159** |
  | supper | 30 | **93** |
  | night_supper | 6 | **13** |

  And the behaviour, measured on the three cuisines batch 1 classified: `japanese` now answers
  **gyudon** at breakfast, **onigiri** in the afternoon, **sushi** at dinner and **okonomiyaki**
  at supper. Before batch 1 it had no typed dish at all, so every period returned the same answer
  chosen by a 1e-6 jitter over a flat field — the forty-way tie at exactly 1.0000 that
  `[AMD-165]` recorded. **A guard that only counted rows would have been green either way**, so
  the new test asserts the top dish DIFFERS across periods, and that the tie band at breakfast and
  afternoon is under ten rather than saturated. The lunch and dinner bands stay wide (20-26), and
  that is honest: a great many dishes genuinely are lunch-and-dinner dishes.

- **⚠ [AND THE `Entries:` HEADER LINE HAD GONE MISSING]** v0.62.903 replaced the overlay header
  wholesale and dropped it with the rest. `serialize()` still emits it, so a future dispatch would
  have re-added it and the diff would have read as the script inventing a line. Restored, with the
  count. It is also the cheapest thing a reader can check against the guard's pinned figure.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **254 files / 5,444 tests green**. No `web/`
  change ⇒ no build due. **3 mutations, 3 caught**, each proved APPLIED against a verified
  snapshot: dropping a whole cuisine's rows (the completeness pin), flattening one cuisine to
  `anytime` so it answers the same dish all day (the new discrimination test), and an invalid
  `type` (the enum check).

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[TEST]** `npm test -- --run`; `node --check` on the overlay; the batch validator's nine
  planted defects.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **1,443 rows remain** — ten batches, ~150 each, packed by cuisine.
  - **`night_supper` is still thin at 13**, and eleven of the thirteen are Singaporean. It will
    stay Singapore-shaped until the batches reach the other 24-hour cuisines, and a suggestion at
    4am will keep leaning local. Stated rather than discovered later.
  - **⚠ These are my classifications, at scale.** The enums are machine-checkable and the periods
    are grounded in when things are sold; whether *okonomiyaki* is fairly a supper dish in
    Singapore, or *soba* fairly `mixed` rather than `vegetarian` because of the dashi, is
    judgement. Worth spot-checking, and no test here can do it.
  - **The lunch and dinner tie bands are 20-26 wide.** `queryFit` and `rotation` are the terms
    that would break those ties, and both are absent without a query or a warm bucket.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Batch 1 of the eleven the operator's chosen arc plans, hand-authored as they chose rather than drafted. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 3/3 mutations caught. No API call. The insert refuses to overwrite an existing row rather than merging over it. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — the validator was proved against planted defects before its clean run was believed. AU-1 — the insert is additive and refuses overwrites; the missing `Entries:` line is restored rather than left. D-205 — `[AMD-169]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Reachability and discrimination are measured by CALLING `_mealFit` and `scoreDishes`, not by reading the table. |

### [AMD-172] Backfill batch 2 — and the prover caught itself

- **[HDR]** v0.62.905 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · anchor **02-09 '26 16:08 SGT**
  (D-203: sensor 16:08:27 beats #1840's merge by 451 s). Carries `[AMD-171]` per **D-205**.

- **[INTENT]** The second of eleven measured batches, with a stated target beyond the row count:
  **`night_supper` should stop being a Singapore-only period.**

- **[DELTA]** **150 rows: `korean`, `malaysian`, `north-indian`, `italian`, `french`, 30 each.**
  Overlay **254 → 404**. Only three drinks in the whole batch, against forty in batch 1, so the
  work was almost entirely `type` and period judgement.

- **⚠ [THE TARGET, MEASURED BEFORE AND AFTER]**

  | | rows | cuisines |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: |
  | before | 13 | **1** — `singaporean` 13 |
  | after | **21** | **3** — `singaporean` 13, `malaysian` 6, `korean` 2 |

  Korean and Malaysian are the two cuisines in this batch with real 24-hour trade in Singapore:
  Tanjong Pagar's Korean fried chicken and the 24-hour ox-bone soup shops; mamak stalls that never
  shut. **North Indian, Italian and French were given none** — three of five cuisines left out of
  a period is the honest answer, not an omission, because none of them has pre-dawn trade here.

  Reachability across all 404: breakfast 95, lunch 253, afternoon 141, dinner 269, supper 130,
  night_supper 21.

  **A new assertion pins the property rather than the number**: `night_supper` must draw on more
  than one cuisine. A floor, not a ratio — the right ratio is not knowable, and a floor rises as
  batches land. It exists because a period that answers only one cuisine passes every other check
  in the file: the rows are valid, the enum is respected, and reachability is satisfied by a single
  dish.

- **⚠ [AND THE PROVER CAUGHT ITSELF, WHICH IS THE FINDING]**

  `tax/prove-validator.js` plants nine defects into a copy of the batch and asserts the validator
  bites on each. Re-pointed at batch 2 it reported **0 of 9 caught** — and the validator was fine.
  Its batch override was a regex, `/const batch = \{[^;]*;/`, which stopped matching the moment
  `validate.js` switched to `const batch = require(...)`. So **the override silently never
  applied, the real (clean) batch ran nine times, and the harness printed nine verdicts it had
  never measured.**

  That is precisely the mutation-harness defect from earlier in this arc — a checker reporting
  outcomes it did not obtain — reappearing in the checker written to prevent exactly that class of
  thing. It now **throws** when the substitution does not apply, rather than reporting a MISS.

  Repaired, it read 8 of 9. The ninth was **a mis-specified plant, not a validator gap**: the
  "already exists" case pointed at `korean::kimchi`, which is NEW in this batch and therefore not
  an overwrite at all. Re-pointed at `japanese::sushi`, a row batch 1 actually landed → **9 of 9**.
  Only then was batch 2's own 0-defect result read as a result.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **254 files / 5,445 tests green**. No `web/`
  change ⇒ no build due. **4 mutations, 4 caught**, each proved APPLIED against a verified
  snapshot: dropping a cuisine's rows (completeness pin), flattening `korean` to `anytime` (the
  discrimination test, now covering korean and french as well), **stripping `night_supper` from
  every non-Singaporean row** (the new spread assertion), and an invalid `type`.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **1,293 rows remain**, nine batches.
  - **`night_supper` is 21 rows and still 62 % Singaporean.** Better than one cuisine; not yet
    balanced. The batches that will move it further are the ones carrying `hokkien`, `teochew`,
    `hong-kong`, `taiwanese` and `turkish` — all with genuine late trade.
  - **⚠ These remain my classifications.** Whether *bibimbap* is fairly `rice` rather than `veg`,
    whether a `cassoulet` belongs at `supper` in Singapore, and whether `naengmyeon` really wants
    the afternoon slot are judgements a native eater would settle faster than I can. The enums are
    machine-checked; the assignments are not.
  - **`italian` and `french` are almost entirely lunch-and-dinner**, which widens those two tie
    bands rather than narrowing them. Expected, and the reason `queryFit` matters more than the
    taxonomy for a query that names a dish.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Batch 2 of the operator's chosen arc, hand-authored, with the `night_supper` skew named as its target and measured either side. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 4/4 mutations caught. No API call. The inserter refuses overwrites, and the prover now refuses to report an outcome it did not measure. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — the spread was measured before and after, never carried; the validator was re-proved on this batch's own data rather than trusted from batch 1. D-205 — `[AMD-171]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | Spread, reachability and discrimination all measured by CALLING, not by reading the table. |

### [AMD-174] Backfill batch 3 — and a test that was satisfied by randomness

- **[HDR]** v0.62.906 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · anchor **02-09 '26 17:56 SGT**
  (D-203: sensor 17:56:07 beats #1841's merge by 1,395 s). Carries `[AMD-173]` per **D-205**.

- **[DELTA]** **147 rows: `spanish` (30), `mexican` (30), `peranakan` (29), `indonesian` (29),
  `thai` (29).** Overlay **404 → 551**. Reachability: breakfast 122, lunch 352, afternoon 203,
  dinner 366, supper 164, night_supper 21.

  **No row in this batch claims `night_supper`, and that is a decision.** Batch 2 moved that period
  from one cuisine to three by giving it to Korean and Malaysian, which have genuine 24-hour trade
  here. None of these five does — a Thai kitchen on Golden Mile, a Peranakan restaurant and a tapas
  bar all shut. Adding a 4am slot to improve a number would be the same error as the unreachable
  periods v0.62.903 fixed, only inverted.

  Two rows use the neutral `drink` again — `mezcal` and `tequila reposado`, drunk at room
  temperature, neither hot nor cold. And `thai milk tea (cha yen)` is typed `cold-drink` despite
  `kind: 'food'` in the overlay: it is a drink, and the weather term should treat it as one.

- **⚠ [TWO MUTATIONS SURVIVED, AND BOTH ARE THE SAME LESSON FROM DIFFERENT SIDES]**

  **1. The discrimination test was satisfied by RANDOMNESS.** Flattening every `thai` row to
  `mealTime: ["anytime"]` left the suite green. `anytime` short-circuits `_mealFit` to 0.7 at every
  period, so every dish ties — and the 1e-6 jitter, keyed on the bucketId, which differs per
  period, still returned a different top dish at each. The assertion "the top dish differs across
  periods" was true, and true for the wrong reason. **That is precisely the defect the test was
  written to catch — the forty-way tie at 1.0000 broken by a jitter — reappearing one level in.**

  Closed by asserting the difference comes from SCORING: at three of the four periods the winner
  must EXACT-match that period (`_mealFit === 1`), which only the taxonomy can produce. Three of
  four rather than four, so a cuisine with no breakfast trade is not pushed into inventing one.

  **2. The drink/course rule was enforced only on the way IN.** Adding `course: "dessert"` to
  `mexican::mezcal` in the shipped overlay left the suite green: that rule lived in the batch
  *validator*, which runs over a batch before insertion and never over the table. A row could
  therefore reach the overlay carrying a course it should not have — by a hand edit, a bad merge,
  or a batch that skipped validation. **A rule enforced only on the way in is not enforced on what
  is there.** The guard now checks both directions over the shipped 551.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **254 files / 5,446 tests green**. No `web/`
  change ⇒ no build due.

  **The validator was re-pointed at batch 3 AND re-proved on its own data before its clean run was
  read** — 9 of 9 planted defects caught, then 147 rows at 0 defects. That order is the whole
  point: last batch the prover itself was lying.

  **5 mutations: 3 caught, 2 SURVIVED and bought tests**, then all 5 caught on re-run — plus the
  two batch-2 mutations re-run to confirm the guard did not lose them.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **1,146 rows remain**, eight batches.
  - **`night_supper` is unchanged at 21 rows / 3 cuisines.** By design this batch, but it means the
    period is still 62 % Singaporean and will stay so until `hokkien`, `teochew`, `hong-kong`,
    `taiwanese` and `turkish` land.
  - **⚠ The discrimination test now asserts 3 of 4 periods exact-match.** That threshold is a
    judgement, not a measurement — chosen so a cuisine with no genuine breakfast is not forced to
    claim one. If a later batch trips it honestly, the right response is to look at the cuisine,
    not to lower the number.
  - **These remain my classifications**, unreviewed by anyone who eats these cuisines daily.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Batch 3 of the operator's chosen arc, hand-authored, with the `night_supper` abstention stated as a decision. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | 5 mutations: 3 caught, 2 survivors closed and re-proved. No API call. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ✅ | D-199 — the prover was re-proved on this batch's own data rather than trusted from batch 2, and the two survivors were treated as findings rather than tuned away. D-205 — `[AMD-173]` rides this PR. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | The discrimination check now reads `_mealFit` on the winning dish rather than comparing names, which is what separates a score from a coin toss. |

### [AMD-176] Backfill batch 4 — `night_supper` past half, and a floor that moved

- **[HDR]** v0.62.907 · branch `claude/handover-july-11-49uzvf` · anchor **02-09 '26 18:18 SGT**
  (D-203: sensor 18:18:59 beats #1842's merge by 517 s). Carries `[AMD-175]` per **D-205**.

- **[DELTA]** **162 rows: `south-indian` (28), `german` (28), `british` (28), `sichuan` (26),
  `chinese` (26), `filipino` (26)** — the largest and widest batch of the arc. Overlay
  **551 → 713**, past **40 %** of the 1,697.

- **⚠ [THE TARGET, AND IT LANDED]**

  | | rows | cuisines | Singaporean share |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | before batch 2 | 13 | 1 | **100 %** |
  | after batch 2 | 21 | 3 | 62 % |
  | **after batch 4** | **29** | **5** | **45 %** |

  `{singaporean 13, malaysian 6, south-indian 6, korean 2, chinese 2}`. **Below half for the first
  time.** Singapore's Indian-Muslim and South Indian eateries — Tekka, Race Course Road, the
  24-hour prata shops — genuinely run round the clock, and dosa/idli/vada with a filter coffee at
  4am is the canonical local pre-dawn meal; batch 0 had already given
  `singaporean::thosai sambal` that period for exactly this reason, so the `south-indian` slug
  carrying it is consistency rather than invention. Two `chinese` rows join them —
  `jianbing` and `baozi`, sold from pre-dawn carts. **`german`, `british`, `sichuan` and
  `filipino` get none.**

  Reachability across all 713: breakfast 159, lunch 441, afternoon 258, dinner 470, supper 204,
  night_supper 29.

- **⚠ [AND THE FLOOR RATCHETED]** The spread assertion was `> 2` with the comment *"raise it when a
  batch earns it"*. Batch 4 earned it, so it is `> 3`. **A floor that never moves has stopped being
  one** — and it is the only thing that stops the Singaporean share sliding back as later batches
  add rows without adding cuisines.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **254 files / 5,446 tests green**. No `web/`
  change ⇒ no build due. The validator was **proved at 9/9 on batch 4's own data before its clean
  run was read**, then 162 rows at 0 defects.

  **5 mutations, 5 caught**, each proved APPLIED against a verified snapshot: dropping `british`'s
  rows (completeness pin), flattening `south-indian` to `anytime` (which now fails on the
  exact-match assertion rather than merely on name-difference), **stripping `night_supper` from
  south-indian and chinese** (the ratcheted floor), giving `cask ale` a `course` (the shipped-table
  rule batch 3 bought), and an invalid `type`.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened. Nothing deployed from here.

### [AMD-178] Backfill batch 5 — past halfway, and two more guards that were watching the wrong thing

- **[HDR]** v0.62.908 · anchor **02-09 '26 18:48 SGT** (D-203: sensor 18:48:36 beats #1843's merge
  by 460 s). Carries `[AMD-177]` per **D-205**.

- **[DELTA]** **149 rows: `hokkien` (25), `teochew` (25), `lebanese` (25), `greek` (25),
  `turkish` (25), `vietnamese` (24).** Overlay **713 → 862 — 51 %, past halfway.** From here the
  taxonomy describes more of the catalogue than it does not.

  Reachability: breakfast **201** (159 before — phở, banh mi, com tam, simit, manakish), lunch 537,
  afternoon 310, dinner 575, supper 243, night_supper 32.

- **⚠ [`teochew` EARNED THE PERIOD; `hokkien` AND `turkish` DID NOT]**

  | | rows | cuisines | Singaporean share |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
  | after batch 4 | 29 | 5 | 45 % |
  | **after batch 5** | **32** | **6** | **41 %** |

  Teochew porridge shops and the 24-hour fishball-noodle and fish-soup coffeeshops open before
  dawn, and `singaporean::teochew porridge`, `::bak chor mee` and `::sliced fish soup` already
  carry the period — so the teochew slug mirroring them is consistency, not a new claim. **Hokkien
  mee and fried oyster are hawker-centre supper food and those stalls shut before dawn; Turkish
  döner runs late here but not pre-dawn.** A wider number was available and was not taken, and the
  guard now records that as a reason rather than a gap.

- **⚠ [TWO MUTATIONS SURVIVED AGAIN, AND BOTH GUARDS WERE WATCHING THE WRONG THING]**

  **1. The discrimination test checked a hand-picked list.** Flattening every `vietnamese` row to
  `anytime` left the suite green — because `vietnamese` was not on the list. The list had grown by
  about two names per batch while the table grew by 150 rows a batch, so it was watching **eleven
  of twenty-six** classified cuisines. Same shape as the `runSearch` census that pinned one line of
  seventeen. It now runs over **every fully-classified cuisine**, and the count is pinned; measured
  first, all 26 pass, so **no exemption list is needed — and an exemption nobody needs is a hole
  nobody is watching**.

  **2. The spread floor was ratcheted to one below the achieved value.** Stripping `teochew`'s
  `night_supper` rows took the count 6 → 5, and `> 4` accepts 5. **A floor set one below what has
  been achieved lets each batch silently undo the previous one's gain** — which is exactly what the
  mutation did, to the cuisine this very batch had just added. The contributor **set** is now
  pinned, each with the reason it earned the period, in the `ERASURE_EXEMPT` shape already used in
  `user-data.js`: covered, or exempt with a stated reason, and removing one is a deliberate edit
  rather than a number that quietly still passes.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **254 files / 5,446 tests green**. The
  validator was proved at **9/9** on batch 5's own data before its clean run was read, then 149
  rows at 0 defects. **5 mutations: 3 caught, 2 SURVIVED and bought tests**, then all 5 caught on
  re-run — plus batch 3's and batch 4's mutations re-run to confirm the guard did not lose them.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **835 rows remain**, six batches.
  - **⚠ Three of the five batches so far have had a surviving mutation, and every one was a guard
    measuring the wrong side of something** — a fixture that moved with its constant, a rule
    enforced on the way in but not on what is there, a test satisfied by the jitter it forbade, a
    list that stopped growing with the table, a floor set below what it was protecting. The
    pattern is stable enough to expect a sixth; the mutation pass is the only thing that has found
    any of them.
  - **These remain my classifications.** `hokkien` and `teochew` overlap heavily with
    `singaporean` by construction — several dishes appear in both, classified identically, which
    is correct and means the two slugs will sometimes answer the same thing.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

### [AMD-179] · #1844 merged — batch 5 on production · 02-09 '26 19:16 SGT

- **[HDR]** `8c6eec5e` · v0.62.908 · merged 02-09 '26 **19:05:59 SGT**. Merge record per rule 1;
  **held in the working tree and folded forward into this PR per D-205**, not opened as its own.

- **[INTENT]** Verify by content from the merged tree what the PR body claimed, rather than
  restating it from memory (D-123).

- **[DELTA]** 26 checks written against `8c6eec5e`, all 26 pass: version `0.62.908`; overlay
  **862** entries with the header's `Entries:` matching the real count; all six batch-5 cuisines
  present at their stated row counts (hokkien 25, teochew 25, lebanese 25, greek 25, turkish 25,
  vietnamese 24); every key `slug::dish` and lowercased; no invalid type enum; no drink carrying a
  course; `night_supper` at **32 rows across exactly 6 cuisines** with `teochew` present and
  `hokkien` and `turkish` absent, as the PR said in both directions; Singaporean share **41 %**
  (13/32); the completeness pin at 862; the discrimination test pinning 26 cuisines; and the
  `EARNED` set matching the measured contributor set exactly, each of the six carrying prose.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** ⚠ **The first verification run reported 12 of 23 checks FAILING, and
  every one of the twelve was wrong.** The extractor matched `'slug::dish':` with single quotes and
  read `mealTime:` as a scalar string; the file uses **double quotes** and `mealTime` is an
  **array**. So it parsed **zero** rows out of a correct 862-row file.

  What makes that worth a journal entry rather than a shrug is what the *passes* did. Four checks
  reported **PASS on the empty parse**: "overlay keys unique — 0 distinct", "no drink carries a
  course — 0 violations", "hokkien did NOT take night_supper — absent, as stated", and "EARNED
  names match the measured set — []". Every one of those is vacuously true of nothing. **A check
  that cannot distinguish "the property holds" from "there was nothing to check" is not a check**,
  and three of the four would have gone on reporting PASS for the rest of the arc. The rewritten
  verifier `require()`s the module instead of regexing it and **aborts before printing anything if
  the parse yields zero keys** — the same discipline `prove-validator.js` gained in batch 2, when
  it printed nine outcomes it had never measured.

  This is the twelfth self-inflicted measurement error in this arc and, like the others, it was
  found by a **disagreement** — the header said 862 and the diff showed +151 lines while my count
  said 0 — never by trusting one reading.

- **[STATUS]** PROD — on `main` at `8c6eec5e`, deploying to Railway on merge.

- **[TEST]** `__tests__/dish-taxonomy.test.js` 12 tests, green on the merged tree.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]** None for #1844. The reachability convention below is carried into `[AMD-180]`.

  | `package.json` | 0.62.908 → 0.62.909 |
  | `doc/.serial-state.yml`, `doc/Journal/` | anchors rotated, `commit` 1494, `journal` 1211 |

  **`pakistani` earned `night_supper`; `hong-kong` did not.** Nihari takes its name from *nahar*,
  the dawn, and it and paya are pre-dawn dishes in their own cuisine; the Desker Road and Serangoon
  Road eateries keep those hours. That is the same consistency argument `south-indian` had in batch
  4, not a new claim. Hong Kong's cha chaan teng and dai pai dong genuinely run past midnight — **in
  Hong Kong**. The HK cafés here shut by about 22:00, and the period is about where a diner in
  *Singapore* can eat at 3am. A wider number was available at every batch so far and has not once
  been taken.

  `night_supper`: **32 → 36 rows, 6 → 7 cuisines, Singaporean share 41 % → 36 %.**

  proved at **9/9 on batch 6's own data** before its clean run was read, then 163 rows at 0
  defects. **7 mutations, all proved APPLIED against a verified snapshot, all 7 caught — and the
  two that matter were caught by the assertion intended to catch them, checked in the output
  rather than assumed.**

  **⚠ Batch 5's lesson was still live, and a mutation proved it.** M7 strips `chinese`'s
  `night_supper` rows: the count goes 7 → 6, and batch 5's floor of **`> 4` would have accepted
  that**. Moving the floor to the **achieved** value rather than one below it is what fails it —
  `expected 6 to be greater than or equal to 7`. The batch-5 entry described this defect correctly
  and then fixed only the half of it that the `EARNED` set covers; the floor itself was left one
  short. **Naming a defect is not the same as closing it.**

  **⚠ And a hole nothing was watching.** Every assertion in that test looks for *loss*: the floor
  only rises, and `lost` only reports names that went missing. **A batch could hand `night_supper`
  to any cuisine at all and the suite stayed green.** M6 does exactly that to `french::croissant`
  and is now caught — `expected [ 'french' ] to deeply equal []` — by a new check asserting the
  contributor set contains *nothing but* named entries. Same bidirectional shape as the erasure
  guard in `__tests__/forgetme-erasure.test.js`: covered, or exempt with a stated reason, and
  anything else fails. **That citation was itself wrong on first write** — I named a file
  `forget-namespaces.test.js` that does not exist — and was corrected by checking rather than by
  remembering. Eleventh comment trap of the arc, first one I authored into a fix.

  **⚠ A naming defect in my own reporting, five batches old.** The figures this journal has been
  calling *reachability* count only **explicitly assigned** periods; the 11 `anytime` rows from
  batch 1 (all Singaporean kueh and snacks) are reachable at every period and were excluded. Both
  counts are correct, but only one of them is what the word means. Recorded in both conventions
  from here:

  | period | explicit (as reported before) | inclusive of `anytime` |
  | :--- | ---: | ---: |
  | breakfast | 228 | 239 |
  | lunch | 607 | 618 |
  | afternoon | 371 | 382 |
  | dinner | 688 | 699 |
  | supper | 275 | 286 |
  | night_supper | 36 | 47 |

  The guards use the **explicit** count, and correctly: an `anytime` row does not demonstrate that
  a period has a genuine claimant, which is the whole thing the spread floor exists to assert.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened.

  contributor check.

### [AMD-181] · v0.62.910 — the owner's chat id leaves the public tree · 02-09 '26 19:32 SGT

- **[HDR]** Not a planned item. The pre-push secret scan on `[AMD-180]` flagged the operator's
  Telegram chat id inside a `doc/.serial-state.yml` line my diff was **relocating**, not adding —
  and following that led to a pre-existing exposure across the whole repository.

- **[INTENT]** Operator, asked directly with four options and the trade-offs of each, chose
  **"Scrub current files only"**: replace it across the tree, add a guard so it cannot return, and
  leave git history alone. Their reasoning is sound and worth recording — rewriting history across
  1,800+ merged pull requests breaks every existing clone and every commit link, for a value that
  **grants no access to anything**.

- **[DELTA]**

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | 26 tracked files | **1,056 occurrences** replaced with a synthetic `100000001` |
  | `__tests__/no-owner-chat-id.test.js` | **new**, 3 tests — pins the SHA-256, never the value |
  | `user-data.js`, `__tests__/forgetme-erasure.test.js` | a note that the id is a synthetic stand-in |
  | `scripts`/harness (scratchpad) | `mutate2.sh` → v3 |
  | `package.json` | 0.62.909 → 0.62.910 |

  **⚠ THE FIRST COUNT WAS WRONG BY A FACTOR OF TWENTY-TWO.** `git grep -c` reported **47**, and I
  reported 47 to the operator when asking them to decide. It counts matching **lines**; the twelve
  files under `log/` are single-line JSON arrays holding hundreds of occurrences each. The real
  figure is **1,056**. The operator's answer would not have changed — but they were given a number
  that made the problem look one twentieth of its size, and *"47 places"* is what the question said.
  Fourth wrong first measurement of the session, and the first that reached the operator.

  **The mapping is not arbitrary.** `user-data.js` explains that a bare trailing wildcard on the id
  would also match the ten-digit id sharing its prefix. Because the longer value is literally the
  shorter one plus a digit, a plain string replace carries the pair across intact — the example
  still demonstrates the thing it is there to demonstrate. Asserted, not assumed.

  **The guard does not contain what it forbids.** A test storing the id would have republished it,
  in the file whose purpose is to say it must not be republished — the same self-defeating shape as
  a scanner allowlist that quotes the secret. It pins the **SHA-256** and hashes candidate digit
  runs to compare; the digest is one-way and cannot match anything else.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **255 files / 5,449 tests green**. Residual
  scan across the tree: **0**.

  **⚠ AND THE MUTATION HARNESS FAILED AGAIN, IN THE SAME CLASS IT WAS BUILT TO CLOSE.** The three
  mutations proving this guard bites all reported **"caught"** on the first run. None of them ran.
  A quoted heredoc passed the literal string `$TF` through as the test path; vitest answered *"No
  test files found, exiting with code 1"*; and the harness read exit-1 as a catch. **"No test files
  found" and a real assertion failure are both exit 1, and only one of them is evidence.**

  That is the third time this exact class has appeared: v1 of the harness failed to snapshot and
  printed four unmeasured outcomes; `prove-validator.js`'s regex override stopped matching and
  printed nine. It is now v3 — the target must exist, **and** the output must show tests actually
  executed. Re-run, the three are genuinely caught, each by the intended assertion:
  `user-data.js` named, `log/Depoly_26May2026` named (proving the scan reaches **inside** the
  single-line JSON shape that hid 1,009 of the 1,056), and the scan floor failing `expected 0 to be
  greater than 500` when the reader is disabled.

  The guard carries that floor for a reason recorded in its own header: **four checks passed
  vacuously on an empty parse earlier the same day** (`[AMD-179]`). A scan reporting "0 hits" after
  reading nothing is indistinguishable from a scan reporting "0 hits" after reading everything.

- **⚠ [THE GUARD CAUGHT ITS OWN AUTHOR]** Minutes after it went green, the anchor rotation for
  *this entry* failed it: `expected [ 'doc/.serial-state.yml' ] to deeply equal []`. The
  `last_anchor_note` I had just written to describe the scrub spelled out the ten-digit id — which
  has the forbidden one as its **prefix**. So the metadata explaining the removal reintroduced the
  value, in the same commit, sixteen minutes after 1,056 copies of it were taken out.

  **Then this paragraph did it again.** The first draft of the sentence above QUOTED the offending
  note verbatim to show what had gone wrong, and the guard failed the whole suite a second time,
  inside a minute. Both times the mechanism was identical: the id is not mentioned by name, it
  arrives as the **prefix of a larger number** that reads as a different value entirely — which is
  precisely why `user-data.js` needed that example in the first place, and precisely why no secret
  scanner ever caught it. Describing the trap is not standing outside it.

  Eleventh self-referential trap of the arc, and the first where the prose describing a fix **was**
  the regression — twice. Also the second thing today caught by a guard rather than by me; the
  first was a stop hook noticing a dirty tree. Had the guard been written after the rotation
  instead of before it, both would have shipped.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — rides #1845 with `[AMD-180]`.

- **[TEST]** `__tests__/no-owner-chat-id.test.js`, 3 tests, proved by 3 mutations.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **⚠ THE SAME LOGS CARRY THE OPERATOR'S GPS COORDINATES, AND THIS PR DOES NOT TOUCH THEM.**
    `log/` holds **50** `[set-location] chat=… → 1.4790,103.3898` lines at four-decimal precision —
    roughly 11 m — plus higher-precision `"lat"` values in the JSON payloads. Read together these
    are a movement history for a single identified person. **That is arguably more sensitive than
    the id that prompted this work**, and it is out of scope only because the operator was asked
    about the id and answered about the id. Widening a security scrub past what was authorised is
    the wrong instinct; not telling them is worse. Raised with them; awaiting a decision.
  - **Git history still contains the id** — by explicit decision, not oversight.
  - The guard scans **tracked** files. A value in an untracked file is invisible to it, which is
    exactly how the NUL byte in `[AMD-176]` shipped a commit late.

- **[CONFIRMATION GATES]**

  | Gate | State | Evidence |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | G1 — scope understood | ✅ | Exactly the option the operator picked; the coordinates found alongside were reported, not silently included. |
  | G2 — no destructive or external action | ✅ | No history rewrite, no force-push over anyone's work. 3 mutations, each applied and restored. |
  | G3 — standing rule followed | ⚠️ | P7 was honoured — the operator chose before anything was changed. But the figure they chose on (**47**) was wrong; the correct one (**1,056**) is recorded here and was reported back to them. |
  | G4 — verification by content | ✅ | 0 residual across the tree, and the guard proved to bite before its clean run was trusted. |

  stops and addresses were never connected. Scope chosen by the operator after the investigation:
  all three, with map pins showing the **translated name only** and proper nouns **transliterated**
  rather than left English.

- **[DELTA]**

  | File | Change |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | `web/_shared/lib/sg-terms-i18n.js` | **new** — 61 SG place words × 8 locales (**488 cells**), the abbreviation map, and `NOT_TERMS` naming the three that expand but must not translate |
  | `web/_shared/lib/sg-place-text.js` | **new** — composes vocabulary + proper nouns into one local string; returns `changed:false` rather than an English echo |
  | `web/_shared/lib/station-display.js` | **new** — the single answer to "what does this station read as?" |
  | `web/_shared/lib/station-card-labels.js` | +5 keys × 9 locales |
  | the three `mapOverlays.js` copies | identical edits: pill, station card, bus popup, 12 postal sites, 2 stalls sites |
  | `web/transport/src/components/MrtMapPanel.jsx` | passes `lang` explicitly to the pill |
  | `__tests__/helpers/mask-comments.js` | **new** — a corrected, regex-aware masker, replacing three broken copies |
  | `__tests__/map-locale-text.test.js` | **new**, 15 tests |

  **The station fix needed no authoring at all.** `stationName('Simei','zh')` has returned 四美地铁站
  the whole time; the overlays simply never asked. `MrtMapPanel.jsx:719` was already calling it
  correctly for the popup while the pill for the same station on the same map got
  `(nice + ' station')`. One fact, three renderings, one of them right.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **256 files / 5,464 tests green**. All seven
  Mini App builds succeed. **8 mutations, all proved applied against a verified snapshot, all 8
  caught, and for every one the failing assertion was read from the output rather than assumed.**

  **⚠ FOUR CORRECTIONS DURING THE WORK, none of which any test would have caught.**

  1. **Proper nouns were being matched token by token.** That put `ang×61`, `mo×61`, `kio×61` at the
     top of the authoring list as three separate items. 앙모키오 is a reading of the **whole name**;
     "Ang" alone has none, and three concatenated fragments would not be one either. Matching now
     takes the longest run and falls back. Getting this wrong would not have failed anything — it
     would have produced **1,472 cells of plausible-looking nonsense**. Mutation L4 proves the
     phrase matcher is load-bearing: token-wise, `Ang Mo Kio Ave 3` comes out `Ang Mo Kio 3애비뉴`.
  2. **Head-final word order.** "Opp Blk 123" rendered as 对面座123 — every morpheme correct, the
     sentence nonsense. Chinese says **123座对面**, Korean **123동 맞은편**. This is not stylistic: a
     rider reading 对面座123 cannot tell which side of the road to stand on, which is the one thing
     a bus stop name exists to say.
  3. **The joiner was keyed on the locale, not the script.** "18 Raffles Quay" in Chinese came out
     `18Raffles码头`, because the rule was "zh gets no spaces" while neither token either side of
     that gap was Chinese. Unknown proper nouns stay English **by design**, so every string here is
     mixed-script. And Korean is not Chinese — it spaces its words; only a road-type suffix and a
     counter attach.
  4. **`St` was not expanding**, because `sg-address.js` special-cases it by word index (Saint at
     the front, Street elsewhere) rather than putting it in the map. Copying the map verbatim
     copied the hole. `st: 'street'` would rewrite "St Andrew's Road" to "Street Andrew's Road" in
     six addresses this repo ships; mutation L8 proves it.

  **⚠ AND THE SHARED `maskComments` HELPER IS REGEX-BLIND.** `mapOverlays.js:266` contains
  `.replace(/"/g, '&quot;')` — a regex literal holding a quote. The helper reads it as opening a
  string, scans to the next quote, and desyncs to end of file: **83 comment lines unmasked here and
  512 in `index.js`**. So a guard asking "does the CODE contain X?" has been scanning 512 lines of
  prose in the file it exists to protect. Measured, not suspected — and measured **both ways**:
  `bot-ternary-sweep` and `bot-commands` pass identically with the corrected version, so the defect
  was **latent for them and live for this PR**, where it reported a correct fix as broken. Extracted
  to `__tests__/helpers/mask-comments.js`; all three tests now import it.

  This is the same defect the journal already records for the `runSearch` census — a check that
  cannot tell code from a comment — **still live inside the helper written against it**.

  **⚠ A PRIOR DECISION REVERSED, ON ITS OWN TERMS.** `mrt-stations-i18n.test.js` asserted the pill
  was *deliberately* not translated: *"this environment cannot render the map to check the result …
  so the pill keeps the English name UNTIL SOMEONE CAN LOOK AT IT."* The operator has now looked and
  reported it. That is the deferral reaching its stated end condition, not an override. The size
  concern is now measured rather than guessed — mean pill-label width in Latin-character
  equivalents across all 189 stations: **zh −27 %, ko −46 %, ja −29 %** (every CJK locale narrower),
  ru +2 %, de +1 %, **fr +5 %, es +10 %, id +17 %**. Worst single label: "Prince Edward Road" in
  Spanish at 35 against English's widest at 26.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **⚠ 283 proper-noun phrases remain unauthored**, measured: 375 distinct phrases across the
    repo's 439 road names, 77 already in the station table. Until they land, an unknown noun stays
    English — correct behaviour, but partial coverage. **This is the next batch.**
  - **Coverage is partial by construction.** The repo can see 439 roads; Singapore has thousands and
    bus stops arrive live from LTA. No table can close that; the fallback is the design.
  - **English output changed**: the pill now reads "Simei Station" where it read "Simei station",
    because it uses the same template the info card already used.
  - **⚠ Nobody has looked at the rendered map.** Every claim here is about strings. Whether
    123座对面 fits a bus popup, or a Spanish station pill at 35 character-equivalents collides on a
    dense map, is the one thing no test in this repo settles.
  - The three overlay copies remain three copies; this PR did not consolidate them.

  year, size, character, signature dishes. They **declined profile on that false basis** and chose
  it once corrected. A wrong answer that shapes someone else's decision is worse than a wrong
  answer that only shapes mine, and this is the second time today a figure I gave them was wrong

  after: **114 unchanged, 9 gained, 0 altered.** A containment fallback that ran first could
  displace a working join; a mutation moving it above the postal key is caught for that reason.

  `Jurong West 505 → Jurong West Hawker Centre`): **at least two distinctive tokens**, and a
  candidate set of any size but one is **refused outright** rather than guessed at.

  testing the parser directly against the phrasing NEA used *before* — which is the only way to
  hold a guard against data that is not currently in the file.

  operator marked it ready. Merge record per rule 1, folded forward per D-205 with the work below.

- **[DELTA — the merge record]** **33 checks against the merged tree, 33 pass.** 123 centres,
  511 windows, **7 partial across exactly 3 centres** (Circuit Road, Haig Road, Bendemeer), photo
  on 123 of 123 with **no `http` surviving**, description still 123 and `marketStalls` still 83
  (neither regressed), Bendemeer carrying NEA's wording verbatim; `partialFrom` exported and
  correct on both phrasings plus the negative controls; `closureKey` putting partial ahead of kind;
  amber a third colour; the card's `mgmt` line at 10px, italic, hint colour; the photo lazy with an
  `onError`; the pin badge reading `PART`; the API forwarding both fields; three i18n keys present;
  and the guard testing `partialFrom` **directly**, which is the fix for the unreachable guard.

  **One check is worth naming: the shipped `data/hawker-closures.json` is byte-identical to a fresh
  build from the CSV.** Regenerated in an isolated copy of the merged tree and compared. The file
  is generated and the rule is "never by hand"; a diff of the JSON cannot tell a hand edit from a
  regeneration, and this can.

- **[INTENT — v0.62.915]** The Sketchbook work the operator queued behind the Hawker card, and
  their question that started it: *"does the sketchbook been update with translation including
  extra data fields to store translations (eatery, address, etc) when saved"*. They chose all four
  options, with *"Hawker work first — park this"*. This unparks it.

- **[DELTA]** Two halves.

  **Half one — seven sites that each restated which locales the app speaks.** Every one was correct
  on the day it was typed; `['en','fr']` was the whole list once. What made them defects is that
  `i18n.js` grew to nine and none of them heard about it.

  | site | was | effect |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | `index.js` ×4 (copy-all / copy-one / warm-start / copy-syntax) | `['en','fr']` | seven locales' TMA toggle **discarded**; the stale Redis `/language` pref used instead |
  | `clip-store.js` | a five-locale ternary ladder | a card saved by a zh/ja/es/ko reader came back **`en`** |
  | `open-hours.js` `fmtTime` | ko absent from the 24-hour branch | a Korean hours line ending in an **English "AM"** |
  | `bot-fun-facts.js` | its own `_OVERLAY_LANGS`, three locales behind the lib's | **216 measured bodies discarded** |

  The bot's set is **deleted, not widened**: the body now resolves through the lib's `factBody()`,
  the one place that knows the precedence. A second copy of a rule is the defect; a wider second
  copy is the same defect with a later expiry date. `_FF_HEADERS` went 5 → 9 and a `_FF_SOURCE`
  table was added, because widening the body alone would have printed a Chinese fact under an
  English heading over an English "Source".

  **Half two — the Sketchbook, which was two gaps at opposite ends of one wire.**
  `ResultCard.copy()` has forwarded `nameLocal`/`nameReading`/`namePronounce`/`nameGloss` since
  v0.62.840 and `VenueCard.jsx` rendered `{v.name}` and stopped — four fields persisted, none read.
  `addressLocal` was the reverse: rendered on the live card since v0.62.895 and **never put into
  the copy payload at all** — one field read, never persisted. Both now go through the shared
  `pickNameGuide` / `pickAddressGuide`; no third copy of either rule.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **258 files / 5,516 tests green**.
  `npm run build` in `web/` clean. **10 mutations, all caught.**

  **⚠ TWO SOURCE-SCAN PINS BROKE — THE SEVENTH AND EIGHTH THIS ARC — AND BOTH WERE RIGHT TO.**
  `bot-ternary-sweep.test.js` pinned `record.lang === 'fr' ? 'fr'` in `clip-store.js` and
  `const safeLang = lang === 'fr' ? 'fr' : 'en'` in `bot-fun-facts.js` as its examples of *locale
  plumbing that must never become a `t()` call*. That purpose survives; the spelling changed. Both
  are asserted **by calling** now — the repair that file already applied to `pipeline.js`, and its
  own comment already called this "the sixth". `clip-store.js` gained `_normaliseRecord` /
  `_denormaliseRecord` exports so the clamp could be tested behaviourally at all: while they were
  private, scanning for the ladder was the *only* way to guard the rule.

  **⚠ AND A K6 FIXTURE HAD PINNED THE DEFECT.** `hours-locale-tier1.test.js` asserted
  `'오늘 휴무 · 일 11:30 AM 영업 시작'` — a Korean sentence, a Korean day label, and an English AM.
  K6 moved the *example* locale and copied the output it happened to produce. That is how a fixture
  ends up asserting the bug it walked past, and it is why the corrected line now also asserts the
  property (`not.toMatch(/AM|PM/)`) rather than only the new string.

  **The census is the guard, not four line numbers.** `__tests__/locale-allowlist-census.test.js`
  walks every runtime source file, masks comments (the **thirteenth** occurrence of the
  self-referential trap — the prose describing the fix necessarily contains `['en','fr']`), and
  fails on any narrow locale allow-list without a named exemption and a stated reason. Pinning the
  seven fixed sites would guard the past; the failure mode is an eighth copy.

  **The measurements, taken rather than assumed:** the fun-facts overlay carries **6 locales × 72
  facts**; the bot read 3, discarding **216**. `mgmt`-style hand-checks aside, the clip round trip
  was proved by pushing all nine locales through `normalise → denormalise` and confirming `pt` and
  `undefined` still clamp to `en`.

  **A self-check of my own was wrong first.** The census's "can actually fire" test asserted that
  masking removes `['en','fr']` from this very file — and failed, because the file states it
  **twice**: once in prose and once as the fixture on the line above. The fixture is *code*, and
  masking correctly left it. A self-check has to separate the occurrence it expects to survive from
  the one it expects to disappear; it is asserted on a synthetic pair now. A second draft pinned an
  exact count of 8 occurrences in `index.js` and is a floor instead — an unrelated comment edit
  should not fail a locale guard.

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened.

- **[TEST]** New `__tests__/locale-allowlist-census.test.js` (12). `name-guide-precedence.test.js`
  18 (was 11) with the Sketchbook round trip. `bot-fun-facts.test.js` 20 (was 14).

  guards:

    array survives in `index.js` CODE** while the four comments recording the fix do (so the

    discard, a hand-authored flat `ja` still beats the overlay, `fr` is unchanged, `pt` falls back,

  the corrected assertion and the `not.toMatch(/AM|PM/)` property present. 38 of 38.

  This is the **twelfth** occurrence of the self-referential comment trap and the **second in two
  PRs** where it caught a *verifier* rather than a guard — `[AMD-187]` records the same shape on
  `_containMatch`. Worth stating plainly: I masked comments in the same script for `index.js` and
  for `bot-fun-facts.js`, and did not for this one. The trap is not that masking is hard; it is that
  it has to be applied at **every** site that reads a file the fix describes, and a missed one looks
  exactly like a real defect in already-merged work.

- **[STATUS]** PROD — deployed by Railway from `main`. **Committed to the branch rather than left
  in the working tree.** D-205 forbids opening a PR whose only content is a merge record; it does
  not forbid committing one. The `2,715` ledger row records exactly the failure of holding a record
  in an ephemeral container — a stop hook, not I, noticed the tree was dirty — so the commit rides
  on `origin/<branch>`, which is where the next substantive PR is built anyway. No PR is opened.

  **⚠ AND THE ANCHOR GUARD CAUGHT THIS ENTRY'S OWN ARITHMETIC.** `last_anchor_source` first stated
  the max() delta as **1,311 s**; the guard computed **1,310** from the two timestamps the field
  itself names. The cause is worth writing down: the delta was taken with `Math.round` against a
  sensor carrying sub-second precision (`01:34:39.xxx`), so it rounded away from the whole-second
  value actually recorded. Fourth arithmetic slip this guard has caught in a field that asserts
  `computed_with_a_deterministic_tool` — and the first where the tool ran and the *rounding* lied.

  keys against 29, with `ln: lane` and `pl: place` missing server-side, so the bot rendered "Ln"
  where the Mini App rendered "Lane". The same header names `sg-nouns-i18n.generated.js` as the home
  of proper nouns; **that file has never existed either**. This is worse than an unguarded
  invariant, because a reader who checks whether it is guarded finds a sentence saying yes.

  **⚠ THE REGISTER TURNED THREE CANDIDATES AROUND.** `beach×8`, `cross×6` and `middle×1` look like
  obvious semantic heads — Beach Road, Upper Cross Street, Middle Road — and the register renders

  both `k:'p'` in the station table) and `chinese` (Chinese Garden is 裕华园, not 中华花园). **A
  frequency count says which words are common; only the register says which ones MEAN anything, and
  it disagreed with the frequency count on five of thirteen.**

  `expect(() => placeStrings('/nonexistent-dir')).toThrow()`, and a missing directory makes
  `fs.readdirSync` throw `ENOENT` **before the guard is ever reached**. Deleting the guard entirely
  left it green: it was proving that Node throws on a missing path, not that the script refuses to
  report on nothing. Fixed with directories that **exist** — one empty, one holding JSON with no
  address fields. Same shape as the census self-check twelve hours earlier, and as `[AMD-183]`'s
  verifier that parsed zero rows and printed four vacuous passes.

  because it is deliberately absent from `ABBREV` — the vocabulary's own ambiguity rule, unread by
  the script composing with it.

### [AMD-192] · v0.62.917 — the proper nouns land, and zh is the column that cannot be guessed · 03-09 '26 07:03 SGT

- **[HDR]** Rides `[AMD-191]` (`6db458f9`, #1851's merge record) per D-205.

- **[INTENT]** Operator, verbatim: *"do the ¶C·1 - 185 proper nouns. 226 phrases uncovered by the
  station table, 545 occurrences, dish-taxonomy batch 7 . 72 Korean fun facts / finish all in few
  PR"*. This is the first of the three.

- **[DELTA]** `web/_shared/lib/sg-nouns-i18n.generated.js` — **195 rows**, the file
  `sg-terms-i18n.js` has named as the home of proper nouns since v0.62.911 and which v0.62.916
  recorded as never having existed. Three batches by frequency: 29 at ≥4, 65 at 2–3, 101 singletons.
  `sg-place-text.js` consults it **after** the station table; `SG_TERMS` gained the six ordinals
  (69 → 75); the harvester gained noise and institution filters for the parse artefacts that were
  reaching the inventory as things to author a Japanese reading *for* — `Departure Hall`, `Tower A`,
  `Ura`, `Still`.

  **Coverage: 1,202 of 1,203 occurrences, 99.9%.** The single miss is `Changi Interneational` — a
  **typo in `data/`**, left unauthored on purpose. Encoding someone's slip into four locales is
  worse than the English fallback, and leaving it unanswered is the only thing that surfaces it.

- **[THE RULE THAT SHAPES EVERY ROW]** **ru/ja/ko are transliteration; zh is not, and cannot be
  guessed.** A Japanese reading of "Whampoa" follows from how the word is said. 黄埔 follows from a
  19th-century merchant's native county. Singapore's Chinese place names are historical, not
  phonetic: Bukit Merah is 红山, a *translation*; Beach Road is 美芝路, a *transliteration* of a word
  that has an obvious translation the register declined to use.

  So **`zh` is optional, and every one present carries its evidence** — a `src` naming a station
  register row, or a `why` stating where else it is established. **122 of 195 have a `zh`; all 122
  are cited.** A row without one is the file saying nobody here knows the official name, and a
  Chinese reader then sees the English, which is the documented fallback.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **260 files / 5,555 tests green**.
  `npm run build` clean. **9 mutations caught**; three findings are worth more than the count.

  **⚠ TWO MUTATIONS SURVIVED, AND BOTH ARE STRUCTURAL.** Moving the noun table *above* the register
  changed nothing, and collapsing the cell-level fall-through to a row-level return changed nothing
  — because the two tables are **disjoint by construction** (the harvester only reports what the
  register does not cover) and the register has **zero missing cells** across zh/ru/ja/ko, measured
  rather than assumed. Both paths are correct and unreachable. Kept and **named** — in the code and
  in an assertion that fires the day either fact stops holding — rather than deleted or left
  looking tested. Exactly `[AMD-187]`'s `both`-clause shape.

  **⚠ A THIRD WALKED PAST A GUARD CONDITIONED ON THE FIELD IT EDITS.** Flipping a row from `k:'p'`
  to `k:'s'` and giving it invented French left every test green: the "no Latin-script locale is
  invented for a proper noun" check only looks at rows where `k === 'p'`, so changing the kind
  **exempts a row from its own guard**. Fixed by guarding the kind — a semantic row is a claim that
  the register translates the name, and it now costs the same evidence a `zh` does.

  **⚠ AND THE `src` PREFIX CHECK CAUGHT A REAL CASE.** 15 of 16 cited elements lead their register
  form; `Canning` does not — 康宁 sits *inside* 福康宁 because the register translated "Fort" to 福
  and put it first. Weakening prefix to substring across the board would have discarded that, so
  substring is the rule and **prefix is kept as its own assertion with the one exception named**.

  Two of my own checks were wrong first, both caught by the guard rather than by me: the `why`
  length floor rejected `"Adam Road is 亚当路"` (16 chars) — which is exactly what the field is for —
  so the rule is now *quote a Chinese form OR say more than twenty characters*; and the anchor's
  arithmetic guard rejected a stated 1,629-second delta that computes to **1,628**. Fourth time that
  guard has caught a figure in a field asserting a deterministic tool produced it — and this time a
  tool *did*: `Math.round` over a millisecond difference, rounding up what the field states as whole
  seconds between two truncated clock readings. **A deterministic tool measuring the wrong quantity
  is still a wrong number.**

- **[STATUS]** DRAFT — PR opened.

- **[TEST]** New `__tests__/sg-nouns.test.js` → 22.

- **[KNOWN GAPS]**
  - **73 of 195 rows have no Chinese**, by design. Filling them needs a source this repo does not
    have; inferring them would be the 1,472-cells-of-nonsense failure `sg-place-text.js` warns of.
  - **No native speaker has read any of the 585 readings.** Unchanged from every locale release
    this arc.
  - Still owed from the operator's instruction: **dish-taxonomy batch 7** and the **72 Korean fun
    facts**. Two PRs to go.
  - **⚠ Nobody has looked at anything rendered** across #1846–#1851. Six PRs.

### [AMD-193] · #1852 merged, and batch 7 — where the guard narrowed its own batch · 03-09 '26 07:17 SGT

- **[HDR]** `148da64c` · v0.62.917 · merged 03-09 '26 **07:10:34 SGT**. Merge record per rule 1,
  folded forward per D-205 with the work below.

- **[DELTA — the merge record]** **23 checks against the merged tree, 22 pass and one is a
  correction I owe.** 195 noun rows, all three readings on every one, **every row proper** (none
  claims the register translates it), **122 with a zh and every one cited**, every `src` naming a
  real station row with the element inside its register form, 73 rows honestly carrying no zh and
  answering `null` rather than echoing English, no proper row inventing a French rendering;
  `SG_TERMS` at 75 with `Sixth Avenue` composing to 第六道 — the register's own value; both
  unreachability facts intact (zero missing register cells, tables disjoint).

  **⚠ AND A FIGURE I PUBLISHED IN FOUR PLACES WAS OFF BY ONE.** `[AMD-192]` says *"Coverage: 1,202
  of 1,203 occurrences"*, and so do #1852's body, its commit message and my reply. **The measured
  figure is 1,201 of 1,202.** I took the answered count as the total and added the single miss to
  it — a total that already contained it. The percentage is unchanged at 99.9 %, and the prior
  sentence stands as written above per AU-1 rather than being edited away; this is the correction.

  Worth naming the mechanism, because it is not the same as the earlier wrong figures. The 375, the
  chat-id 47 and the "profile data does not exist" were all things I remembered instead of
  measuring. **This one I measured, and then did arithmetic on the measurement.** A number can be
  correctly obtained and still wrong by the time it reaches a sentence.

- **[INTENT — v0.62.918]** Second of the operator's three: *"do the ¶C·1 - 185 proper nouns …
  dish-taxonomy batch 7 . 72 Korean fun facts / finish all in few PR"*.

- **[DELTA]** Batch 7 — **152 rows**, overlay **1,025 → 1,177**, past **69 %** of the 1,697.
  bengali, russian, scandinavian, brazilian, south-african, hakka, sri-lankan. Four cuisines left
  the "partially classified" state, taking the fully-classified count 33 → 40.

- **[VERIFICATION (sandbox)]** `npm test -- --run` → **260 files / 5,555 tests green**.
  The validator was **proved to bite on 9 of 9 planted defects** before the batch was run through
  it; the batch then validated at **0 defects**. **6 mutations, all caught.**

  **⚠ THE GUARD NARROWED ITS OWN BATCH, AND THAT IS THE ENTRY.** Batch 7 was authored with **four**
  new `night_supper` contributors, and the bidirectional EARNED check — written in batch 6 for
  exactly this — rejected all four as unnamed. Two survived the review it forced:

  | kept | why |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | sri-lankan | the Race Course Road kottu stalls, whose clatter runs past midnight |
  | bengali | the kati-roll shops around Desker Road and Little India |

  | removed | why not |
  | :--- | :--- |
  | brazilian (caipirinha) | late-night in São Paulo; Brazilian bars here are not a 3am trade |
  | south-african (gatsby) | late-night in Cape Town; there is no South African late trade here |

  The criterion was already written down — *"the period is about where a diner in Singapore can eat
  at 3am, not about a cuisine's home city"*, the sentence that declined hong-kong in batch 6 and
  hokkien and turkish in batch 5. **I walked past my own rule while authoring, and a guard I wrote
  two batches ago caught it.** Fourth batch running where the `night_supper` decision needed
  narrowing rather than widening. The floor is ratcheted to the **achieved** value (9), not one
  below it — the lesson batch 5's surviving mutation taught.

  **⚠ AND THE DISCRIMINATION GUARD CAUGHT A REAL GAP IN THE AUTHORING.** `hakka` exact-matched only
  **2 of 4** periods, so at two of them the winner was the tie-break rather than the taxonomy — I
  had given it nothing at breakfast or supper. Fixed by authoring **what is true**: yong tau foo is
  a supper dish in Singapore coffeeshops, and lei cha is eaten at breakfast. Not by lowering the
  threshold, which was the other way out.

  **One mutation reported NOT MEASURED rather than counted.** The harness refused it because
  vitest collected no tests — shell escaping had mangled the substitution into invalid JS. Re-run
  with the mutation in a **file** rather than a shell argument, it was caught. That is the v4
  harness doing what the v3 rebuild was for: the same escaping class that once printed three
  catches that never ran.
